**Purpose:** Develop flag detection and virtual Chern arithmetic alongside indexed support fibers, excision, Koszul classes and curve dévissage. Place the article's support definitions here and prepare the Bertini and exterior-Euler interfaces needed by its final geometric arguments.

## Bigraded cohomology and the projective bundle contract

[Open item](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory) · level 0


### Definition
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Fix a category $\mathcal C$ of schemes and scheme morphisms that is closed under the following operations: for every $X$ in $\mathcal C$, every finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module $E$ of constant positive rank, and every morphism $f:Y\to X$ in $\mathcal C$, the projective bundle $\pi:\mathbb P_X(E)\to X$, its base change $\mathbb P_Y(f^*E)\to Y$, and the induced base-change morphism are in $\mathcal C$. A finite locally free module of rank $r$ is an $\mathcal O_X$-module locally isomorphic to $\mathcal O_X^r$; a line bundle is one of rank one.

Here $f^*E$ and $f^*L$ mean pullback of sheaves of modules; the maps on cohomology denoted $f^*$ below are separate specified data. Direct sums of abelian groups mean tuples with finitely many nonzero coordinates and componentwise addition; this convention applies both to $H^{*,*}(X)=\bigoplus_{(a,b)\in\mathbb Z^2}H^{a,b}(X)$ and to the finite direct sum in clause 4. An isomorphism of abelian groups means a bijective group homomorphism. Bilinear means additive in each argument. A homogeneous element lies in a single bidegree, and a degree-preserving map carries each bidegree group to the group of the same bidegree. Rings are unital, and the zero ring with $1=0$ is allowed.

An **additively oriented bigraded theory with the projective bundle contract** on $\mathcal C$ consists of the following data and axioms.

1. For each $X$ and each $(a,b)\in\mathbb Z^2$, an abelian group $H^{a,b}(X)$. Their direct sum $H^{*,*}(X)$ is an associative unital ring, with unit $1_X\in H^{0,0}(X)$ and bilinear multiplication sending $H^{a,b}(X)\times H^{c,d}(X)$ to $H^{a+c,b+d}(X)$. It is graded-commutative in the first degree: $xy=(-1)^{ac}yx$ for homogeneous $x$ and $y$ of first degrees $a$ and $c$.

2. For every morphism $f:Y\to X$ in $\mathcal C$, a degree-preserving unital ring homomorphism $f^*:H^{*,*}(X)\to H^{*,*}(Y)$. Pullback is contravariantly functorial: $(\operatorname{id}_X)^*=\operatorname{id}$ and $(f\circ g)^*=g^*f^*$.

3. A natural group homomorphism $c_1:\operatorname{Pic}(X)\to H^{2,1}(X)$ for every $X$. Here $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ is the group of isomorphism classes of line bundles under tensor product, and the target uses addition. Naturality means $f^*c_1(L)=c_1(f^*L)$. Thus $c_1(L\otimes M)=c_1(L)+c_1(M)$ and $c_1(\mathcal O_X)=0$.

4. For every rank-$r>0$ bundle $E$ on $X$, use the quotient convention $\mathbb P_X(E)=\operatorname{Proj}_X\operatorname{Sym}(E)$, with tautological quotient line bundle $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P_X(E)}(1)$. Put $\xi=c_1(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P_X(E)}(1))$. For every $(a,b)$, the homomorphism
$$\bigoplus_{m=0}^{r-1}H^{a-2m,b-m}(X)\longrightarrow H^{a,b}(\mathbb P_X(E)),\qquad (x_m)_m\longmapsto\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\pi^*(x_m)\xi^m$$
is an isomorphism. This is the **projective bundle contract**. The rank-zero case is not included in this isomorphism.

The contract is an abstract set of data and axioms. It does not itself assert that motivic cohomology satisfies them; that assertion requires the separate motivic projective-bundle input.

### Justification
The category and scheme-morphism definitions specify the objects, morphisms, identities and composition used here. The finite-locally-free definition supplies the sheaf type, local trivializations and well-defined rank; the rank-one case agrees with the line bundles in the Picard definition. The published projective-bundle definition supplies the quotient construction, its tautological quotient and base change under the stated choice assumption. Choice is inherited from that construction; no additional choice of cohomology classes is made in this definition.

Module pullback is the tensor-product construction of [math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces). To check its rank condition, trivialize $E$ on an open $U\subseteq X$ and put $V=f^{-1}(U)$. Write $\mathcal A=(f|_V)^{-1}\mathcal O_U$ and $\varphi:\mathcal A\to\mathcal O_V$ for the structure map. Inverse image commutes with restriction to these opens. Applying the coordinate projections and insertions of $\mathcal O_U^r$ to neighbourhood representatives gives inverse maps between $(f|_V)^{-1}(\mathcal O_U^r)$ and $\mathcal A^r$: a finite tuple of representatives can be represented on their common neighbourhood, obtained by finite intersection. These maps respect restriction and pass uniquely to sheafification. Thus on $V$ the construction identifies $(f^*E)|_V$ with $\mathcal O_V\otimes_{\mathcal A}\mathcal A^r$. The maps $a\otimes(b_i)_i\mapsto(a\varphi(b_i))_i$ and $(a_i)_i\mapsto\sum_i a_i\otimes e_i$, where $e_i$ are the standard coordinate vectors, are inverse $\mathcal O_V$-linear morphisms by additivity and the balancing relation $(ac)\otimes v=a\otimes cv$; their composites fix the coordinate vectors and elementary tensors, which generate. The formulas commute with restriction, so the sheaf tensor construction and sheafification universal property give inverse morphisms of sheaves. Thus $(f^*E)|_V\cong\mathcal O_V^r$. The sets $V$ cover $Y$, so pullback preserves finite local freeness of constant rank $r$, including rank one. Pullback carries isomorphisms to isomorphisms by applying inverse image and tensoring to a map and its inverse. Consequently $c_1(f^*L)$ is defined and depends only on the class of $L$. For line bundles the canonical comparison $(a\otimes\ell)\otimes(b\otimes m)\mapsto ab\otimes(\ell\otimes m)$ gives $f^*L\otimes f^*M\cong f^*(L\otimes M)$: on common trivializing opens it is the invertible multiplication map $\mathcal O_V\otimes_{\mathcal O_V}\mathcal O_V\to\mathcal O_V$, and the formulas agree on overlaps, so the sheaf gluing and locality axioms give inverse global maps. The rank-one calculation also gives $f^*\mathcal O_X\cong\mathcal O_Y$. For composable $Z\xrightarrow{g}Y\xrightarrow{f}X$, a trivialization of $L$ on $U$ gives trivializations of $g^*f^*L$ and $(f\circ g)^*L$ on $g^{-1}f^{-1}U$; identify their frames. On an overlap the transition unit for either is the image of the original unit under the composite structure-sheaf map, so these identifications glue. The identity morphism gives the identity identification in the same way. Hence module pullback induces the contravariantly functorial group maps on Picard classes used by the naturality condition. These local sheaf calculations do not require the trivializing opens to be objects of $\mathcal C$.

The group, ring and homomorphism definitions supply the additive groups, associative unital rings, group homomorphisms and unital ring homomorphisms in clauses 1–4. The inline direct-sum construction is an abelian group: the support of a sum is contained in the union of the two finite supports, and negation preserves support. The published Picard definition supplies the group of line-bundle classes, so the domain of $c_1$ and its additivity condition are well-defined. By clause 2, $\pi^*$ is additive; by clause 1, multiplication by the fixed class $\xi^m$ is additive. Therefore each displayed summand is a homomorphism of abelian groups; its bidegree is $(a,b)$ because $\xi$ has degree $(2,1)$ and $\pi^*$ preserves degrees. The sum is finite, and requiring the resulting map to be an isomorphism is a coherent condition on the specified data.

The conditions are consistent: taking every $H^{a,b}(X)$ to be the zero group, with its zero ring multiplication and unit $0$, every pullback and every $c_1$ to be the unique map, makes both sides of the contract zero. All identities, products, naturality equations and the required isomorphisms then hold between one-element groups. This example establishes only consistency of the abstract contract, not its realization by motivic cohomology.


## Relative cofibers and quotient complexes

[Open item](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-relative-cone-quotient-model) · level 0


### Statement
Let $\mathcal A$ be an abelian category, in particular the category of abelian groups, and let $f:A\to B$ be a cochain map. Use cohomological shifts $A[s]^n=A^{n+s}$, $d_{A[s]}=(-1)^sd_A$, and put
$$Q_f^n=B^n\oplus A^{n+1},\qquad d(b,a)=(d_Bb+f(a),-d_Aa).$$
This is the cone of $f$. The maps $b\mapsto(b,0)$ and $(b,a)\mapsto a$ give the distinguished relative cofiber triangle $A\xrightarrow f B\to Q_f\to A[1]$ in $D(\mathcal A)$, under the derived-localization size convention stated below.

If each $f^n$ is monic (injective for abelian groups), define the quotient complex $C_f=B/f(A)$ by $C_f^n=\operatorname{coker}(f^n)$ and the differential induced by $d_B$. Projection $\pi:Q_f\to C_f$, $(b,a)\mapsto[b]$, is a quasi-isomorphism, meaning that every $H^n(\pi)$ is an isomorphism.

For a motivic application, let $u:M(U)\to M(X)$ be a separately supplied open-inclusion map in a triangulated category $\mathcal T$. A chosen relative motive here means an object $R$ together with a distinguished triangle $M(U)\xrightarrow u M(X)\to R\to M(U)[1]$. Suppose a functor $T:D(\mathcal A)\to\mathcal T$, an isomorphism $\eta:T(A[1])\to T(A)[1]$, and an isomorphism of triangles from the image of the complete cone triangle to this chosen triangle are supplied. Its components $\alpha:T(A)\to M(U)$, $\beta:T(B)\to M(X)$ and $\gamma:T(Q_f)\to R$ must commute with all three arrows, using $\eta$ at the connecting arrow; in particular $\beta T(f)=u\alpha$. If $f$ is termwise monic, these data give $R\cong T(C_f)$ via $T(\pi)\gamma^{-1}$. Thus a quotient-complex model for a relative motive requires this cofiber-compatible comparison in addition to a monomorphic complex model of the first arrow. No monomorphism for an arbitrary map of motives, existence of such a comparison, or unique choice of relative cone is asserted. The notation $M(U),M(X)$ denotes supplied objects; constructing the motive functor and its open-inclusion map remains application data.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** Cochain complexes $A,B$ in an abelian category and a cochain map $f$. Termwise monicity is assumed only for the quotient conclusion. The motivic application additionally assumes exactly the category, chosen triangle, functor, shift identification and complete triangle comparison in the Statement. Coordinate formulas denote biproduct morphisms, so no elementwise realization of a general abelian category is assumed.

**Goal:** Establish the cone differential, the monomorphic quotient model and its conditional transport to the chosen relative object.

[F1] An abelian category has the additive structure, kernels, cokernels and invertible coimage-to-image comparisons of [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category). The category of abelian groups is an example by [math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category). These structures supply the biproducts, quotient objects and subobject calculations used below.

[F2] Cochain objects and the relation $d_Bf=fd_A$ use [math:def-cochain-complex-in-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-cochain-complex-in-an-abelian-category) and [math:def-cochain-map](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-cochain-map). Cohomology is $H^n(C)=\ker(d_C^n)/\operatorname{im}(d_C^{n-1})$ as in [math:def-cohomology-object-of-a-cochain-complex](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-cohomology-object-of-a-cochain-complex). A complex is called contractible here if it has degree $-1$ morphisms $h$ satisfying $dh+hd=\operatorname{id}$.

[F3] The shifts, cone and derived localization are those of [math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category); quasi-isomorphisms become invertible in that localization. Whenever this derived category is used, adopt that supplier's standing size hypothesis: a small category of complexes or supplied small cofinal denominator families. Its cone triangles are distinguished by [math:thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure). No general local-smallness assertion for arbitrary unbounded derived categories is added.

[F4] A short exact sequence means exactness at all three nonzero terms, as in [math:def-exact-sequence-and-short-exact-sequence-in-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-and-short-exact-sequence-in-an-abelian-category). A termwise short exact sequence of cochain complexes in any abelian category has the long exact cohomology sequence of [math:thm-long-exact-sequence-in-cohomology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-sequence-in-cohomology).

[F5] The application category has the specified translation and distinguished triangles of [math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category). The comparison functor obeys the identity and composition laws of [math:def-functor-and-contravariant-functor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-functor-and-contravariant-functor). An isomorphism of triangles consists of three isomorphisms commuting with the first, second and connecting arrows, as in [math:def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles). The supplied $\eta$ makes the image of this particular cone triangle a triangle in $\mathcal T$; the supplied complete comparison makes it distinguished. Global exactness of $T$ is not needed or assumed.

[F6] A monomorphism is the kernel of its cokernel by [math:thm-every-monomorphism-is-the-kernel-of-its-cokernel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-every-monomorphism-is-the-kernel-of-its-cokernel). A morphism is monic exactly when its kernel is zero and epic exactly when its cokernel is zero by [math:cor-a-morphism-in-an-abelian-category-is-monic-exactly-when-its-kernel-is-zero-and-epic-exactly-when-its-cokernel-is-zero](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-a-morphism-in-an-abelian-category-is-monic-exactly-when-its-kernel-is-zero-and-epic-exactly-when-its-cokernel-is-zero). A morphism both monic and epic is an isomorphism by [math:thm-an-abelian-category-is-balanced](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-an-abelian-category-is-balanced).

### Proof

1.1 Applying the displayed differential twice gives first coordinate $d_B^2b+d_Bf(a)-fd_Aa=0$ and second coordinate $d_A^2a=0$. Thus $Q_f$ is a cochain complex; its displayed maps give the cone triangle of [F3]. If $f$ is termwise monic, $d_B$ descends to the cokernels since $d_Bf=fd_A$, so $C_f$ is a cochain complex and $\pi$ is a cochain map. In each degree $\pi$ is epic. Its kernel $K^n$ identifies with $A^n\oplus A^{n+1}$ through $(a_0,a_1)\mapsto(fa_0,a_1)$, since by [F6] a monomorphism identifies its domain with the kernel of its cokernel. The induced differential is $d_K(a_0,a_1)=(d_Aa_0+a_1,-d_Aa_1)$. Hence $0\to K\to Q_f\xrightarrow\pi C_f\to0$ is termwise short exact. All coordinate identities are identities of biproduct morphisms. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, given, algebra, construct]

2.1 On $K$ put $h(a_0,a_1)=(0,a_0)$, of degree $-1$. Then $d_Kh(a_0,a_1)=(a_0,-d_Aa_0)$ and $hd_K(a_0,a_1)=(0,d_Aa_0+a_1)$, so $d_Kh+hd_K=\operatorname{id}$. For the cycle inclusion $z:Z^n(K)\to K^n$ this identity gives $z=d_Khz$. Thus every cycle factors through the boundary subobject; the reverse inclusion follows from $d_K^2=0$. Consequently $H^n(K)=0$. Applying [F4] to step 1.1 gives the exact segment $0=H^n(K)\to H^n(Q_f)\xrightarrow{H^n(\pi)}H^n(C_f)\to H^{n+1}(K)=0$. Exactness gives zero kernel and cokernel for $H^n(\pi)$, so [F6] makes it monic, epic and hence an isomorphism for every $n$. This proves the asserted quasi-isomorphism. It is invertible in $D(\mathcal A)$ by [F3]. Under the additional application data, $\gamma:T(Q_f)\to R$ is an isomorphism and $T(\pi)$ is an isomorphism: the functor sends the two inverse identities for $\pi$ in the derived category to inverse identities. Therefore $T(\pi)\gamma^{-1}:R\to T(C_f)$ is an isomorphism, proving precisely the conditional quotient interpretation in the Statement. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, given, step 1.1, algebra] ∎


## Purity twists and cohomological shifts with explicit premises

[Open item](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-purity-shift-index) · level 0


### Statement
Let $\mathcal T$ be a locally small triangulated category with its specified translation $[1]$ and inverse $[-1]$. Let $\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb Z$ be a specified set of weights and let $(E_r)_{r\in\Lambda}$ be objects of $\mathcal T$. Fix $A\in\mathcal T$, $c\in\mathbb Z_{\geq0}$, $a\in\mathbb Z$ and $j\in\Lambda$ with $j-c\in\Lambda$. The notation $A(c)$ denotes a specified object of $\mathcal T$; no twist functor or invertibility is presupposed. Suppose that supplied isomorphisms, natural in the object variables wherever those variables vary, identify
$$\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A(c),E_j[n])\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A,E_{j-c}[n])$$
for every $n\in\mathbb Z$. Then
$$\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A(c)[2c],E_j[a])\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A,E_{j-c}[a-2c]).$$

For the mapping-complex assertion additionally fix an abelian category $\mathcal B$ and a supplied bifunctor $\mathcal R:\mathcal T^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal T\to D(\mathcal B)$, under the standing size bounds of this derived-category notation. Write $R\operatorname{Hom}(V,W)=\mathcal R(V,W)$ for this supplied cochain-model mapping construction. Assume specified natural isomorphisms for all $V,W\in\mathcal T$
$$\mathcal R(V[s],W)\cong\mathcal R(V,W)[-s]\qquad(s\in\mathbb Z),$$
and a supplied twist comparison $\mathcal R(A(c),E_j)\cong\mathcal R(A,E_{j-c})$. These are additional data, not consequences asserted for an arbitrary stable enhancement. Then, with $\simeq$ denoting isomorphism in $D(\mathcal B)$,
$$R\operatorname{Hom}(A(c)[2c],E_j)\simeq R\operatorname{Hom}(A,E_{j-c})[-2c],$$
and its degree-$a$ cohomology is naturally isomorphic to $H^{a-2c}R\operatorname{Hom}(A,E_{j-c})$. The usual abelian-group-valued case takes $\mathcal B=\mathbf{Ab}$, the category of abelian groups. No cancellation, invertibility of twists, or definition of negative-weight coefficients is inferred without the supplied comparison. In particular the indexing rule applies only within the stated weight range.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The locally small triangulated category, the integer indices $c,a,j$, the weight set $\Lambda$, the objects and the displayed Hom comparisons in the Statement. Only for the mapping-complex conclusion, also assume the specified $\mathcal R$ with its source-shift and twist-comparison isomorphisms. These comparisons are hypotheses of this conditional lemma, not imported purity or cancellation results.

**Goal:** Compute the shift without concealing the required twist comparison.

[F1] In the cochain convention, $C[s]^n=C^{n+s}$ with differential $d_{C[s]}^n=(-1)^s d_C^{n+s}$, and $H^a(C[s])=H^{a+s}(C)$. This convention is used in the supplied target $D(\mathcal B)$. [math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category)

[F2] Translation in $\mathcal T$ is an additive autoequivalence with specified inverse and coherent iterates. Applying $[-s]$ to both arguments therefore gives $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(V[s],W[a])\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(V,W[a-s])$. This Hom-set calculation uses no distinguished-triangle axiom. [math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category), [math:def-category-with-translation](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-category-with-translation)

[F3] For the mapping-complex conclusion, $\mathcal R(V[s],W)\cong\mathcal R(V,W)[-s]$ and $\mathcal R(A(c),E_j)\cong\mathcal R(A,E_{j-c})$ are exactly the supplied isomorphisms of the Statement. Here their cochain target and its shift convention have the meaning in [F1]; existence of these data is assumed, not established by [F1] or [F2].

[F4] The additional category $\mathcal B$ is assumed abelian in the sense of the published definition. The example $\mathbf{Ab}$ satisfies that definition by the published theorem. These inputs specify the target setting; they do not construct the supplied mapping bifunctor. [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category), [math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category)

[F5] For each integer $a$, cohomology factors through the derived localization as a functor $H^a:D(\mathcal B)\to\mathcal B$, under the standing size hypotheses of [F1]. It therefore sends a derived-category isomorphism and its inverse to inverse morphisms in $\mathcal B$. [math:prop-cohomology-factors-through-the-derived-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-cohomology-factors-through-the-derived-category)

[F6] A bifunctor here means a functor from the product category $\mathcal T^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal T$. Thus morphisms $f:V'\to V$ and $g:W\to W'$ give $\mathcal R(f,g):\mathcal R(V,W)\to\mathcal R(V',W')$, obeying the functor identity and composition laws. A supplied natural isomorphism has a natural inverse and commuting naturality squares; applying a functor or composing such comparisons preserves those squares. These definitions specify the meaning of the assumed data without asserting their existence. [math:def-opposite-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-opposite-category), [math:def-product-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-product-category), [math:def-functor-and-contravariant-functor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-functor-and-contravariant-functor), [math:def-natural-isomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-natural-isomorphism)

### Proof

1.1 Set $s=2c$ in [F2]. Applying the chosen inverse translation $[-2c]$ is fully faithful, and the coherence isomorphisms identify its two arguments with $A(c)$ and $E_j[a-2c]$. Hence $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A(c)[2c],E_j[a])\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A(c),E_j[a-2c])$. Apply the supplied Hom twist comparison at the integer $n=a-2c$ to obtain $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal T}(A,E_{j-c}[a-2c])$. This proves the group assertion within the specified weight range. [F2, given]

2.1 Under the additional mapping-complex premises [F3], first apply its source-shift isomorphism with $V=A(c)$, $W=E_j$ and $s=2c$, obtaining $\mathcal R(A(c)[2c],E_j)\cong\mathcal R(A(c),E_j)[-2c]$. Apply the shifted twist-comparison isomorphism to obtain $\mathcal R(A,E_{j-c})[-2c]$. By [F5], applying the cohomology functor $H^a$ preserves this derived-category isomorphism; [F1] then identifies the cohomology of the shifted target with $H^{a-2c}\mathcal R(A,E_{j-c})$. Both computations require the already supplied object $E_{j-c}$ and the respective twist comparison. They construct neither an object outside the weight set nor a twist-adjunction or purity theorem. Thus both conclusions retain exactly the stated coefficient range. In both computations, naturality wherever the supplied comparisons are natural follows by composing their commuting naturality squares and applying the shift and cohomology functors as appropriate. [F1, F3, F5, F6, given, step 1.1] ∎


## External degree-zero abelian dévissage

[Open item](item-thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-abelian-devissage-input) · level 0


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $\mathcal B\subseteq\mathcal A$ be essentially small abelian categories, with $\mathcal B$ full, inclusion exact, and $\mathcal B$ closed under subobjects and quotients taken in $\mathcal A$. Here essentially small means equivalent to a small category, whose objects and morphisms form sets. Assume every object $M$ of $\mathcal A$ has a finite decreasing filtration $M=M_0\supseteq M_1\supseteq\cdots\supseteq M_n=0$ by subobjects, with all quotients $M_r/M_{r+1}$ in $\mathcal B$. The inclusion induces an isomorphism of Grothendieck groups
$$G_0(\mathcal B)\xrightarrow{\sim}G_0(\mathcal A).$$
Here $G_0(\mathcal A)$ is the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes of objects with $[M]=[M']+[M'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to M'\to M\to M''\to0$, using an equivalent small presentation. The inclusion map sends $[B]$ to the class of the same object in $\mathcal A$. Its inverse sends $[M]$ to $\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}[M_r/M_{r+1}]$ for any permitted finite filtration (the empty sum is zero). This sum is independent of filtration and is additive in short exact sequences. Extension-closure of $\mathcal B$ inside $\mathcal A$ is not a hypothesis.

### Statement references
[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category)

[math:def-subcategory-and-full-subcategory](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subcategory-and-full-subcategory)

### Source contract
**Given:** AC and the exact abelian inclusion and filtration hypotheses above, after choosing equivalent small presentations.

**Goal:** Import the established degree-zero filtration theorem and its inverse formula.

[F1] Weibel, The K-book, Dévissage Theorem II.6.3 with its proof supplies the filtration isomorphism and inverse formula. Sections II.6.1.1–II.6.1.3 and II.6.1.5 supply the group presentation, passage to equivalent small categories and induced inclusion map, as recorded in the single source reference.

The proof uses common refinements of finite filtrations for independence and concatenates a subobject filtration with the inverse image of a quotient filtration for additivity. Geometric applications must separately construct any required ideal-power filtrations and check the categorical hypotheses; no comparison of K-theory spectra is implicit.


## External stable mapping and derived linear target interfaces

[Open item](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input) · level 0


### Statement
Work in a fixed hierarchy of universes, enlarging for Yoneda applications so the source categories are small there; all mapping spaces and diagrams under discussion are small in the chosen universe. An infinity-category retains mapping spaces and coherent higher composition. A coherent diagram is a functor from its indexing infinity-category; a limit is characterized by its mapping-space universal property, and a colimit by the dual property. A stable infinity-category $\mathcal C$ has a zero object, finite limits and colimits, with pushout squares exactly pullback squares. Its opposite is stable; its homotopy category $h\mathcal C$, with morphisms $\pi_0\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C}$, is triangulated by suspension and cofiber triangles. A cofiber of $A\to B$ is a pushout $B\coprod_A0$; a fiber of $V\to W$ is a pullback $V\times_W0$.

For an object $E$ of an infinity-category $\mathcal D$, the functor $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal D}(E,-)$ preserves existing small limits. In particular $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C}(-,E)$ is left-exact on $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}$.

For any stable infinity-category $\mathcal D$, every left-exact functor $F:\mathcal D\to\mathcal S$ has an essentially unique left-exact lift $\widehat F:\mathcal D\to\mathrm{Sp}$ with $\Omega^\infty\widehat F\simeq F$. Here $\mathcal S$ denotes spaces, left-exact means preserving finite limits, and $\mathrm{Sp}$ denotes spectra, equivalently coherent towers $Y_n\simeq\Omega Y_{n+1}$. The category $\mathrm{Sp}$ is stable, so its terminal object is zero.

For a commutative ring $R$, let $HR$ be its Eilenberg-Mac Lane ring spectrum. The derived infinity-category $\mathcal D(R)$ of complexes of $R$-modules localized at quasi-isomorphisms is equivalent to $\operatorname{Mod}_{HR}$. The forgetful functor $\operatorname{Mod}_{HR}\to\mathrm{Sp}$ detects equivalences and computes finite limits. The notation $D(R)$ means $h\mathcal D(R)$.

### Statement references
[math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category)

### Source contract
**Given:** The stated universes, a stable infinity-category, and, only for the linear target, a commutative ring $R$.

[F1] The stable-category and spectrum-lifting interfaces are imported from the first source record. The conventional meaning of triangulated category is supplied by [math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category).

[F2] The forgetful limit criterion and conservativity are imported from the second source record.

[F3] The derived-module comparison is imported from the third source record.

[F4] The corepresentable mapping-space limit interface is the Yoneda and pointwise-limit consequence in the fourth source record.

### External input
Import [F1], [F2], [F3] and [F4] with the hypotheses and reading limits in their source records. Infinity-categories, mapping spaces and coherent diagrams, including the mapping-space universal properties of limits and colimits, are literature inputs. No construction of these foundations is claimed here. In particular this input neither supplies an enhancement of an arbitrary triangulated category nor represents any specified cohomology theory by mapping objects.


## Projective bundle pullback detects cohomology classes

[Open item](item-lem-projective-pullback-detection) · level 1


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and let $H^{*,*}$ be an additively oriented bigraded theory with the projective bundle contract on a category $\mathcal C$ as specified in the contract. For a finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module $E$ of constant rank $r>0$, the pullback $\pi^*:H^{*,*}(X)\to H^{*,*}(\mathbb P_X(E))$ along the projective bundle projection is injective. Under the contract's direct-sum isomorphism, $\pi^*$ is the map from the summand indexed by zero. Consequently, pullback along any finite composite of such projective-bundle projections is injective. This conclusion does not assert injectivity for arbitrary pullbacks.

### Statement references
[def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The Axiom of Choice, the category and theory in the statement, an object $X$ of $\mathcal C$, and a finite locally free module $E$ of constant rank $r>0$. A finite locally free module of rank $r$ is locally isomorphic to $\mathcal O_X^r$; $\mathbb P_X(E)$ uses the quotient convention.

**Goal:** Prove the stated injectivity and its preservation under finite iteration.

[F1] The projective bundle contract says that, for each $(a,b)\in\mathbb Z^2$, the map $\Phi_{a,b}:\bigoplus_{m=0}^{r-1}H^{a-2m,b-m}(X)\to H^{a,b}(\mathbb P_X(E))$ sending $(x_m)$ to $\sum_m\pi^*(x_m)\xi^m$ is an isomorphism, where $\xi$ is the first Chern class of the tautological quotient line bundle. Pullbacks preserve bidegrees and are contravariantly functorial; $H^{*,*}$ is the direct sum of its bidegree groups ([def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory)).

### Proof
**Proof technique:** Identify pullback with a direct-summand inclusion and compose injective maps.

1.1 Fix $(a,b)$. Since $r>0$, the index zero occurs in the direct sum of [F1]. Let $j_0$ insert $x\in H^{a,b}(X)$ in that summand and put zero in all other summands. The coordinate projection onto that summand is a left inverse to $j_0$, so $j_0$ is injective. By the displayed formula, $\Phi_{a,b}j_0(x)=\pi^*(x)\xi^0=\pi^*(x)$. As $\Phi_{a,b}$ is an isomorphism, $\pi^*:H^{a,b}(X)\to H^{a,b}(\mathbb P_X(E))$ is injective. [F1, construct]

2.1 A class in $H^{*,*}(X)$ has finitely many nonzero bidegree components, and pullback preserves bidegrees. If its pullback vanishes, each component pulls back to zero and is therefore zero by step 1.1. Thus $\pi^*$ is injective on the whole bigraded group. For a finite tower of projections satisfying the same positive-rank hypotheses at each stage, each pullback is injective by the same argument; their composite is injective, and functoriality identifies it with pullback along the composite projection. The empty composite gives the identity. [F1, step 1.1] ∎


## Flag towers with line-bundle quotients

[Open item](item-lem-flag-tower-line-quotients) · level 1


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $X$ be a scheme and $E$ a vector bundle of constant rank $r\geq 0$. There is a finite tower $q:Y\to X$, obtained by successively projectivizing the current positive-rank kernel in the line-quotient convention, such that $q^*E$ has a filtration
$$0=F_0\subset F_1\subset\cdots\subset F_r=q^*E$$
whose successive quotients $F_i/F_{i-1}$ are line bundles. Each inclusion is locally split. For $r=0$ take $Y=X$ and the zero filtration. No global splitting of the extensions is asserted.

If $X$ is quasi-compact and quasi-separated, then $Y$ is also quasi-compact and quasi-separated. For finitely many vector bundles, each of constant rank, their towers can be concatenated to obtain one such $Y$ and a filtration for every pulled-back bundle. More generally, over a quasi-compact and quasi-separated $X$, the same simultaneous conclusion holds for finitely many finite locally free bundles of possibly nonconstant rank, with a locally varying length of filtration: construct the tower separately on the finitely many open-and-closed common rank strata and take their finite disjoint union.

A **vector bundle** is a finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module; its rank is the number of basis elements on a local free chart. A **line bundle** is a locally free module of rank one. A filtration is **locally split** when each inclusion is locally the inclusion of a direct summand. A scheme is **quasi-compact** if every open cover has a finite subcover, and **quasi-separated** if the intersection of any two quasi-compact open subsets is quasi-compact. A common rank stratum is the subset on which every bundle in the given finite family has its prescribed rank.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The Axiom of Choice, a scheme $X$, and the finite locally free bundles specified in the statement.

**Goal:** Construct the tower and locally split line-quotient filtrations, and prove the stated quasi-compactness and quasi-separatedness properties.

[F1] For a finite locally free bundle $G$ on a scheme $S$, its projective bundle $\pi:\mathbb P_S(G)\to S$ represents invertible quotients and has a tautological surjection $\pi^*G\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal O(1)$ onto a line bundle. The frames and base-change identifications in the projective-bundle definition identify its restriction over a trivializing open $U$ for $G$ of positive rank $n$ with $\mathbb P^{n-1}_U$. The representing theorem supplies the tautological line quotient; the definition supplies this local identification. Both inherit the Axiom of Choice. [math:thm-projective-bundle-represents-line-quotients](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-projective-bundle-represents-line-quotients), [math:def-projective-bundle-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-projective-bundle-scheme)

[F2] The kernel of a surjection of finite locally free modules is finite locally free; the surjection splits locally and the kernel rank is the difference of ranks. [lem-surjection-kernel-bundle](item-lem-surjection-kernel-bundle)

[F3] Over an affine base, projective space has finitely many standard affine charts, and its diagonal over the base is a closed immersion. These constructions restrict over open base subschemes. [math:def-relative-projective-space-standard-charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-relative-projective-space-standard-charts), [math:lem-projective-space-diagonal-closed](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-projective-space-diagonal-closed)

[F4] The Axiom of Choice supplies the choices inherited from [F1], the affine quasi-compactness and closed-immersion results below, and the selection of trivializing covers. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F5] A scheme has affine neighbourhoods at every point. For a finite locally free sheaf, the rank is independent of the chosen local basis and is locally constant; rank zero means the zero sheaf. Pullback along a morphism of schemes is the module sheaf $f^*G=\mathcal O_T\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal O_S}f^{-1}G$. On a local free chart it takes the standard basis to the standard basis, and matrices of module maps to their images under the structure ring maps. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme), [math:def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank), [math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces)

[F6] A morphism is affine if the inverse image of every affine open of the target is affine. A fibre product of affine schemes over an affine scheme is affine, namely $\operatorname{Spec}(B\otimes_A C)$ for ring maps $A\to B,A\to C$. Under AC, closed immersions survive base change, and a closed subscheme of $\operatorname{Spec}A$ is $\operatorname{Spec}(A/I)$ for an ideal $I$. [math:def-affine-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-morphism-schemes), [math:thm-affine-fibre-product-tensor-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-fibre-product-tensor-ring), [math:lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change)

[F7] In an affine spectrum, the distinguished opens $D(f)=\{\mathfrak p:f\notin\mathfrak p\}$ form a basis, and with their restricted structure sheaves they are affine schemes $\operatorname{Spec}(A_f)$. Under AC, every affine scheme is quasi-compact. Thus the affine neighbourhoods in [F5] can be refined inside any specified open subset. [math:def-affine-scheme-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme-spectrum), [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion), [math:cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact) On $\operatorname{Spec}A$, $\Gamma(D(f),\mathcal O)=A_f$, with restriction given by localization; in particular $\Gamma(\operatorname{Spec}A,\mathcal O)=A$ by taking $f=1$. Thus the nonvanishing locus of a section corresponding to $a\in A$ is the distinguished open $D(a)$. [math:thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme)

[F8] Locally ringed spaces glue along open overlap isomorphisms satisfying identity and cocycle conditions. Applying this with empty overlaps between distinct schemes gives their disjoint union as a locally ringed space; it is a scheme because every point has an affine neighbourhood in its component. Compatible morphisms of schemes on an open cover glue uniquely. [math:thm-gluing-ringed-and-locally-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-ringed-and-locally-ringed-spaces), [math:lem-morphism-schemes-local-on-source-target](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-morphism-schemes-local-on-source-target), [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) Compatible sheaves of modules on a fixed ringed space glue by the compatible-family construction. For a disjoint open cover the overlap conditions are vacuous; maps between the glued module sheaves are defined on section families componentwise. [math:thm-gluing-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-sheaves)

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Iterate tautological quotients, pull locally split exact sequences to the final stage, and check the topology on affine charts.

1.1 Set $X_0=X$ and $K_0=E$. For $j=1,\ldots,r$, provided $K_{j-1}$ has rank $r-j+1$, put $X_j=\mathbb P_{X_{j-1}}(K_{j-1})$ with projection $p_j$, and define $K_j$ as the kernel of the tautological surjection $p_j^*K_{j-1}\twoheadrightarrow L_j:=\mathcal O_{X_j}(1)$. By [F1], $L_j$ is a line bundle, and by [F2] $K_j$ is a vector bundle of rank $r-j$ and the resulting short exact sequence is locally split. Induction constructs all $r$ stages. The rank-zero bundle $K_r$ is zero. For $r=0$ no stage is taken. [given, F1, F2, F5, construct]

2.1 Put $Y=X_r$ and $q=p_1\circ\cdots\circ p_r$, with the identity map when $r=0$. Pull every sequence of step 1.1 to $Y$ along the composite of the subsequent projections. Refine its splitting neighbourhoods to free charts for kernel and quotient. On such a chart the inclusion and surjection are coordinate inclusion and projection. By [F5], pullback takes these matrices to the same coordinate matrices over the source structure sheaf, so the pulled-back sequence is split exact and its kernel is a locally direct summand. The same matrix rule is compatible with composition of the structure ring maps, identifying iterated pullback with pullback along the composite. Write $\overline K_j$ for the pullback of $K_j$ to $Y$, and $\overline L_j$ for the pullback of $L_j$. Then $\overline K_0=q^*E$, $\overline K_r=0$, and $0\longrightarrow\overline K_j\longrightarrow\overline K_{j-1}\longrightarrow\overline L_j\longrightarrow0$ is locally split, with $\overline L_j$ a line bundle. Set $F_i=\overline K_{r-i}$. This gives the required filtration, with $F_i/F_{i-1}\cong\overline L_{r-i+1}$. Pullback preserves local freeness because it takes a local free basis to a local free basis. [F1, F5, step 1.1, construct]

3.1 We prove the topological assertion for one projective-bundle stage $\pi:P=\mathbb P_S(G)\to S$ with $S$ quasi-compact and quasi-separated and $G$ of positive constant rank. Choose a finite affine cover $U_1,\ldots,U_m$ of $S$ on which $G$ is free: refine a trivializing cover by affine opens and use quasi-compactness. By [F1] and [F3], each $\pi^{-1}(U_i)$ is covered by finitely many standard affine charts, so $P$ is quasi-compact. [F1, F3, F4, F5, F7, construct, step 2.1]

4.1 Let $A$ and $B$ be two of those affine charts, lying over $U_i$ and $U_j$. The intersection $U_i\cap U_j$ is quasi-compact because $S$ is quasi-separated; cover it by finitely many affine opens $W_\nu$. The inverse images of $W_\nu$ in $A$ and $B$ are affine: each chart is affine over its base open, and the inverse image of an affine open under an affine morphism is affine. They are affine opens of $P_{W_\nu}\cong\mathbb P^{n-1}_{W_\nu}$. Their intersection is affine, since the diagonal of this projective space over the affine scheme $W_\nu$ is closed by [F3]: a pair of maps into the two opens maps into that diagonal exactly when its two maps to projective space agree, so its inverse image is the intersection. By [F6] this inverse image is a closed subscheme of the affine fibre product of the two affine opens, and is therefore affine. These finitely many intersections cover $A\cap B$, so $A\cap B$ is quasi-compact. Here every affine open is quasi-compact by [F7]. To check the stated definition of quasi-separatedness, take any two quasi-compact opens and cover each by finitely many principal affine opens lying inside the chosen chart cover, using [F7]. The intersection of a principal open $D_A(f)$ with a principal open $D_B(g)$ is the nonvanishing locus of the product of the restrictions of $f$ and $g$ on $A\cap B$. On a quasi-compact scheme, the nonvanishing locus of any global function is quasi-compact: take a finite affine cover and apply [F7] to its principal opens. Thus each such intersection is quasi-compact; the intersection of the original two opens is a finite union of these intersections, hence quasi-compact. Thus $P$ is quasi-separated. [F1, F3, F6, F7, step 3.1, algebra]

5.1 Applying steps 3.1–4.1 successively to the finitely many stages in step 1.1 shows that $Y$ is quasi-compact and quasi-separated whenever $X$ is. The rank-zero case is immediate because $Y=X$. [step 1.1, step 3.1, step 4.1]

6.1 For a finite family of constant-rank bundles, first construct the tower for the first bundle, then pull the second bundle back to its top and construct its tower, and continue. Pullback of each previously constructed locally split filtration remains a locally split filtration with line-bundle quotients, by the argument of step 2.1. The concatenation is finite, and step 5.1 applies at every stage when the initial scheme is quasi-compact and quasi-separated. [step 2.1, step 5.1, construct]

7.1 For a finite family with locally varying ranks on a quasi-compact quasi-separated $X$, each rank function is well defined and locally constant by [F5]: chart ranks agree on overlaps, and a local free chart has a fixed number of basis elements. Hence every common rank stratum is open, its complement is the union of the other open strata, and it is therefore also closed. The common strata form an open cover of $X$; quasi-compactness makes the set of nonempty strata finite. Each is quasi-compact as a closed subset of $X$ and quasi-separated as an open subscheme: intersections of its quasi-compact opens are intersections in $X$. Apply step 6.1 on each stratum and take the finite disjoint union of the resulting towers. By [F8] this is a scheme, with the component structure sheaves on its disjoint open components, and the component maps to $X$ glue to a morphism $q:Y\to X$. For each bundle $E$ in the family, let $N$ be the maximum of $0$ and its finitely many stratum ranks. On a component where its filtration has length $r$, extend it to indices $0,\ldots,N$ by repeating its final term at indices greater than $r$. For each index these component modules give gluing data on the fixed ringed space $Y$: distinct components have empty overlaps, so compatibility is automatic. By [F8] the modules glue. On an open $V\subseteq Y$, a section of a glued module is a family of sections on $V\cap Y_\alpha$; apply the component filtration maps to this family to define the glued maps, which are linear and commute with restriction. Their composites and inclusions agree with the component maps. The identification of the glued final module with $q^*E$ follows by restricting the pullback formula in [F5] to each component. Local splitting, local freeness and the line-quotient identifications can all be checked on those open components, where they were proved in step 6.1. Discarding the repeated final terms on each component gives exactly the asserted locally varying filtration; no line-bundle claim is made for the zero quotients introduced by padding. The finite disjoint union is quasi-compact and quasi-separated, since intersections between distinct components are empty and the same properties hold within each component. [F5, F8, step 6.1, construct]

8.1 Steps 1.1–2.1 construct the flag tower and its line quotients; steps 5.1 and 6.1–7.1 prove the remaining claims. Only local splittings have been used, so no global direct-sum decomposition is claimed. [step 2.1, step 5.1, step 6.1, step 7.1] ∎


## Chern coefficients of the projective relation

[Open item](item-lem-chern-relation-uniqueness) · level 1


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the data and axioms of [def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory). Let $E$ be a finite locally free module of constant rank $r>0$ on an object $X$ of the specified category. Write $\pi:\mathbb P_X(E)\to X$ for the projective bundle of one-dimensional quotients and $\xi=c_1(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P_X(E)}(1))\in H^{2,1}(\mathbb P_X(E))$ for the first Chern class of its tautological quotient line bundle.

There are unique classes $c_i(E)\in H^{2i,i}(X)$ for $1\leq i\leq r$ such that
$$\xi^r+\sum_{i=1}^r(-1)^i\pi^*c_i(E)\xi^{r-i}=0.$$
They are natural under every morphism $f:Y\to X$ in the specified category: $f^*c_i(E)=c_i(f^*E)$. For the rank-zero bundle, set its total Chern series $c_t(0)=1$ separately; equivalently, $c_0(0)=1$ and $c_i(0)=0$ for $i>0$. No coefficient is recovered from its empty projective bundle.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] By [def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory), pullbacks are functorial degree-preserving ring homomorphisms, first Chern classes of line bundles are natural, and the projective bundle map $\bigoplus_{m=0}^{r-1}H^{a-2m,b-m}(X)\to H^{a,b}(\mathbb P_X(E))$, $(x_m)\mapsto\sum_m\pi^*x_m\xi^m$, is an isomorphism. The contract specifies the category closure under positive-rank projective bundles and their base changes, as well as the module pullback and finite locally free rank conventions.

[F2] The quotient construction in [math:def-projective-bundle-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-projective-bundle-scheme) is $\mathbb P_X(E)=\operatorname{Proj}_X\operatorname{Sym}(E)$; its symmetric algebra is quasi-coherent, generated in degree one, and satisfies $f^*\operatorname{Sym}(E)\cong\operatorname{Sym}(f^*E)$. Applying [math:thm-relative-proj-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-relative-proj-base-change) to this graded algebra identifies $\mathbb P_Y(f^*E)$ with $\mathbb P_X(E)\times_XY$ and identifies the pullback of $\mathcal O(1)$ with the corresponding $\mathcal O(1)$. No flatness assumption on $f$ is required. The same projective-bundle definition gives $\mathbb P_X(0)=\varnothing$.

[A1] The Axiom of Choice, as formulated in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice), is inherited from the projective-bundle and relative Proj constructions in [F2] and assumed in [F1]. Its use here is to discharge those supplier hypotheses when forming the projective bundle in step 1.1 and its base-change comparison in step 2.1; the coefficient calculation makes no further choice of elements. For the rank-zero convention, $c_t(0)$ denotes $\sum_{i\geq0}c_i(0)t^i$, where $t$ is a formal variable; the prescribed series $1$ has constant coefficient $1$ and every positive coefficient $0$.

### Proof

**Given:** The theory and bundle in the statement, and a morphism $f:Y\to X$ in the specified category.

**Goal:** Establish existence, uniqueness, bidegrees and base-change naturality of the coefficients, with the separate rank-zero convention.

1.1 Apply the isomorphism in [F1] in bidegree $(2r,r)$ to $-\xi^r$. It gives a unique tuple $a_m\in H^{2(r-m),r-m}(X)$, $0\leq m<r$, with $-\xi^r=\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\pi^*a_m\xi^m$. Set $c_i(E)=(-1)^i a_{r-i}$. Then $c_i(E)$ has bidegree $(2i,i)$ and the displayed relation holds. Conversely, any tuple satisfying that relation gives the same expansion of $-\xi^r$, so the isomorphism makes every coefficient unique. [A1, F1, F2, given]

2.1 Let $g:\mathbb P_Y(f^*E)\to\mathbb P_X(E)$ be the base-change morphism and $\pi_Y$ the projection. The symmetric-algebra comparison and the relative Proj twist compatibility in [F2] identify $g^*\mathcal O_{\mathbb P_X(E)}(1)$ with $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P_Y(f^*E)}(1)$; hence naturality of $c_1$ gives $g^*\xi=\xi_Y$. Pulling back the relation in step 1.1 and using $\pi\circ g=f\circ\pi_Y$ gives the same relation for $f^*E$ with coefficients $f^*c_i(E)$. Their degrees are preserved, so uniqueness in step 1.1 applied over $Y$ yields $f^*c_i(E)=c_i(f^*E)$. For rank zero, [F2] gives the empty projective bundle, and the separately prescribed series $1$ has the stated coefficients and is preserved by unital pullback; no projective-bundle isomorphism for rank zero is invoked. This proves the statement. [A1, F1, F2, given, step 1.1] ∎


## Extending a Whitney class to K zero

[Open item](item-lem-virtual-chern-extension-interface) · level 1


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $H$ be an additively oriented bigraded theory with the projective bundle contract on a category $\mathcal C$ of schemes. For each $X$ in $\mathcal C$, suppose that every finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module $E$ is assigned a series
$$c_t(E)=1+\sum_{i\geq 1}c_i(E)t^i,\qquad c_i(E)\in H^{2i,i}(X),$$
depending only on its isomorphism class. Assume that these series are natural under morphisms in $\mathcal C$ and satisfy the Whitney identity $c_t(E)=c_t(E')c_t(E'')$ for every short exact sequence $0\to E'\to E\to E''\to0$ of finite locally free modules.

Write $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(X)$ for the abelian group generated by the isomorphism classes $[E]$ of finite locally free modules, with the relations $[E]=[E']+[E'']$ for all such exact sequences. Then there is a unique group homomorphism
$$c_t:K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(X)\longrightarrow \left\{1+\sum_{i\geq1}a_i t^i:a_i\in H^{2i,i}(X)\right\},$$
where the target group uses multiplication of formal series, taking $[E]$ to $c_t(E)$. Every element can be written $[E]-[F]$, and its image is $c_t(E)c_t(F)^{-1}$. The construction is natural in $X$. When $X$ is locally Noetherian, $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(X)$ is the vector-bundle Grothendieck group $K^0(X)$ of the cited library definition. The extension is conditional on the stated exact-sequence Whitney identity; the individual coefficients are not asserted to be additive. In particular, the second coefficient obeys the cross-term formula $c_2(x+y)=c_2(x)+c_1(x)c_1(y)+c_2(y)$.

A finite locally free module is an $\mathcal O_X$-module locally isomorphic to a finite direct sum of copies of $\mathcal O_X$. Naturality of the given series means $f^*c_i(E)=c_i(f^*E)$ for every morphism $f:Y\to X$ in $\mathcal C$. A short exact sequence here is an exact sequence of sheaves whose three terms are finite locally free. Formal-series multiplication uses the finite coefficient sums $(ab)_n=\sum_{j=0}^n a_jb_{n-j}$.

### Statement references
[def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory)

[math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The category, theory, natural bundle series and exact-sequence Whitney identity in the statement, together with the Axiom of Choice.

**Goal:** Construct the unique natural extension, establish the difference formula and the stated coefficient identity, and identify the locally Noetherian case.

[F1] The theory has associative unital bigraded rings, multiplication of bidegree $(0,0)$, graded commutativity in the first degree, and degree-preserving contravariant unital ring pullbacks. These are part of the given theory's contract ([def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory)).

[F2] Under Choice, the published vector-bundle Grothendieck group for a locally Noetherian scheme has exactly the generator-and-exact-sequence presentation stated above ([math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme), [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)).

[F3] Compatible sheaves of modules on an open cover of a fixed ringed space glue, with the specified identifications, uniquely up to unique compatible isomorphism. Apply this to finite free local modules and invertible transition matrices satisfying the identity and cocycle conditions ([math:thm-gluing-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-sheaves)).

[F4] For a scheme morphism $f:Y\to X$, module pullback is $f^*E=\mathcal O_Y\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal O_X}f^{-1}E$, a construction on the underlying ringed spaces ([math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces)). On any morphism of ringed spaces, pullback carries a finite free module of rank $n$ to a finite free module of rank $n$, including $n=0$ ([math:ex-pullback-free-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-pullback-free-module)). This module pullback is distinct from the cohomology pullback in [F1].

[F5] The Axiom of Choice permits a simultaneous choice of a trivializing neighbourhood and a frame at each point of a fixed bundle's underlying scheme ([math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)).

[A1] The assigned bundle series are invariant under isomorphism, natural, and multiplicative in every short exact sequence, as stated in the hypotheses.

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Construct the target group coefficient by coefficient and descend a map through the presentation.

1.1 Put $R_X=\bigoplus_{i\geq0}H^{2i,i}(X)$. By [F1], this is a commutative graded ring: the first degree of each homogeneous summand is even. The displayed target consists of series with constant coefficient $1$ and coefficient of $t^i$ in $H^{2i,i}(X)$. Finite coefficient sums show that it is closed under associative, commutative multiplication and contains $1$. For $a(t)=1+\sum_{i\geq1}a_i t^i$, define $b_0=1$ and recursively $b_n=-\sum_{j=1}^n a_jb_{n-j}$. Each $b_n$ has bidegree $(2n,n)$, and the coefficient of $t^n$ in $a(t)b(t)$ is zero for $n>0$. Thus $b(t)$ is an inverse in the displayed set, which is therefore an abelian group. [F1, construct]

1.2 The bundle isomorphism types form a set. For a given bundle, use [F5] to choose for each point $x$ a trivializing open $U_x$ containing $x$, a finite rank $n_x$ and a frame. Record the opens, ranks and invertible transition matrices satisfying the identity and cocycle conditions. All such recorded data form a set: their index set is the fixed underlying set of points of $X$, the opens belong to its topology, the ranks belong to $\mathbb N$, and each matrix has finitely many entries in the set of sections $\mathcal O_X(U_x\cap U_y)$. By [F3], the modules $\mathcal O_{U_x}^{n_x}$ and these overlap maps glue to a finite locally free module. Every bundle is isomorphic to the gluing of its recorded data. Quotienting the set of data by isomorphism of the glued modules therefore gives a set $S$ of bundle isomorphism types; the empty scheme is covered by the empty datum and gives its unique module. Form the abelian group of finitely supported functions $S\to\mathbb Z$ with pointwise addition, and quotient by the subgroup generated by the exact-sequence relation vectors. This constructs the presented group. The split sequence $0\to E\to E\oplus F\to F\to0$ gives $[E\oplus F]=[E]+[F]$, and the zero sequence gives $[0]=0$. Collecting positive and negative terms in a finite expression now writes every element as $[E]-[F]$, using the zero bundle for an empty sum. [F3, F5, construct, algebra]

2.1 By [A1] and step 1.1, the rule sending a finitely supported function $m:S\to\mathbb Z$ to the finite product $\prod_{s\in S}c_t(s)^{m(s)}$ defines a homomorphism to the target. Isomorphism invariance makes $c_t(s)$ well defined; the product is finite and negative powers exist by step 1.1. This sends the generator $[E]$ to $c_t(E)$. For every defining exact-sequence relation its image is $c_t(E)c_t(E')^{-1}c_t(E'')^{-1}=1$ by the Whitney identity. It therefore descends to the quotient. Its value on $[E]-[F]$ is $c_t(E)c_t(F)^{-1}$. Since the classes $[E]$ generate the quotient, these prescribed values also make the extension unique. [A1, step 1.1, step 1.2]

3.1 For $f:Y\to X$, trivialize $E$ on an open $U\subseteq X$ and put $V=f^{-1}U$. The module-pullback construction in [F4] commutes with restriction to $V$: the same neighbourhood representatives, their sheafification and tensor restrictions define pullback along $f|_V:V\to U$. The finite-free calculation in [F4] therefore gives $(f^*E)|_V\cong\mathcal O_V^n$ when $E|_U\cong\mathcal O_U^n$. These inverse-image opens cover $Y$, so $f^*E$ is finite locally free even when ranks vary. Pullback preserves isomorphisms by applying the construction to a map and its inverse. A local matrix for a bundle morphism is pulled back by applying the structure-sheaf map to its entries, as follows by evaluating the tensor formula on each basis vector. To treat an exact sequence, at each point trivialize its quotient term and lift its finitely many basis germs through the sheaf epimorphism; after shrinking to a common neighbourhood these lifts map to the basis sections and define a splitting. Inverse image and tensor on module morphisms preserve sums, compositions and identities by their additive defining operations, so preserve the splitting equations and hence the resulting split exact sequence. Exactness is local, and thus pullback preserves every relation in the presentation. It follows that $[E]\mapsto[f^*E]$ defines a homomorphism on the presented groups. For composable scheme morphisms, identify the frames of the iterated and composite pullbacks on inverse-image trivializing opens. Their transition matrices are the images of the original matrices under the same composite structure-sheaf map, so these identifications agree on overlaps and glue by [F3]. Identity pullback similarly preserves each frame. Hence the maps on bundle classes are contravariantly functorial. None of these opens needs to be an object of $\mathcal C$; these are computations on underlying ringed spaces. By [F1], cohomology pullback acts coefficientwise on the target series and preserves products and inverses. The assumed naturality gives $f^*c_t(E)=c_t(f^*E)$ on generators, hence on every virtual class by step 2.1. For locally Noetherian $X$, [F2] identifies this same presentation with the published $K^0(X)$; no locally Noetherian hypothesis is used in the preceding construction. [F1, F2, F3, F4, A1, step 2.1]

4.1 Comparing the coefficient of $t^2$ in $c_t(x+y)=c_t(x)c_t(y)$ gives $c_2(x+y)=c_2(x)+c_1(x)c_1(y)+c_2(y)$. Thus coefficientwise additivity does not follow from the group homomorphism: for example, in the formal series group over the ring $\mathbb Z[u]$ of finite polynomials in $u$ with integer coefficients, the homomorphism $n\mapsto(1+ut)^n$ has second coefficient zero at $1$ and $u^2$ at $2$. This formal example illustrates the cross term; it makes no additional assertion about a particular cohomology theory. The construction and all claimed properties follow. [step 1.1, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## Dual conventions for the exterior Euler class

[Open item](item-lem-exterior-euler-duality) · level 1


### Statement

Let $X$ be a scheme and $E$ a locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module of constant finite rank $r\geq 0$. Write $E^\vee=\mathcal Hom_{\mathcal O_X}(E,\mathcal O_X)$ and $\det E=\Lambda^r E$. For every $0\leq i\leq r$ there is a canonical isomorphism
$$\Lambda^{r-i}E\cong\det E\otimes\Lambda^iE^\vee.$$

Let $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(X)$ be the vector-bundle Grothendieck ring: its additive group is generated by isomorphism classes $[F]$ of finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-modules, subject to $[F]=[F']+[F'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to F'\to F\to F''\to0$ of such modules; multiplication is induced by tensor product and its identity is $[\mathcal O_X]$. Define the exterior Euler class of a rank-$r$ bundle by
$$\lambda_{-1}(E)=\sum_{i=0}^{r}(-1)^i[\Lambda^i E].$$
Then
$$\lambda_{-1}(E)=(-1)^r[\det E]\,\lambda_{-1}(E^\vee).$$
The class $[\det E]$ is a unit, so $\lambda_{-1}(E)=0$ if and only if $\lambda_{-1}(E^\vee)=0$.

The exterior powers, their local increasing-wedge bases, and the determinant line are as in [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles). A line bundle is a locally free module of rank one; a unit in a ring is an element with a multiplicative inverse.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** A scheme $X$ and a locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module $E$ of constant finite rank $r\geq0$.

**Goal:** Construct the stated canonical exterior-power isomorphisms and prove the Euler-class identity and simultaneous vanishing.

[F1] On a trivializing open set for $E$ with ordered basis $e_1,\ldots,e_r$, the increasing wedges of these basis vectors form bases for the exterior powers; $\det E=\Lambda^rE$ is a line bundle. These constructions are functorial in $E$. [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles)


[F2] Internal Hom has sections $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_U}(F|_U,G|_U)$ on $U$; the sheaf tensor product is the sheafification of $U\mapsto F(U)\otimes_{\mathcal O_X(U)}G(U)$. In particular Hom out of $\mathcal O_U^n$ is computed by the images of its $n$ standard sections. [math:def-sheaf-hom](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-hom) [math:def-sheaf-tensor-product](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-tensor-product)

[F3] Exactness means equality of image and kernel sheaves and can be tested on stalks. A surjective sheaf map therefore permits local lifts of each prescribed section near any point: lift its germ and shrink until its image equals that section. [math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves) [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

[F4] Balanced pairings induce unique maps from module tensor products. Over a commutative ring, tensor products have natural symmetry and associativity maps. Maps from the tensor-product presheaf to a sheaf extend uniquely through sheafification. Compatible local maps of module sheaves glue uniquely by applying the sheaf axiom to their values on sections. [math:thm-universal-property-of-module-tensor-products](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-universal-property-of-module-tensor-products) [math:thm-symmetry-and-associativity-over-a-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-symmetry-and-associativity-over-a-commutative-ring) [math:thm-sheafification-universal-property](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sheafification-universal-property) [math:def-sheaf-on-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-on-topological-space)

[F5] A scheme has a structure sheaf; module sheaves and their morphisms have sectionwise module operations and scalar-compatible restrictions. Finite locally free means locally isomorphic to $\mathcal O_U^n$, allowing the integer $n$ to vary between trivializing opens. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) [math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space) [math:def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank)

[F6] For a positive-size square matrix over a commutative ring, $\det$ is the signed permutation sum. For the size-zero matrix in the empty-wedge case, set its determinant equal to $1$. [math:def-determinant-of-a-square-matrix](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-determinant-of-a-square-matrix)

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Use the perfect exterior-product pairing and then reindex the finite alternating sum.

1.1 The presentation of $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(X)$ is well defined: isomorphism classes of finite locally free modules form a set, since bundles can be encoded by open covers, finite ranks and transition matrices satisfying the overlap identities. Explicitly, the free abelian group here consists of integer-valued functions of finite support on the set of bundle classes; quotient by the subgroup of finite integer combinations of the stated exact-sequence relations. Tensoring an exact sequence of finite locally free modules with another such module preserves exactness: near each point trivialize the quotient, locally lift its finitely many basis sections by [F3], and intersect the finitely many resulting neighbourhoods. Those lifts define a local splitting, so the sequence is locally a split sequence of modules. On a further trivializing open for the tensor factor, tensoring is a finite direct sum, which preserves that split sequence; [F3] then gives global exactness. The resulting tensor sheaf is finite locally free, with local basis the pairs of basis vectors of the two factors, by [F2] and [F4]. Consequently tensor product respects the relations in each variable and gives the stated ring multiplication; associativity and commutativity follow from the module tensor maps in [F4], extended through sheafification and glued. The identity map is induced by $a\otimes s\mapsto as$, with inverse $s\mapsto1\otimes s$. [given, F2, F3, F4, F5, construct, algebra]

1.2 For each $i$, exterior multiplication gives a canonical pairing $\Lambda^{r-i}E\otimes\Lambda^iE\longrightarrow\det E.$ It induces a map $\Lambda^{r-i}E\to\mathcal Hom(\Lambda^iE,\det E)$. On a trivializing open set, the increasing wedge indexed by a subset $J$ of size $r-i$ pairs to zero with every increasing $i$-wedge except the one indexed by the complementary subset, with which it pairs to $\pm e_1\wedge\cdots\wedge e_r$. Thus its matrix is a signed permutation matrix, including in characteristic two, and the induced map is an isomorphism. Because the pairing is defined by exterior multiplication, these local isomorphisms agree on overlaps. [F1, F2, F4, algebra]

2.1 Locally, $E^\vee$ has the dual basis $e_1^\vee,\ldots,e_r^\vee$. The canonical map $\Lambda^i E^\vee\to(\Lambda^i E)^\vee$ sends $\phi_1\wedge\cdots\wedge\phi_i$ to the functional $v_1\wedge\cdots\wedge v_i\mapsto\det(\phi_a(v_b))$. It sends each increasing dual wedge to the dual of the corresponding increasing wedge basis vector, hence is an isomorphism. Likewise the evaluation map $\det E\otimes(\Lambda^i E)^\vee\to\mathcal Hom(\Lambda^iE,\det E)$ is locally an isomorphism. The signed permutation formula in [F6] is multilinear and alternating (terms with repeated rows or columns cancel in pairs), so the displayed functional factors through the exterior quotient in [F1]. These formulas commute with restrictions and are independent of the chosen frames; their local inverses consequently agree on overlaps and glue by [F4]. Combining their inverses with step 1.2 gives the asserted canonical isomorphism. The same argument covers $i=0$, $i=r$, and $r=0$, using the empty wedge. [F1, F2, F4, F6, step 1.2, algebra]

3.1 Taking classes in the ring and substituting $j=r-i$ gives $\begin{aligned} \lambda_{-1}(E)&=\sum_{i=0}^r(-1)^i[\Lambda^iE] =\sum_{j=0}^r(-1)^{r-j}[\det E][\Lambda^jE^\vee]\\ &=(-1)^r[\det E]\lambda_{-1}(E^\vee). \end{aligned}$ The dual line $(\det E)^\vee$ has the property that the evaluation map $\det E\otimes(\det E)^\vee\to\mathcal O_X$ is an isomorphism locally and hence globally. Thus $[(\det E)^\vee]$ is the inverse of $[\det E]$. Multiplication by the unit $(-1)^r[\det E]$ is bijective, proving simultaneous vanishing. This establishes all claims. [F1, F2, F4, step 1.1, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## Pointed spectrum lifts through a discrete target are unique

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-fd6358d333ef-spectrum-lifts-to-discrete-target) · level 1


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the compatible universe bounds for spaces and coherent functor categories. Let $\mathcal X$ be an infinity-category with finite limits and small mapping spaces, and write $*$ for its terminal object and $\mathcal X_*=\mathcal X_{*/}$ for its pointed objects. Define $\mathrm{Sp}(\mathcal X)=\operatorname{Exc}_*(S_*^{\mathrm{fin}},\mathcal X)$, the full category of reduced excisive functors, and let $\Omega^\infty$ be evaluation at $S^0$, with its canonical pointing. Reduced means taking the point to a terminal object; excisive means taking pushout squares to pullback squares. A zero-truncated object $T$ means that $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal X}(B,T)$ is a discrete space for every $B$.

Let $F\to E\xrightarrow{q}D$ be a fiber sequence of spectrum objects, including its defining pullback $F=E\times_D0$, and set $T=\Omega^\infty D$. Then $\Omega^\infty F\simeq\Omega^\infty E\times_T*$ as pointed objects. For a pointed $A$ and fixed pointed map $f:A\to\Omega^\infty E$, define its lift space as the homotopy fiber over $f$ of $\operatorname{Map}_*(A,\Omega^\infty F)\to\operatorname{Map}_*(A,\Omega^\infty E)$; thus a lift includes its homotopy over $\Omega^\infty E$. This space is the path space from $qf$ to zero in $\operatorname{Map}_*(A,T)$. If $T$ is zero-truncated and $qf$ is pointed nullhomotopic, the lift space is contractible. 

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The universe bounds, finite limits, spectrum objects, pointed map and specified fiber sequence in the Statement. All pullbacks and fibers are homotopy pullbacks and homotopy fibers.

**Goal:** Identify the full space of lifts, including its homotopies, and deduce uniqueness when the target is discrete.

[A1] Assume [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fixed nested universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$, using [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention). Universe existence is a separate assumption. Use the compatible bounds of [thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input), enlarging the ambient universe for the stated locally small category and its functor categories as necessary. They support the imported categorical formalism and spectrum construction; the local universal-property calculations introduce no additional choice principle.

[F1] [thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input) supplies infinity-categories, coherent mapping spaces, their limit universal properties, and the category $S_*^{\mathrm{fin}}$ of finite pointed spaces. Pointed objects are arrows from the terminal object, and pointed maps include the compatible homotopy at that arrow.

[F2] Reference 0 supplies the reduced excisive spectrum-object definition, closure under finite limits, the constant-terminal zero object and the evaluation description of $\Omega^\infty$.

[F3] Reference 1 supplies pointwise functor limits, the undercategory mapping-space fiber formula, the mapping-space definition of zero-truncation and closure of discrete spaces under finite limits.

### External input
Import exactly [F2] and [F3] from [Higher Algebra](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HA.pdf) and [Higher Topos Theory](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HTT.pdf), at the locators and hypotheses recorded in references 0 and 1. These foundational premises are imported; the following preservation, lift-space and uniqueness deductions are local.

### Proof
1.1 By [F2], the full spectrum-object category is closed under finite limits in its functor category, and by [F3] those limits are pointwise. Evaluation at $S^0$ therefore carries the defining square for $F$ to the pullback $\Omega^\infty E\times_T*$; the zero functor evaluates to $*$. The canonical pointings are obtained from the value at the point. The same square is a pullback among pointed objects: a cone of maps from a pointed object into it is a cone in $\mathcal X$ together with the compatible pointing, and its universal property is the limit universal property of [F1] with that pointing retained. Applying this property to maps from $A$ gives $\operatorname{Map}_*(A,\Omega^\infty F)\simeq\operatorname{Map}_*(A,\Omega^\infty E)\times_{\operatorname{Map}_*(A,T)}*$. Its homotopy fiber over $f$ consists precisely of paths from $qf$ to the zero map, with all their higher homotopies. [A1, F1, F2, F3, given]

2.1 Suppose $T$ is zero-truncated. By the undercategory formula in [F3], $\operatorname{Map}_*(A,T)$ is the homotopy fiber of $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal X}(A,T)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal X}(*,T)$ over the chosen point of $T$. Both spaces are discrete by the definition in [F3], so their homotopy fiber is discrete by its finite-limit assertion. A pointed nullhomotopy of $qf$ puts the two endpoints in the same component of this discrete space. Its path space between those endpoints is therefore contractible: after equivalence with a set it is the singleton of the constant path. The description in step 1.1 proves the asserted contractibility of the lift space and the claimed uniqueness of pointed lifts over the fixed map. [F1, F3, step 1.1, given] ∎


## Formal Chern arithmetic through degree three

[Open item](item-lem-third-order-chern-inversion) · level 2


### Statement
Let $R$ be a commutative ring with unit. A formal series $C(t)=1+\sum_{i\geq1}a_i t^i$ has a multiplicative inverse, and, modulo terms of degree at least four,
$$C(t)^{-1}=1-a_1t+(a_1^2-a_2)t^2+(-a_1^3+2a_1a_2-a_3)t^3.$$
For $D(t)=1+\sum_{i\geq1}b_i t^i$, the coefficients in degrees one, two and three of $C(t)D(t)$ are respectively $a_1+b_1$, $a_2+a_1b_1+b_2$, and $a_3+a_2b_1+a_1b_2+b_3$.

More generally, let $G$ be an abelian group and let $c_t:G\to 1+tR[\![t]\!]$ be a group homomorphism, where the target uses series multiplication. Write $c_t(x)=1+\sum_{i\geq1}c_i(x)t^i$. If $r\geq1$ and $H$ is a subgroup of $G$ on which $c_i$ vanishes for every $1\leq i<r$, then $c_r:H\to(R,+)$ is a group homomorphism. In particular, under the hypotheses of the virtual Chern extension, these formulas apply to its total Chern series, with $R=\bigoplus_{i\geq0}H^{2i,i}(X)$. If $c_1(x)=c_1(y)$, then $c_2(x-y)=c_2(x)-c_2(y)$.

Here $R[\![t]\!]$ denotes the ring of formal sequences of coefficients, with multiplication defined by the finite convolution formula $[t^n](AB)=\sum_{i=0}^n[t^i]A\,[t^{n-i}]B$. The notation $1+tR[\![t]\!]$ means the series with constant coefficient one, and $[t^n]A$ denotes the coefficient of $t^n$. Equality modulo terms of degree at least four means equality of coefficients in degrees zero through three. A virtual Chern class is an element of the bundle Grothendieck group equipped with the extended series described by the cited interface.

### Statement references
[lem-virtual-chern-extension-interface](item-lem-virtual-chern-extension-interface)

[math:def-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-commutative-ring)

[math:def-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group)

[math:def-subgroup](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subgroup)

[math:def-group-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-homomorphism)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** A commutative unital ring $R$, the series $C$ and $D$, and, for the additivity assertion, the group homomorphism $c_t$, integer $r$ and subgroup $H$ specified in the statement.

**Goal:** Establish the inverse and product formulas, the subgroup additivity assertion and the stated conditional virtual Chern consequences.

[F1] Under its stated hypotheses, including the Axiom of Choice and the exact-sequence Whitney identity, the virtual Chern extension supplies a homomorphism from the bundle Grothendieck group to the multiplicative group of total series. Its coefficients lie in $H^{2i,i}(X)$, and $\bigoplus_{i\geq0}H^{2i,i}(X)$ is commutative because the first grading of each summand is even. [lem-virtual-chern-extension-interface](item-lem-virtual-chern-extension-interface)

[F2] The commutative-ring interface supplies a unital ring with an abelian additive group, associative commutative multiplication and distributivity. The group interface supplies the abelian-group laws; a subgroup contains zero and is closed under addition and inverses. A group homomorphism is a function preserving the group operation. These are the algebraic meanings used here; identity and inverse preservation are derived in step 2.1. [math:def-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-commutative-ring), [math:def-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group), [math:def-subgroup](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subgroup), [math:def-group-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-homomorphism)

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Compare coefficients of formal series.

1.1 Convolution of two constant-one series again has constant coefficient one.
