It is commutative by the ring laws in [F2], and associative because either parenthesization of a triple product has degree-$n$ coefficient $\sum_{i+j+k=n}a_i b_j d_k$, a finite sum; the series $1$ is its identity. Set $q_0=1$ and recursively $q_n=-\sum_{i=1}^n a_iq_{n-i}$ for $n\geq1$. Each $q_n$ is uniquely determined by the earlier coefficients using a finite sum, so $Q(t)=\sum_{n\geq0}q_nt^n$ is a well-defined formal series. The coefficient of $t^n$ in $C Q$ is zero for $n>0$ and one for $n=0$; commutativity gives $Q C=1$ as well. Thus the constant-one series form an abelian group. The recursion yields $q_1=-a_1$, $q_2=a_1^2-a_2$, and $q_3=-a_1^3+2a_1a_2-a_3$, proving the inverse formula. [given, F2, construct, algebra]

1.2 The convolution formula gives $[t^n](CD)=a_n+b_n+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_i b_{n-i}$ for $n\geq1$, taking the constant coefficients to be one. Substituting $n=1,2,3$ gives the three displayed product coefficients. [given, F2, algebra]

2.1 First apply the homomorphism law in [F2] to $0+0$: writing $U=c_t(0)$ gives $U=U^2$. Multiply this equality by the inverse supplied in step 1.1 to obtain $1=U$. For every $z\in G$, the same law gives $c_t(z)c_t(-z)=c_t(0)=1$, so $c_t(-z)=c_t(z)^{-1}$, by multiplying by $c_t(z)^{-1}$. Now let $x,y\in H$. Subgroup closure in [F2] ensures $0,x+y,-x\in H$. The homomorphism property gives $c_t(x+y)=c_t(x)c_t(y)$. Comparing degree $r$ as in step 1.2 gives $c_r(x+y)=c_r(x)+c_r(y)+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}c_i(x)c_{r-i}(y)$. Each summand vanishes by the hypothesis on $H$, including the empty-sum case $r=1$. Since $c_t(0)=1$, one has $c_r(0)=0$; applying the addition formula to $x+(-x)=0$ and adding $-c_r(x)$ to both sides gives $c_r(-x)=-c_r(x)$. Thus $c_r$ is a group homomorphism. [given, F2, step 1.1, step 1.2, algebra]

3.1 Under the hypotheses in [F1], the total series satisfy the hypotheses just used, so all preceding formulas apply to virtual classes. For $x,y$ with common first coefficient $u$, steps 1.1 and 1.2 give $c_2(x-y)=c_2(x)+(u^2-c_2(y))-u^2=c_2(x)-c_2(y)$. This proves the stated virtual consequences and completes the proof. [F1, F2, step 1.1, step 1.2, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## Exterior-square line factors on a flag space

[Open item](item-lem-exterior-square-root-formula) · level 2


### Statement
Let $S$ be a scheme and $E$ a rank-three vector bundle with a filtration $0=F_0\subset F_1\subset F_2\subset F_3=E$ whose successive quotients $L_i=F_i/F_{i-1}$ are line bundles and whose inclusions are locally split. Then $\Lambda^2E$ has a locally split filtration with successive line-bundle quotients $L_1\otimes L_2$, $L_1\otimes L_3$, and $L_2\otimes L_3$, in that order. There is an isomorphism $\det E\cong L_1\otimes L_2\otimes L_3$.

Assume the Axiom of Choice and that $S$ is locally noetherian. In the vector-bundle Grothendieck ring $K^0(S)$, write $[L_i]$ for the class of $L_i$. Then $[\Lambda^2E]=[L_1][L_2]+[L_1][L_3]+[L_2][L_3]$ and $[\det E]=[L_1][L_2][L_3]$. If $c_1$ is any homomorphism from the Picard group of $S$ to an abelian group, and $\ell_i=c_1(L_i)$, the first Chern classes of the three exterior-square line factors are respectively $\ell_1+\ell_2$, $\ell_1+\ell_3$, and $\ell_2+\ell_3$.

A vector bundle is a finite locally free $\mathcal O_S$-module, and a line bundle has rank one. Locally split means that every inclusion in the filtration is locally the inclusion of a direct summand. The Picard group consists of line-bundle isomorphism classes under tensor product. The stated homomorphism condition means $c_1(L\otimes M)=c_1(L)+c_1(M)$; these line-factor classes are the exterior-square roots relative to the chosen flag. In particular, under the Axiom of Choice, the flag-tower supplier provides such a filtration after pulling a rank-three bundle back to its flag tower.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** A scheme $S$, a rank-three bundle $E$, and the locally split line-quotient filtration in the statement. For the $K^0$ assertions, also assume the Axiom of Choice and that $S$ is locally noetherian. For the root assertion, also assume the stated additive map $c_1$.

**Goal:** Construct the exterior-square filtration and determinant isomorphism and derive the displayed class formulas.

[A1] The Axiom of Choice is used only through the flag-tower supplier [F2] and the Grothendieck-ring supplier [F3]; the filtration and determinant constructions for a given flag require no additional Choice assumption. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F1] Exterior powers and determinant are functorial for bundle maps; the exterior power of a free module has the basis of increasing wedges. In particular the determinant of a rank-three bundle is its third exterior power. [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles) [math:lem-exterior-algebra-basis-monomials](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-exterior-algebra-basis-monomials)

[F2] Under the Axiom of Choice, the flag tower of a rank-three bundle carries a locally split filtration with line-bundle quotients. [lem-flag-tower-line-quotients](item-lem-flag-tower-line-quotients)

[F3] Under the Axiom of Choice, for a locally noetherian scheme $S$, $K^0(S)$ is generated by vector-bundle classes with the relations $[B]=[A]+[C]$ for short exact sequences $0\to A\to B\to C\to0$ of vector bundles; multiplication is induced by tensor product. [math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme)

[F4] A scheme is a locally ringed space locally isomorphic to affine schemes. Locally noetherian means admitting an affine open cover by spectra of noetherian rings. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme)

[F5] A module sheaf has section modules and scalar-compatible restriction maps, and its morphisms are restriction-compatible sectionwise linear maps. Finite locally free means locally isomorphic to a finite power of the structure sheaf. The sheaf tensor product is the sheafification of the sectionwise module tensor product. Balanced sectionwise pairings induce maps from module tensor products; compatible maps from a presheaf to a sheaf extend uniquely across sheafification. [math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space) [math:def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank) [math:def-sheaf-tensor-product](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-tensor-product) [math:thm-universal-property-of-module-tensor-products](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-universal-property-of-module-tensor-products) [math:thm-sheafification-universal-property](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sheafification-universal-property)

[F6] Image sheaves and cokernel sheaves are the sheafifications of the sectionwise image and cokernel presheaves; a quotient by a subsheaf is the cokernel of its inclusion. Exactness means equality of the image and kernel sheaves at each term. Compatible local sections glue uniquely. In particular, local module maps agreeing on overlaps glue by applying this axiom to the image of each section; local inverse maps glue as well, since on overlaps both invert the same map. [math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves) [math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves) [math:def-sheaf-on-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-on-topological-space)

[F7] The Picard group is the abelian group of line-bundle isomorphism classes, with multiplication induced by sheaf tensor product. In particular tensor products of line bundles are line bundles. The map $c_1$ in the statement is an assumed homomorphism on this group, not an asserted construction of a Chern theory. [math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme)

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Construct the filtration by wedge images and identify its quotients on bases adapted to the flag.

1.1 By [F5], wedge multiplication from [F1] is a balanced sectionwise pairing and therefore induces sheaf maps $F_1\otimes F_2\to\Lambda^2E$ and $F_1\otimes E\to\Lambda^2E$. Let $G_1$ and $G_2$ be their image sheaves as in [F6]. They are subsheaves with $G_1\subseteq G_2\subseteq\Lambda^2E$. Locally the two short exact sequences defining the flag split, and, after intersecting finitely many trivializing neighbourhoods, the three line quotients are trivial. Lifting their generators through these splittings gives a local basis $e_1,e_2,e_3$ of $E$ with $F_1$ generated by $e_1$ and $F_2$ by $e_1,e_2$. On such a chart tensor products of free sheaves have the elementary tensor basis: the sectionwise map $e_i\otimes e_j\mapsto e_{ij}$ extends by the module tensor universal property in [F5], and its inverse sends each $e_{ij}$ to $e_i\otimes e_j$; these maps commute with restrictions and extend across sheafification. By [F1], the first wedge image is generated by $e_1\wedge e_2$, the second by $e_1\wedge e_2,e_1\wedge e_3$, and $\Lambda^2E$ by these two wedges and $e_2\wedge e_3$. These sectionwise images are already free subsheaves, so [F6] identifies them with $G_1$ and $G_2$ on the chart. Thus these subsheaves are locally direct summands and their successive quotients are line bundles. [given, F1, F5, F6, construct]

2.1 Wedge multiplication induces maps $L_1\otimes L_2\to G_1$, $L_1\otimes L_3\to G_2/G_1$, and $L_2\otimes L_3\to\Lambda^2E/G_2$. To define them, lift quotient sections only on splitting neighbourhoods. In the first map changing the lift from $L_2$ adds a wedge of two sections of $F_1$, which vanishes. In the second, changing the lift from $L_3$ adds a section of $G_1$. In the third, changing the lift from $L_2$ adds a section of $G_2$, while changing the lift from $L_3$ adds a wedge of two sections of $F_2$, which belongs to $G_1\subseteq G_2$ by step 1.1. Thus the assignments are independent of lifts, balanced and compatible with restriction. By [F5] they induce maps on the tensor sheaves on each splitting neighbourhood; their independence of lifts makes them agree on overlaps, so [F6] glues them globally. On the adapted basis of step 1.1 they send the tensor products of quotient basis vectors to the corresponding wedge generators and hence are isomorphisms. Their local inverses glue by [F6], so they are global isomorphisms. This proves the claimed filtration without choosing a global splitting. [F1, F5, F6, step 1.1, construct]

2.2 Define $L_1\otimes L_2\otimes L_3\to\Lambda^3E$ by wedging local lifts to their flag terms. Changing a lift adds a wedge containing two vectors from $F_1$ or three from $F_2$, which vanishes on the adapted basis of step 1.1. Thus the balanced local assignments induce tensor-sheaf maps by [F5] and agree on overlaps, so they glue by [F6]. On that basis the map sends the tensor product of quotient generators to $e_1\wedge e_2\wedge e_3$, a generator by [F1]. The local inverse maps glue by [F6], giving an isomorphism to $\det E=\Lambda^3E$. [F1, F5, F6, step 1.1, construct]

3.1 Under the additional $K^0$ hypotheses, apply [F3] to the short exact sequences $0\to G_1\to G_2\to G_2/G_1\to0$ and $0\to G_2\to\Lambda^2E\to\Lambda^2E/G_2\to0$. They are exact in the sense of [F6], since on the adapted charts they are coordinate inclusions followed by coordinate projections, and all terms are vector bundles by steps 1.1 and 2.1. Using step 2.1 and the tensor-product multiplication gives the displayed formula for $[\Lambda^2E]$. Step 2.2 and the same multiplication give the determinant formula. [A1, F3, F6, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 2.2, assume-hyp]

4.1 By [F7], the three tensor-product line bundles identified in step 2.1 represent the products of the corresponding classes in the Picard group. Apply the assumed homomorphism $c_1$ to those products. Additivity gives the three pairwise sums. Finally, [F2] supplies the required flag after pullback to the flag tower when the Axiom of Choice is assumed. All stated conclusions follow. [A1, F2, F7, step 2.1, step 2.2, step 3.1, assume-hyp] ∎


## Finite-dimensional families of generated bundle functionals

[Open item](item-lem-regular-section-parameter-family) · level 2



### Statement
Let $k$ be a field, $U$ a $k$-scheme, and $E$ a vector bundle of constant rank $r$ on $U$. Write $E^\vee=\mathcal Hom_{\mathcal O_U}(E,\mathcal O_U)$, and suppose that global sections $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m$ generate $E^\vee$, meaning that the morphism $q:\mathcal O_U^m\to E^\vee$ sending the standard basis to these sections is surjective. Here $m$ and $r$ may be zero.

The affine parameter space $\mathbb A_k^m=\operatorname{Spec}k[T_1,\ldots,T_m]$ carries a universal functional $\Phi=\sum_i T_i\phi_i$ on the pullback of $E$ to $U\times_k\mathbb A_k^m$. Its zero-incidence subscheme $I$ is the closed subscheme defined locally by the coefficients of $\Phi$ in a frame of $E^\vee$. For every point $u\in U$, evaluation gives a surjective linear map $\kappa(u)^m\to E^\vee\otimes_{\mathcal O_U}\kappa(u)$, and this remains surjective after any field extension of $\kappa(u)$.

For every closed subscheme $Z\hookrightarrow U$, the incidence subscheme constructed from the restricted sections on $Z$ is canonically $I\times_U Z$, viewed as a closed subscheme of $Z\times_k\mathbb A_k^m$. More generally, for any $k$-scheme $T$ and any $T$-valued parameter $(a_1,\ldots,a_m)$, the pullback of $I$ to $U\times_k T$ is the zero subscheme of the functional $\sum_i a_i\phi_i$.

### Statement references
[lem-surjection-kernel-bundle](item-lem-surjection-kernel-bundle)

[def-unimodular-sections](item-def-unimodular-sections)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The field $k$, the $k$-scheme $U$, the rank-$r$ vector bundle $E$, and the surjective generating map $q$ in the statement. A $k$-scheme is a scheme with a specified morphism to $\operatorname{Spec}k$; all parameter morphisms are over $k$.

**Goal:** Construct the universal zero-incidence subscheme, prove fiberwise surjectivity of evaluation, and identify its base changes.

[F1] A vector bundle is a finite locally free module; its dual is the sheaf of homomorphisms to the structure sheaf, and a functional is a section of that dual. Only these scheme-local definitions from [def-unimodular-sections](item-def-unimodular-sections) are used, not its Choice-dependent affine module correspondence.

[F2] A surjective morphism between finite locally free sheaves splits locally. [lem-surjection-kernel-bundle](item-lem-surjection-kernel-bundle)

[F3] A scheme is locally an affine scheme, whose coordinate ring is its ring of global functions; scheme morphisms are morphisms of locally ringed spaces. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) [math:def-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme) [math:def-morphism-of-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-of-schemes)

[F4] Scheme fiber products exist and represent compatible pairs of morphisms. Their projections define base change. [math:def-fibre-product-schemes-universal-property](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-fibre-product-schemes-universal-property) [math:thm-fibre-products-of-schemes-exist](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fibre-products-of-schemes-exist)

[F5] Pullback is $f^*M=\mathcal O_{X'}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal O_X}f^{-1}M$ for $f:X'\to X$, and takes a finite free module to the corresponding finite free module. Internal Hom has sections the module-sheaf morphisms on each open set. [math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces) [math:ex-pullback-free-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-pullback-free-module) [math:def-sheaf-hom](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-hom)

[F6] The quotient spectrum $\operatorname{Spec}(A/J)\to\operatorname{Spec}A$ is a closed immersion without a choice principle. A closed immersion means a homeomorphism onto a closed subset with surjective structure-sheaf map. Affine schemes glue along compatible open isomorphisms. Distinguished opens are localization spectra, their function restrictions are localization maps, and localization commutes with quotient rings. [math:lem-quotient-spectrum-map-is-a-closed-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-quotient-spectrum-map-is-a-closed-immersion) [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) [math:thm-gluing-affine-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-affine-schemes) [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion) [math:thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme) [math:thm-localisation-commutes-with-quotients](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-localisation-commutes-with-quotients)

[F7] The image sheaf of a module-sheaf morphism is the sheafification of its sectionwise image. The scheme-theoretic inverse image of a closed subscheme under $f$ is its fiber product, a closed subscheme with ideal $\operatorname{im}(f^*\mathcal J\to\mathcal O_{X'})$. [math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves) [math:def-scheme-theoretic-inverse-image-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-theoretic-inverse-image-subscheme)

[F8] Morphisms from any scheme $T$ to $\operatorname{Spec}A$ correspond naturally to ring maps $A\to\Gamma(T,\mathcal O_T)$. A coefficient-ring map and prescribed images of the indeterminates determine a unique map from the polynomial ring, also for the empty family. [math:thm-morphisms-into-affine-scheme-global-sections](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-morphisms-into-affine-scheme-global-sections) [math:thm-universal-property-of-a-polynomial-ring-on-a-family](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-universal-property-of-a-polynomial-ring-on-a-family)

[F9] The fiber of $M$ at $u$ is $M_u\otimes_{\mathcal O_{U,u}}\kappa(u)$, where $\kappa(u)=\mathcal O_{U,u}/\mathfrak m_u$. Tensoring module maps preserves composition and identities, hence preserves a displayed right-inverse identity. [math:def-fibre-of-module-at-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-fibre-of-module-at-point) [math:prop-functoriality-of-module-tensor-products](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-functoriality-of-module-tensor-products)

### Proof
**Proof technique:** Use local frames, affine quotient charts and the locally split generating map.

1.1 By [F3] and [F4], form $X=U\times_k\mathbb A_k^m$ with projection $p:X\to U$. Pulling a frame of $E$ and its dual back gives dual free frames by [F5]; evaluation on these frames identifies $p^*(E^\vee)$ with $(p^*E)^\vee$. Thus the functions $T_i$ and pulled-back sections define $\Phi=\sum_i T_i p^*\phi_i:p^*E\to\mathcal O_X$. Cover $X$ by affine opens $V=\operatorname{Spec}A$ inside these frame charts and write the coefficients as $f_1,\ldots,f_r\in A$. Put $J_V=(f_1,\ldots,f_r)$ and use the closed immersion $\operatorname{Spec}(A/J_V)\to V$ of [F6]. On distinguished opens these quotient charts restrict to the quotients by the restricted coefficients, because localization commutes with quotients. On overlaps a change of frame multiplies the coefficient vector by an invertible matrix, so the generated ideal sheaves agree; after affine distinguished-open refinements the quotient identifications are the identity modulo that same ideal. They therefore satisfy the identity and cocycle conditions and glue by [F6] to a scheme $I$ over $X$. Its image is locally $V(J_V)$, hence closed, and its structure-sheaf map is locally surjective, so [F6] makes $I\to X$ a closed immersion. Its ideal sheaf is exactly $\operatorname{im}\Phi$ by [F7], since both are locally the sheaf generated by the $f_j$. For rank zero the coefficient list is empty, $J_V=0$, and $I=X$. [F1, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, given, construct]

1.2 By [F2], $q$ has a right inverse on a neighbourhood of each $u\in U$. Take stalks and tensor this identity with $\kappa(u)$ using [F9]. In local free coordinates the resulting maps have the same matrices with coefficients reduced to $\kappa(u)$, so their composite is the identity and the evaluation map $\kappa(u)^m\to E^\vee_u\otimes_{\mathcal O_{U,u}}\kappa(u)$ is surjective. Tensoring the right-inverse identity with any extension field of $\kappa(u)$ again preserves it by [F9], proving the field-extension assertion. This also covers zero ranks and empty schemes. [F1, F2, F9, given]

2.1 By [F8] and its naturality for the structure map to $\operatorname{Spec}k$, a $k$-morphism $a:T\to\mathbb A_k^m$ is equivalent to a tuple $a_i\in\Gamma(T,\mathcal O_T)$: the corresponding $k$-algebra map sends $T_i$ to $a_i$. Here and in the statement, these are the $T$-valued parameters. By [F4], $a$ induces $\operatorname{id}_U\times a:U\times_k T\to X$. Pulling back the local coefficient expressions of step 1.1 substitutes the pullbacks of $a_i$ for $T_i$. By [F7], the inverse-image ideal is generated locally by precisely these coefficients; it is consequently the zero-locus ideal sheaf of the functional $\sum_i a_i\phi_i$, where both factors are pulled back to $U\times_k T$. Thus the scheme-theoretic pullback is the asserted zero subscheme. For $m=0$ there is a unique empty tuple and the same argument applies. [F4, F5, F7, F8, step 1.1, given, algebra]

3.1 For a closed subscheme $Z\hookrightarrow U$, write $E|_Z$ and $E^\vee|_Z$ for their module pullbacks. Pulling back a frame and its dual by [F5] gives the canonical identification $E^\vee|_Z\cong(E|_Z)^\vee$: both sides have the corresponding dual basis, and evaluation makes these identifications agree under changes of frame. The same right-inverse argument as in step 1.2, or its local matrices pulled back to $Z$, shows that the restricted sections still generate. By [F7], pulling the coefficient ideal of step 1.1 back to $Z\times_k\mathbb A_k^m$ gives exactly the zero-locus ideal of those restricted sections. This inverse image is canonically $I\times_U Z$: both schemes represent a point of $I$ together with a point of $Z$ having the same image in $U$, by [F4]. Together with steps 1.1 through 2.1 this proves all the assertions. [F1, F4, F5, F7, step 1.1, step 1.2, step 2.1] ∎

### Remarks
This construction alone does not assert smoothness, irreducibility, codimension, or avoidance for any parameter. Generation of $E$ does not by itself supply the assumed generation of $E^\vee$.


## A rank-two bundle with a trivial quotient and determinant

[Open item](item-lem-trivial-determinant-rank-two-kernel) · level 2


### Statement
Let $V$ be a scheme and $Q$ a rank-two vector bundle on $V$ with $\det Q\cong\mathcal O_V$. If $Q\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal O_V$ is a surjective morphism, its kernel is isomorphic to $\mathcal O_V$, so there is a short exact sequence $0\to\mathcal O_V\to Q\to\mathcal O_V\to0$.

Assume also the Axiom of Choice and that $V$ is locally Noetherian. Then $[Q]=2[\mathcal O_V]$ in the vector-bundle Grothendieck group $K^0(V)$, whether or not the sequence splits globally. If in addition $P$ is a vector bundle in a short exact sequence $0\to Q\to P\to\mathcal O_V\to0$, then $[P]=3[\mathcal O_V]$ in $K^0(V)$.

Here a vector bundle of rank $r$ is an $\mathcal O_V$-module locally isomorphic to $\mathcal O_V^r$; a line bundle is a rank-one vector bundle. The determinant of a rank-$r$ bundle is its top exterior power $\Lambda^r E$, and a trivial determinant means an isomorphism with $\mathcal O_V$. A short exact sequence is exact at each term, equivalently on stalks. Under the stated Choice and locally Noetherian hypotheses, $K^0(V)$ is the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes $[E]$ of vector bundles, with $[E]=[E']+[E'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to E'\to E\to E''\to0$.

### Statement references

[lem-surjection-kernel-bundle](item-lem-surjection-kernel-bundle)

[lem-determinants-of-short-exact-sequences](item-lem-determinants-of-short-exact-sequences)

[def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles)

[math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme)

[math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme)

[math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme)

[math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space)

[math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves)

[math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves)

[math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** A scheme $V$, a rank-two vector bundle $Q$, an isomorphism $\det Q\cong\mathcal O_V$, and a surjection $Q\to\mathcal O_V$. For the group assertions, also the Axiom of Choice and locally Noetherianity of $V$; for the final assertion, the displayed short exact sequence involving $P$.

**Goal:** Identify the kernel with $\mathcal O_V$ and prove the stated group identities under their additional hypotheses.

[F1] The kernel of a surjection of finite locally free sheaves is finite locally free, with rank equal to the difference of ranks; the surjection splits locally. [lem-surjection-kernel-bundle](item-lem-surjection-kernel-bundle)

[F2] A short exact sequence $0\to E'\to E\to E''\to0$ of vector bundles has a canonical isomorphism $\det E'\otimes\det E''\cong\det E$. [lem-determinants-of-short-exact-sequences](item-lem-determinants-of-short-exact-sequences)

[F3] The determinant of a rank-one bundle $L$ is $\Lambda^1L$, canonically $L$; in particular $\det\mathcal O_V\cong\mathcal O_V$. [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles) For every line bundle $M$, $M\otimes\mathcal O_V\cong M$: the Picard group has line-bundle isomorphism classes as elements, tensor product as its operation, and $[\mathcal O_V]$ as its identity. Only this line-bundle unit law is needed below. [math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme)

[F4] Under Choice and locally Noetherianity, isomorphic vector bundles have the same generator in $K^0(V)$, and every short exact sequence of vector bundles gives the additive relation in the statement. [math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme) [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) Choice is retained here as an explicit hypothesis of the Grothendieck-group supplier, invoked in steps 3.1 and 4.1; the kernel and determinant argument in steps 1.1 and 2.1 requires no Choice assumption.

[F5] A scheme is a locally ringed space locally isomorphic to affine schemes. An $\mathcal O_V$-module is a sheaf of abelian groups with sectionwise $\mathcal O_V(U)$-module structures compatible with restriction; its morphisms are sectionwise linear sheaf maps. Locally Noetherian means admitting an affine open cover by spectra of Noetherian rings. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) [math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space) [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme)

[F6] A sequence of module sheaves is exact when each image sheaf equals the next kernel sheaf. Kernels are computed on sections, with their induced module structures. For a short exact sequence, the first nonzero map identifies its source with the kernel of the next, and the last nonzero map is surjective as a sheaf map. Exactness can equivalently be checked on every stalk of the underlying abelian sheaves; in particular a surjective morphism here means a surjection of sheaves, not necessarily a surjection on sections over every open set. [math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves) [math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves) [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

### Proof

**Proof technique:** direct application of the determinant and exact-sequence relations.

1.1 Let $L$ be the kernel of the given surjection. By [F1], $L$ is a vector bundle of rank $2-1=1$, and $0\to L\to Q\to\mathcal O_V\to0$ is short exact. [given, F1, F6]

2.1 By [F2] applied to that sequence, $\det L\otimes\det\mathcal O_V\cong\det Q$. By [F3], $\det L\cong L$ and $\det\mathcal O_V\cong\mathcal O_V$, so the line-bundle unit law gives $L\cong L\otimes\mathcal O_V\cong\det Q\cong\mathcal O_V$, using the given trivialization. Substitution in the sequence of step 1.1 gives the asserted short exact sequence. [given, F2, F3, step 1.1]

3.1 Under the additional Choice and locally Noetherian hypotheses, [F4] applied to that sequence gives $[Q]=[L]+[\mathcal O_V]=2[\mathcal O_V]$. This uses the exact-sequence relation and does not require a global splitting. [given, F4, step 2.1]

4.1 If the additional sequence $0\to Q\to P\to\mathcal O_V\to0$ is given, [F4] gives $[P]=[Q]+[\mathcal O_V]=3[\mathcal O_V]$. Thus all conclusions hold under their stated hypotheses. [given, F4, step 3.1] ∎


## Finite clopen common rank strata

[Open item](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata) · level 2


### Statement
Assume AC. Let $X$ be a quasi-compact quasi-separated scheme and $E_1,\ldots,E_m$ a finite family of finite locally free sheaves, with no fixed-rank assumption. The nonempty common rank loci $X_\alpha$, where $\alpha=(r_1,\ldots,r_m)$ and $\operatorname{rank}E_i=r_i$, form a finite partition by open-and-closed qcqs subschemes. Their disjoint union is canonically isomorphic to $X$. Each $E_i|_{X_\alpha}$ has constant rank $r_i$. The simultaneous flag towers $q_\alpha:Y_\alpha\to X_\alpha$ form a qcqs finite disjoint union $q:Y=\coprod_\alpha Y_\alpha\to X$. Rank-zero bundles have empty filtrations and require no projectivization; if every rank on a stratum is zero the tower is the identity there. The empty scheme has the empty partition and identity tower.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, the scheme $X$ and its finite family of bundles. A vector bundle means a finite locally free sheaf of $\mathcal O_X$-modules. Its rank at a point is the chart-independent finite free rank supplied by [F3]. Quasi-compact means that every open cover has a finite subcover. As in [F1], quasi-separated means that the intersection of any two quasi-compact open subsets is quasi-compact; qcqs means both properties. An open-and-closed subset is also called clopen.

**Goal:** Establish finiteness, category membership and the stated componentwise construction.

[F1] The flag construction gives finite projective towers for positive-constant-rank bundles, preserves qcqs, and uses the identity for rank zero. Its constant-rank simultaneous construction supplies locally split line-quotient filtrations for a finite family. Only that constant-rank part is used here. [lem-flag-tower-line-quotients](item-lem-flag-tower-line-quotients)

[F2] AC is retained for the choices used in the flag construction of [F1]; the finite rank partition itself needs no additional choice. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F3] Finite locally free sheaves have well-defined, locally constant finite rank functions: chart ranks agree at overlaps by invariant basis number for the nonzero local stalk rings. Restriction to an open subscheme preserves local freeness and this rank function. Rank zero means the zero sheaf. [math:def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank)

[F4] A scheme is a locally ringed space locally isomorphic to affine schemes; the empty space is allowed. An open subscheme $U$ is $(U,\mathcal O_X|_U)$. A morphism of schemes is a morphism of locally ringed spaces. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme), [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme), [math:def-morphism-of-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-of-schemes)

[F5] Compatible locally ringed spaces glue along open overlaps. For pairwise empty overlaps this constructs their disjoint union with its componentwise structure sheaf. [math:thm-gluing-ringed-and-locally-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-ringed-and-locally-ringed-spaces)

### Proof

1.1 By [F3], each rank function is well-defined and locally constant. A common rank locus is the finite intersection of its rank fibers, hence open; its complement is the union of the other such open loci, hence also open. Thus every common rank locus is clopen, and they partition $X$. Give each locus the restricted structure sheaf of [F4], so it is an open subscheme. The restrictions of the bundles have the prescribed constant ranks by [F3]. [given, F3, F4]

2.1 These pairwise disjoint loci form an open cover of $X$. Quasi-compactness gives a finite subcover; disjointness means every nonempty locus occurs in that subcover, so there are finitely many. Each locus is quasi-compact: extend any of its relative open covers by $X\setminus X_\alpha$ to cover $X$ and take a finite subcover. It is quasi-separated, since its quasi-compact opens are also quasi-compact opens in $X$, with the same intersections. Glue the loci along empty overlaps using [F5]. The resulting locally ringed space is a scheme by [F4], since its affine neighbourhoods are those in the components. Its open sets and sections identify with those of $X$: on an open $V$ the sheaf axiom gives $\mathcal O_X(V)\cong\prod_\alpha\mathcal O_X(V\cap X_\alpha)$. The component inclusions therefore induce the canonical scheme isomorphism $\coprod_\alpha X_\alpha\cong X$. [F4, F5, step 1.1, construct]

3.1 On each locus apply the constant-rank simultaneous construction in [F1] to the restricted bundles and concatenate the finitely many towers. Each $Y_\alpha$ is qcqs. Glue these schemes along empty overlaps using [F5], obtaining $Y$ with sections $\mathcal O_Y(V)=\prod_\alpha\mathcal O_{Y_\alpha}(V\cap Y_\alpha)$; [F4] again gives a scheme. Finitely many quasi-compact components make $Y$ quasi-compact by taking a finite subcover on each component. For any two quasi-compact opens $A,B$ in $Y$, the sets $A\cap Y_\alpha$ and $B\cap Y_\alpha$ are quasi-compact: the components are closed, and the closed-subset cover argument of step 2.1 applies. They are open in $Y_\alpha$, so their intersections are quasi-compact by its quasi-separatedness. Their finite union $A\cap B$ is quasi-compact. Thus $Y$ is quasi-separated. Define $q$ on $Y_\alpha$ as $q_\alpha$ followed by $X_\alpha\hookrightarrow X$. These maps give a continuous map, and their sheaf maps define its sheaf map componentwise by the displayed product. At each point its stalk map is the local homomorphism of the corresponding component map, so [F4] makes $q$ a scheme morphism. [F1, F2, F4, F5, step 2.1, construct]

4.1 The zero-rank cases use the zero sheaf of [F3] and the identity convention of [F1]. If every rank on a stratum is zero, its concatenation is empty and its tower is the identity. If the bundle family is empty and $X$ is nonempty, there is one common rank locus, indexed by the empty tuple, and its tower is also the identity. If $X$ is empty, there are no nonempty loci; the empty disjoint union is the empty scheme and its unique endomorphism is its identity, by [F4]. No bound on the dimension of $X$, no noetherianity, connectedness or integrality, and no global rank constancy was used. This proves the claims. [F1, F3, F4, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 3.1] ∎


## Line-quotient classes across clopen rank strata

[Open item](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-clopen-line-quotient-classes) · level 3


### Statement
Assume AC. For the simultaneous componentwise flag tower $q:Y=\coprod_\alpha Y_\alpha\to X$ of a finite bundle family on qcqs $X$, let $j_\alpha:Y_\alpha\hookrightarrow Y$ be its clopen inclusions. If $E_i$ has rank $r_{i,\alpha}$ on the stratum $X_\alpha$, its line-quotient filtration on $Y_\alpha$ gives
$$[q_\alpha^*(E_i|_{X_\alpha})]=\sum_{s=1}^{r_{i,\alpha}}[L_{i,\alpha,s}].$$
Here $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}$ denotes the group generated by isomorphism classes of finite locally free sheaves with the relation $[B]=[A]+[C]$ for every short exact sequence $0\to A\to B\to C\to0$. Extension by zero along a clopen inclusion preserves finite local freeness and gives the global equality
$$[q^*E_i]=\sum_\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r_{i,\alpha}}[j_{\alpha*}L_{i,\alpha,s}]\quad\text{in }K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(Y).$$
Each $j_{\alpha*}L$ has rank one on $Y_\alpha$ and zero elsewhere, so it is not a line bundle on all of $Y$ when the complement is nonempty. If all original ranks are constant, the ordinary simultaneous tower instead has global line quotients and gives a global sum of line-bundle classes. For varying ranks such an unqualified global line-bundle sum is false: on $X=\operatorname{Spec}k\coprod\operatorname{Spec}k$, the bundle $0\coprod\mathcal O$ cannot be a finite sum of global line-bundle classes, nor can its pullback along a surjective componentwise flag tower.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC and the family, partition and tower in the Statement; an empty sum is zero.

**Goal:** Prove the valid class identities and the precise obstruction to a global line sum.

[F1] The partition is finite, clopen and qcqs. [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata)

[F2] On each constant-rank stratum, the tower supplies locally split filtrations with line-bundle quotients; for constant ranks it supplies them globally. [lem-flag-tower-line-quotients](item-lem-flag-tower-line-quotients)

[F3] The defining relations of $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}$ are those stated above; they apply to locally split filtrations. AC is inherited from the tower. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F4] A sequence of sheaves of abelian groups is exact if and only if its sequence on every stalk is exact. We apply this to the underlying abelian sheaves of the module sheaves. [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

[F5] A morphism of sheaves of sets is an isomorphism if and only if its map on every stalk is a bijection. For a module-linear morphism the inverse, when it exists, is also module-linear, since applying the original injective map checks addition and scalar multiplication. [math:thm-sheaf-morphism-isomorphism-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sheaf-morphism-isomorphism-stalkwise)

[F6] A finite locally free sheaf has local charts $\mathcal O^r$ with chart-independent rank; a line bundle has rank one everywhere, and rank zero is the zero sheaf. [math:def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-free-sheaf-finite-rank)

[F7] Restriction to an open $U$ evaluates a sheaf on the opens contained in $U$. Pullback along $f:T\to S$ is $f^*G=\mathcal O_T\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal O_S}f^{-1}G$; on a local free chart it gives the corresponding free chart on the inverse-image open, as used in [F2]. [math:def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace), [math:def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-pullback-module-ringed-spaces)

[F8] Direct image along an inclusion $j:V\hookrightarrow Y$ has $(j_*L)(U)=L(U\cap V)$, with restrictions inherited from $L$. For an $\mathcal O_V$-module, the $\mathcal O_Y(U)$-action is through restriction to $U\cap V$. [math:def-direct-image-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-direct-image-sheaf)

[F9] The fibre of a module sheaf $G$ at $y$ is $G(y)=G_y\otimes_{\mathcal O_{Y,y}}\kappa(y)$, where $\kappa(y)$ is the residue field of the local stalk ring. On a free chart $\mathcal O^r$ this is $\kappa(y)^r$, so its dimension is the chart rank. [math:def-fibre-of-module-at-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-fibre-of-module-at-point)

### Proof

1.1 For a stratum filtration $0=F_0\subset\cdots\subset F_r=q_\alpha^*E_i$ with quotients $L_s$, each short exact sequence $0\to F_{s-1}\to F_s\to L_s\to0$ gives $[F_s]=[F_{s-1}]+[L_s]$. Starting at $[F_0]=0$ and adding the finitely many relations proves the componentwise identity, including $r=0$. The same argument proves the global line-quotient sum when ranks are constant and [F2] gives a global filtration. [F2, F3, algebra]

1.2 For $j_\alpha:Y_\alpha\hookrightarrow Y$, use the section formula of [F8] to define $j_{\alpha*}L$ and its module maps. This is a sheaf: a cover of an open $U$ intersects $Y_\alpha$ in a cover of $U\cap Y_\alpha$, and compatible sections glue uniquely there in $L$. On the open $Y_\alpha$ it is $L$, and on the open complement it is zero. If $L$ is finite locally free, these descriptions are local free charts of its extension, of the original ranks on the stratum and rank zero elsewhere by [F6]. Applied to a short exact sequence, extension has the original exact sequence on stalks in $Y_\alpha$ and the zero exact sequence on all other stalks. Thus [F4] proves exactness on the underlying abelian sheaves, hence exactness of the module sequence. Consequently extension preserves every relation of $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}$ and induces a homomorphism on that group. [F1, F3, F4, F6, F8, construct]

2.1 For a finite locally free sheaf $G$ on $Y$, define a module-linear map $\rho:G\to\bigoplus_\alpha j_{\alpha*}(G|_{Y_\alpha})$ by restricting sections to each component, using [F7] and [F8]. Here the finite direct sum is the sheaf whose sections are tuples of component sections, with componentwise operations and restrictions; the sheaf axiom holds coordinatewise. On the component $Y_\alpha$ only its own summand is nonzero, so $\rho$ is the identity there and is an isomorphism on every stalk. By [F5] it is an isomorphism of sheaves and its inverse is module-linear. This is a finite direct sum by [F1], so the split short exact sequences for successive partial sums and [F3] give $[G]=\sum_\alpha[j_{\alpha*}(G|_{Y_\alpha})]$. Apply this to $q^*E_i$, whose restriction to $Y_\alpha$ is $q_\alpha^*(E_i|_{X_\alpha})$ by the componentwise construction and the local pullback description in [F7]. Then push forward the equalities of step 1.1 by the exact extensions of step 1.2. This proves the stated global equality with supported, possibly varying-rank bundles, also for the empty partition. [F1, F3, F5, F7, F8, step 1.1, step 1.2, construct]

3.1 Rank at a point defines a homomorphism $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}(Y)\to\mathbb Z$. Indeed, for a short exact sequence of vector bundles, [F4] makes its stalk map onto the quotient surjective. Lift the finitely many elements of a local basis of the quotient to germs in the middle sheaf, represent them on neighbourhoods, and shrink to a common neighbourhood where their images are that basis. Extending linearly gives a local splitting. The middle fibre is therefore the direct sum of the outer fibres, so their dimensions, and hence their ranks by [F6] and [F9], add. A sum of $N$ global line-bundle classes has rank $N$ at every point. The displayed two-component bundle has ranks zero and one, so its class cannot equal such a sum. Pullback preserves ranks by [F7], and any surjective componentwise tower has nonempty preimages of both components, giving the same contradiction upstairs. This refutes the unqualified varying-rank line-sum assertion while retaining all valid identities above. [F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, F9, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## A coherent spectral realization of derived abelian complexes

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization) · level 4


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible universes of the derived and spectral interfaces. Let $\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ be the stable infinity-localization of unbounded cochain complexes of abelian groups at quasi-isomorphisms; its homotopy category is $D(\mathbb Z)$. Let $H\mathbb Z$ denote the discrete Eilenberg–Mac Lane ring spectrum of the integers. Fix the unit-preserving equivalence $\Phi:\operatorname{Mod}_{H\mathbb Z}\simeq\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ and an inverse with its equivalence data. Here a module spectrum is a spectrum with an $H\mathbb Z$ action including its associative and unital coherences; morphisms are coherent linear maps. Write $U$ for the forgetful functor to spectra and define
$$\mathcal H:=U\Phi^{-1}:\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sp}.$$
This is a coherent functor preserving small limits, zero objects, fibers and integer shifts, with natural identifications
$$\pi_i\mathcal H(C)\cong H^{-i}(C),\qquad \mathcal H(C[r])\simeq\mathcal H(C)[r]$$
for every derived complex $C$ and all integers $i,r$. Different inverse data for the fixed equivalence give naturally equivalent functors. Thus composing any supplied coherent complex-valued diagram with $\mathcal H$ gives a coherent spectral diagram, including its restriction arrows, limits and fibers, independent up to equivalence of those inverse data. No lift of an arbitrary ordinary $D(\mathbb Z)$-valued diagram is asserted.

### Statement references
[thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input)

[thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input)

[def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** Choice, compatible universes, the enhanced derived category, the fixed equivalence $\Phi$ and its inverse data, and the integer indices in the Statement.

**Goal:** Construct the coherent realization and justify its exactness, indexing and independence from inverse data.

[F1] [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input) supplies $\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ by taking the ring to be $\mathbb Z$. Its cochain shift has $C[r]^q=C^{q+r}$ and differential $(-1)^r d_C$. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) supplies spectra, their limits and fibers, and $\pi_i(E)=\pi_0\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb S[i],E)$.

[F2] [thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input) supplies the derived/module target and coherent mapping-space formalism. Reference 0 specifies the unit-preserving equivalence $\Phi$, the underlying-spectrum limit criterion for all small diagrams, and the unit mapping equivalence. The proof of its Theorem 7.1.2.13 identifies $\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathbb Z)}(\mathbb Z,C[n])$ with $H^n(C)$ after reversing homological grading.

[A1] Assume [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and the same universe existence and size conventions as [F1]. Choice is inherited from the supplied unbounded enhancement and spectral foundations; no choice of a representative complex for each value of a diagram is made.

### External input
Reference 0 imports the normalized derived/module equivalence, the module limit criterion and the unit mapping formula used in [F2]. Their hypotheses are checked with $R=\mathbb Z$ and spectra as ambient category. These established inputs are not proved here. Their composition into $\mathcal H$ and the indexing calculation are the local argument below.

### Proof
1.1 Under [A1], [F1] and [F2] supply actual enhanced categories and functors. Compose the inverse equivalence with $U$ to obtain $\mathcal H$ on objects, arrows and all higher simplices. An equivalence preserves limits by their mapping-space universal property, and $U$ computes them by [F2]; hence $\mathcal H$ preserves small limits. The zero module has zero underlying spectrum. It follows that $\mathcal H$ preserves fibers and loops. Suspension is inverse to loops in both stable categories, so it also preserves suspension and every integer shift. For two inverse functors $E,E'$ to the fixed $\Phi$, their coherent unit and counit give $E\simeq E\Phi E'\simeq E'$. Applying $U$ gives a natural equivalence of realizations. Applying such an equivalence to a coherent diagram and its limiting cones gives equivalent diagrams and limits, including the fiber projections. [F1, F2, A1, given]

2.1 For $N=\Phi^{-1}C$, shift compatibility and the unit mapping equivalence in [F2] give $\pi_i(UN)=\pi_0\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb S,UN[-i])\cong\pi_0\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{H\mathbb Z}}(H\mathbb Z,N[-i])$. The unit-preserving equivalence carries this to $\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathbb Z)}(\mathbb Z,C[-i])=H^{-i}(C)$ by [F2]. All identifications are natural in enhanced arrows. Precomposing $\mathcal H$ with a supplied coherent functor therefore gives the asserted coherent realization of that entire functor; step 1.1 gives its limit, fiber and choice comparisons. This proves the claim for supplied enhanced diagrams without constructing an enhancement from ordinary derived-category arrows. [F1, F2, A1, step 1.1] ∎


## Finite derived products and motivic coefficient operations

[Open item](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products) · level 5


### Statement
For a finite family $C_\alpha\in D(\mathbb Z)$ and $i\in\mathbb Z$,
$$H^i\!\left(\prod_\alpha C_\alpha\right)\cong\prod_\alpha H^i(C_\alpha).$$
For every positive integer $n$, writing $C/n=C\otimes^{\mathbf L}\mathbb Z/n$, one also has
$$\left(\prod_\alpha C_\alpha\right)/n\simeq\prod_\alpha(C_\alpha/n).$$
These equivalences are induced by the coordinate projections. Consequently a finite-product decomposition of derived complexes induces the corresponding decomposition on integral and finite-coefficient cohomology, and each such joint restriction map is injective. The empty product is zero throughout.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** A finite index set $A$, a family $(C_\alpha)_{\alpha\in A}$ of objects of $D(\mathbb Z)$, an integer $i$, and $n>0$.

**Goal:** Prove the two projection-induced comparisons and joint injectivity, including $A=\varnothing$.

[F1] Cohomology is functorial on $D(\mathbb Z)$. [lem-author-b747b28c359f-derived-cohomology](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-derived-cohomology)

[F2] The functor $C\mapsto C/n=C\otimes^{\mathbf L}_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Z/n$ is computed on any cochain representative by the cone of multiplication by $n$, without boundedness assumptions. [lem-author-b747b28c359f-derived-finite-coefficients](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-derived-finite-coefficients)

[F3] Use the same-object localization $D(\mathbb Z)=K(\operatorname{Ab})[\mathrm{qis}^{-1}]$ and its standing size conventions. The category $\operatorname{Ab}$ is abelian. A cochain complex has groups $C^d$ and homomorphisms $d^d:C^d\to C^{d+1}$ with $d^{d+1}d^d=0$. [math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category), [math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category), [math:def-cochain-complex-in-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-cochain-complex-in-an-abelian-category)

[F4] The canonical functor from complexes to their homotopy category is additive, under the cochain reindexing $C^\sharp_d=C^{-d}$. Quasi-isomorphisms in $K(\operatorname{Ab})$ form a two-sided multiplicative system. With the size conventions in [F3], addition of roofs makes its localization additive; localization preserves zero objects and finite biproducts. A finite biproduct has inclusions $j_\alpha$ and projections $p_\alpha$ satisfying $p_\alpha j_\beta=\delta_{\alpha\beta}$ and $\sum_\alpha j_\alpha p_\alpha=1$, where the first right-hand side is the identity for equal indices and the zero morphism otherwise. [math:prop-the-canonical-functor-from-complexes-to-the-homotopy-category-is-additive](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-the-canonical-functor-from-complexes-to-the-homotopy-category-is-additive), [math:lem-quasi-isomorphisms-admit-the-roof-calculus-in-the-homotopy-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-quasi-isomorphisms-admit-the-roof-calculus-in-the-homotopy-category), [math:lem-addition-of-roofs-makes-an-additive-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-addition-of-roofs-makes-an-additive-localization)

[F5] Write $Z^d(C)=\ker d^d$, $B^d(C)=\operatorname{im}d^{d-1}$ and $H^d(C)=Z^d(C)/B^d(C)$. [math:def-cohomology-object-of-a-cochain-complex](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-cohomology-object-of-a-cochain-complex)

[F6] For a cochain map $f:C\to D$, use $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^d=D^d\oplus C^{d+1}$ with differential $(y,x)\mapsto(d_Dy+f x,-d_Cx)$. This is the chain-cone convention reindexed by $C^\sharp_d=C^{-d}$; the cochain-map condition makes its differential square zero. [math:def-mapping-cone-of-a-chain-map](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-mapping-cone-of-a-chain-map)

### Proof

1.1 In the same-object model [F3], choose cochain representatives and set $P^d=\prod_{\alpha\in A}C_\alpha^d$ with coordinatewise differential. Finite direct sums and products of abelian groups coincide, so the coordinate inclusions and projections are cochain maps satisfying the identities in [F4]. Those identities persist under the two additive functors from complexes through $K(\operatorname{Ab})$ to $D(\mathbb Z)$. For every object $T$ and tuple of derived morphisms $f_\alpha:T\to C_\alpha$, the morphism $f=\sum_\alpha j_\alpha f_\alpha:T\to P$ satisfies $p_\alpha f=f_\alpha$. If $g$ has these same coordinates, then $g=(\sum_\alpha j_\alpha p_\alpha)g=\sum_\alpha j_\alpha f_\alpha=f$. This is exactly the categorical product universal property in the derived category. By [F5], $Z^d(P)=\prod_\alpha Z^d(C_\alpha)$ and $B^d(P)=\prod_\alpha B^d(C_\alpha)$: the boundary equality follows by lifting finitely many image elements to preimages. The map from $Z^d(P)$ to $\prod_\alpha(Z^d(C_\alpha)/B^d(C_\alpha))$ is surjective by finitely many choices of representatives and has kernel $B^d(P)$. It therefore induces the first displayed isomorphism, with components $H^d(p_\alpha)$ by [F1]. This also shows that finite degreewise products preserve quasi-isomorphisms. If $A$ is empty, $P$ is the zero complex; the functors in [F4] preserve its zero-object property, so it is terminal and represents the empty product, with zero cohomology. All selections here are finite. [given, F1, F3, F4, F5, construct, algebra]

2.1 Multiplication by $n$ on $P$ is coordinatewise. By [F6], the map $\operatorname{Cone}(n:P\to P)^d\to\prod_\alpha\operatorname{Cone}(n:C_\alpha\to C_\alpha)^d$ sends $((y_\alpha)_\alpha,(x_\alpha)_\alpha)$ to $((y_\alpha,x_\alpha))_\alpha$. It is an isomorphism in each degree and respects the differentials $(y,x)\mapsto(dy+nx,-dx)$, so it is an isomorphism of cochain complexes. By [F2] and the product universal property in step 1.1, this is the second displayed equivalence in $D(\mathbb Z)$; its components are precisely the coefficient functor applied to $p_\alpha$. Applying [F1] and the first comparison of step 1.1 gives the corresponding finite-coefficient cohomology isomorphism. If $C\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_\alpha C_\alpha$ is a finite-product decomposition, functoriality transports both comparisons to the joint maps from $C$, integrally and after applying $-/n$. A tuple is zero exactly when every coordinate is zero, so those joint cohomology maps are injective. For the empty family the cone and all cohomology groups are zero, and these conclusions still hold. [F1, F2, F6, step 1.1, algebra] ∎


## From simplicial sequential spectra to the fixed coherent realization

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-a9ee7502efb6-simplicial-spectra-realization) · level 5


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and compatible nested Grothendieck universes containing $\omega$ and all the following data; their existence is a size hypothesis. Put $K=\Delta[1]/\partial\Delta[1]$. A simplicial sequential spectrum $E$ consists of pointed simplicial sets $E_n$, for $n\geq0$, and sphere-first maps $K\wedge E_n\to E_{n+1}$. Its morphisms are families of based level maps commuting with these maps; $N$ denotes the ordinary category nerve, whose simplices are composable strings of arrows. Define a stable weak equivalence to mean an isomorphism on every group $\operatorname*{colim}_{n\gg0}\pi_{n+i}(|E_n|)$, with bonding maps induced by the structure maps and the ordered suspension, for each integer $i$.

Let $\mathcal R$ be the functor for topological sequential prespectra in the stable-spectra interface. Levelwise geometric realization defines the functor
$$J(E)=\mathcal R(|E|):N(\mathrm{Spt}_{\mathrm{sSet}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sp}.$$
It sends stable weak equivalences to equivalences. No Kan or cofibrancy assumption on the given $E_n$ is needed. Consequently it can be applied to every value, arrow and composition of a strict presheaf of these spectra, preserving pointwise stable weak equivalences. A strict presheaf here is an ordinary functor $I^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Spt}_{\mathrm{sSet}}$ from a small ordinary category.

For an unbounded cochain complex $C$ of abelian groups, use the ordinary additive Eilenberg–Mac Lane model $\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(C)$: its $n$th pointed simplicial set is the underlying set of $\mathrm{DK}(\tau_{\geq0}(C[n])^\sharp)$, with the sphere-first bonding maps specified below. Here $(C[n])^\sharp_q=C[n]^{-q}$, $C[n]^a=C^{a+n}$ with differential $(-1)^n d_C$, and smart truncation of a homological complex retains positive degrees, replaces degree zero by its cycles, and kills negative degrees. The functor $\mathrm{DK}$ is inverse normalization in the mapping-space convention $N'_q(M)=\bigcap_{i=1}^q\ker d_i$ with differential $d_0$; zero is the basepoint. For cochain complexes $A,D$ of abelian groups, write $B(A,D)=\mathrm{DK}(\tau_{\geq0}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(A^\sharp,D^\sharp))$ for the simplicial dg mapping object, with Alexander–Whitney composition as specified in [F5]. Let $u_n:K\to B(C[n],C[n+1])$ be the loop at zero whose oriented nondegenerate edge has normalized degree-one coefficient the graded identity $C[n]^\sharp_q\to C[n+1]^\sharp_{q+1}$. This has coefficient $+1$, without a degree-dependent sign. Evaluation at $1$ identifies $B(\mathbb Z[0],C[n])$ with the stated $n$th level. Define its bonding map by composition:
$$\sigma_n:K\wedge B(\mathbb Z[0],C[n])\xrightarrow{u_n\wedge1}B(C[n],C[n+1])\wedge B(\mathbb Z[0],C[n])\xrightarrow{\circ}B(\mathbb Z[0],C[n+1]).$$
Composition is zero when either input is zero, so the displayed smash maps are defined. In particular the new circle is the left composition input. There is a natural equivalence
$$J(\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(C))\simeq\mathcal H(C),$$
where $\mathcal H=U\Phi^{-1}$ is the fixed unit-preserving realization. This is a comparison of functors on cochain complexes of abelian groups, compatible with shifts and every cochain map, hence with whole presheaves of such complexes and cochain maps. It induces the identity on the degree-zero integer generator. For the unnormalized alternating-face chain complex $s(M)$ of a simplicial abelian group $M$, read as the cochain complex $C^a=s(M)_{-a}$ with the same differential, it also agrees, through the natural normalization equivalence, with its ordinary additive realization; no sign automorphism is introduced.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The size assumptions, simplicial sequential spectra and cochain complexes of abelian groups in the Statement.

**Goal:** Construct the missing functor and compare the ordinary additive models with the fixed coherent realization.

[F5] [math:def-hom-complex-of-chain-complexes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-hom-complex-of-chain-complexes) supplies $\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(A,D)_r=\prod_q\operatorname{Hom}(A_q,D_{q+r})$ with $\partial f=d_Df-(-1)^rf d_A$. Concretely, let $Q_q$ be the free chain complex on the nondegenerate ordered faces of $\Delta[q]$, with alternating deletion boundary. The first-face Dold–Kan model has $B(A,D)_q=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ch}}(Q_q,\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(A^\sharp,D^\sharp))$. For $y\in B(D,T)_q$ and $x\in B(A,D)_q$, composition on a face is
$$ (y\circ x)[i_0,\ldots,i_r]=\sum_{p=0}^r y[i_0,\ldots,i_p]\circ x[i_p,\ldots,i_r].$$
Let $\mathrm{Ch}_\Delta$ have cochain complexes of abelian groups as objects and these $B(A,D)$ as mapping objects. Thus this is a specified simplicial category, not a choice of maps on homotopy groups. The identification with $Q_q$ uses the natural quotient-by-degeneracies description of normalized free simplex chains. Constructions 1.3.1.16 and Proposition 1.3.1.17 give a functor from the coherent nerve of this single simplicial category to its dg nerve; its formula on a degree-one mapping simplex retains that coefficient with positive sign.

[F6] The canonical cone square of [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input), for the zero map $A\to0$, has cofiber $A[1]$ and nullhomotopy $h^a:A^a\to A[1]^{a-1}$ equal to the identity. It is a coherent square in the dg nerve, and its image under derived localization is still a cofiber square. The same supplied stable structure makes it a fiber square. [thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input) supplies preservation of limits by $\operatorname{Map}(\mathbb Z,-)$. These inputs determine the target's loop equivalence with its nullhomotopy, rather than just its groups.

Reference 0 imports realization, normalization, Dold–Kan homotopy groups and the Kan loop comparison in [F1], [F3] and [F4]. Reference 1 imports the Alexander–Whitney enrichment and nerve comparison in [F3] and [F5], and the unit-mapping and derived/module inputs in [F2]. ### Proof
1.1 Realize the levels and structure maps using [F1]: $S^1\wedge|E_n|\cong|K\wedge E_n|\to|E_{n+1}|$. The strong monoidal comparison preserves compositions and the chosen order of the new circle. Each realized level is based CW, so $\mathcal R$ applies without a Kan assumption on $E_n$. Compose these functors to define $J$ on the nerve of the ordinary category, retaining its compositions and all nerve simplices. If $f$ is a stable weak equivalence as defined, [F1] identifies its stable groups with those used by $\mathcal R$, so $J(f)$ is an equivalence. Applying this single functor to a strict presheaf gives an entire coherent diagram, and a strict presheaf map gives its coherent natural transformation. This establishes the required model passage before using any localization of presheaves. [A1, F1, given, construct]

2.1 Let $C$ be a cochain complex of abelian groups and put $M_n(C)=B(\mathbb Z[0],C[n])$. First check the proposed bonding map. With $D_n=C[n]^\sharp$, the degree-one graded identity $v_n:D_n\to D_{n+1}$ is closed, since $\partial v_n=d_{D_{n+1}}v_n+v_nd_{D_n}=0$: the two differentials are $(-1)^{n+1}d_C$ and $(-1)^nd_C$. It therefore determines $u_n$ with both faces zero. Formula [F5] constructs $\sigma_n$ as a simplicial map; associativity and its zero-input property give the specified sphere-first structure. For every cochain map $f$, the identity $f[n+1]v_n=v_n f[n]$ holds as graded maps, so these bonding maps commute strictly with every $f$ and its composites. Now use the single functor $L:N_{\mathrm{hc}}(\mathrm{Ch}_\Delta)\to N_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathrm{Ch})\to\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ from [F3], [F5] and [F2], where $N_{\mathrm{hc}}$ denotes the coherent nerve and the dg category uses grading reversal. It induces maps $\eta_n:M_n(C)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)}(\mathbb Z,C[n])$ in spaces, natural with all composition coherences. Each is an equivalence: to check this, take a K-injective resolution $C[n]\to I$ from [F2]. On the simplicial side evaluation at $1$ and [F3] make $M_n(C)\to B(\mathbb Z[0],I)$ a weak equivalence. On the derived side the resolution is an equivalence. Into the local object $I$, the reflective adjunction and the fully faithful K-injective model identify the mapping space with $B(\mathbb Z[0],I)$, including the comparison induced by $L$. This proves the assertion about the already defined $\eta_n$; no independently chosen inverses are assembled. Crucially, apply $L$ also to $u_n$ and its composition pairing. The positive degree-one coefficient is unchanged by [F5]'s nerve-comparison formula. It is exactly the identity nullhomotopy $h^a=1$ in [F6]'s cone square for $C[n]\to0$, with cofiber $C[n+1]$. Hence the loop obtained by composing $u_n$ with a map from $\mathbb Z$ is the loop comparison obtained by applying $\operatorname{Map}(\mathbb Z,-)$ to that fiber square. This identifies the actual adjoint of $\sigma_n$, including its sign and circle orientation, with the target's canonical loop map under $\eta_n$ and $\eta_{n+1}$. All its coherence data are images of these cone squares and the associative simplicial composition under the same $L$. Their naturality in $f$ is the strict identity above; iterating the squares preserves their specified pastings. In particular the adjoints $M_n(C)\to\Omega M_{n+1}(C)$ are weak equivalences, since the target loop maps and the $\eta_n$ are equivalences. These levels are Kan by [F3]; its realization/loop comparison makes their realizations an Omega-prespectrum. By [F2], the unit-preserving $\Phi$ and the module unit-mapping equivalence identify the entire derived tower naturally with $\Omega^\infty\mathcal H(C)[n]$, respecting the same fiber squares. Thus [F1] and step 1.1 identify $J(\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(C))$ naturally with $\mathcal H(C)$. For every integer $r$, the level $n$ of $\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(C[r])$ equals level $n+r$ of $\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(C)$ whenever $n+r\geq0$: their differentials are both $(-1)^{n+r}d_C$ and their bonding loops are the same graded identities. The resulting common late tails, and [F1]’s tail/shift comparison, give compatibility with every integer shift. At $C=\mathbb Z[0]$, $\sigma_0$ sends the positively oriented circle and $a\in\mathbb Z$ to the degree-one coefficient $a$; evaluation at $1$ therefore fixes the positive integer generator. For the unnormalized description, [F4]'s natural quasi-isomorphisms become maps of these spectra by the strict naturality just proved, and become equivalences under $J$; their inclusions retain the original cycle coefficients. This gives the claimed normalization comparison without a sign automorphism. The same single natural equivalence applies to every arrow and composite of a presheaf of cochain complexes of abelian groups. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, step 1.1] ∎

## Derived Nisnevich descent on a finite clopen partition

[Open item](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-clopen-derived-descent) · level 7


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the compatible Grothendieck-universe and site-size conventions of the supplied unbounded derived construction, with $\omega$ in the base universe. Write $D(\mathbb Z)=\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ for the enhanced localization of unbounded cochain complexes of unital $\mathbb Z$-modules at quasi-isomorphisms, retaining its coherent mapping spaces. Let $F:\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}\to D(\mathbb Z)$ be a homotopy-coherent Nisnevich sheaf on a category of schemes within these size bounds. Suppose the empty scheme and a finite clopen partition $X=\coprod_{\alpha\in A}X_\alpha$, its inclusions and their iterated fiber products lie in $\mathcal C$. Here a clopen partition consists of pairwise disjoint open-and-closed subschemes covering $X$, with the restricted structure sheaves; empty pieces and an empty index set are allowed. Require every augmentation to $X$ and every face and degeneracy map between the chosen iterated fiber products to be a morphism of $\mathcal C$. Require also the canonical scheme isomorphism from each such product to $X_\alpha$ when all indices equal $\alpha$, or to $\varnothing$ otherwise, and its inverse, to be morphisms of $\mathcal C$. Then $F(\varnothing)\simeq0$, and restriction induces a natural equivalence
$$F(X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\prod_{\alpha\in A}F(X_\alpha).$$
Here sheaf means that the canonical comparison for every covering family whose full augmented Cech system of intersection objects and morphisms is available in $\mathcal C$ is an equivalence to the homotopy limit of its Cech descent diagram; the empty covering family is included. All products and limits are coherent ones in the enhanced category, not limits in its ordinary homotopy category. A totalization is the limit of a cosimplicial diagram, indexed by the simplex category $\Delta$ of the nonempty finite ordered sets $[n]=\{0,\ldots,n\}$ and all order-preserving maps. Its nerve has $r$-simplices the strings of $r$ composable maps, with faces given by composition or endpoint deletion and degeneracies by identity insertion. These indexing diagrams and cover index sets are small relative to the chosen derived enhancement. The empty product is its zero object. No assertion about injectivity for general overlapping open covers is made.
