### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The Choice and size assumptions, the enhanced target, the sheaf $F$ and the finite partition in the Statement.

**Goal:** Compute descent on this particular finite cover, including the empty case.

[A1] Choice and the explicit universe assumptions are retained when applying the unbounded enhancement supplier; existence of the chosen universes is a size assumption, not a consequence asserted from Choice. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention)

[A2] The category contains every augmentation, face and degeneracy map of the chosen intersection objects and both directions of their canonical isomorphisms with the pieces or the empty scheme, as required in the Statement. Thus $F$ is defined on all these maps, and sends each specified isomorphism to an equivalence. These canonical maps have their scheme fiber-product and open-restriction meanings. [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) [math:lem-fibre-product-open-restriction](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-fibre-product-open-restriction)

[F1] A Nisnevich cover is an etale family with residue-field-preserving lifts of every point. Open immersions identify the source with the restricted open subscheme and are etale, including the empty immersion. Thus their local rings and residue fields agree with those of the target at corresponding points. Fiber products with open subschemes are inverse-image open subschemes. [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes) [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme) [math:ex-localization-etale-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-localization-etale-open-immersion) [math:lem-fibre-product-open-restriction](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-fibre-product-open-restriction)

[F2] The ring-$\mathbb Z$ case of the enhancement supplier constructs the stable presentable infinity-category $D(\mathbb Z)$ under [A1]; stable includes a zero object. Reference 0 supplies its small coherent limits and the terminal-cone and mapping-space characterizations of limits and products. A cone with vertex $L$ is a coherent family of maps from $L$ to the diagram; terminality means that its space of factorizations from every other cone is contractible. [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input)

[F3] By the assumed sheaf condition, $F(X)$ is the totalization of the cosimplicial diagram $C^\bullet$ with
$$C^n=\prod_{(\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_n)\in A^{n+1}}F(X_{\alpha_0}\times_X\cdots\times_X X_{\alpha_n}).$$
By [A2], applying $F$ to the actual Cech face and degeneracy maps defines its structure maps, induced by coordinate projections and repetitions. Descent for the empty family gives the terminal object: every degree is an empty product. This is the assumed derived sheaf condition for the covering families of [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface), not an assertion that ordinary sheaf values compute derived sections.

[F4] Reference 1 supplies the identity-cone limit for a constant diagram indexed by a weakly contractible simplicial set. Reference 2 supplies contractibility of the nerve of an ordinary category with a terminal object and its implication of weak contractibility. The constant diagram has one value and identity structure maps, with their constant coherences. Weak contractibility means that projection of the indexing simplicial set to a point is a weak homotopy equivalence.

### External input
Import the coherent-limit interface and existence theorem of reference 0, the constant-cone criterion of reference 1, and the nerve-contractibility facts of reference 2, only with their stated hypotheses and reading limits. The enhanced target itself is supplied by the ring-$\mathbb Z$ specialization of [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input) under [A1]. The two-step computation below is a local application of these inputs; no reconstruction of their foundational proofs is claimed.

### Proof

1.1 By [F1] the clopen inclusions cover $X$ in the Nisnevich topology: each point lies in a piece with the same local ring and residue field. The empty family covers the empty scheme since its point-lifting condition is vacuous. In that empty-cover case [F3] is the constant diagram at the terminal object. The simplex category has terminal object $[0]$, since there is exactly one order-preserving map $[n]\to[0]$ for every $n$; hence [F4] makes its nerve weakly contractible and makes the identity cone on any constant diagram a limit cone. Thus empty-cover descent identifies $F(\varnothing)$ with the terminal object of $D(\mathbb Z)$, which is its zero object by [F2]. If $A$ is empty then $X=\varnothing$, and the desired equivalence is already proved. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4]

2.1 Suppose $A$ is nonempty, and put $P=\prod_{\alpha\in A}F(X_\alpha)$. Repeated use of the inverse-image description in [F1] identifies each iterated fiber product in [F3] canonically with $X_\alpha$ when all indices equal $\alpha$, and with the empty open subscheme otherwise. By [A2] these isomorphisms and their inverses lie in $\mathcal C$, so applying $F$ identifies the diagonal factors with $F(X_\alpha)$ and the mixed factors with $F(\varnothing)$. Projecting $C^n$ onto its diagonal factors therefore gives an equivalence $C^n\to P$: every omitted factor is terminal by step 1.1, and the product mapping-space property in [F2] makes insertion or deletion of terminal factors an equivalence. These projections form a coherent natural equivalence $C^\bullet\simeq\operatorname{const}_{\Delta}P$. Indeed every order-preserving map sends the diagonal tuple to the same diagonal tuple, and the corresponding scheme map between the canonical repeated intersections is the identity on $X_\alpha$; the canonical intersection identifications respect composition. Contravariant functoriality of $F$ preserves these coherences, and the discarded terminal factors have contractible spaces of choices. Equivalent diagrams have equivalent coherent cone categories, so their terminal cones correspond by [F2]. By [F4] and the terminal-object calculation in step 1.1 the limit of this constant diagram is $P$. Applying descent [F3] gives the displayed equivalence; its components are the restriction maps because the augmentation followed by a diagonal projection is restriction to $X_\alpha$. These projections commute with morphisms of sheaves and pullback of this partition whenever defined in $\mathcal C$, proving naturality. [A2, F1, F2, F3, F4, step 1.1, construct] ∎


## External regular perfection and the standard derived t structure

[Open item](item-thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-regular-cohomology-input) · level 7


### Statement
For the enhanced perfect-category assertion, assume the Axiom of Choice and fix suitable universes. Let $U$ be a regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme. Inside the specified sheaf-derived infinity-category, the full subcategory of perfect objects is precisely the full subcategory of objects with bounded coherent cohomology. The standard cohomological truncation functors $\tau_{\leq n},\tau_{\geq n}$ and their truncation cofiber sequences are those of the ambient derived t-structure, whose heart is ordinary sheaves of modules.

For a locally Noetherian scheme, coherent sheaves form an abelian full subcategory, with kernels, images and cokernels computed as module sheaves. A complex having coherent cohomology and only finitely many nonzero cohomology sheaves remains so under standard truncation.

Here a **regular scheme** means a locally Noetherian scheme every point of which is regular, that is, every local ring is regular local. Finite-dimensional refers to the chain dimension of the underlying space, with the empty-space convention. Bounded coherent cohomology means that every $H^i$ is coherent and $H^i=0$ outside some finite interval of integers, depending on the object.

### Source contract
[A1] The stated [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and universe bounds discharge the selected enhancement premises of [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) and the derived-enhancement input in [F2]. They apply to the enhanced perfect-category assertion. The coherent-sheaf assertion in the second paragraph is the ordinary local statement of [F1], and its truncation-of-cohomology assertion uses [F4]; neither invokes that enhanced realization.

**Given:** For the enhanced assertion, the Axiom of Choice, suitable universes, and the specified infinity-localization of Zariski module cochain complexes on $U$, and its underlying ordinary derived category.

**Goal:** Import local regular perfection and standard truncation, without asserting global vector-bundle resolutions.

[F1] Stacks Lemmas 36.11.6(1) and 36.11.8 establish exactly the equality of object conditions in the Statement; the introduction to §36.11 gives the coherent abelian subcategory. Reference 0 is the imported source.

[F2] Higher Algebra Proposition 1.3.5.21(2) with Definition 1.3.5.16 and Remark 1.3.5.19 supplies the ambient derived t-structure and its heart, with cochain index reversal. Definitions 1.2.1.1 and 1.2.1.4, Proposition 1.2.1.5, Notation 1.2.1.7 and Remark 1.2.1.8 supply its enhanced interpretation, truncation functors and truncation cofiber sequences. [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input) specifies this actual localization and establishes that its category of all module sheaves is Grothendieck abelian. Reference 1 is the imported source.

[F3] The formulation uses [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme), [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point), [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme) and [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space). A regular scheme is defined in the Statement using the regular-point supplier, without adding smoothness over a field. The suitable universes are the ones assumed by the enhanced suppliers, with the closure convention of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention); their existence is a standing size assumption, not a consequence of Choice.

[F4] [math:def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category) fixes the cohomological t-structure and heart terminology. [math:def-canonical-truncation-of-a-complex](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-canonical-truncation-of-a-complex) fixes the kernel/cokernel truncations, and [math:lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology) gives their cohomology identities and descent to the ordinary derived category: retained cohomology sheaves are unchanged and deleted ones are zero. These identities supply the truncation assertion in the second paragraph of the Statement; [F2] supplies the actual enhanced functors and cofiber sequences.

[F5] [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category) specifies the abelian-category condition, and [math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves) specifies kernels, cokernels and images in module sheaves. Reference 0, via the coherent abelian subcategory in the introduction to Stacks §36.11, supplies closure under these ambient operations on a locally Noetherian scheme.

### External input
Import [F1]–[F2] under their exact hypotheses and reading limits. This is an equality of full subcategories defined by objects in the already specified enhancement; it is not an inference of an enhanced equivalence from an abstract triangulated equivalence. The regular-perfect result is local on affine neighbourhoods. Neither a global bounded locally free resolution nor an equivalence with $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}_T)$ is needed or asserted.


## External projective bundle input for the actual motivic complex

[Open item](item-thm-author-ab0a6fc202cb-elmanto-morrow-projective-input) · level 8


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice throughout. Fix Grothendieck universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$, containing all fields, schemes and bundle data under consideration. Existence of these universes is a separate size assumption, inherited together with Choice from the actual motivic-complex supplier.

Let $\mathbb F=\mathbb Q$ or $\mathbb F_p$, let $X$ be a qcqs $\mathbb F$-scheme, and let $E$ be finite locally free of constant rank $r+1>0$. Here qcqs means quasi-compact and quasi-separated. Write $\mathbb Z(t)_{\mathrm{mot}}$ for the actual contravariant Elmanto–Morrow derived-complex functor, with its source multiplicative structure and natural first Chern operation. For $j\geq0$, the map
$$\Phi:\bigoplus_{s=0}^{r}\mathbb Z(j-s)_{\mathrm{mot}}(X)[-2s]\xrightarrow{\sum c_1(\mathcal O(1))^s\pi^*}\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{sub},X}(E))$$
is an equivalence of derived complexes. The source projectivization $\pi$ classifies locally split line subbundles of $E$, and $\mathcal O(1)$ is the dual of its tautological line. Negative-weight complexes in this formula are zero by the source convention. Pullback $\pi^*$ is evaluation of the actual contravariant functor on $\pi$; the summand map is its composition with multiplication by the $s$-th power of that first Chern class. For $s=0$ this multiplication is the identity. Consequently the actual pullback has a retraction in $D(\mathbb Z)$.

Here $D(\mathbb Z)$ is the homotopy category of the supplied derived enhancement of cochain complexes of $V$-small abelian groups, considered in $W$, with quasi-isomorphisms inverted. The shift convention is $C[n]^a=C^{a+n}$ with differential $(-1)^n d_C$. The finite direct sum carries its degreewise coordinate inclusions and projections.

For the native quotient-convention application, retain the same global Choice and universe assumptions; Choice is also inherited from the library's relative Proj construction. If $G$ is finite locally free of constant rank $r+1>0$, the same formula and retraction hold for $\mathbb P_X(G)$ in that convention: apply the subbundle formula to $E=G^\vee$. The canonical identification with $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{sub},X}(G^\vee)$ preserves the projection and identifies its $\mathcal O(1)$ with the native tautological quotient.

A first Chern class has cohomological degree two and weight one, so its $s$-fold action has map type $\mathbb Z(t)_{\mathrm{mot}}(T)[-2s]\to\mathbb Z(t+s)_{\mathrm{mot}}(T)$ for nonnegative $t,s$. For a negative source weight the summand map is the zero map from the zero object. The zero-power action is the identity. These are the operations and conventions imported from reference 1.

Choice is also inherited from relative Proj and the selection of trivializing covers in [F5]. The finite coordinate calculation below requires no additional choice. ### Proof
**Given:** The global Choice and universe assumptions [A1], and the prime field, qcqs scheme, positive constant rank and weight in the Statement, with all relevant field, scheme and bundle data in $U$.

**Goal:** Derive the pullback retraction and the native-convention application from the explicit imported equivalence.

1.1 Under [A1], by [F1] and [F2], $\Phi$ is an equivalence with $\Phi i_0=\pi^*$, since its zero-power action is the identity and [F3] identifies its pullback with the actual functor map. In $D(\mathbb Z)$ put $\rho=p_0\Phi^{-1}$. Then $\rho\pi^*=p_0\Phi^{-1}\Phi i_0=p_0i_0=1$ by [F6]. This proves the claimed retraction for every nonnegative weight, including zero with the stated vanishing of negative summands. [F1, F2, F3, F6, A1]

2.1 For a test scheme $f:T\to X$, a quotient $f^*G\twoheadrightarrow L$ with $L$ a line bundle splits locally: trivialize $G,L$ near a point and write the surjection as a row $(a_0,\ldots,a_r)$; some $a_k$ is a unit near that point, and $a_k^{-1}e_k$ lifts the frame of $L$, while $e_i-a_i a_k^{-1}e_k$ for $i\ne k$ is a free basis of its kernel. Dualizing this local direct-sum decomposition gives a locally split line subbundle $L^\vee\hookrightarrow f^*(G^\vee)$. Conversely a locally split line subbundle $M\hookrightarrow f^*(G^\vee)$ dualizes to the quotient $f^*G\twoheadrightarrow M^\vee$. By [F4], biduality makes these operations inverse on isomorphism classes, and pullback compatibility makes them natural in $T$. Apply the representing properties in [F1] and [F5] to the universal bundles: they give inverse scheme morphisms over $X$, because each composite recovers the same universal subbundle or quotient. The source tautological subline becomes $L^\vee$, so its dual $\mathcal O(1)$ becomes exactly $L$, the native universal quotient. Under [A1] these native objects exist as specified in [F5]; [F4] gives $G^\vee$ the required positive constant rank. Thus [F1] applies to $G^\vee$, and naturality of [F2] and [F3] transports both its map and the retraction from step 1.1 to the native bundle. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, A1, step 1.1] ∎


## Finite ideal-power filtrations and coherent sheaves on the closed subscheme

[Open item](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-ideal-filtration) · level 8


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $i:T\hookrightarrow U$ be any closed subscheme of the regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme $U$, with coherent defining ideal $\mathcal I=\ker(\mathcal O_U\to i_*\mathcal O_T)$. Write $\mathrm{Coh}(X)$ for the category of coherent $\mathcal O_X$-modules and all their module-sheaf morphisms. Let $\mathcal A_T$ be its full subcategory for $X=U$ on modules $M$ with $\operatorname{Supp}(M)\subseteq |T|$, and let $\mathcal B_T$ be the full subcategory of $\mathcal A_T$ on modules annihilated by $\mathcal I$. For every $M\in\mathcal A_T$, some integer $N\geq1$ satisfies $\mathcal I^NM=0$. Here $\mathcal I^rM$ is the subsheaf locally generated by products of $r$ sections of $\mathcal I$ acting on sections of $M$, with $\mathcal I^0M=M$. The finite filtration
$$M\supseteq\mathcal IM\supseteq\cdots\supseteq\mathcal I^NM=0$$
has coherent quotients annihilated by $\mathcal I$. Closed direct image identifies $\mathrm{Coh}(T)$ exactly with the full abelian subcategory $\mathcal B_T\subseteq\mathcal A_T$. This inclusion is exact and closed under subobjects and quotients, although it need not be closed under extensions in $\mathcal A_T$. The empty schemes and the zero module are included.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** $U,T,i,\mathcal I$ and $M$ as stated, and the Axiom of Choice. Regular means that every point is regular in the local-ring sense; finite-dimensional means that lengths of strict chains of nonempty irreducible closed subsets are bounded. Neither extra restriction on $U$ is needed in the ordinary coherent-sheaf argument below. No regularity of $T$ is assumed.

**Goal:** Verify the exact hypotheses for abelian dévissage using an actual finite filtration.

[A1] The Axiom of Choice is [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice). It is inherited through the selected affine quotient, associated-sheaf and coherent finite-type interfaces in [F3]–[F4]. The exponent calculation and finite selections use no additional choice; no enhanced perfect-category or nonconnective model is used.

[F1] The unconditional coherent-sheaf clause of [thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-regular-cohomology-input](item-thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-regular-cohomology-input) gives the coherent abelian category on $U$, with kernels, images and cokernels computed as module sheaves. Abelian is used in the sense of [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category), and exact means preservation of short exact sequences computed in module sheaves. Exactness of stalks in [F5] then makes $\mathcal A_T$ an abelian full subcategory: kernels and cokernels of maps between supported objects remain supported, as do the zero object and finite direct sums; the coimage-to-image isomorphisms are inherited from $\mathrm{Coh}(U)$. Short exact sequences between such objects also have supported middle term whenever that term is coherent.

[F2] [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) defines a closed immersion by a homeomorphism onto a closed subset and a surjection of structure sheaves. [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) supplies a finite affine cover by spectra of Noetherian rings; [math:def-noetherian-ring-and-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-ring-and-module) says that every ideal of a Noetherian ring is finitely generated. Regular points are defined in [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point). Coherent modules are those of [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme), and [math:def-support-module-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-support-module-sheaf) defines support by the nonzero stalks. These conventions specify the categories in the Statement. Direct image is explicitly $(i_*F)(V)=F(i^{-1}V)$, with the $\mathcal O_U$-action induced by the structure-sheaf map.

[F3] The spectrum and basic opens are those of [math:def-affine-scheme-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme-spectrum): its points are primes and $D(f)=\{\mathfrak p:f\notin\mathfrak p\}$; put $V(J)=\{\mathfrak p:J\subseteq\mathfrak p\}$. [math:thm-affine-closed-immersions-quotient-rings](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-closed-immersions-quotient-rings) identifies the restriction of $i$ over $\operatorname{Spec}A$ with $\operatorname{Spec}(A/J)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}A$, for its defining ideal $J$, with underlying closed set $V(J)$. By [math:thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sections-basic-open-affine-scheme), on $D(f)$ the direct image of the quotient structure sheaf has sections $(A/J)_{\bar f}$, so its kernel ideal sheaf is $\widetilde J$. [math:thm-affine-quasi-coherent-equivalence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-quasi-coherent-equivalence) identifies modules and quasi-coherent sheaves, including their morphisms; quasi-coherence means the local associated-module condition of [math:def-quasi-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-coherent-module-scheme). Under this equivalence [math:lem-associated-sheaf-stalk-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-associated-sheaf-stalk-localization) identifies stalks with $P_{\mathfrak p}$ and [math:lem-associated-sheaf-sections-basic-open](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-associated-sheaf-sections-basic-open) identifies sections on $D(f)$ with $P_f$, compatibly with restrictions. By [math:def-localisation-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-localisation-of-a-module), $p/1=0$ in $P_f$ precisely when some nonnegative power of $f$ annihilates $p$.

[F4] [math:thm-coherent-sheaves-abelian-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-coherent-sheaves-abelian-noetherian-scheme) gives coherent if and only if quasi-coherent of finite type on a locally Noetherian scheme, and closure under kernels, images, cokernels and finite direct sums. Use the equivalent local finite-section form of finite type in [math:def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf). The affine finite-type test imported in External input identifies this condition for $\widetilde P$ with finite generation of $P$ over the affine coordinate ring. Combining that test with the coherent-if-and-only-if-finite-type assertion above and the affine equivalence in [F3], on each Noetherian affine chart coherent sheaves correspond exactly to finite modules. This deduction uses the declared statements and the explicit imported test. [math:thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations) ensures that $A/J$ is again Noetherian, including when it is the zero ring.

[F5] [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise) tests exactness on stalks; a module sheaf with all stalks zero is zero (a section with zero germs vanishes on a neighbourhood cover and hence vanishes). [math:thm-gluing-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-gluing-sheaves) glues compatible local module sheaves. Their morphisms glue uniquely by the sheaf axiom, by gluing their values on local sections. For a closed inclusion, neighbourhoods induced from the ambient space are cofinal among neighbourhoods in the closed subspace, so $i_*F$ has stalk $F_x$ at $x\in T$ and zero stalk off $T$. For the affine example below, [math:thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization) identifies the local ring of $\operatorname{Spec}R$ at $\mathfrak p$ with $R_{\mathfrak p}$.

### External input
The affine finite-type test is imported from [The Stacks Project, Lemma 28.17.1, Tag 01PB](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01PB): for a commutative ring $A$ with identity and any $A$-module $P$, the associated quasi-coherent sheaf $\widetilde P$ on $\operatorname{Spec}A$ has finite type if and only if $P$ is finitely generated over $A$. No Noetherian hypothesis is needed for this test. Finite type is used in the local finite-section sense of [F4]. The statement and its published argument are literature-derived external input; the filtration and closed-immersion application in the Proof are locally authored deductions.

For this repair the complete statement and proof of Tag 01PB were read. Its proof uses a finite distinguished-open cover and Algebra Lemma 10.23.2; those underlying results are imported, without a claimed reconstruction here. Apply the test in [F4] to the affine modules representing coherent restrictions of $M$ and to modules over $A/J$ in step 2.1. On the Noetherian rings $A$ and $A/J$, [F4]'s coherent finite-type equivalence applies. If a ring is zero, its only unital module is zero and its spectrum is empty, so the same finite-type equivalence holds directly.

### Proof
1.1 If $U=\varnothing$, then $M=0$ and take $N=1$. Otherwise choose a finite affine cover of $U$ by spectra of Noetherian rings. On a member $\operatorname{Spec}A$, write $M=\widetilde P$ with $P$ finite and $\mathcal I=\widetilde J$ for a finite ideal $J=(f_1,\ldots,f_r)$, using the affine equivalence and its compatibility with kernels. For every $f_j$, the sheaf $M$ has zero stalks on $D(f_j)$ because its support is in $V(J)$; hence it is zero there, and $P_{f_j}=0$. For a finite generating list $p_1,\ldots,p_s$ of $P$, the fraction criterion gives powers of $f_j$ killing each $p_a$. Their maximum, enlarged to at least $1$, gives $n_j\geq1$ with $f_j^{n_j}P=0$; if $P=0$, take $n_j=1$. Every monomial of degree $N_A=1+\sum_j(n_j-1)$ in the $f_j$ contains one such power, so $J^{N_A}P=0$. If $J=0$, take $N_A=1$. The maximum of $1$ and the finitely many $N_A$ gives a global $N$: higher ideal powers lie in lower ones, and a sheaf is zero if its restrictions on a cover are zero. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, given, choose, algebra]

2.1 For each $q\geq0$, on each chosen affine the finitely many monomials of degree $q$ in generators of $J$ generate $J^q$, so $\mathcal I^qM$ is locally the image of a map from a finite direct sum of copies of $M$ to $M$. It and $\mathcal I^qM/\mathcal I^{q+1}M$ are therefore coherent, supported on $|T|$, and the quotient is annihilated by $\mathcal I$. Step 1.1 gives the finite filtration. For the category assertion, the stalk calculation in [F5] proves that $i_*$ is exact. On an affine quotient $\operatorname{Spec}(A/J)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}A$, an $A$-module $P$ killed by $J$ has the unique action $(a+J)p=ap$, and maps between such modules are $A$-linear exactly when they are $A/J$-linear. A finite generating list over either ring generates over the other. By [F3]–[F4], this identifies the coherent sheaves on the quotient with the coherent sheaves on the affine target annihilated by $J$: direct image has sections $P_{\bar f}=P_f$ on $D(f)$, where $\bar f$ is the image in $A/J$. These local identifications restrict compatibly; on overlaps refine by affine target opens, where the quotient action and every factored morphism are unique. They consequently satisfy the cocycle identities and glue by [F5]. Thus $i_*$ is fully faithful with image exactly $\mathcal B_T$. Kernels, cokernels and finite sums of modules killed by $\mathcal I$ are killed by $\mathcal I$, proving abelianity and exact inclusion; coherent subobjects and quotients have the same property. To verify that extension closure can fail within the stated geometric scope, take the ring $R$ of rational functions $a(t)/b(t)$ with $a,b\in\mathbb Q[t]$ and $b(0)\ne0$. Every nonzero element is $t^n$ times a unit, with $n\geq0$. The least such order in any nonzero ideal generates it, so $R$ is Noetherian, with only primes $(0)$ and $(t)$. By the affine structure-stalk identification in [F5], its local rings are its fraction field and $R$. Evaluation at $t=0$ identifies $R/(t)$ with $\mathbb Q$, and multiplication by $t$ identifies $R/(t)$ with $(t)/(t^2)$ by cancellation. Thus $R$ has dimension one and cotangent dimension one, while its fraction field has both dimensions zero. Hence $U=\operatorname{Spec}R$ is regular Noetherian of dimension one. For $T=\operatorname{Spec}(R/(t))$, the exact sequence $0\to(t)/(t^2)\to R/(t^2)\to R/(t)\to0$ gives coherent sheaves supported on $T$. Both end terms are killed by $(t)$, whereas the middle term is not, since $t\notin(t^2)$. This proves the asserted possible failure of extension closure. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1, construct, algebra] ∎


## Finite-clopen motivic restriction over every field

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-e61a86f4a27f-all-field-clopen-restriction) · level 8


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Fix compatible Grothendieck universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$ and the site-size bounds of the supplied motivic and derived constructions; their existence is a separate assumption. Let $k\in U$ be any field and $X$ a qcqs $k$-scheme within these bounds. Let $X=\coprod_{\alpha\in A}X_\alpha$ be a finite clopen partition: the $X_\alpha$ are pairwise disjoint open-and-closed subschemes with restricted structure sheaves covering $X$. Empty pieces and the empty partition of the empty scheme are allowed. Every piece is required to be qcqs.
For every integer $j\geq0$, write $M_j$ for the supplied Elmanto–Morrow derived motivic sheaf on qcqs $k$-schemes, restricted from the prime subfield of $k$. Restriction induces an equivalence of complexes and, for every integer $a$, a group isomorphism
$$M_j(X)\simeq\prod_{\alpha\in A}M_j(X_\alpha),\qquad H^a(M_j(X))\xrightarrow{\cong}\prod_{\alpha\in A}H^a(M_j(X_\alpha)).$$
Here $H^{a,j}_{\rm mot}(T,\mathbb Z)$ means $H^a(M_j(T))$; the maps in the second formula are the cohomological restrictions. The empty product is zero, so $H^{a,j}_{\rm mot}(\varnothing,\mathbb Z)=0$. There is no algebraic-closure, smoothness, finite-type, noetherian or dimension hypothesis.

### Statement references
[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention)

[math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field)

[math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base)

[math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme)

[lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes)

[math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes)

[lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The field, qcqs scheme, finite partition, weight and Choice and size assumptions in the Statement.

[A1] Assume [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention) with the separate existence and size assumptions stated above. These are inherited from the unbounded derived construction. The finite partition calculation adds no arbitrary choice.

[F1] [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes) supplies $M_j$ on the category $\mathcal C_k$ of all size-bounded qcqs $k$-schemes and all $k$-morphisms, for every field $k$ and $j\geq0$. The enhanced target $\mathcal D_\infty(\mathbb Z)$ is stable and presentable, has a terminal zero object and small coherent limits, and has ordinary derived category $D(\mathbb Z)$ as its homotopy category. Limits represent coherent cones, naturally in the vertex, using the supplied mapping spaces. For a covering sieve $R$ on $X$, regarded as the category of its arrows $T\to X$ and commuting triangles, restriction gives $M_j(X)\simeq\lim_{R^{\mathrm{op}}}M_j(T)$. In particular $M_j(\varnothing)\simeq0$. This is the covering-sieve interface actually supplied.

[F2] Reference 0 supplies the left cofinality criterion: for a functor $e:I\to K$ of ordinary categories, if every comma category $(e\downarrow r)$ has weakly contractible nerve, then $N(e)$ is left cofinal. Here $(e\downarrow r)$ has objects $(i,e(i)\to r)$ and arrows $i\to i'$ commuting with those maps to $r$. The nerve $N$ has strings of composable arrows as simplices, as in [F1]; ordinary nerves are infinity-categories since composition fills their inner horns. Reference 2 supplies weak contractibility for the nerve of any category with an initial or final object. Reference 1, with $U$ the identity functor, then preserves and detects coherent limit cones upon restriction along $e$. We use these only for categories small in $V$ and the enhanced target of [F1].

[F3] [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products) gives the projection-induced isomorphism $H^a(\prod C_\alpha)\cong\prod H^a(C_\alpha)$ for finite products in $D(\mathbb Z)$, including the empty family, with zero cohomology for the zero complex. Only its integral assertion is used.

[F4] The formulation types are [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field), [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base) and [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme). An open immersion identifies its source with an open subscheme carrying the restricted structure sheaf, as in [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes), and is a monomorphism by [math:lem-open-immersion-monomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-open-immersion-monomorphism). Thus a factorization through any fixed piece is unique. By [math:def-morphism-of-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-of-schemes), morphisms are maps of locally ringed spaces: there is no map from a nonempty scheme to the empty scheme, and exactly one map from the empty scheme to any scheme, since both its underlying map and its structure-sheaf map are unique.

[F5] [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) supplies the generated Nisnevich topology and proves that open covering families are Nisnevich, including the empty family on the empty scheme. Thus, for nonempty $X$, the sieve of arrows factoring through at least one piece of the partition is covering.

### External input
References 0 and 1 import the left cofinality criterion and its consequence for coherent limit cones; reference 2 imports the nerve-contractibility facts used to check that criterion. Their exact statements, hypotheses and current reading limits are recorded in sources.references. The motivic functor, enhanced target and covering-sieve descent are supplied by [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes), and integral cohomology of finite derived products by [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products). The finite-sieve calculation below is a local argument.

### Proof
**Goal:** Compute the restriction product directly from covering-sieve descent and then pass to cohomology.

1.1 Work under [A1]. If $X=\varnothing$, every piece is empty and [F1] gives zero for all values; any product of these terminal objects is terminal, proving the first formula, including the empty partition. Suppose $X\ne\varnothing$, and put $B=\{\alpha\in A:X_\alpha\ne\varnothing\}$. Let $R$ be the covering sieve of [F5]. Its arrow category contains the full subcategory $J$ on the inclusions $X_\alpha\to X$ for $\alpha\in B$ and one empty scheme over $X$. By disjointness and [F4], the only nonidentity arrows of $J$ are the unique maps $\varnothing\to X_\alpha$. Consider $e:J^{\mathrm{op}}\to R^{\mathrm{op}}$. For $r=(T\to X)$, objects of $(e\downarrow r)$ are factorizations $T\to Y\to X$ through objects $Y$ of $J$, with the opposite direction on commuting triangles. If $T$ is nonempty, membership in $R$ gives a factorization through a piece; disjointness makes its index unique and [F4] makes its map unique. The comma category then has just that object and its identity. If $T$ is empty, all its maps to objects of $J$ are unique, so the comma category is $J^{\mathrm{op}}$, with final object the chosen empty scheme. These comma categories have weakly contractible nerves by [F2]. Thus $e$ is left cofinal, and [F1]–[F2] identify $M_j(X)$ with the coherent limit of the star diagram $M_j(X_\alpha)\to M_j(\varnothing)$ on $J^{\mathrm{op}}$. Its central value is terminal. For any vertex $C$, its coherent cone space consists of maps $C\to M_j(X_\alpha)$, a map $C\to M_j(\varnothing)$, and the compatibilities along those arrows. The map to the central value and all these compatibilities have contractible choice spaces since $\operatorname{Map}(C,M_j(\varnothing))$ is contractible. There are no composable nonidentity arrows in this star; higher compatibilities are precisely the coherences into that terminal mapping space. Consequently this cone space is naturally $\prod_{\alpha\in B}\operatorname{Map}(C,M_j(X_\alpha))$. The coherent product universal property in [F1] identifies the star limit with $\prod_{\alpha\in B}M_j(X_\alpha)$. Reinserting the empty pieces adds terminal factors and gives the product over $A$. Its coordinate maps are the original restrictions throughout. [A1, F1, F2, F4, F5, given, construct]

2.1 Taking components of the finite product of mapping spaces in step 1.1 gives the product universal property in the ordinary homotopy category $D(\mathbb Z)$ of [F1]. Apply cohomology and the finite-product isomorphism of [F3]. Its coordinates are the cohomological restrictions, so this proves the second formula for every integer $a$. The empty product and empty-scheme value are zero by [F1] and [F3], with zero cohomology. The restriction equivalence commutes with maps respecting the indexed partitions, since its coordinates come from the original coherent functor. The argument imposes none of the additional geometric restrictions excluded by the Statement. [F1, F3, step 1.1] ∎


## Finite rank strata detect Elmanto-Morrow motivic cohomology

[Open item](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-motivic-rank-restriction) · level 9


### Statement
Assume AC and the article's field convention: $k$ is algebraically closed unless otherwise specified. Let $X$ be any qcqs $k$-scheme and $X=\coprod_{\alpha\in A}X_\alpha$ the finite common rank partition of finitely many finite locally free sheaves. For the actual Elmanto-Morrow motivic complex of every weight $j\geq0$, restriction gives
$$\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(X)\simeq\prod_\alpha\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(X_\alpha).$$
Thus for every $i\in\mathbb Z$ and $R=\mathbb Z$ or $\mathbb Z/n$ with $n>0$, the joint restriction map
$$H^{i,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,R)\xrightarrow{\cong}\prod_\alpha H^{i,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X_\alpha,R)$$
is an isomorphism and in particular injective. No smoothness, finite-type, noetherian, dimension or regularity hypothesis is required. Weights $j<0$ are not defined here unless an additional zero-weight convention has explicitly been adopted.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, the field, scheme, finite bundle family and integer/coefficient bounds in the Statement, with the standing size assumptions [A1].

**Goal:** Prove the exact motivic detection property required by a varying-rank flag tower.

[A1] Use the compatible universes $U\in V\in W$, with $\omega\in U$, of [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes): scheme data are in $U$, the scheme category and its sieve categories are $V$-small, and coefficient limits are taken in the supplied enhancement in $W$. Universe closure has the meaning of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention). Existence of these universes is a standing assumption separate from AC. AC is retained from the motivic construction; the finite coefficient calculation needs only finite choices. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F0] The field, qcqs $k$-scheme category, structure maps and finite locally free sheaves are those of [def-article-conventions](item-def-article-conventions). The present statement explicitly allows arbitrary qcqs schemes, including affine ones without a finite-type assumption.

[F1] The nonempty common rank strata form a finite clopen partition by qcqs open subschemes, with their restricted structure sheaves. If $X$ is empty the partition is empty. [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata)

[F2] For $j\geq0$, [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes) supplies the actual motivic functor $P=\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}$ on qcqs $k$-schemes, already restricted from the prime field. Its enhanced target has small coherent limits and a zero object, which is terminal. For each Nisnevich covering sieve $R$ on $X$, restriction is the limit cone $P(X)\simeq\lim_{R^{\mathrm{op}}}P(T)$. In particular $P(\varnothing)\simeq0$. The supplier gives the mapping-space characterization of these limits and identifies the homotopy category with $D(\mathbb Z)$.

[F3] A sieve is closed under precomposition; the sieve generated by a family consists of arrows factoring through a member. [def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification](item-def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification) Open covering families, including the empty family on the empty scheme, are Nisnevich by [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface). An open immersion is a monomorphism, so factorization through a fixed rank-stratum inclusion is unique. [math:lem-open-immersion-monomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-open-immersion-monomorphism)

[F4] Integral motivic groups apply $H^i$ to $P$; finite coefficients apply the fixed functor $-\otimes^{\mathbf L}\mathbb Z/n$ first, for $n>0$. Both operations commute with finite derived products, including the empty product, and the comparison components are induced by projections. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology), [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products)

[F5] References 0 and 1 give the left-cofinality criterion and restriction of limit cones, respectively. For an ordinary functor $e:I\to J$, the test category $(e\downarrow r)$ has objects $(d,e(d)\to r)$ and morphisms $h:d\to d^{\prime}$ satisfying the commuting-triangle equation. These comma categories are those of [math:def-comma-slice-and-coslice-categories](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-comma-slice-and-coslice-categories). Their nerves identify degreewise with $N(I)\times_{N(J)}N(J)_{/r}$, since both describe strings of these commuting triangles. Reference 3 makes the ordinary nerves infinity-categories. Thus weak contractibility of every test nerve allows restriction of a coherent limit cone along $N(e)$.

[F6] Reference 2 supplies weak contractibility of the nerve of any small ordinary category with a terminal object; this also applies to the one-object identity category. All uses of cofinality below concern coherent limits, rather than limits in the ordinary derived category.

### External input
References 0 and 1 import the coherent cofinality and limit-cone results; reference 2 imports nerve contractibility, and reference 3 the ordinary-nerve interface. Their exact statements, hypotheses and reading limits are recorded in sources.references. The finite covering-sieve computation below is locally authored. The actual motivic sheaf and its restriction to $k$-schemes are used through [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes), without reconstructing the Elmanto–Morrow construction. No general sieve-to-Čech theorem is assumed.

### Proof

1.1 Put $P=\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}$ on the supplied category of qcqs $k$-schemes. All following categories and limits satisfy [A1]. If $X=\varnothing$, [F1] and [F2] already give the empty product assertion. Otherwise let $R$ be the sieve generated by the inclusions $i_\alpha:X_\alpha\hookrightarrow X$. It covers by [F1] and [F3]. Its objects are maps $r:T\to X$ factoring through one such inclusion, and its arrows are triangles over $X$. Let $D$ be its full subcategory on these inclusions and the unique map $\varnothing\to X$, and let $e:D^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow R^{\mathrm{op}}$ be the inclusion. These are categories of $k$-morphisms, so the motivic functor of [F2] is defined on every arrow. By disjointness and [F3], $D^{\mathrm{op}}$ has precisely the nonidentity arrows $i_\alpha\to(\varnothing\to X)$. For an object $r:T\to X$ of $R$, the test category $(e\downarrow r)$ in $R^{\mathrm{op}}$ records factorizations $T\to X_\alpha\to X$ or $T\to\varnothing\to X$. If $T$ is nonempty, the generating-sieve condition gives a factorization; its index is unique because the pieces are disjoint, and the factorization is unique by monomorphy. No map from nonempty $T$ to $\varnothing$ exists. The test category is therefore a one-object identity category. If $T$ is empty, its maps to all pieces and to $\varnothing$ are unique, and the latter factorization is terminal in the test category: the arrows $i_\alpha\to(\varnothing\to X)$ give its unique terminal arrows. Thus every test nerve is weakly contractible by [F6]. Applying [F5], $N(e)$ is left cofinal and the limit cone of [F2] restricts to a limit cone on $D^{\mathrm{op}}$. [A1, F0, F1, F2, F3, F5, F6, given, construct]

2.1 For nonempty $X$, the restricted diagram is the finite star $P(X_\alpha)\to P(\varnothing)$, with $P(\varnothing)$ terminal by [F2]. Its limit is $Q=\prod_\alpha P(X_\alpha)$: for every test object $L$, a coherent cone consists of maps $L\to P(X_\alpha)$, a map $L\to P(\varnothing)$, and the compatibility homotopies to that terminal value. The space of the latter choices is contractible, since $\operatorname{Map}(L,P(\varnothing))$ is contractible; the star has no composable nonidentity arrows imposing further conditions. Thus its cone space is naturally $\prod_\alpha\operatorname{Map}(L,P(X_\alpha))$, represented by $Q$ by the product mapping-space property in [F2]. The restricted cone of step 1.1 is consequently this product cone, whose maps are precisely $P(i_\alpha)$. This proves the displayed equivalence of actual complexes, including the empty case already treated. It also gives the finite product in the ordinary derived homotopy category: taking components of the finite product of mapping spaces gives the product of the sets of components. Apply [F4] to obtain the stated integral and finite-coefficient group isomorphisms, in every integer degree, with components the restrictions. Every isomorphism is injective. Only the supplied qcqs sheaf and finite partition were used, with no smoothness, finite-type, noetherian, dimension or regularity assumption. The definition in [F4] covers only $j\geq0$, so no negative-weight claim is made without the stated additional convention. [F1, F2, F4, step 1.1, construct] ∎


## Bottom supported Bloch groups on a codimension two curve

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom) · level 9


### Statement
Assume AC. Let $U$ be a smooth finite-type scheme of pure dimension three over a field $k$, and let $C\subset U$ be an integral closed curve of codimension two. In the supported Bloch complexes, $z_C^j(U,n)=0$ for $j=0,1$ and every $n\geq0$. Moreover
$$H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)=\mathbb Z[C],$$
with $[C]$ the positive generator represented by the integral reduced curve. Restriction to any open $V\subset U$ containing its generic point $\eta$ sends this generator to $[C\cap V]$ and is an isomorphism of these degree-zero groups. No smoothness or normality of $C$ is assumed.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, $U,C,k$, and $\eta$ as above. All products and open subschemes carry the $k$-scheme structures used in the supplied Bloch definition. A curve has dimension one, and pure dimension three means that every irreducible component has dimension three. The generic point $\eta$ has closure $|C|$. The notation $\mathbb Z[C]$ means the group of integral multiples of the single formal cycle $[C]$, not its image in an ambient Chow group.

**Goal:** Compute the two lower supported complexes and the bottom weight-two supported group, with its sign and its restriction to every generic neighbourhood.

[A1] AC is assumed for the supplied Bloch construction, smooth affine reduction, affine quasi-compactness, affine closed-immersion description and affine dimension formula used below. No smoothness, normality, or geometric integrality of $C$ is assumed. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F1] The unnormalized Bloch definition supplies $\Delta^n$, admissibility on every coordinate face, the quotient-length face coefficients, the alternating differential, zero terms in negative degrees, and $H_0=\ker\partial_0/\operatorname{im}\partial_1$. The supported complex is its free subgroup on admissible integral cycles with support in $C\times\Delta^n$. These are the supplied conventions for scheme products, scheme-theoretic intersections, stalk codimension and module length. [def-bloch-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-bloch-higher-cycle-complex) [def-supported-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-supported-higher-cycle-complex)

[F2] The ground field is a commutative field; a $k$-scheme is a scheme with a structure morphism to $\operatorname{Spec}k$. Smoothness and finite type refer to this morphism; finite type includes quasi-compactness. An open $V\subset U$ carries its restricted structure sheaf and open immersion into $U$. Integral means nonempty, reduced and irreducible, equivalently having domain rings on all nonempty affine opens. Closed subschemes are closed immersions. The generic point has dense closure, and dimension is the supremum of lengths of strict chains of nonempty irreducible closed subsets. [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes) [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism) [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme) [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) [math:def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset) [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space) [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field) [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base) [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes)

[F3] The smooth finite-type geometric reduction supplies Noetherianity, disjoint integral open-and-closed components, and an integral smooth affine cover. For integral finite-type schemes over a field, nonempty opens have the same dimension; the finite-type cycle geometry interface supplies this assertion without a separatedness hypothesis. Only these clauses of the two geometric suppliers are used. An affine chart is an open isomorphic to $\operatorname{Spec}R$ with its structure sheaf; affine schemes are quasi-compact under AC. A finite cover by affine charts of finite-type $k$-algebras therefore gives a finite-type $k$-scheme: the local condition is witnessed on those charts, and a finite union of quasi-compact opens is quasi-compact. [thm-author-89250b1d710c-smooth-local-geometry-input](item-thm-author-89250b1d710c-smooth-local-geometry-input) [thm-adjudicate-16c74c1a77ef-finite-type-cycle-geometry-input](item-thm-adjudicate-16c74c1a77ef-finite-type-cycle-geometry-input) [math:cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact)

[F4] The simplex and its faces have affine product charts obtained by eliminating one barycentric coordinate: over $\operatorname{Spec}A$ the ring is $A[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$. Over a domain this is a domain. For a domain $R$, $\operatorname{Frac}(R)$ is its localization at the nonzero elements. Algebraic independence means that no nonzero polynomial over the base field evaluates to zero; transcendence degree counts a transcendence basis. The fraction field of $A[x_1,\ldots,x_n]$ is $\operatorname{Frac}(A)(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$: both fields consist of fractions of polynomials after clearing the finitely many coefficient denominators. The variables are algebraically independent over $\operatorname{Frac}(A)$ by coefficientwise equality of polynomials, and generate that rational-function field. Thus finite transcendence degrees increase by $n$, using tower additivity. [lem-author-89250b1d710c-face-coordinate-geometry](item-lem-author-89250b1d710c-face-coordinate-geometry) [math:cor-multivariate-polynomial-ring-over-a-domain-is-a-domain](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-multivariate-polynomial-ring-over-a-domain-is-a-domain) [math:cor-transcendence-degree-tower-additivity](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-transcendence-degree-tower-additivity) [math:def-field-of-fractions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field-of-fractions) [math:def-algebraically-independent-finite-tuples-over-a-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-algebraically-independent-finite-tuples-over-a-field)

[F5] For a finite-type $k$-domain $R$, $\dim R=\operatorname{trdeg}_k\operatorname{Frac}(R)$. For each prime $\mathfrak p$, the affine dimension formula gives $\operatorname{ht}\mathfrak p=\dim R-\dim(R/\mathfrak p)$. The prime-localization bijection identifies this height with $\dim R_{\mathfrak p}$; the affine stalk is canonically $R_{\mathfrak p}$, so this is the stalk codimension in [F1]. A closed subscheme of $\operatorname{Spec}R$ is a quotient spectrum; its coordinate ring is finite type since the images of finitely many algebra generators still generate it. [math:thm-affine-domain-dimension-transcendence-degree](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-domain-dimension-transcendence-degree) [math:thm-dimension-formula-for-affine-domains](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-dimension-formula-for-affine-domains) [math:cor-primes-of-a-prime-local-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-primes-of-a-prime-local-ring) [math:thm-affine-closed-immersions-quotient-rings](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-closed-immersions-quotient-rings) [math:thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization)

[F6] Restriction to any open in a smooth finite-type scheme defines a chain map on Bloch complexes, sends a nonempty integral generator to its open restriction, and preserves its face coefficients. The supplier also establishes that the open is smooth and finite type. Since a supported generator restricts into $(C\cap V)\times\Delta^n$, this map restricts to the supported subcomplexes. [lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction](item-lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction)

[F7] A closed subset of a scheme has a unique reduced induced closed subscheme. Consequently two integral closed subschemes with the same underlying closed subset are equal. [thm-adjudicate-d1c21a67502d-reduced-closed-subscheme-input](item-thm-adjudicate-d1c21a67502d-reduced-closed-subscheme-input)

### Proof

1.1 By [F3], $U$ has disjoint integral components of dimension three and a cover by integral smooth affine opens. Any nonempty such open in a component has dimension three by [F3]. Since $U$ is quasi-compact, take a finite such cover; the closed intersections with $C$ are quotient spectra by [F5], so $C$ is finite type. The nonempty charts in this cover of $C$ have dimension one by [F3]. For an integral finite-type affine chart $\operatorname{Spec}A$ of dimension $d$, [F4] and [F5] give $\dim A[x_1,\ldots,x_n]=d+n$. These formulas apply to the ambient component charts and to the charts of $C\times\Delta^n$. Each product of an integral component, or of $C$, with the simplex is integral: its affine charts are domains by [F4], and any two nonempty such charts meet, since their base opens in the integral scheme meet and the simplex has a $k$-point. The finite polynomial-chart covers also show that these products are finite type, by [F3]; the same quotient-chart argument applies to their closed subschemes. Thus each ambient component product has dimension $3+n$ and the support product has dimension $1+n$, using [F3] to pass between their nonempty opens and the whole integral schemes. At the generic point of any integral closed cycle $W$ in the support, choose an ambient affine chart. There its defining prime $\mathfrak p$ has quotient of dimension at most $1+n$, because its spectrum is a closed subspace of the support chart. By [F5] its stalk codimension is at least $(3+n)-(1+n)=2$. Hence there are no generators in codimensions zero or one, proving $z_C^j(U,n)=0$ for $j=0,1$ in all $n\geq0$. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, given]

2.1 The same affine dimension calculation shows that a codimension-two integral cycle supported in $C$ has dimension one, and in $C\times\Delta^1$ it has dimension two. A proper irreducible closed subset of an integral finite-dimensional scheme has smaller dimension: append the whole space to any chain in that subset. Thus their supports must be all of $C$ and all of $C\times\Delta^1$, respectively; [F7] identifies their reduced scheme structures. The curve is admissible in degree zero, so $z_C^2(U,0)=\mathbb Z[C]$. The product $C\times\Delta^1=C\times\mathbb A^1$ is admissible: its only proper nonempty coordinate faces are the endpoints, and each intersects it in $C$, of codimension two in $U$. Write $t$ for the affine coordinate. On any nonempty affine chart $\operatorname{Spec}B$ of $C$, the local ring of this product at the generic point of the endpoint $t=a$, $a=0,1$, is $B[t]_{(t-a)}=\operatorname{Frac}(B)[t]_{(t-a)}$. Its quotient by $t-a$ is the residue field $\operatorname{Frac}(B)$. As a module over the local ring this is simple: any nonzero element generates it by multiplication by scalars from the residue field. Thus the quotient-length coefficient of each face is one. The alternating boundary is therefore $[C]-[C]=0$. As this product is the only degree-one generator, $\operatorname{im}\partial_1=0$; [F1] gives $H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)=\mathbb Z[C]$ with the stated positive generator. For any $V$ containing $\eta$, [F6] gives the supported chain map and a smooth finite-type target. The open $C\cap V$ is nonempty and integral, and the ambient components meeting it still have dimension three by [F3]. The calculation just made applies to this pair as well. Restriction sends the single degree-zero generator to $[C\cap V]$ with coefficient one, so it induces the asserted isomorphism and preserves its positive sign. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, step 1.1] ∎


## Coherent descent for shifted derived abelian sections

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-28f1411ede08-derived-one-space-descent) · level 9



### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible Grothendieck universe bounds, including enlarged bounds for spaces and sheaf categories; universe existence is a separate assumption. Let $\mathcal C$ be a small site presented by covering families, with the iterated fibre products of their members in $\mathcal C$. Let $A$ be an abelian sheaf on this site, and fix one nonnegative injective resolution $0\to A\to I^0\xrightarrow{d^0}I^1\xrightarrow{d^1}I^2\to\cdots$ in abelian sheaves on $\mathcal C$.

Use cochain shifts $C[1]^q=C^{q+1}$ with differential $-d_C^{q+1}$, and the specified Eilenberg–Mac Lane realization $HC$, with $\pi_n(HC)=H^{-n}(C)$. Set $D_I(X)=\Omega^\infty H(I^\bullet(X)[1])$, where $\Omega^\infty$ is the zeroth space. Restrictions of this single complex of sheaves give a simultaneous pointed space-valued presheaf. It is a sheaf of spaces and computes $\Omega^\infty H(R\Gamma(X,A)[1])$ using derived evaluation on this same site. Its loop sheaf is canonically the discrete abelian sheaf $A$.

Here a sheaf of spaces means homotopy descent: for every specified cover $\{U_i\to X\}$, its value at $X$ is equivalent to the homotopy limit of the cosimplicial diagram whose degree $r$ is $\prod_{i_0,\ldots,i_r}D_I(U_{i_0}\times_X\cdots\times_X U_{i_r})$, with maps induced by the projections and diagonals. Limits are coherent limits of spaces. The construction and comparison are pointed and additive, natural in $X$ and in maps of the chosen sheaf resolutions. The loop identification uses $A\hookrightarrow I^0$ without negating $A$.

### Statement references
[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[def-author-cf6ede58b450-site-plus-data](item-def-author-cf6ede58b450-site-plus-data)

[lem-author-a44f2061570d-site-derived-input](item-lem-author-a44f2061570d-site-derived-input)

[math:thm-dold-kan-equivalence-for-simplicial-modules](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-dold-kan-equivalence-for-simplicial-modules)

[thm-author-80e2d7bee57f-eilenberg-maclane-input](item-thm-author-80e2d7bee57f-eilenberg-maclane-input)

[lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index)

[def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The fixed site, compatible universe bounds, abelian sheaf $A$ and single resolution $I^\bullet$ in the Statement.

**Goal:** Supply coherent restriction maps and descent for the space-valued realization, including its loop normalization.

[A1] Choice is assumed as in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) for the resolution and homotopical inputs. Keep the fixed compatible bounds throughout; no universe existence follows from Choice.

[F1] [def-author-cf6ede58b450-site-plus-data](item-def-author-cf6ede58b450-site-plus-data) supplies small sites, sheaves, restriction functors and the relative universe bounds. A covering-family presentation means that isomorphisms cover, composites of covering families cover, and pullbacks of covering families exist and cover; take the topology generated by these families.

[F2] [lem-author-a44f2061570d-site-derived-input](item-lem-author-a44f2061570d-site-derived-input) supplies the abelian category of sheaves, existence of the stated resolution, and the fact that $I^\bullet(X)$ computes right-derived evaluation. The injective resolution is an exact coaugmented sheaf complex; its kernels are computed sectionwise.

[F3] [thm-author-80e2d7bee57f-eilenberg-maclane-input](item-thm-author-80e2d7bee57f-eilenberg-maclane-input) fixes the underlying Omega spectrum used for $H$. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) fixes cochain-to-chain reindexing, the shift differential and the natural homotopy-group formula. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) supplies the coherent spectrum associated to this Omega tower, its zeroth space and coherent limits of spaces.

[F4] [math:thm-dold-kan-equivalence-for-simplicial-modules](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-dold-kan-equivalence-for-simplicial-modules) supplies normalization and its inverse on nonnegative chain complexes, here for the constant ring $\mathbb Z$. Write $\operatorname{DK}$ for that inverse. Apply it sectionwise to a presheaf of complexes; functoriality gives all restrictions and their compositions.

[F5] Source reference 0 supplies covering Čech descent for $E_I=\operatorname{DK}(T_I)$, where $T_I$ is the chain complex of sheaves with $T_{I,1}=I^0$, $T_{I,0}=Z^1I=\ker(d^1:I^1\to I^2)$, boundary $d^0$, and zero other terms. In the exact sheaf resolution, $Z^1I$ is the sheaf image of $d^0$. Its sections need not be the image of $d^0(X)$.

[F6] Source reference 1 supplies the natural zeroth-space model $\Omega^\infty HB\simeq W(B)\cong\operatorname{DK}(\tau_{\geq0}B)$ for a chain complex $B$. Here $HB$ denotes the chain-indexed version of [F3]. The smart truncation $\tau_{\geq0}B$ has $B_n$ for $n>0$, $\ker(B_0\to B_{-1})$ for $n=0$, zero for $n<0$, and the induced differential.

### External input
Reference 0 imports the descent fact [F5], using the $n=1$ case of its injective-resolution construction. Reference 1 imports the zeroth-space construction [F6] for the same underlying Omega-spectrum realization as [F3]. Their proofs and foundations remain imported within the recorded reading limits. The signed comparison and its application to the chosen derived evaluation below are local.

### Proof
1.1 By [F1]–[F2], the resolution and its sheaf kernel $Z^1I$ are defined on one fixed site. Form the two-term chain complex $T_I$ in [F5] and evaluate it at each $X$. Since $d^1d^0=0$, its boundary lands in $Z^1I(X)$; the next boundary is zero. All restrictions commute with this boundary. Apply [F4] to obtain the pointed additive simplicial presheaf $E_I(X)=\operatorname{DK}(T_I(X))$, with compatible restrictions. Its degree-zero term uses sections of the sheaf kernel, exactly as in [F5]. Under [A1], that imported fact gives the covering Čech descent in the Statement, so $E_I$ is a sheaf of spaces. The site is small in the fixed bounds, $A$ is already a sheaf, and $I^\bullet$ is its nonnegative injective sheaf resolution, which checks every hypothesis of this application. [A1, F1, F2, F4, F5, construct]

2.1 Reindex the shifted cochain complex $I^\bullet(X)[1]$ as the chain complex $B(X)_n=I^{1-n}(X)$ with boundary $-d^{1-n}$, using [F3]. Its smart truncation in [F6] has $I^0(X)$ in degree one and $Z^1I(X)$ in degree zero, with boundary $-d^0$. The map from $T_I(X)$ to this truncation which is the identity in degree one and minus the identity in degree zero is a chain isomorphism: both boundary composites equal $-d^0$. It commutes with every restriction and is additive. Apply [F4] and [F6] to get a natural pointed additive equivalence $E_I(X)\simeq D_I(X)$. Thus the coherent presheaf $D_I$ has the descent established in step 1.1, and [F2] identifies its evaluated complex with $R\Gamma(X,A)[1]$. By [F3], $\pi_nD_I(X)=H^{1-n}(I^\bullet(X))$ for $n\geq0$; these vanish for $n\geq2$ since $I^\bullet$ is nonnegative. Its loop space is therefore discrete with group $\ker d^0(X)=A(X)$, using exactness at $I^0$ and sectionwise kernels in [F2]. The chain comparison is the identity on that degree-one kernel, so the loop identification is the identity on $A$, as claimed. This also proves naturality in maps of the fixed resolutions, since kernels, truncation, the sign map and both realization functors commute with those maps. [A1, F2, F3, F4, F6, step 1.1] ∎


## Detection on a simultaneous flag tower

[Open item](item-lem-flag-tower-cohomology-detection) · level 10


### Statement
Assume AC. Let $H^{*,*}$ be an additively oriented bigraded theory with the projective bundle contract on a scheme category $\mathcal C$. For a finite family of vector bundles $E_1,\ldots,E_m$ of constant ranks on an object $X$, the simultaneous flag tower $q:Y\to X$ induces injective pullback $q^*:H^{a,b}(X)\to H^{a,b}(Y)$ for every $(a,b)\in\mathbb Z^2$. Each $[q^*E_i]$ in the Grothendieck group of vector bundles is the sum of the classes of its global line-bundle filtration quotients, with the empty sum for rank zero.

More generally let $X$ be qcqs and the ranks merely locally constant. Suppose the finitely many common rank strata $X_\alpha$, their inclusions, the component flag towers $q_\alpha:Y_\alpha\to X_\alpha$, their finite disjoint union $Y$, its component inclusions, and $q:Y\to X$ all belong to $\mathcal C$. If the joint restriction $H^{a,b}(X)\to\prod_\alpha H^{a,b}(X_\alpha)$ is injective for every bidegree, then $q^*$ is injective in every bidegree. This additional joint-restriction hypothesis is explicit: it is not part of the abstract projective bundle contract.

For the actual Elmanto-Morrow motivic theory of the article, on qcqs schemes over its field $k$ (algebraically closed unless otherwise specified), joint restriction to these strata is an isomorphism. Hence the componentwise flag tower detects $H^{i,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(-,R)$ for every $i\in\mathbb Z$, $j\geq0$, and $R=\mathbb Z$ or $\mathbb Z/n$, $n>0$. This uses the actual derived motivic complexes and requires no smoothness, noetherianity, finite-type or dimension assumption. If the source's explicit extension $\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}=0$ for $j<0$ is adopted, the same statement holds for all integer weights, with zero groups in negative weights; the current nonnegative-weight definition alone makes no separate negative-weight assertion.

For varying ranks the line-quotient class equality holds separately on every $Y_\alpha$. Globally it holds after extending those quotient bundles by zero along the clopen inclusions; these extensions are vector bundles of rank one on that component and zero elsewhere, rather than global line bundles. A finite sum of global line-bundle classes is asserted only in the constant-rank situation. Here $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}$ denotes the group on finite locally free sheaves modulo short-exact-sequence relations. None of these statements asserts injectivity of $q^*$ on that group.

Consequently any equality $u=v$ between classes of the same cohomological bidegree whose two sides pull back naturally can be checked on the appropriate detecting tower: $q^*u=q^*v$ implies $u=v$.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, the theory and category, and the bundles above; in the abstract varying-rank case also the explicit membership and joint-restriction assumptions. For the actual motivic application use the stated qcqs field, weight and coefficient hypotheses, retaining the compatible universe and site-size conventions of the supplied motivic and derived constructions in [F4]–[F6]. These standing size assumptions are distinct from AC.

**Goal:** Preserve constant-rank detection, prove conditional abstract varying-rank detection, supply its actual motivic application, and state precisely the class equalities used.

[F1] The constant-rank simultaneous tower is a finite composite of positive-constant-rank projective bundles, with locally split line-quotient filtrations; it is qcqs over a qcqs base. [lem-flag-tower-line-quotients](item-lem-flag-tower-line-quotients)

[F2] In the abstract contract, pullback along such a finite composite is injective in every bidegree; pullbacks are contravariantly functorial group homomorphisms. [lem-projective-pullback-detection](item-lem-projective-pullback-detection), [def-bigraded-oriented-theory](item-def-bigraded-oriented-theory)

[F3] The common rank partition and componentwise tower are finite and qcqs, with identity constructions at rank zero. [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-common-rank-strata)

[F4] The current rank-restriction supplier directly gives the finite-clopen restriction equivalence for the actual motivic complexes and the induced cohomological restriction isomorphisms, integrally and with finite coefficients, for the stated qcqs common rank partition and weights. Its proof uses covering-sieve descent on the prime-field functor. Apply that conclusion with its compatible size conventions; no additional Čech-descent assertion is needed here. The motivic-cohomology definition fixes the actual restricted prime-field functor and its pullbacks. [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-motivic-rank-restriction](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-motivic-rank-restriction), [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology)

[F5] The actual motivic projective-bundle supplier gives the derived equivalence and its index-zero pullback retraction for qcqs schemes over a prime field, positive constant bundle rank and nonnegative weight. Its native quotient-convention conclusion includes the canonical transport from line subbundles by dualizing the bundle and identifying the tautological lines. Its source negative-weight convention is zero. [thm-author-ab0a6fc202cb-elmanto-morrow-projective-input](item-thm-author-ab0a6fc202cb-elmanto-morrow-projective-input)

[F6] Integral cohomology and the fixed finite-coefficient construction are functors on the derived category; in particular, they preserve retraction identities. Finite products commute with cohomology and this finite-coefficient construction, modeled by the multiplication-by-$n$ cone for $n>0$. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology), [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-finite-derived-products)

[F7] For the finite bundle family on qcqs $X$, the clopen class supplier gives componentwise line-quotient sums and their global vector-bundle class formula by extension by zero along the finite clopen inclusions. Its qcqs hypothesis is retained in this application; the unrestricted constant-rank formula will instead be proved directly from [F1] and the defining group relations. [lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-clopen-line-quotient-classes](item-lem-author-ab0a6fc202cb-clopen-line-quotient-classes)

[F8] AC is inherited through the flag construction and is retained throughout. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### Proof

1.1 In the constant-rank abstract case, apply [F1] to the finite bundle family and [F2] to every stage. Their finite composite is injective in every integer bidegree; if there are no stages the map is the identity and remains injective. [F1, F2, F8, given]

1.2 For the constant-rank case, take the global locally split filtration $0=F_0\subset\cdots\subset F_{r_i}=q^*E_i$ from [F1], and write $L_{i,s}=F_s/F_{s-1}$. By the inline definition of $K^0_{\mathrm{vb}}$ in the Statement, the short exact sequence $0\to F_{s-1}\to F_s\to L_{i,s}\to0$ gives $[F_s]=[F_{s-1}]+[L_{i,s}]$. The zero sequence gives $[0]=[0]+[0]$, hence $[0]=0$. Adding the finitely many filtration relations therefore gives $[q^*E_i]=\sum_{s=1}^{r_i}[L_{i,s}]$, including the empty sum for $r_i=0$. This uses no qcqs assumption and no application of [F7]. It asserts neither globally split extensions nor cohomological detection from a K-group equality. [F1, given, algebra]

1.3 For the actual motivic theory, take one positive-constant-rank quotient-projective stage $\pi:\mathbb P(G)\to S$ of a component tower. The base $S$ is qcqs by [F1] and [F3]. Composing its structure morphism to $\operatorname{Spec}k$ with that to the prime subfield gives the underlying prime-field scheme used in the actual functor of [F4], without changing $S$ or $\pi$. Apply the native quotient-convention conclusion of [F5]: its supplied duality argument identifies this projective bundle with the source line-subbundle projectivization of $G^\vee$, preserving projection and the quotient $\mathcal O(1)$. The positive constant rank is unchanged. In the resulting equivalence, the index-zero summand is $\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(S)$ and its map is precisely $\pi^*$. The inverse equivalence followed by projection onto that summand is a retraction. Applying the cohomology functor of [F6] preserves this identity, so $\pi^*$ is injective in every integral degree $i$ for $j\geq0$. [F1, F3, F4, F5, F6, construct]

2.1 In the abstract varying-rank case, let $\iota_\alpha:X_\alpha\hookrightarrow X$ and $j_\alpha:Y_\alpha\hookrightarrow Y$ be the inclusions. The construction satisfies $qj_\alpha=\iota_\alpha q_\alpha$. All the bundles on $X_\alpha$ have constant ranks, so step 1.1 gives injectivity of each $q_\alpha^*$. [F3, given, step 1.1, step 1.2]

3.1 If $q^*x=0$, then functoriality gives $q_\alpha^*\iota_\alpha^*x=j_\alpha^*q^*x=0$. Step 2.1 yields $\iota_\alpha^*x=0$ for every $\alpha$. The stated joint-restriction hypothesis then implies $x=0$. This proves conditional varying-rank detection without adding an unproved axiom to the abstract contract. [F2, given, step 2.1]

4.1 Applying the finite-coefficient cone to the equivalence from step 1.3 and using [F6] gives the same zero-summand retraction and injectivity for $R=\mathbb Z/n$. Compose these injections through every finite component tower; identity stages at rank zero preserve the conclusion. Joint restriction to the common rank strata is injective by [F4]. The functorial argument of step 3.1 therefore proves the asserted actual motivic injectivity for both coefficient choices. The optional negative-weight convention gives zero source and target groups and so extends it trivially when that convention is explicitly used. [F3, F4, F5, F6, step 3.1, step 1.3]

5.1 Finally [F7] gives all stated varying-rank class equalities, with supported extensions in the global formula, and explains why they cannot in general be called sums of global line-bundle classes. If $q^*u=q^*v$ in any of the detecting cases, functorial additive pullback gives $q^*(u-v)=0$, and the relevant injection from step 1.1, step 3.1 or step 4.1 implies $u-v=0$. Naturality identifies pulled-back expressions with those formed from the pulled-back bundles, which proves the final identity-detection assertion and completes the proof. [F2, F7, step 1.1, step 3.1, step 4.1] ∎


## Support fibers with explicit cohomological indexing

[Open item](item-def-supported-complex-indexing) · level 11


### Definition
