Let $X$ be a scheme, $T\subseteq X$ a closed subset of its underlying topological space, and $U=X\setminus T$ its open complement. Fix an integer $j$ and a restriction map $f:C_j(X)\to C_j(U)$ of cochain complexes of abelian groups. A cochain complex $A$ consists of abelian groups $A^n$ and homomorphisms $d_A:A^n\to A^{n+1}$ with $d_A^2=0$; its cohomology is $H^n(A)=\ker(d_A:A^n\to A^{n+1})/\operatorname{im}(d_A:A^{n-1}\to A^n)$. A restriction map here means a cochain map: $d f=f d$. A quasi-isomorphism is a cochain map inducing an isomorphism in every cohomological degree.

Define the **support complex** $C_{j,T}(X)$ to be the homotopy fiber of $f$. Explicitly, for $A=C_j(X)$ and $B=C_j(U)$, one model has
$$C_{j,T}(X)^n=A^n\oplus B^{n-1},\qquad d(a,b)=(d_Aa,\,f(a)-d_Bb).$$
The projection $(a,b)\mapsto a$ is the support-to-global map. Define the **supported cohomology group** by
$$H_T^{a,j}(X)=H^a(C_{j,T}(X)).$$

For derived-category statements use $D(\mathbf{Ab})$, the localization of the homotopy category of unbounded cochain complexes of abelian groups at quasi-isomorphisms, with the standing size convention of a small category of complexes or supplied small cofinal denominator families. Here cochain maps are identified up to cochain homotopy, meaning $f-g=d h+h d$ for a degree-minus-one homomorphism $h$. Write $Q$ for localization. The cochain shift is $A[k]^n=A^{n+k}$ with differential $(-1)^k d_A$; the cone convention is $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^n=B^n\oplus A^{n+1}$ with differential $(b,a)\mapsto(d_Bb+f(a),-d_Aa)$. A fiber of a derived morphism $\alpha:A\to B$ means an object with a distinguished triangle $F\to A\xrightarrow{\alpha}B\to F[1]$ in this derived category. Choose a roof $A\xleftarrow{s}A'\xrightarrow{g}B$ representing $\alpha=Q(g)Q(s)^{-1}$, where $s$ is a quasi-isomorphism and $g$ is a cochain map. Use the displayed fiber model for $g$, with support-to-global map its projection to $A'$ followed by $Q(s)$. Its derived isomorphism class, together with the map to $A$, is independent of these choices up to an isomorphism commuting with that map. Such an isomorphism is not asserted unique or canonically chosen.

For an application to derived sections, assume the Axiom of Choice, the fixed site and universe bounds of the derived-sections interface, and supplied compatible restriction data. That interface can then provide the two complexes and their derived restriction morphism.

For spectral realization assume the Axiom of Choice and the size and pointed-space conventions of the Eilenberg–Mac Lane indexing interface. Write $HC_{j,T}(X)$ for its realization of a chosen support-complex model, and write $[2j]$ for suspension of a spectrum by $2j$. For every integer $i$ the indexing conventions are
$$\pi_i HC_{j,T}(X)\cong H_T^{-i,j}(X),\qquad \pi_i\bigl(HC_{j,T}(X)[2j]\bigr)\cong H_T^{2j-i,j}(X).$$
The two spectra in these formulas are distinguished: the second is shifted and the first is not. This auxiliary convention does not identify either spectrum with the article's support object without a separately specified comparison. The article's printed Definition 2.7 formula is retained separately in its article item.

### Definition references
[def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites)

[lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index)

[math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category)

[math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category)

[math:thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure)

[math:def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three)

[math:cor-triangulated-five-lemma](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-triangulated-five-lemma)

[math:cor-short-five-lemma-for-quasi-isomorphisms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-short-five-lemma-for-quasi-isomorphisms)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The scheme, closed subset, complexes and restriction data of the Definition. Derived-category assertions use the stated size convention. Derived sections and spectral realization use the indicated additional Choice, site and size hypotheses.

**Goal:** Justify the explicit fiber, strict-square comparison, independence of derived representatives with its projection, and both spectral indexing formulas.

[F1] Schemes, including their underlying topology, have the meaning of [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme). The complex, map, quasi-isomorphism, homotopy, shift and cone formulas used here are specified in the Definition.

[F2] Abelian groups and homomorphisms form an abelian category by [math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category). The roof localization and its standing size convention are those of [math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category).

[F3] [math:thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure) supplies the triangulation by localized cone triangles and the cohomology functors and their long exact sequences. Its exact shift and signed rotation conventions apply here.

[F4] [math:def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three) completes a commuting square of the first two arrows of distinguished triangles to a morphism of triangles. [math:cor-triangulated-five-lemma](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-triangulated-five-lemma) makes its remaining component an isomorphism when the other two are isomorphisms.

[F5] [math:cor-short-five-lemma-for-quasi-isomorphisms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-short-five-lemma-for-quasi-isomorphisms) applies to a morphism of short exact sequences of complexes with quasi-isomorphisms on the two outer terms.

[F6] [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) supplies derived evaluation under its stated Choice, site and universe hypotheses. Restriction data remain an explicit application premise.

[F7] [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) applies to any unbounded cochain complex of abelian groups under its ZFC, size and pointed-space conventions and gives the unshifted and spectrally shifted homotopy-group formulas.

[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is used when invoking the supplied derived-sections construction in [F6] or spectral realization in [F7]. The explicit differential and the individual roof and triangle comparisons add no choice hypothesis beyond their declared suppliers.

### Justification
1.1 The differential squares to zero since its second component after two applications is $f(d_Aa)-d_B(f(a)-d_Bb)=0$, and projection to $A$ is a cochain map. The signed permutation $(a,b)\mapsto(b,-a)$ identifies this model with $\operatorname{Cone}(f)[-1]$ so that the first map in the rotated cone triangle is the displayed projection. Thus [F2] and [F3] give its fiber triangle. For a strictly commuting square $v f=f'u$ of cochain maps, $(a,b)\mapsto(u(a),v(b))$ is a cochain map on the fiber models and commutes with their projections. It fits into a morphism of short exact sequences $0\to B[-1]\to F(f)\to A\to0$, where the first arrow is $b\mapsto(0,b)$. If $u,v$ are quasi-isomorphisms, so is $v[-1]$ since $H^n(B[-1])=H^{n-1}(B)$, and [F5] makes the fiber map a quasi-isomorphism. For an arbitrary derived restriction morphism, [F2] supplies a roof with actual cochain maps $s,g$; applying this same cone construction to $g$ and transporting its triangle along $Q(s):A'\to A$ gives the specified fiber triangle and support-to-global map. [F1, F2, F3, F5, given, construct, algebra]

2.1 For two such choices, rotate their fiber triangles to $A\xrightarrow{\alpha}B\to F[1]\to A[1]$ and $A\xrightarrow{\alpha}B\to F'[1]\to A[1]$. Apply [F4] with identities on $A,B$ to obtain an isomorphism $F[1]\to F'[1]$ of the third objects. Shifting back gives an isomorphism $F\to F'$ commuting with the projections to $A$, since the triangle morphism also commutes with the final maps to $A[1]$ and the same signed rotation is used in both triangles. The same argument with commuting isomorphisms on $A,B$ covers a change of the two derived objects themselves. It asserts existence of a compatible isomorphism, without asserting uniqueness or functoriality of cone choices. By [F3], applying cohomology gives isomorphic supported groups and compatible support-to-global homomorphisms. In the derived-sections application invoke [F6] under [A1] and its stated restriction and size data. Finally the fiber complex constructed in step 1.1 is an unbounded cochain complex of abelian groups, so [F7] under [A1] yields $\pi_i HC_{j,T}(X)=H^{-i}(C_{j,T}(X))$ up to its specified natural isomorphism and gives the second formula after spectral suspension by $2j$. These are precisely the two displayed indexing identifications; no comparison with the article's separately named object follows from them. [F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, A1, step 1.1] ∎


## Open excision for a descent theory

[Open item](item-lem-open-excision-for-support-fibers) · level 12


### Statement
For the spectrum-valued assertion, assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed universe and smallness conventions of the stable spectra interface. The explicit cochain-complex assertion uses only the supplied complexes and maps.

Let $X$ be a scheme, $T\subseteq X$ a closed subset, $W\subseteq X$ an open subset containing $T$, $U=X\setminus T$, and $V=W\cap U=W\setminus T$. Suppose a contravariant spectrum-valued theory $E$ is defined on these four opens, with coherent restriction maps, and that its restriction square
$$\begin{matrix} E(X)&\longrightarrow&E(W)\\ \downarrow&&\downarrow\\ E(U)&\longrightarrow&E(V) \end{matrix}$$
is homotopy cartesian: the induced map $E(X)\to E(W)\times_{E(V)}E(U)$ is an equivalence, where the product is a homotopy pullback. Define $E_T(X)=\operatorname{fib}(E(X)\to E(U))$ and $E_T(W)=\operatorname{fib}(E(W)\to E(V))$. Restriction induces an equivalence $E_T(X)\simeq E_T(W)$. It is natural for compatible maps of such coherent squares, and the square formed by the support-to-global maps and restriction to $W$ commutes coherently.

The same conclusion holds for cochain complexes of abelian groups, with equivalence replaced by quasi-isomorphism, under the following explicit form of two-open descent. Write $A=C(X)$, $B=C(W)$, $C=C(U)$, and $D=C(V)$, and suppose the restriction square is strictly commutative. If $u:B\to D$ and $v:C\to D$ are its lower restriction maps, require the canonical map
$$A\longrightarrow P:=\operatorname{fib}\bigl(B\oplus C\xrightarrow{u-v}D\bigr),\qquad a\longmapsto (a|_W,a|_U,0),$$
to be a quasi-isomorphism, using the explicit fiber complex specified in [F2]. Then restriction induces a quasi-isomorphism $C_T(X)\to C_T(W)$, compatible with the support-to-global maps and natural for maps of such squares. In particular it induces isomorphisms on supported cohomology in every degree. A quasi-isomorphism is a cochain map inducing isomorphisms on all cohomology groups.

These are conditional excision statements: a particular theory must supply the indicated restriction data and descent hypothesis, as well as any quasi-compactness, size, or other conditions required for that hypothesis. No descent property is asserted merely from the existence of a support fiber.

### Statement references

[math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme)

[def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

[def-supported-complex-indexing](item-def-supported-complex-indexing)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The opens and either a coherent spectrum square or a strictly commutative cochain-complex square as in the statement, satisfying the corresponding displayed descent hypothesis, with the stated spectral foundations when using spectra.

**Goal:** Prove the restriction equivalence or quasi-isomorphism of support fibers and its stated compatibility and naturality.

[F1] In the stable spectra interface, fibers and products over a common object are coherent homotopy pullbacks, characterized by their universal properties. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

[F2] For a cochain map $f:M\to N$, the support-fiber model has degree-$n$ group $M^n\oplus N^{n-1}$ and differential $(m,n)\mapsto(d_Mm,f(m)-d_Nn)$. A commutative square of cochain maps induces a map of these fibers, and if its two vertical maps are quasi-isomorphisms then so is the induced map on fibers. The support-to-global map is projection to the first factor. [def-supported-complex-indexing](item-def-supported-complex-indexing)

[F3] Schemes, including their underlying topological spaces, are as in [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme). The closed subset, open neighbourhood, complements and intersection in the Statement refer to that underlying topology; no scheme-theoretic structure on the closed subset is used.

[A1] The spectrum-valued assertion uses the assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and the fixed universe and smallness conventions of [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface). These are the foundations of the supplied coherent spectra and pullbacks in [F1]. The cochain-complex assertion uses only the explicit complex, cohomology and cochain-homotopy conventions in [F2], without invoking derived sections or spectral realization.

### Proof

**Proof technique:** Paste homotopy pullbacks for spectra; for complexes, compare the explicit descent model with the fiber over the target complex and give a split contraction.

1.1 Use the opens of [F3]. In the spectrum case, under [A1], the descent hypothesis identifies $E(X)$ with $E(W)\times_{E(V)}E(U)$, compatibly with its two projections. Taking the homotopy pullback of the projection to $E(U)$ along $0\to E(U)$ gives $E_T(X)\simeq (E(W)\times_{E(V)}E(U))\times_{E(U)}0\simeq E(W)\times_{E(V)}0=E_T(W)$. The middle equivalence follows by the universal property of homotopy pullbacks: a map into either side is a map into $E(W)$ together with a coherent nullhomotopy of its composite to $E(V)$. This is the restriction-induced equivalence. The universal property makes it natural for compatible maps of coherent squares. Its projection to $E(W)$ is the composite of the projection $E_T(X)\to E(X)$ with restriction $E(X)\to E(W)$, coherently as part of the pullback diagram. This gives the required support-to-global compatibility. [F1, F3, A1, given]

2.1 For complexes, let $q:A\to P$ be the descent quasi-isomorphism and $p:P\to C$ the projection. The equality $pq=A\to C$ gives a commutative square with vertical maps $q$ and $\operatorname{id}_C$. By [F2], its map $\operatorname{fib}(A\to C)\to\operatorname{fib}(P\to C)$ is a quasi-isomorphism. Write an element of $\operatorname{fib}(P\to C)^n$ as $(b,c,z,t)$ in $B^n\oplus C^n\oplus D^{n-1}\oplus C^{n-1}$. Its differential is $\delta(b,c,z,t)=(d_Bb,d_Cc,u(b)-v(c)-d_Dz,c-d_Ct)$. Define $r(b,c,z,t)=(b,z+v(t))$ in $\operatorname{fib}(B\to D)^n$. This is a cochain map, since the second component after applying $r\delta$ is $u(b)-d_D(z+v(t))$. It is split surjective as a cochain map with section $s(b,z)=(b,0,z,0)$. Its kernel consists of $(0,c,-v(t),t)$, so identifies with $\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{id}_C)$ with differential $(c,t)\mapsto(d_Cc,c-d_Ct)$. The degree-minus-one map $h(c,t)=(t,0)$ satisfies $dh+hd=\operatorname{id}$ on this kernel. More explicitly, on the whole source let $H(b,c,z,t)=(0,t,0,0)$; direct substitution gives $\delta H+H\delta=\operatorname{id}-sr$, while $rs=\operatorname{id}$. Hence $r$ and $s$ induce inverse cohomology maps: cochain-homotopic maps agree on cohomology because their difference on a cocycle is a coboundary. Thus $r$ is a quasi-isomorphism. The composite with the map induced by $q$ sends $(a,t)$ to $(a|_W,v(t))$, exactly restriction between the support-fiber models. It is a quasi-isomorphism, commutes strictly with the support-to-global projections and restriction $A\to B$, and is natural for maps of the given strictly commutative squares. Taking cohomology gives the claimed isomorphisms in every degree. Together with step 1.1 this proves both conditional assertions. [F2, F3, given, construct, algebra, step 1.1] ∎


## Perfect residue classes at smooth closed points

[Open item](item-lem-koszul-point-support-class) · level 12


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $X$ be a finite-type scheme over a perfect field $k$, and let $x$ be a closed point at which $X$ is smooth over $k$. Write $i:\operatorname{Spec}\kappa(x)\to X$ for the closed immersion of the point. There is a smooth affine open neighbourhood $V$ of $x$ on which the ideal of $x$ is generated by a regular sequence whose images in $\mathcal O_{X,x}$ form a regular system of parameters. Its Koszul complex is a finite free resolution of $i_*\kappa(x)|_V$. Thus $i$ is a regular immersion, and the residue sheaf $i_*\kappa(x)$, placed in degree zero, is perfect on $X$ and supported on $\{x\}$.

Consequently it has a distinguished class $[i_*\kappa(x)]$ in $K_{0,\{x\}}(X)$, whose image in $K_0(X)$ is its ordinary perfect-complex class. This class is independent of the choice of neighbourhood and parameters. No claim that it generates the supported group is made here; that identification requires a separate purity or dévissage result.

A perfect field is a field whose algebraic extensions are separable. A regular local ring is a noetherian local ring whose dimension equals the dimension of its maximal ideal modulo its square over its residue field. A regular system of parameters is a minimal generating sequence of that maximal ideal of length equal to the dimension. A sequence is regular if each element is a non-zero-divisor modulo its predecessors and the final quotient is nonzero. A regular immersion is a closed immersion whose ideal is locally generated by a regular sequence. The Koszul complex on a sequence $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ has terms $\bigwedge^j A^d$ and differential given by contraction with $(f_1,\ldots,f_d)$; for the empty sequence it is $A$ in degree zero. The residue sheaf $i_*\kappa(x)$ has stalk $\kappa(x)$ at $x$ and zero stalks elsewhere.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, a finite-type $k$-scheme $X$ over a perfect field, and a smooth closed point $x$. Use the same universe bounds as the supplied perfect categories and nonconnective K-theory.

**Goal:** Construct the local Koszul resolution, prove global perfectness and support, and identify the resulting supported class.

[F1] A smooth finite-type scheme over a perfect field has regular local rings under AC. A finite-type algebra over a field is a quotient of a finite-variable polynomial algebra, hence is noetherian; quotients and localizations of noetherian rings remain noetherian. ([math:thm-regular-equals-smooth-over-perfect-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-regular-equals-smooth-over-perfect-field), [math:def-finite-type-and-module-finite-algebras](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-finite-type-and-module-finite-algebras), [math:lem-finite-variable-polynomial-algebras-over-fields-are-noetherian-direct](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-variable-polynomial-algebras-over-fields-are-noetherian-direct), [math:thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations))

[F2] Under AC, lifts of a cotangent-space basis in a regular local ring form a regular system of parameters. Every such system is a regular sequence, and its Koszul complex resolves the residue field. ([math:lem-regular-system-of-parameters-equivalent-basis](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-regular-system-of-parameters-equivalent-basis), [math:thm-regular-local-rings-are-domains-and-cohen-macaulay](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-regular-local-rings-are-domains-and-cohen-macaulay), [math:lem-regular-local-residue-field-koszul-resolution](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-regular-local-residue-field-koszul-resolution), [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice))

[F3] Perfectness is the local condition of being represented by a bounded complex of finite locally free modules. The supported perfect category consists of perfect objects whose cohomology stalks vanish outside the specified subset; it is stable and idempotent complete. ([def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes))

[F4] For qcqs schemes the supported K-spectrum is the nonconnective K-theory of the supported perfect category, and its exact inclusion induces the support-to-global map. The interface supplies essential smallness and permits passage to an equivalent small presentation. For an idempotent-complete stable category, the degree-zero comparison sends each object to its K-theory class and is natural for exact functors. ([def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface), [thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input](item-thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input))

[F5] A $k$-scheme means a scheme over $\operatorname{Spec}k$. An affine open has its restricted structure sheaf and an affine presentation; a finite-type morphism is locally of finite type and quasi-compact. Quasi-compactness of a morphism means that inverse images of quasi-compact opens are quasi-compact. Smoothness uses local standard smooth presentations, so a smooth point has a principal affine neighbourhood on which the morphism is smooth. Principal localization identifies $\operatorname{Spec}A_g$ with $D(g)$, and the stalk at a prime is the corresponding prime localization. ([math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base), [math:def-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme), [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme), [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism), [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism), [math:def-smooth-morphism-classical](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-classical), [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion), [math:thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization))

[F6] Closed affine points correspond to maximal ideals, and their residue fields are $\kappa(x)=\mathcal O_{X,x}/\mathfrak m_x\cong A/\mathfrak m$. The quotient map defines a closed immersion into the affine chart. A closed immersion means a homeomorphism onto a closed subset with surjective structure-sheaf map. On a noetherian affine chart the associated sheaf of $A/\mathfrak m$ is its closed-point skyscraper. ([math:cor-closed-points-of-spectrum-are-maximal-ideals](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-closed-points-of-spectrum-are-maximal-ideals), [math:def-residue-field-scheme-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-residue-field-scheme-point), [math:lem-quotient-spectrum-map-is-a-closed-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-quotient-spectrum-map-is-a-closed-immersion), [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes), [math:ex-skyscraper-coherent-closed-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-skyscraper-coherent-closed-point))

[F7] In a noetherian ring every ideal is finitely generated, and every submodule of a finite module is finitely generated. Localization is exact and commutes with quotient modules and direct sums. In the fraction construction, $m/1=0$ in $S^{-1}M$ exactly when some $s\in S$ annihilates $m$. Thus a finite module whose localization at $\mathfrak m$ is zero is killed by a single element outside $\mathfrak m$: take the product of annihilating denominators for a finite generating list. ([math:def-noetherian-ring-and-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-ring-and-module), [math:thm-finitely-generated-modules-over-noetherian-rings-are-noetherian](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-finitely-generated-modules-over-noetherian-rings-are-noetherian), [math:thm-localisation-of-modules-is-exact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-localisation-of-modules-is-exact), [math:thm-localisation-of-modules-commutes-with-quotients-and-sums](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-localisation-of-modules-commutes-with-quotients-and-sums), [math:def-localisation-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-localisation-of-a-module))

[F8] Over any commutative ring, a finite regular sequence with finite free coefficient module has a finite free Koszul resolution of its quotient. This interface does not require the ring to be local. The empty sequence resolves the ring itself. ([math:cor-koszul-complex-resolves-a-regular-quotient](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-koszul-complex-resolves-a-regular-quotient))

[F9] Associated sheaf is the module-to-quasi-coherent-sheaf equivalence on an affine scheme. For an $A$-module $M$, its associated sheaf $\widetilde M$ has sections $M_g$ on $D(g)$, with canonical localization restrictions. Its stalk at a prime is the localized module, naturally in the module; a sequence of sheaves is exact if and only if all its stalk sequences are exact. In particular, localizing an exact module resolution and then using these stalk identifications proves exactness of its associated sheaf resolution. ([math:thm-affine-quasi-coherent-equivalence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-quasi-coherent-equivalence), [math:lem-associated-sheaf-sections-basic-open](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-associated-sheaf-sections-basic-open), [math:lem-associated-sheaf-stalk-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-associated-sheaf-stalk-localization), [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise))

[F10] A noetherian ring has noetherian spectrum. Under AC every open subspace of a noetherian topological space is quasi-compact. A scheme is quasi-separated when every intersection of two affine opens is quasi-compact, and qcqs means quasi-compact and quasi-separated. ([math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum), [math:lem-noetherian-subspaces-and-compact-opens](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-subspaces-and-compact-opens), [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme))

### Proof

1.1 Start with a finite-type affine chart at $x$ from [F5] and shrink it to a principal affine neighbourhood on which the structure morphism is smooth. Its coordinate ring is still of finite type over $k$: adjoining the inverse of the shrinking element adds one algebra generator. Write this neighbourhood as $U=\operatorname{Spec}A$. The closed point corresponds to a maximal ideal $\mathfrak m$ by [F6]. By [F1] and [F5], $A$ is noetherian and $R=A_{\mathfrak m}=\mathcal O_{X,x}$ is regular local. Choose a basis of its finite-dimensional cotangent space and lift it to a regular system of parameters $t_1,\ldots,t_d$ by [F2]. Write each $t_i=a_i/s_i$ with $a_i\in\mathfrak m$ and $s_i\notin\mathfrak m$, and put $f_i=a_i$. Multiplication of individual basis vectors by these units preserves the cotangent basis, so the images of $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ are also a regular system of parameters by [F2]. For $d=0$ use the empty tuple. To construct the stated morphism $i$, compose $\operatorname{Spec}(A/\mathfrak m)\hookrightarrow U$ from [F6] with the open inclusion into $X$. Its underlying image is the closed singleton $\{x\}$. On $U$ its structure-sheaf map is surjective by the quotient construction; on $X\setminus\{x\}$ it is the map to the zero sheaf. These opens cover $X$, so the stalk criterion [F9] and the closed-immersion definition [F6] make $i$ a closed immersion into $X$. The identification $A/\mathfrak m\cong\kappa(x)$ is canonical. [given, F1, F2, F5, F6, F9, choose, construct]

2.1 The finite $A$-module $\mathfrak m/(f_1,\ldots,f_d)$ localizes to zero at $\mathfrak m$, since the $f_i$ generate $\mathfrak mR$. By [F7] it is killed by some $h_0\notin\mathfrak m$. For each $j$, put $M_j=A/(f_1,\ldots,f_{j-1})$. The kernel of multiplication by $f_j$ on $M_j$ is finite by [F7]. Exactness of localization identifies its localization at $\mathfrak m$ with the multiplication kernel over $R$, which is zero by [F2]. Thus some $h_j\notin\mathfrak m$ kills this kernel. Set $h=h_0h_1\cdots h_d$ and $V=D(h)=\operatorname{Spec}A_h$ using [F5]. On $V$ the $f_i$ generate $\mathfrak mA_h$, and exactness of localization and its compatibility with quotients make every multiplication by $f_j$ on $A_h/(f_1,\ldots,f_{j-1})$ injective. The quotient $A_h/\mathfrak mA_h$ is canonically $A/\mathfrak m\ne0$: localization commutes with this quotient and $h$ has nonzero, hence invertible, image in the field $A/\mathfrak m$. Therefore all the preceding quotients are nonzero as well, and the sequence is regular on $A_h$. This includes $d=0$, when $\mathfrak mA_h=0$ and the empty sequence is regular. [F2, F5, F6, F7, step 1.1, construct, algebra]

3.1 Apply [F8] over the actual ring $A_h$, with coefficient module $A_h$, to the regular sequence from step 2.1. It gives a finite free resolution of $A_h/\mathfrak mA_h$. Place its exterior-power term $\bigwedge^j A_h^d$ in cochain degree $-j$, with contraction differential, and augment degree zero to the quotient. By [F9] its associated sheaf complex is exact: at every prime its augmented stalk complex is the localization of that exact module resolution, which remains exact by [F7]. A free module $A_h^r$ has associated sheaf $\mathcal O_V^r$, since on each basic open both have sections given by the same $r$-fold free module over the localized ring, with the same restriction maps. The resolved sheaf is $i_*\kappa(x)|_V$ by [F6], applied to the noetherian ring $A_h$ and its maximal ideal $\mathfrak mA_h$. Explicitly, for any open $W\subseteq X$, the globally defined residue sheaf has sections $\kappa(x)$ if $x\in W$ and zero otherwise, with identity restrictions between opens containing $x$ and scalar action through $\mathcal O_X(W)\to\mathcal O_{X,x}\to\kappa(x)$. This is the sheaf direct image along the constructed $i$; its restriction to $V$ is precisely that skyscraper. Thus the Koszul sheaf complex resolves this restriction. Since its ideal is generated by a regular sequence on a neighbourhood of the only point of the closed subscheme, $i$ is a regular immersion under the inline definition in the Statement. [F1, F6, F7, F8, F9, step 1.1, step 2.1]

4.1 The stalks of $i_*\kappa(x)$ vanish away from $x$: because $\{x\}$ is closed, each other point has a neighbourhood disjoint from it. Its stalk at $x$ is the nonzero field $\kappa(x)$ by the explicit section formula in step 3.1. On $V$ step 3.1 gives a bounded finite free resolution; on $X\setminus\{x\}$ the sheaf is zero, represented by the zero complex. These opens cover $X$, so [F3] makes the residue sheaf, placed in degree zero, perfect with support exactly $\{x\}$. The globally defined object is this sheaf; no extension by zero of a locally free resolution is used. [F3, step 3.1, given]

5.1 Since $\operatorname{Spec}k$ has one point, it is quasi-compact; the quasi-compact structure morphism in [F5] therefore makes $X$ quasi-compact. Local finite type gives an affine cover with finite-type coordinate rings; choose a finite subcover $U_1,\ldots,U_n$. All these rings are noetherian by [F1], so each $U_a$ has noetherian underlying space by [F10]. For any open $W\subseteq X$, each $W\cap U_a$ is quasi-compact by [F10]. An open cover of $W$ restricts to a cover of each such intersection; take its finite subcover there and unite these finitely many finite subcovers. Thus every open $W$ is quasi-compact. In particular all intersections of affine opens are quasi-compact, proving quasi-separatedness by [F10]; hence $X$ is qcqs. Apply [F4] to the supported perfect object of step 4.1, using the stable idempotent-complete supported category from [F3] and its equivalent small presentation supplied by [F4]. Its object class lies in $K_{0,\{x\}}(X)$, and naturality for the exact inclusion sends it to the class of the same object in $K_0(X)$. Every choice of parameters and neighbourhood in steps 1.1 through 3.1 resolves the same globally defined sheaf, so the supported object and its class are independent of those choices. AC is retained through [F1], [F2], [F4], [F6], [F9] and [F10], and the universe bounds are those of [F4]. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F9, F10, step 4.1, choose] ∎


## Compatible comparisons transport relative objects and support fibers

[Open item](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-compatible-relative-transport) · level 12


### Statement
In a triangulated category, consider two specified distinguished triangles $A\xrightarrow{f} B\to Q\to A[1]$ and $A'\xrightarrow{f'} B'\to Q'\to A'[1]$. If isomorphisms $u:A\to A'$ and $v:B\to B'$ satisfy $vf=f'u$, there exists an isomorphism $w:Q\to Q'$ completing a morphism of triangles; uniqueness of $w$ is not asserted. In particular any two choices of a cone for the same first arrow are isomorphic compatibly with the first two objects, without making cones a functor on an arbitrary triangulated category.

For the support setting, take $X,T,U=X\setminus T$ and cochain complexes of abelian groups as in the support-fiber interface. For a strictly commuting square between restriction maps $f:C(X)\to C(U)$ and $g:D(X)\to D(U)$, with vertical quasi-isomorphisms $u:C(X)\to D(X)$ and $v:C(U)\to D(U)$, the coordinatewise map $F(f)\to F(g)$ on the explicit support fibers is a quasi-isomorphism and respects their projections to the global complexes. Here a quasi-isomorphism means a cochain map inducing isomorphisms in all cohomological degrees, and $F(f)^n=C(X)^n\oplus C(U)^{n-1}$ with differential $(a,b)\mapsto(d a,f(a)-d b)$.

More generally work in $D(\mathbf{Ab})$, the localization of the homotopy category of unbounded cochain complexes of abelian groups at quasi-isomorphisms, under the standing size convention of a small category of complexes or supplied small cofinal denominator families. Suppose isomorphisms $u:C(X)\to D(X)$ and $v:C(U)\to D(U)$ in this derived category satisfy $vf=gu$ there. For any chosen fiber triangles $F\xrightarrow{p}C(X)\xrightarrow{f}C(U)\to F[1]$ and $G\xrightarrow{q}D(X)\xrightarrow{g}D(U)\to G[1]$, there exists an isomorphism $h:F\to G$ with $qh=up$ completing a morphism of these triangles. For cochain representatives one may use the explicit support-fiber models above; strict representatives for the entire comparison square are not required. No unique or functorial choice of $h$ is asserted.

Consequently compatible derived comparisons on the ambient scheme and its open complement suffice to transport a supplied purity calculation for its support object by this construction, together with the support-to-global map. The purity identification and the compatible comparisons remain application premises. A separately supplied direct compatible comparison of support objects may also suffice; no universal necessity of ambient and open comparisons is asserted. Unrelated isomorphisms of individual cohomology groups do not by themselves supply the commuting derived comparison used here.

### Statement references
[def-supported-complex-indexing](item-def-supported-complex-indexing)

[math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category)

[math:def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles)

[math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The two distinguished triangles and their commuting first-arrow square; separately the strict cochain square, or the commuting square of derived isomorphisms and chosen fiber triangles, with the size convention in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish cone transport, strict support-fiber transport, and derived support-fiber transport, retaining the support-to-global maps and the nonuniqueness qualifications.

[F1] A triangulated category has its specified additive translation and distinguished triangles. A morphism of triangles is a triple commuting with all three arrows, including the translated first component. [math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category), [math:def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-morphism-and-isomorphism-of-triangles)

[F2] TR3 supplies some third map completing a commuting first-arrow square to a morphism of distinguished triangles. [math:def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category-axiom-tr-three)

[F3] Representable Hom gives long exact sequences on distinguished triangles, with maps induced by the triangle arrows. [math:thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle)

[F4] The cochain, cohomology, quasi-isomorphism, shift, cone and explicit support-fiber conventions, including the projection $F(f)\to C(X)$, are those of the support-fiber interface. In particular $A[k]^n=A^{n+k}$ has differential $(-1)^k d_A$, and $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^n=B^n\oplus A^{n+1}$ has differential $(b,a)\mapsto(d_Bb+f(a),-d_Aa)$. [def-supported-complex-indexing](item-def-supported-complex-indexing)

[F5] Abelian groups and homomorphisms form an abelian category. A short exact sequence of cochain complexes in an abelian category has its natural long exact cohomology sequence. [math:thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-abelian-groups-form-an-abelian-category), [math:thm-long-exact-sequence-in-cohomology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-sequence-in-cohomology)

[F6] The derived category is the roof localization of the homotopy category at quasi-isomorphisms under the stated size convention, with the cochain shifts and cone convention of [F4]. [math:def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-derived-category-of-an-abelian-category)

[F7] The derived category is triangulated by localized cone triangles, and localization is exact. [math:thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-derived-category-inherits-a-triangulated-structure)

[F8] Signed rotations and integral translates of distinguished triangles are distinguished. For $A\to B\to K\xrightarrow{r}A[1]$, inverse rotation starts $K[-1]\xrightarrow{-r[-1]}A\to B$. [math:def-rotation-of-a-triangle](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-rotation-of-a-triangle), [math:prop-distinguished-triangles-are-closed-under-shifts-and-both-rotations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-distinguished-triangles-are-closed-under-shifts-and-both-rotations)

### Proof

1.1 By [F1] and [F2], choose $w$ completing the given triangle square. For every object $T$, [F3] gives a commuting ladder of exact five-term sequences centered on $\operatorname{Hom}(T,Q)\to\operatorname{Hom}(T,Q')$, whose other four vertical maps are isomorphisms induced by $u,v$ and their translates. Here is the exactness argument, to be used again below. Denote a general such ladder by $E_1\xrightarrow{d_1}E_2\xrightarrow{d_2}E_3\xrightarrow{d_3}E_4\xrightarrow{d_4}E_5$ and its primed row, with vertical maps $a_i$, all except $a_3$ isomorphisms. For $x'\in E'_3$, put $y=a_4^{-1}d'_3x'$. Injectivity of $a_5$ gives $d_4y=0$, so $y=d_3x$ for some $x\in E_3$. The difference $x'-a_3x$ lies in $\ker d'_3=\operatorname{im}d'_2$; lifting its preimage through $a_2$ and adding its $d_2$ image to $x$ proves surjectivity of $a_3$. If $a_3x=0$, injectivity of $a_4$ gives $x=d_2z$ for some $z\in E_2$. Then $a_2z\in\ker d'_2=\operatorname{im}d'_1$. Lift a preimage through $a_1$; injectivity of $a_2$ shows $z\in\operatorname{im}d_1$, hence $x=0$. Thus $\operatorname{Hom}(T,w)$ is bijective. Taking $T=Q'$ gives $h:Q'\to Q$ with $wh=\operatorname{id}_{Q'}$; injectivity for $T=Q$ applied to $w(hw-\operatorname{id}_Q)=0$ gives $hw=\operatorname{id}_Q$. Hence $w$ is an isomorphism of the third objects, and [F1] makes the triple an isomorphism of triangles. Applying this with identity maps on the first two objects compares any two cone choices. TR3 supplies existence and does not assert uniqueness, so this reasoning does not select a functorial cone. [F1, F2, F3, given, algebra]

2.1 In the strict cochain case [F4] gives the coordinatewise map $(a,b)\mapsto(u(a),v(b))$. Its compatibility with differentials is exactly $vf=gu$, and its projections commute with $u$. The inclusion $b\mapsto(0,b)$ and the projection give a degreewise short exact sequence $0\to C(U)[-1]\to F(f)\to C(X)\to0$, and likewise for $g$. By [F5], their comparison gives a commuting ladder of long exact cohomology sequences. The outer vertical maps in the five-term segment centered on $H^n(F(f))\to H^n(F(g))$ are isomorphisms: they come from $u,v$ and $H^n(B[-1])=H^{n-1}(B)$. The chase in step 1.1 proves the fiber map is a quasi-isomorphism. For the general derived comparison use [F6] and [F7] with the abelian category in [F5]. For any cochain map $f:A\to B$, the signed permutation $J:F(f)\to\operatorname{Cone}(f)[-1]$, $J(a,b)=(b,-a)$, is a cochain isomorphism: both $dJ(a,b)$ and $Jd(a,b)$ equal $(f(a)-d_Bb,-d_Aa)$. Under inverse rotation of its cone triangle, the map $\operatorname{Cone}(f)[-1]\to A$ is minus the shifted cone projection by [F8], and its composite with $J$ is $a$. Thus localization identifies the explicit model, with its support-to-global map, as a fiber triangle. Arbitrary derived morphisms and their chosen fiber triangles have the meaning of [F4] and [F6]. Rotate the two specified fiber triangles to $C(X)\to C(U)\to F[1]\to C(X)[1]$ and $D(X)\to D(U)\to G[1]\to D(X)[1]$ using [F8]. Apply step 1.1 to $u,v$ to obtain an isomorphism $w:F[1]\to G[1]$ of their third objects. Put $h=w[-1]$ using the translation coherence. The final square of that triangle morphism reads $(-q[1])w=u[1](-p[1])$; shifting back and cancelling signs gives $qh=up$. Its other squares give the claimed morphism of fiber triangles. This argument uses only a square commuting in the derived category. Composing with any supplied purity identification transports that calculation and its global map, establishing the stated sufficient conditions without a uniqueness or necessity claim. [F1, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8, given, step 1.1, construct, algebra] ∎


## Inherited bounded t structure on supported perfect complexes

[Open item](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart) · level 12


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed suitable universes of the supplied enhanced constructions. For a Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme $U$ that is regular at every point and any closed subscheme $i:T\hookrightarrow U$, put $\mathcal C_T=\mathrm{Perf}_{|T|}(U)$ in the supplied enhancement and let $\mathcal A_T$ be coherent $\mathcal O_U$-modules with support contained in the underlying closed set $|T|$. Ambient cohomological truncations restrict to a bounded t-structure on $\mathcal C_T$, with heart $\mathcal A_T$ embedded as sheaves in degree zero. In particular $\mathcal A_T$ is abelian, and its short exact sequences give actual cofiber sequences in $\mathcal C_T$.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fixed universes discharge the enhanced regular-perfect and derived-category premises in [F1] and [F6] and the specified perfect/nonconnective interfaces in [F2]. The universes satisfy [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and are enlarged for categories as required by [F6]. Their existence is a standing size assumption, not a consequence of Choice. These assumptions are retained in steps 1.1–2.1; the finite cohomology and stalk-support calculations introduce no additional choice.

**Given:** The Axiom of Choice and $U,T,i,\mathcal C_T,\mathcal A_T$ as stated, with the fixed suitable universes.

**Goal:** Work directly with the supported perfect category and its cohomology, without an enhancement comparison to complexes in a coherent abelian category.

[F1] [thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-regular-cohomology-input](item-thm-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-regular-cohomology-input) supplies local regular perfection, bounded coherent cohomology and the actual ambient derived t-structure with ordinary module sheaves as heart. On this locally Noetherian scheme it also supplies the abelian full category of coherent sheaves with kernels, images and cokernels computed as module sheaves.

[F2] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) gives the stalkwise support criterion and the full stable idempotent-complete supported subcategory, with ambient finite colimits. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes the essentially small common-family realization used here and the chosen K functor; no K-theory comparison is used in this lemma.

[F3] The geometric hypotheses and the coherent modules use [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme), [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point), [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) and [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme). Regularity means regularity of every local ring, without a base-field smoothness hypothesis. Finite dimension is the chain dimension of [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space), with its empty-space convention.

[F4] [math:def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category) specifies the cohomological t-structure axioms and heart. [math:def-truncation-functors-and-cohomology-of-a-t-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-truncation-functors-and-cohomology-of-a-t-structure) defines boundedness: each object lies in $\mathcal C_T^{\le n}\cap\mathcal C_T^{\ge -n}$ for some integer $n$. [math:lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology) supplies the retained/zero cohomology identities for the ordinary derived truncations; [F1] supplies their enhanced lifts and actual truncation cofiber sequences.

[F5] [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category) specifies the abelian-category condition. Kernels, images and cokernels are the module-sheaf operations of [math:def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-kernel-cokernel-image-sheaves), and short exact sequences have the meaning of [math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves). [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise) identifies exactness with stalkwise exactness; apply it to the underlying abelian sheaves of the module sheaves. Thus these operations preserve vanishing of all stalks outside $|T|$.

[F6] [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input) specifies the actual infinity-localization of module cochain complexes, the formula $H^n=\ker d^n/\operatorname{im}d^{n-1}$, and its cochain cone cofibers: $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^n=E^n\oplus C^{n+1}$ with differential $(e,c)\mapsto(d_Ee+fc,-d_Cc)$. Quasi-isomorphisms become equivalences in this localization.

### Proof
1.1 By [F1], kernels, images and cokernels of maps of coherent sheaves are coherent. By [F5], their stalks vanish off $|T|$ when the source and target do. Zero sheaves and finite direct sums also have this support, so the full subcategory $\mathcal A_T$ is additive and inherits kernels and cokernels from the abelian coherent category. Its coimage-to-image comparison is the same isomorphism as there, proving that it is abelian. Every $M\in\mathcal A_T$ placed in degree zero has bounded coherent cohomology, hence is perfect by [F1] and lies in $\mathcal C_T$ by [F2]. For a short exact sequence $0\to M'\xrightarrow{f}M\xrightarrow{q}M''\to0$ in this abelian category, its ambient module-sheaf sequence is exact because kernels and cokernels are inherited. The cone of $f[0]$ in [F6] is the two-term cochain complex $M'\xrightarrow{f}M$ in degrees $-1,0$. The map to $M''[0]$ given by $q$ in degree zero has $H^{-1}=\ker f=0$, $H^0=\operatorname{coker}f=M''$, and zero cohomology in all other degrees, so it is a quasi-isomorphism. This cone represents the actual ambient cofiber by [F6], and its equivalent object $M''[0]$ is in $\mathcal C_T$; ambient cofibers are inherited by [F2]. Thus the short exact sequence gives an actual cofiber sequence in $\mathcal C_T$. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F5, F6]

2.1 For $E\in\mathcal C_T$, [F1] gives bounded coherent cohomology. By [F4], each ambient truncation retains the original cohomology sheaves in its retained degrees and has zero cohomology in the deleted degrees. It therefore remains bounded coherent, perfect by [F1], and supported on $|T|$ by [F2]. Intersect the two ambient t-structure subcategories with $\mathcal C_T$: shift closure and orthogonality restrict because this is a full stable subcategory, and the ambient truncation cofiber sequences lie in it by the preceding calculation. These are precisely the t-structure axioms in [F4]. If $H^i(E)=0$ outside $[a,b]$, the canonical vanishing conditions give $E\in\mathcal C_T^{\le b}\cap\mathcal C_T^{\ge a}$; taking $n\ge\max\{b,-a,0\}$ proves boundedness in the sense of [F4]. By the ambient heart identification in [F1], an object of the restricted heart is equivalent to an ordinary module sheaf in degree zero. Its coherent supported cohomology makes that sheaf an object of $\mathcal A_T$, and conversely every $M[0]$ with $M\in\mathcal A_T$ belongs to this heart by step 1.1. This proves the stated heart and, with step 1.1, the cofiber assertion. No global locally free presentation was used. [A1, F1, F2, F4, step 1.1] ∎


## Additivity of the supported coherent Euler class

[Open item](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-euler) · level 13


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed suitable universes of the supplied supported perfect construction. Let $U$ be a regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme and $i:T\hookrightarrow U$ a closed subscheme. Set $\mathcal C_T=\operatorname{Perf}_{|T|}(U)$ and let $\mathcal A_T$ be the abelian category of coherent $\mathcal O_U$-modules supported on $|T|$, embedded in $\mathcal C_T$ in degree zero. Use the supplied essentially small realizations. Write $K_0^\triangle(h\mathcal C_T)$ for the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes of objects with $[F]=[E]+[G]$ for every distinguished triangle $E\to F\to G\to E[1]$. Write $G_0(\mathcal A_T)$ for the free abelian group on isomorphism classes in $\mathcal A_T$ modulo $[M]=[M']+[M'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to M'\to M\to M''\to0$.

The finite Euler assignment
$$\chi_T([E])=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}(-1)^n[H^n(E)]$$
defines a homomorphism $\chi_T:K_0^\triangle(h\mathcal C_T)\to G_0(\mathcal A_T)$. It sends $[M[0]]$ to $[M]$ for every $M\in\mathcal A_T$. For $T=U$, write $\mathcal C_U=\operatorname{Perf}(U)$ and $\mathcal A_U=\operatorname{Coh}(U)$, with the same group conventions. The inclusions $J:\mathcal C_T\hookrightarrow\mathcal C_U$ and $I:\mathcal A_T\hookrightarrow\mathcal A_U$ induce homomorphisms $J_\triangle$ and $I_0$ on these groups, and
$$\chi_U\circ J_\triangle=I_0\circ\chi_T.$$

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fixed suitable universes discharge the supported-heart construction in [F1], the essentially small realization in [F4], the enhanced derived construction in [F5], and the Noetherian-open results in [F6], used in steps 1.1–2.1. Universe existence is a separate standing size assumption, not a consequence of Choice. The finite alternating-sum argument requires no additional choice.

**Given:** $U,T,i,\mathcal C_T,\mathcal A_T$ as stated, the Axiom of Choice, and the fixed suitable universes of the supplied constructions.

**Goal:** Verify the Euler assignment on every triangle relation and identify both forget-support homomorphisms.

[F1] [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart) supplies these geometric hypotheses, the actual bounded t-structure on $\mathcal C_T$, finite coherent supported cohomology, and its abelian heart $\mathcal A_T$. Kernels, images and cokernels in this heart are the ambient module-sheaf operations. The same assertions with $T=U$ give the ambient coherent heart $\mathcal A_U$.

[F2] [lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation](item-lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation) identifies the triangle group with the object/cofiber presentation, preserving object classes. Apply it to an equivalent small model; isomorphic objects and corresponding triangles give identical generators and relations in either presentation.

[F3] [math:def-grothendieck-group-of-an-essentially-small-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-an-essentially-small-abelian-category) gives the precise target group, its generators, its short exact relations and $[0]=0$. Applied to either heart, it gives the group convention in the Statement.

[F4] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) supplies the full stable supported and ambient categories with their finite colimits inherited from the same derived category. Hence $J$ preserves zero objects and cofiber sequences. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes compatible essentially small realizations for qcqs schemes, meaning that the scheme and every intersection of two affine opens are quasi-compact. Step 1.1 checks this hypothesis using [F6]. A heart is full and closed under isomorphism in its homotopy category, so its isomorphism classes form a subset of the set of ambient object classes; both hearts here are essentially small. No nonconnective K-theory comparison is invoked.

[F5] [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input) supplies the module-sheaf abelian category, its derived enhancement, cochain cone cofibers, and the representation of each derived arrow, after an equivalence of its target, by a cochain map. [math:prop-cohomology-factors-through-the-derived-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-cohomology-factors-through-the-derived-category) makes cohomology functorial and invariant under derived isomorphism. Apply [math:thm-the-cone-long-exact-sequence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-cone-long-exact-sequence) in the abelian category of all $\mathcal O_U$-modules, using $C^\sharp_n=C^{-n}$ and $H_n(C^\sharp)=H^{-n}(C)$. The supplied cochain cone $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^n=F^n\oplus E^{n+1}$ with differential $(y,x)\mapsto(d_Fy+fx,-d_Ex)$ reindexes to exactly that theorem's chain cone. Thus every derived cofiber triangle, and hence every triangle in $h\mathcal C_T$, has the exact cohomology sequence with successive terms $H^n(E),H^n(F),H^n(G),H^{n+1}(E)$. Its terms lie in $\mathcal A_T$ by [F1], and exactness is inherited there because its kernels and images are ambient ones.

[F6] [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) gives a finite affine open cover by spectra of Noetherian rings. Under the assumed Choice, [math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum) makes each such affine Noetherian as a topological space, and [math:lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact) makes each of its open subsets quasi-compact.

### Proof
1.1 Every open subset of $U$ is covered by its intersections with the finite affine cover in [F6]. Each intersection is quasi-compact by [F6]; for any open cover choose a finite subcover on each intersection and combine the finitely many subcovers. Thus every open subset of $U$ is quasi-compact, in particular $U$ and every intersection of two affine opens. Hence [F4] applies and supplies the essentially small categories and hearts needed for the group presentations. Cohomology is invariant under isomorphism by [F5], and boundedness in [F1] makes the displayed assignment a finite sum on every object class. For a triangle $E\to F\to G\to E[1]$, [F5] gives its long exact cohomology sequence; truncate its zero ends to obtain a finite exact sequence in $\mathcal A_T$. In a finite exact sequence $0\to X_a\to\cdots\to X_b\to0$, let $B_j$ be the image of $X_j\to X_{j+1}$, with $B_{a-1}=B_b=0$. Exactness and the abelian-category axioms give short exact sequences $0\to B_{j-1}\to X_j\to B_j\to0$. Their relations in [F3] give $[X_j]=[B_{j-1}]+[B_j]$. Multiplying by alternating signs and summing cancels each image class and leaves zero, using $[0]=0$. Applied to the cohomology sequence, this yields $\sum_n(-1)^n([H^n(E)]-[H^n(F)]+[H^n(G)])=0$, so $\chi_T([F])=\chi_T([E])+\chi_T([G])$. The triangle presentation in [F2] therefore gives the asserted homomorphism. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6]

2.1 For $M[0]$, the only nonzero cohomology is $H^0=M$, so its image is $[M]$. By [F4], $J$ preserves cofibers, and therefore triangles under [F2]; sending $[E]$ to $[JE]$ respects every source relation and defines $J_\triangle$. The inclusion $I$ preserves short exact sequences because both hearts have the same underlying module-sheaf kernels and cokernels by [F1]; hence $[M]\mapsto[IM]$ respects [F3] and defines $I_0$. Apply step 1.1 with $T=U$ to define $\chi_U$. Forgetting support leaves each complex and each cohomology sheaf unchanged, so $H^n(JE)=I(H^n(E))$. Substituting into the same finite sum proves $\chi_U(J_\triangle[E])=I_0(\chi_T[E])$ on every generator, hence on the whole group. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## Exact true-triangle and Waldhausen determinant one-type input

[Open item](item-thm-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-waldhausen-determinant-one-type) · level 13


### Statement
Assume AC and fixed universe bounds. For a small Waldhausen category $\mathcal W$ a determinant functor to a Picard groupoid $\mathcal P$ consists of a functor on weak equivalences and additivity isomorphisms for actual cofibration sequences, satisfying weak-equivalence naturality, staircase associativity and commutativity. It factors essentially uniquely through the universal Picard groupoid $V(\mathcal W)$, which represents the 1-type of $K^W(\mathcal W)$. Hence it gives an actual map of connective K-space 1-types to the nerve of $\mathcal P$, natural in exact functors and compatible with object/automorphism classes. For bounded finite-projective complexes over a commutative unital ring $A$, the Knudsen–Mumford alternating graded determinant is such a functor. Its triangle coherence is for true/enhanced triangles. It is not a claim of natural additivity under every morphism of ordinary distinguished-triangle diagrams.

In this affine application use $\mathcal W(A)=Ch^b(\operatorname{proj}A)$ with degreewise split monomorphisms as cofibrations and quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences. Its canonical comparison with the connective cover of the specified nonconnective perfect K-spectrum on $\operatorname{Spec}A$ is an equivalence. Thus the determinant map transports to that K-space 1-type, naturally under extension of scalars and with the same object and automorphism values. No noetherian, regularity or flatness assumption on $A$ or on a ring map is imposed.

### External input
Import the following definitions and established results at the exact source locations in the four reference records. The numbered proof composes these inputs with the declared local suppliers; it does not reconstruct their published foundations.

A Waldhausen category has a chosen zero object $0$ and subcategories of cofibrations and weak equivalences containing every isomorphism. Every $0\to X$ is a cofibration. Pushouts of a cofibration along any map exist and the resulting map is a cofibration. For a map between two such pushout spans whose three component maps are weak equivalences, the induced map of pushouts is a weak equivalence (gluing). Both designated classes are closed under composition. A cofiber sequence is $X\hookrightarrow Y\to Y/X$, where $Y/X=Y\sqcup_X0$, with that pushout data. An exact functor preserves zero, both designated classes and these pushouts. These are Weibel IV.8.1, p. 66; no saturation or cylinder axiom is part of this general definition.

A Picard groupoid is a symmetric monoidal category in which every arrow is invertible and tensoring by each object is an equivalence, as in MTW §1.1, pp. 143–144. Symmetric monoidal has the meaning of the declared library definition, including associativity, unit and symmetry constraints. A symmetric tensor functor $F:\mathcal P\to\mathcal Q$ carries natural isomorphisms $1_{\mathcal Q}\to F(1_{\mathcal P})$ and $F(x)\otimes F(y)\to F(x\otimes y)$ compatible with associativity, units and symmetry; a tensor natural transformation commutes with the comparison maps (MTW §3.1, p. 194). Its graded-line example on $X$ has objects $(n,L)$, with $n:X\to\mathbb Z$ locally constant and $L$ an invertible sheaf; arrows are line isomorphisms at the same degree. Tensor is $(n,L)\otimes(m,M)=(n+m,L\otimes M)$, with unit $(0,\mathcal O_X)$ and symmetry $a\otimes b\mapsto(-1)^{nm}b\otimes a$. Here line bundles and exterior determinants have the meaning of the declared exterior-determinant supplier, applied on the disjoint open rank loci when rank varies. MTW Example 1.2.7, p. 147, imports the extension of this determinant to bounded complexes. On a bounded complex $P$ its value is $\bigotimes_i(\operatorname{rk}P^i,\det P^i)^{(-1)^i}$, where exponent $-1$ means the tensor inverse (dual line and opposite degree); the tensor order and constraints are those of KM p. 31.

For a small category $C$, its nerve $NC$ has strings of $n$ composable arrows as $n$-simplices, with faces given by deletion/composition and degeneracies by inserting identities, and $BC=|NC|$ is its classifying space (Weibel IV.3.1.4, pp. 24–25). Let $S_n\mathcal W$ have filtrations $0=X_0\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow X_n$ with chosen compatible quotients $X_j/X_i$ and their pushout squares; morphisms preserve that diagram. In $wS_n\mathcal W$ keep its objectwise weak equivalences. The simplicial operators omit or repeat stages, using quotients for the first face. The K-space is $\Omega|NwS_\bullet\mathcal W|$ and iterated S-constructions give its connective spectrum $K^W(\mathcal W)$ (Weibel IV.8.3–8.5.5, pp. 67–69). The canonical one-skeleton map $B(w\mathcal W)\to\Omega^\infty K^W(\mathcal W)$ supplies the object and weak-self-equivalence classes.

For a connective spectrum $E$, its 1-type $\tau_{\leq1}E$ is its Postnikov truncation with homotopy groups in degrees zero and one, retaining the stable k-invariant; $\Omega^\infty$ denotes its infinite loop space. MTW §1.6, pp. 166–167, imports the equivalence between these spectral 1-types and Picard groupoids, including the k-invariant, and identifies the universal $V(\mathcal W)$ with the 1-type of the actual Waldhausen spectrum. Thus $BV(\mathcal W)\simeq\Omega^\infty\tau_{\leq1}K^W(\mathcal W)$ as the corresponding grouplike spaces. A symmetric tensor functor induces the required map on nerves, with the monoidal data retained; no inference from just two abstract groups is used. The universal determinant functor and its factorization are MTW Definition 1.2.9 and Theorem 1.5.4; its construction is natural in exact functors, as recorded on p. 212.

The determinant data are exactly MTW Definition 1.2.3, pp. 145–146: a functor $d:w\mathcal W\to\mathcal P$ and isomorphisms $d(Y/X)\otimes d(X)\to d(Y)$, natural for weak-equivalence diagrams of actual cofiber sequences. For $X\hookrightarrow Y\hookrightarrow Z$, the two composites from $d(Z/Y)\otimes d(Y/X)\otimes d(X)$ to $d(Z)$ agree with the associativity constraint; the two coproduct cofiber sequences agree with the symmetry constraint. KM Definition 2, pp. 37–38, defines true triangles using the category $\mathcal A_3$ of diagrams $E''\to E\to E'$ with zero composite in an abelian category $\mathcal A$: take the full subcategory of $D(\mathcal A_3)$ represented by degreewise short exact sequences of complexes. Its isomorphisms are taken there. They carry more data than ordinary triangle diagrams in $D(\mathcal A)$. KM Definition 4 and Theorem 2, pp. 40–42, import determinant additivity and its naturality, true-triangle compatibility and base-change composition on these data. The p. 41 footnote and p. 44 warning exclude the unrestricted ordinary-triangle interpretation.

For the affine comparison, $\operatorname{proj}A$ consists of finitely generated projective $A$-modules and $Ch^b$ means bounded cochain complexes with chain maps; a quasi-isomorphism is a map inducing isomorphisms on all cohomology modules. Use the actual model and its enhanced localization furnished by the declared enhanced-comparison supplier. BGT §2.2, pp. 13–14, defines localization by inverting weak equivalences, stable infinity-categories by finite limits and colimits with pushout squares equal to pullback squares, and exact functors by preservation of those operations. DHKS-saturation means that a map is a weak equivalence exactly when its image is invertible in the localized homotopy category. BGT Lemma 7.11/Corollary 7.12 require this saturation and factorization into a cofibration followed by a weak equivalence. BGT Remark 9.33 additionally requires idempotent completeness for degree zero: the Yoneda image is closed under retracts. These model and retract premises are supplied in [F3] and [F4], rather than assumed for every Waldhausen category. The connective cover $\tau_{\geq0}E\to E$ preserves all nonnegative homotopy groups and has no negative groups. BGT supplies the comparison through this cover under those hypotheses; [F5] fixes its actual nonconnective target.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** A small Waldhausen category and a determinant functor with the imported axioms; in the affine application, any commutative unital ring $A$, the stated Waldhausen structure on bounded finite-projective complexes, AC and compatible universe bounds.

**Goal:** Supply the determinant map with its exact coherence and verify transport to the specified affine nonconnective K-space.

[F1] Import the KM true-triangle determinant, the MTW determinant/universal-one-type theorems, and the Weibel object types and S-construction, with exactly the definitions, hypotheses and reading limits recorded above.

[F2] Import BGT Lemma 7.11/Corollary 7.12 and Remark 9.33 with factorization, DHKS-saturation and, for degree zero, idempotent completeness.

[F3] [thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons](item-thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons) supplies the specified Waldhausen model, verifies its factorization and DHKS-saturation in Proof 1.2 and 2.2, identifies its enhanced localization by the actual associated-sheaf functor, and identifies the canonical comparison with the specified nonconnective functor. Its positive-degree assertion alone does not supply degree-zero agreement.

[F4] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) fixes the full enhanced perfect category, and [lem-author-4f716d60801a-supported-stability](item-lem-author-4f716d60801a-supported-stability) supplies its stability and idempotent completeness, including the affine case $X=\operatorname{Spec}A$. Thus the underlying localization in [F3] is idempotent complete.

[F5] [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes $K(X)=\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}(X))$ in its common localized family, with the specified inclusion-induced enhanced equivalences and pullbacks. [math:cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact) under AC and [math:thm-affine-schemes-quasi-separated](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-schemes-quasi-separated) show that every affine $X=\operatorname{Spec}A$ is qcqs, so its hypotheses apply here. The compatible identification in [F3] refers to this target.

[F6] [math:def-symmetric-monoidal-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-symmetric-monoidal-category) supplies the symmetric monoidal structure used in the Picard definition. [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles) supplies the line and exterior-determinant constructions and their pullbacks; for a locally varying rank use its constant-rank construction on each disjoint open rank locus.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is assumed for the enhanced realizations and small presentations in [F3]–[F5]. Fixed compatible universes contain all the categories used; a small equivalent Waldhausen presentation retains the cofibration, quotient and factorization constructions, as in [F3]. No extra choice principle is inferred from a finite tensor product.

### Proof
1.1 With the object types and constructions in the External input section, MTW Theorem 1.5.4 and Definition 1.2.9 give a symmetric tensor factorization $V(\mathcal W)\to\mathcal P$ of the specified determinant functor, unique up to the stated compatible tensor natural isomorphism. MTW Theorem 1.6.3 and §1.6 identify $BV(\mathcal W)$ with the 1-type of the actual Waldhausen K-space. Taking the nerve of the factorization therefore gives the asserted map, retaining its object and weak-self-equivalence classes and its naturality under exact functors. For $\mathcal W(A)$, MTW Example 1.2.7 identifies the bounded-complex graded determinant as such a functor. KM Definition 4 supplies its true-triangle naturality, associativity and pullback composition; the graded symmetry in [F6] and MTW §1.1 retains the commutativity sign. Hence this uses actual cofiber/true-triangle diagrams, with no naturality claim for arbitrary ordinary distinguished-triangle diagrams. The universality and spectral comparison are imported theorems, not a reconstruction from $K_0$ and $K_1$. [F1, F6, A1]

2.1 For the stated commutative unital $A$, [F3] supplies the bounded finite-projective Waldhausen model and verifies BGT's hypotheses: mapping cylinders give cofibration/weak-equivalence factorization, and quasi-isomorphisms between these complexes are homotopy equivalences because their bounded acyclic projective cones are contractible. A map inverted in the localization is conversely a quasi-isomorphism, proving DHKS-saturation. That supplier identifies the localization, by the actual associated-sheaf equivalence, with the affine perfect category of [F4] and with the specified realization used in [F5]. Idempotent completeness is supplied by [F4]; it is preserved under these equivalences, so the degree-zero clause of [F2] applies as well as its positive-degree clause. The canonical map $K^W(\mathcal W(A))\to\tau_{\geq0}K(\operatorname{Spec}A)$ is consequently an equivalence, allowing transport of step 1.1 to its K-space 1-type. For a ring map $A\to B$, termwise tensor sends finite projectives to finite projectives, preserves split cofibrations and their pushouts, and preserves quasi-isomorphisms here because it preserves the contractions of their cones. It is therefore an exact functor on these models without flatness assumptions. The natural comparison of [F2]–[F3], the pullback identifications of [F5]–[F6], and KM's composition-compatible pullback data transport the same object and automorphism determinant maps, naturally under these exact functors. These are supplied maps and monoidal naturality data, rather than an inference of an unspecified higher enhancement from an ordinary triangle statement. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, A1, step 1.1] ∎


## Motivic and filtered K theory with support

[Open item](item-def-cohomology-with-support) · level 14


### Definition
Fix a field $k$ and the category $\mathrm{Sch}_k$ of quasi-compact quasi-separated $k$-schemes with the article conventions, together with the Axiom of Choice and the size conventions of the cited interfaces. Let $X$ be an object of this category, let $Y$ be a closed subset of $X$ equipped with its reduced induced closed-subscheme structure, and put $U=X\setminus Y$. This is the unique reduced closed subscheme supported on $Y$; its local rings have no nonzero nilpotent elements. Each open subset $W$ is equipped with the restricted structure sheaf: for an open $A\subseteq W$, $\mathcal O_W(A)=\mathcal O_X(A)$, with the same restriction maps.

For the quotient notation, fix compatible nested universes $\mathscr U\in\mathscr V\in\mathscr W$, with $k,X$ and their open subschemes represented by $\mathscr U$-small data. Take $\mathcal C_k$ to be the full category of such $\mathscr U$-small qcqs $k$-schemes and all their $k$-morphisms. This is a $\mathscr V$-small category; use $\mathscr W$ for sheaf values and enlarge the supplied derived and spectral interfaces compatibly. Equip $\mathcal C_k$ with the topology generated by its Nisnevich covering families: étale maps for which each target point has a lift with isomorphic residue field. This is the size-bounded big Nisnevich site used here.

Let $h_X(T)=\operatorname{Hom}_k(T,X)$ and let $h_U(T)$ be the subset of maps whose image lies in $U$, for $T\in\mathcal C_k$. Such maps factor uniquely through the open subscheme $U$, even when $U$ is not an object of $\mathcal C_k$. Let $\mathbb Z[h_X]$ and $\mathbb Z[h_U]$ denote the associated abelian sheaves of the objectwise free abelian presheaves. Define the **quotient sheaf** $\mathbb Z[X/U]$ as their sheaf cokernel. Explicitly, it is the associated abelian sheaf of $T\mapsto\mathbb Z[h_X](T)/\operatorname{im}(\mathbb Z[h_U](T)\to\mathbb Z[h_X](T))$. Regard it when needed as a complex with this sheaf in degree zero, zero in every other degree and zero differential. This notation records the pair $(X,U)$; the support objects below are defined directly as fibers.

For each $j\geq0$, use the specified coherent motivic-complex functor $\widetilde C_j:\mathcal C_k^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal D(\mathbb Z)$ whose underlying homotopy-category values are $C_j(V)=\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(V)$. Fix the realization functor $\mathcal H$ of the coherent-realization interface and write $HC_j(V):=\mathcal H(\widetilde C_j(V))$. The entire restriction diagram is obtained by this composition, including all higher coherences; it is not obtained by choosing representatives of individual ordinary derived-category arrows.

The supplied functors are defined on qcqs schemes. To cover an arbitrary open $U$, use the following explicit extension convention. For any such open $W\subseteq X$, let $\mathcal Q(W)$ be the poset of quasi-compact open subsets of $W$. For $j\geq0$, put
$$F^jK(W):=\operatorname*{holim}_{V\in\mathcal Q(W)^{\mathrm{op}}}F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(V),\qquad K(W):=\operatorname*{holim}_{V\in\mathcal Q(W)^{\mathrm{op}}}K(V),$$
and put
$$M_j(W):=\operatorname*{holim}_{V\in\mathcal Q(W)^{\mathrm{op}}}HC_j(V).$$
The arrows in each inverse diagram are restrictions. Homotopy limits are coherent limits in the stable infinity-category of spectra. The poset $\mathcal Q(W)$ is small in the chosen universes. For an inclusion $W_1\subseteq W_2$, restrict the limiting cone along $\mathcal Q(W_1)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal Q(W_2)^{\mathrm{op}}$ and use its universal property to obtain the restriction to $W_1$; these maps include their coherent composition data. When $W$ is qcqs, its identity open is terminal in $\mathcal Q(W)$, and these expressions recover the supplied values. For arbitrary $W$, these formulas specify an extension; they do not assert its comparison with any independently constructed theory on non-qcqs schemes.

Define the **filtered support spectrum**, the **K-theory support fiber**, and the **motivic support spectrum** by
$$F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X/U):=\operatorname{fib}(F^jK(X)\to F^jK(U)),\quad K(X/U):=\operatorname{fib}(K(X)\to K(U)),$$
$$M_j(X/U):=\operatorname{fib}(M_j(X)\to M_j(U)).$$
A fiber is the homotopy pullback of the displayed map along the map from the zero spectrum. The compatible stage-to-zero maps give a map $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X/U)\to K(X/U)$. For every integer $i$ define
$$F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_{i,Y}(X):=\operatorname{im}\bigl(\pi_iF^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X/U)\to\pi_iK(X/U)\bigr).$$
The image is the subgroup consisting of values of this homomorphism. Define supported motivic cohomology for every integer $a$ by
$$H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z):=\pi_{-a}M_j(X/U).$$
Equivalently, define the shifted support spectrum $\mathbb Z(j)^{\mathrm{shift}}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X/U):=M_j(X/U)[2j]$. With this explicitly shifted meaning,
$$H^{2j-i,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z)=\pi_i\bigl(\mathbb Z(j)^{\mathrm{shift}}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X/U)\bigr).$$
The support-to-global maps are the first arrows of the defining fiber sequences. The image groups obtained after applying those maps are separate from the supported groups defined here.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** $k$, $X$, $Y$, $U$, $j\geq0$, and integers $i,a$ as above, with the stated choice and size conventions.

**Goal:** Justify the quotient notation, the support fibers and their maps, the image group, and the displayed indexing without assuming injectivity or a comparison with an independently constructed quotient evaluation.

[F1] [def-article-conventions](item-def-article-conventions) specifies the scheme category. [def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification](item-def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification) supplies the small-site and universe conventions; [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) supplies precisely the covering families and generated topology used above. [thm-adjudicate-d1c21a67502d-reduced-closed-subscheme-input](item-thm-adjudicate-d1c21a67502d-reduced-closed-subscheme-input) supplies the unique reduced closed structure on $Y$.
