[F2] [thm-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-abelian-sheaf-input](item-thm-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-abelian-sheaf-input) supplies associated abelian sheaves, free abelian sheaves on presheaves and the sheaf cokernel formula. [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme) supplies the open-subscheme structure by restricting the sheaf, and [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes) specifies its inclusion.

[F3] The assumed Axiom of Choice, [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice), discharges the Choice hypotheses of the supplied perfect-complex filtration and spectral realization. [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) supplies the coherent filtered restriction functor and stage-to-$K$ maps on qcqs schemes. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology) defines global $H^{a,j}=H^a(C_j)$; [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes) supplies its actual enhanced functor $\widetilde C_j$, including restrictions on the chosen category.

[F4] [lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization](item-lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization) supplies the fixed coherent $\mathcal H$, its natural formula $\pi_i\mathcal H(C)=H^{-i}(C)$ up to the specified isomorphism, and its compatibility with fibers and shifts. In particular it applies to whole enhanced motivic diagrams, not just individual complexes.

[F5] [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) supplies coherent limits, fibers, suspension and the abelian groups $\pi_i$. [lem-filtered-support-exactness](item-lem-filtered-support-exactness) supplies the stagewise support construction for a coherent filtered restriction diagram. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) supplies the integer spectral-shift index formula; [def-supported-complex-indexing](item-def-supported-complex-indexing) distinguishes unshifted support from its $2j$-shift.

[F6] [math:cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact) gives quasi-compactness of affine schemes under the already assumed Choice. [math:lem-distinguished-open-refinement-at-a-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-distinguished-open-refinement-at-a-point) refines an open neighbourhood inside an affine chart to a distinguished open. [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion) identifies that distinguished open, with its restricted sheaf, as an affine scheme. Quasi-separatedness has the affine-intersection meaning stated in [def-article-conventions](item-def-article-conventions).

### Justification
1.1 By [F1], the reduced support exists uniquely and the specified category and Nisnevich topology define a small site in the chosen larger universe. By [F2], a scheme map with image in $U$ factors uniquely through its restricted structure: the continuous map has this unique factorization, and on every open of $U$ the sheaf map is the original sheaf map on that same open of $X$. Its stalk maps remain local. Precomposition preserves this property. Thus $h_U\subseteq h_X$ is a subpresheaf and its free abelian sheaf map and cokernel exist with exactly the sheafified formula stated above. For each point of an open $W\subseteq X$, take an affine chart of $X$ and use [F6] to find a distinguished open inside its intersection with $W$. By [F6] this open, with its restricted sheaf, is affine. These neighbourhoods show that $W$ is a scheme and is covered by affine opens, each quasi-compact by [F6]. Any quasi-compact open $V\subseteq X$ is also quasi-separated: two affine opens of $V$ are affine opens of $X$, so their intersection is quasi-compact by quasi-separatedness of $X$. Hence each $V\in\mathcal Q(W)$ is in the supplied qcqs category. By [F3] and [F4], the filtered, underlying-$K$ and realized motivic diagrams are coherent, and their limits exist by [F5] in the chosen size bounds. If $W$ is quasi-compact, it is initial in $\mathcal Q(W)^{\mathrm{op}}$; a cone is then determined by its component at $W$, so the limit is naturally equivalent to the supplied value there. Restricting cones along the displayed inclusions gives the stated restriction maps. The universal property of limits gives their compatibility with composition, including higher coherences. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, given]

2.1 The coherent stage-to-$K$ maps in [F3] induce maps of the limiting restriction arrows. Taking their fibers gives the stated support spectra, stage augmentations and fiber projections, by [F5] and its stagewise support construction. On homotopy groups these are homomorphisms; their images are subgroups because $f(x)-f(y)=f(x-y)$. By [F5], $\pi_i(M_j(X/U)[2j])\cong\pi_{i-2j}M_j(X/U)=H^{2j-i,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z)$. By [F4], $\pi_{-a}M_j(X)\cong H^a(C_j(X))$ for qcqs $X$, naturally in its actual restriction arrows. Thus the fiber projection induces the degree-preserving support-to-global homomorphism with the stated global target. Realization preserves fibers, so for a qcqs complement this is also the realization of the unshifted enhanced cohomological support fiber, compatibly with its projection. The two indexing conventions remain distinct as in [F5]. No injectivity of a support-to-global map or independently constructed quotient-evaluation comparison is used. [F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎

### Source notation and scope
The article prints $H^{2j-i,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z):=\pi_i(\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(X/U))$ at Definition 2.7. The formula above specifies a $2j$-shift on the spectrum occurring on the right so that it is compatible with the cohomological convention of [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology). The unshifted realization instead has $\pi_i=H^{-i}$; the printed notation alone does not specify the comparison between these two conventions. The quotient sheaf and fiber definitions retain the article's pair and fiber sequences, but no identification of the displayed fibers with an independently defined evaluation on that sheaf is asserted here. In particular, $K(X/U)$ is not identified here with the K-theory of the category of perfect complexes supported on $Y$; such an identification needs a localization input with its own hypotheses.


## Euler class of a regular bundle functional

[Open item](item-lem-regular-zero-koszul-euler-class) · level 14


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fix suitable Grothendieck universes containing $\omega$ and the scheme data, with larger universes for the supplied K-theory categories. Their existence is a separate size assumption. Let $X$ be a quasi-compact, quasi-separated scheme, $E$ a finite locally free $\mathcal O_X$-module of constant rank $r\geq 0$, and $s:E\to\mathcal O_X$ an $\mathcal O_X$-linear functional. Let $I=\operatorname{im}(s)$ be its image sheaf, which is a quasi-coherent ideal, and let $i:Z=Z(s)\hookrightarrow X$ be the corresponding closed subscheme. In complexes on $X$, write $\mathcal O_Z$ for the module sheaf $i_*\mathcal O_Z\cong\mathcal O_X/I$. Suppose that at every point $x\in Z$, there is a local basis of $E$ whose germs form a basis of $E_x$ and whose values under $s_x$ form a regular sequence in $\mathcal O_{X,x}$ of length $r$: successively, each value is a non-zero-divisor modulo its predecessors and the final quotient is nonzero. For $r=0$ the sequence is empty and $Z=X$.

The Koszul complex $K(s)$ has $\Lambda^iE$ in cohomological degree $-i$, with differential given by contraction with $s$. It is a perfect complex supported on the underlying closed set of $Z$, and its augmentation is a quasi-isomorphism $K(s)\to\mathcal O_Z$. Define the supported Euler class $e_Z(s)\in K_{0,Z}(X)$ to be the K-zero class of this complex. Under the map induced by the inclusion of supported perfect complexes into all perfect complexes, it maps to

$$\lambda_{-1}(E)=\sum_{i=0}^r(-1)^i[\Lambda^iE]\in K_0(X).$$

For a section $t:\mathcal O_X\to E$, put $E^\vee=\mathcal H om_{\mathcal O_X}(E,\mathcal O_X)$. The corresponding functional is $t^\vee:E^\vee\to\mathcal O_X$, given by $\varphi\mapsto\varphi(t(1))$; under the same regularity hypothesis for its coordinates, the formula uses $E^\vee$ in place of $E$.

### Statement references

[def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles)

[def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes)

[def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface)

[math:thm-qc-ideal-closed-subscheme-correspondence-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-qc-ideal-closed-subscheme-correspondence-complete)

[math:lem-dual-locally-free-and-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-dual-locally-free-and-base-change)

[math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme)

[math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space)

[math:def-stalk-of-presheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-stalk-of-presheaf)

[math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme)

[math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** $X$, $E$, $s$ and the regularity hypothesis in the statement, together with choice and the stated universes.

**Goal:** Construct the supported class, prove the resolution and calculate its image in $K_0(X)$, including the section convention.

[A1] Choice and the universe assumptions are inherited through the sheaf and enhanced K-theory suppliers; the existence of the stated universes is assumed separately from Choice. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention)

[F1] Exterior powers of a finite locally free rank-$r$ module are finite locally free, vanish above degree $r$, and have the usual increasing-wedge bases locally. Their definition by sheafification allows restriction-compatible alternating local formulas to define sheaf maps. [def-exterior-determinant-bundles](item-def-exterior-determinant-bundles)

[F2] For a finite sequence in a commutative ring, the Koszul differential is the alternating deletion formula. If the sequence is regular, its Koszul complex on the ring is a finite free resolution of the quotient by the sequence. Homological degree $i$ is read here as cohomological degree $-i$. [math:def-koszul-complex-of-a-sequence-with-coefficients](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-koszul-complex-of-a-sequence-with-coefficients) [math:cor-koszul-complex-resolves-a-regular-quotient](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-koszul-complex-resolves-a-regular-quotient)

[F3] A bounded complex of finite locally free sheaves is perfect. A perfect complex is supported on a closed set precisely when its restriction to the complement is acyclic; the supported perfect objects form a stable idempotent-complete category. Its cofibers are inherited from the ambient derived enhancement, with cochain shift $A[1]^n=A^{n+1}$ and differential $-d_A$. [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes)

[F4] For qcqs $X$, the supplied K-theory assigns spectra to the perfect and supported perfect categories and a map to the ambient spectrum induced by inclusion. For an essentially small idempotent-complete stable category, its degree-zero group is naturally the group on object classes with the cofiber relations. Bundle classes agree with the object classes of the corresponding degree-zero complexes. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) [thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input](item-thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input) [def-vector-bundle-classes](item-def-vector-bundle-classes)

[F5] A module sheaf is quasi-coherent when it is locally the associated sheaf of a module on an affine chart. Images of morphisms of quasi-coherent sheaves are quasi-coherent; on an affine chart the image sheaf is the associated sheaf of the module image. [math:def-quasi-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-coherent-module-scheme) [math:thm-kernels-cokernels-qc-modules](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-kernels-cokernels-qc-modules)

[F6] Under AC, a quasi-coherent ideal $I\subseteq\mathcal O_X$ determines a closed immersion $i:Z\hookrightarrow X$ with underlying closed set $\{x:I_x\ne\mathcal O_{X,x}\}$ and canonical identification $i_*\mathcal O_Z\cong\mathcal O_X/I$. For $I=0$ it is the identity of $X$; for $I=\mathcal O_X$ it is empty. [math:thm-qc-ideal-closed-subscheme-correspondence-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-qc-ideal-closed-subscheme-correspondence-complete)

[F7] A sequence of sheaves is exact if and only if its sequence of stalks is exact at every point. Applied to kernel, image and quotient sequences, this identifies cohomology stalks with cohomology of stalk complexes and $(\mathcal O_X/I)_x$ with $\mathcal O_{X,x}/I_x$. [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

[F8] In the supplied stable derived enhancement of all $\mathcal O_X$-modules, the cone of a cochain map $f:A\to B$ is $\operatorname{Cone}(f)^n=B^n\oplus A^{n+1}$ with $d(b,a)=(d_Bb+f(a),-d_Aa)$. Its canonical cone square represents the cofiber square after localization at quasi-isomorphisms. [thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-4f716d60801a-derived-enhancement-input)

[F9] The sheaf dual $E^\vee=\mathcal H om(E,\mathcal O_X)$ has sections $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal O_U}(E|_U,\mathcal O_U)$ over $U$. It is finite locally free of the same rank as $E$, with the dual bases on trivializing charts. [math:lem-dual-locally-free-and-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-dual-locally-free-and-base-change)

[F10] A scheme is a locally ringed space covered by affine schemes. Module sheaves have linear section maps commuting with restriction; compatible local sections glue uniquely. Stalks are germs of sections over neighborhoods. Quasi-compact means that every open cover has a finite subcover; quasi-separated means that intersections of affine open subschemes are quasi-compact. [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) [math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space) [math:def-sheaf-on-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sheaf-on-topological-space) [math:def-stalk-of-presheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-stalk-of-presheaf) [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme)

### Proof
**Proof technique:** Construct the zero subscheme and Koszul differential locally, check exactness on stalks, and calculate the finite filtration using an explicit cone-to-quotient comparison.

1.1 On an affine open $U=\operatorname{Spec}A$ trivializing $E$, write $s$ as the row $(a_1,\ldots,a_r)$ and put $J=(a_1,\ldots,a_r)\subseteq A$. The free sheaves $E|_U$ and $\mathcal O_U$ are associated to $A^r$ and $A$: on distinguished opens their sections are $A_f^r$ and $A_f$. Thus both are quasi-coherent by [F5], and the image comparison in [F5] identifies $I|_U$ with $\widetilde J$ as a subsheaf of $\mathcal O_U$. In particular $I$ is a quasi-coherent ideal, locally generated by the $a_j$. These descriptions agree on overlaps because they are the image sheaf of the same global map. Now [F6] constructs $i:Z\hookrightarrow X$ and identifies $\mathcal O_X/I$ with $i_*\mathcal O_Z$. The stalk image is $\operatorname{im}(s_x)=I_x$, by [F7] applied to the kernel-image sequences of $s$. The quotient stalk is $\mathcal O_{X,x}/I_x$ by [F7]. When $r=0$, $I=0$ and $Z=X$. [A1, F1, F5, F6, F7, F10, construct]

2.1 For local sections $v_1,\ldots,v_i$ of $E$, define contraction by $d(v_1\wedge\cdots\wedge v_i)=\sum_{j=1}^i(-1)^{j-1}s(v_j)v_1\wedge\cdots\widehat{v_j}\cdots\wedge v_i$. This alternating, restriction-compatible formula induces a map on the exterior sheafification in [F1]; hence it gives a global map $\Lambda^iE\to\Lambda^{i-1}E$. In a local basis it is the differential in [F2], so $d^2=0$ locally and therefore globally by sheaf locality. The quotient map $\mathcal O_X\to\mathcal O_X/I=i_*\mathcal O_Z$ is an augmentation because its composite with $d:E\to\mathcal O_X$ is zero. [F1, F2, F10, step 1.1, construct]

3.1 At $x\in Z$, take the local basis in the regularity hypothesis. The local wedge bases in [F1] identify the stalk complex with the Koszul complex of the values of $s_x$. The regularity hypothesis and [F2] give cohomology only in degree zero, where the augmentation identifies it with $\mathcal O_{X,x}/I_x$. At $x\notin Z$, $I_x=\mathcal O_{X,x}$ by [F6], so there is $v\in E_x$ with $s_x(v)=1$. Exterior multiplication by $v$ contracts the stalk complex, since $d(v\wedge w)+v\wedge d(w)=w$; the quotient stalk is also zero. The augmented sequence is consequently exact at every stalk, and [F7] makes it exact as a sequence of sheaves. Equivalently its augmentation induces isomorphisms on every cohomology sheaf and is a quasi-isomorphism in [F3]. For $r=0$ it is the identity $\mathcal O_X\to\mathcal O_X$. [F1, F2, F3, F6, F7, F10, step 1.1, step 2.1, assume-hyp, construct]

3.2 In the ambient perfect category, put $F_mK(s)^n=K(s)^n$ for $n\geq m$ and zero for $n<m$, with the induced differential. For $-r\leq m\leq0$ there is a degreewise split short exact sequence $0\to A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{q}C\to0$, where $A=F_{m+1}K(s)$, $B=F_mK(s)$ and $C=K(s)^m$ placed in degree $m$ with zero differential. All three are bounded complexes of finite locally free sheaves. Define $p:\operatorname{Cone}(f)\to C$ by $p(b,a)=q(b)$. This is a cochain map and is degreewise surjective. Its kernel is canonically $A^n\oplus A^{n+1}$ in degree $n$, with differential $(u,v)\mapsto(d_Au+v,-d_Av)$ and contracting homotopy $h(u,v)=(0,u)$: direct substitution gives $dh+hd=1$. At any stalk, a cycle in $C$ lifts to an element of the cone; subtracting $h$ of its differential makes that lift a cycle. A cycle in the cone whose image is a boundary becomes a cycle in the kernel after subtracting the differential of a lift of that boundary's primitive, and this kernel cycle is a boundary by the same contraction. Thus $p$ induces cohomology isomorphisms on all stalks, so it is a quasi-isomorphism by [F7]. The cone cofiber in [F8] is therefore equivalent to $C$, and [F3] places this cofiber sequence in the ambient perfect category. [F1, F3, F7, F8, step 2.1, construct, algebra]

4.1 By [F1], $K(s)$ is bounded with finite locally free terms, so it is perfect. Step 3.1 shows it is acyclic away from $Z$; by [F3] it is an object of $\operatorname{Perf}_Z(X)$. Its object class in the degree-zero K-group is therefore well-defined by [F4], and this is the stated $e_Z(s)$. The construction uses the underlying closed set for support; it does not identify supported K-theory with coherent-sheaf G-theory. [A1, F1, F3, F4, step 3.1]

5.1 By [F4], each cofiber sequence of step 3.2 gives $[F_mK(s)]=[F_{m+1}K(s)]+[K(s)^m[-m]]$. The zero object's class is zero, using the cofiber relation for $0\to0\to0$. The cofiber sequence $A\to0\to A[1]$ from [F8] gives $[A[1]]=-[A]$. Starting with $F_1K(s)=0$ and iterating down to $F_{-r}K(s)=K(s)$ therefore yields $[K(s)]=\sum_{i=0}^r(-1)^i[\Lambda^iE]$, since the term in degree $-i$ is $\Lambda^iE[i]$. Naturality of the object-class map in [F4] sends $e_Z(s)$ to $[K(s)]$ under inclusion, proving the displayed formula. [F3, F4, F8, step 4.1, step 3.2, algebra]

6.1 By [F9], $E^\vee$ is finite locally free of the same rank. A section $t$ induces $t^\vee$ by evaluation: on each open set, $\varphi\mapsto\varphi(t(1))$ is linear and commutes with restriction, so defines a sheaf morphism by [F10]. Its values on a dual basis are the coordinates of $t$ in the original basis. Applying steps 1.1 through 5.1 to this functional on $E^\vee$ under the stated regularity hypothesis gives the section formula with $E^\vee$. [F9, F10, step 5.1, assume-hyp] ∎


## Postnikov inverse and the specified nonconnective degree-zero group

[Open item](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-postnikov) · level 14


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed suitable universes of the supplied supported perfect and nonconnective constructions. For regular Noetherian finite-dimensional $U$ and closed $T$, the coherent Euler map induces a canonical isomorphism
$$\pi_0K_{|T|}(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}G_0(\mathcal A_T).$$
Its inverse takes $[M]$ to the class of the actual perfect object $M[0]$. The same construction for $T=U$ gives $G_0(U)\simeq\pi_0K(U)$, with $G_0(U)$ the coherent-sheaf Grothendieck group. These comparisons commute with inclusion of supported categories.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fixed universes discharge the supported-heart and Euler constructions [F1] in step 1.1 and the essentially small nonconnective realization [F2] in step 2.1. The universes satisfy [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and allow the enlargements required by the supplied constructions. Their existence is a standing size assumption, separate from Choice. The finite truncation calculation introduces no additional choice.

**Given:** The regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme $U$, the closed subscheme $T$, AC and these fixed suitable universes; use the supported perfect category and the nonconnective functor of [F1]–[F2].

**Goal:** Establish the inverse by finite truncation triangles, then pass to the specified nonconnective degree-zero group.

[F1] [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart) supplies $\mathcal C_T=\operatorname{Perf}_{|T|}(U)$ with its inherited bounded cohomological t-structure and abelian heart $\mathcal A_T$, the coherent $\mathcal O_U$-modules whose stalks vanish outside $|T|$. It embeds $M\in\mathcal A_T$ as the actual perfect object $M[0]$ and turns heart short exact sequences into cofiber sequences. [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-euler](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-euler) supplies the homomorphism $\chi_T:K_0^\triangle(h\mathcal C_T)\to G_0(\mathcal A_T)$, its value $\chi_T([M[0]])=[M]$, the essential smallness of both hearts, and the supported/global Euler compatibility. Its construction also checks that Noetherian $U$ is qcqs, as required in [F2].

[F2] [thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input](item-thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input) identifies the object/cofiber Grothendieck group of every small idempotent-complete stable category with $\pi_0$ of the BGT nonconnective functor, by object classes and naturally for exact functors. [lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation](item-lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation) identifies the cofiber and triangle groups and supplies the induced triangulated homotopy category. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes the essentially small idempotent-complete common-family realizations, their exact support inclusions, and this same nonconnective functor; its supported notation is $K_{|T|}(U)=\mathbb K(\mathcal C_T)$.

[F3] [math:def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-canonical-t-structure-on-a-derived-category) fixes the t-structure axioms and heart. [math:def-truncation-functors-and-cohomology-of-a-t-structure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-truncation-functors-and-cohomology-of-a-t-structure) fixes the truncation triangles, the convention $H_t^n(E)=(\tau_{\le n}\tau_{\ge n}E)[n]$, and boundedness. Applied to $h\mathcal C_T$ with the t-structure supplied in [F1], [math:lem-truncation-triangles-are-essentially-unique-and-functorial](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-truncation-triangles-are-essentially-unique-and-functorial) gives the canonical truncation maps, the aisle criteria in (iii), and the iteration isomorphisms in (iv). [math:lem-t-cohomology-is-additive-shift-compatible-and-detects-amplitude](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-t-cohomology-is-additive-shift-compatible-and-detects-amplitude) gives shift compatibility in (ii), amplitude detection for a bounded t-structure in (iv), and the concentration isomorphisms in (v).

[F4] [math:lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-canonical-truncation-is-a-complex-and-has-the-claimed-cohomology) supplies the retained/zero cohomology identities for the ordinary derived truncations. These are the ambient cohomological truncations restricted in [F1]. Thus $H_t^n(E)$ is the sheaf $H^n(E)$ in the heart: its defining double truncation has only the ordinary cohomology $H^n(E)$ in degree $n$, and shifting by $[n]$ puts that sheaf in degree zero. In particular $H^n(\tau_{\le n}E)=H^n(E)$. All the truncation objects belong to $\mathcal C_T$ by [F1].

[F5] [math:def-triangulated-grothendieck-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-grothendieck-group) and [math:def-grothendieck-group-of-an-essentially-small-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-an-essentially-small-abelian-category) specify respectively the triangle and short-exact group presentations used here, on the essentially small categories supplied in [F1]–[F2]. [math:prop-zero-and-split-triangles-are-distinguished](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-zero-and-split-triangles-are-distinguished) supplies the distinguished triangle $A\to0\to A[1]\to A[1]$ in $h\mathcal C_T$.

[F6] The geometric formulation uses [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme), [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point), [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes), [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space) and [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme). Here $U$ is a scheme regular at every point, $T$ is a closed subscheme with underlying closed set $|T|$, and dimension is chain dimension, with the supplied empty-space convention. Regularity is absolute local-ring regularity, without a base-field smoothness assumption. These are exactly the geometric hypotheses and coherent objects of [F1].

### Proof
1.1 By the short-exact/cofiber assertion in [F1], the rule $[M]\mapsto[M[0]]$ respects every short exact relation of [F5] and defines $j_T:G_0(\mathcal A_T)\to K_0^\triangle(h\mathcal C_T)$. The value of $\chi_T$ in [F1] gives $\chi_Tj_T=1$ on the generating sheaf classes. For $E\in\mathcal C_T$, boundedness gives a finite cohomological range $[a,b]$; amplitude detection in [F3] puts $E$ in $\mathcal C_T^{\ge a}\cap\mathcal C_T^{\le b}$. The aisle criteria therefore give $\tau_{\le a-1}E\simeq0$ and $\tau_{\le b}E\simeq E$. For each $a\le n\le b$, apply the truncation triangle at $n-1$ to $Y=\tau_{\le n}E$. Iteration in [F3] identifies its first term with $\tau_{\le n-1}E$. Since $Y\in\mathcal C_T^{\le n}$, concentration in [F3] identifies its third term $\tau_{\ge n}Y$ with $H_t^n(Y)[-n]\simeq H^n(E)[-n]$, using [F4]. This produces the distinguished triangles $\tau_{\le n-1}E\to\tau_{\le n}E\to H^n(E)[-n]\to(\tau_{\le n-1}E)[1]$ in $h\mathcal C_T$. All their objects lie in $\mathcal C_T$ by [F1] and [F4]. The zero triangle of [F5], first for $A=0$, gives $[0]=0$, and then gives $[A[1]]=-[A]$; iteration in either direction gives $[A[m]]=(-1)^m[A]$ for every integer $m$. Summing the finitely many triangle relations from the two endpoints yields $[E]=\sum_{n=a}^b(-1)^n[H^n(E)[0]]=j_T\chi_T([E])$. Thus $j_T\chi_T=1$ on generators and $\chi_T$ and $j_T$ are inverse homomorphisms. [A1, F1, F3, F4, F5, F6, construct, algebra]

2.1 Apply [F2] to an equivalent small presentation of $\mathcal C_T$, which is stable and idempotent complete by that supplied realization. Its cofiber/triangle identification followed by the degree-zero comparison gives the object-class isomorphism $\alpha_T:K_0^\triangle(h\mathcal C_T)\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_0K_{|T|}(U)$. Equivalent presentations preserve the objects and cofiber relations, and the naturality in [F2] makes the resulting map independent of their presentation. Compose $\chi_T\alpha_T^{-1}$; by step 1.1 this is exactly the displayed Euler isomorphism, with inverse $\alpha_Tj_T$ sending $[M]$ to the spectral class of the actual object $M[0]$. Taking $T=U$ gives the same comparison with $G_0(U)$ for the coherent heart $\mathcal A_U=\operatorname{Coh}(U)$. The exact support inclusion in [F2] induces the corresponding triangle and spectral homomorphisms, and forgetting support leaves each complex and cohomology sheaf unchanged. Naturality of $\alpha$ in [F2] and the supported/global compatibility of $\chi$ in [F1] therefore give the asserted commutative support/global square. Positive-degree comparison alone is not used. [A1, F1, F2, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## External actual augmented EM and smooth slice tower comparison

[Open item](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) · level 14


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds for the selected perfect-complex nonconnective $K$-theory and motivic-filtration realizations. Let $F$ be a prime field, meaning $\mathbb Q$ or $\mathbb F_p$ for a prime $p$. Use the nonnegative convention $F^jE=E$ with identity transitions for $j\leq0$. On smooth $F$-schemes in the qcqs domain, the actual natural augmented filtered comparison is
$$\alpha_X:F^\bullet_{\mathrm{cla}}K(X)\longrightarrow F^\bullet_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X).$$
It is an equivalence, its stage-zero map is the identity of $K(X)$, and its graded maps are exactly EM (4.9). Thus it commutes with the actual stage-to-layer maps.

For $X$ smooth of finite type over an arbitrary extension field $k/F$, $X$ is regular Noetherian and qcqs. The actual natural comparison
$$\beta_X:F^\bullet_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)\longrightarrow F^\bullet_{\mathrm{cdh}}KH(X)$$
is an augmented filtered equivalence over $K(X)\to KH(X)$. Here $KH$ is homotopy invariant $K$-theory. EM Remark 3.6 identifies the target with the tower obtained from the slice filtration of the representing motivic spectrum $KGL$. The comparison therefore identifies augmentations and induced cofibers, rather than only cohomology groups. The source-version qualification in External input is part of this contract.

A supported pair here is an open immersion $U\hookrightarrow X$, with closed complement $T=X\setminus U$, for which both $X$ and $U$ belong to the common qcqs domain of the selected comparison. For either tower $F^\bullet E$, with $E=K$ or $KH$ as appropriate, define
$$F^j_TE(X)=\operatorname{fib}(F^jE(X)\to F^jE(U)),\qquad E_T(X)=\operatorname{fib}(E(X)\to E(U)).$$
For every integer $j$ these form an augmented tower on $E_T(X)$, with $F^0_TE(X)\simeq E_T(X)$ and
$$\operatorname{gr}^j_TE(X):=\operatorname{cofib}(F^{j+1}_TE(X)\to F^j_TE(X))\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{gr}^jE(X)\to\operatorname{gr}^jE(U)).$$
The natural comparison induces this supported tower map, commuting with augmentations, transitions and layer projections. It is an equivalence when the comparison is an equivalence at both $X$ and $U$: for $\alpha$, both are smooth $F$-schemes; for $\beta$, both are regular Noetherian $F$-schemes. In particular the latter applies when both are smooth finite-type $k$-schemes for any $k/F$. The notation $K_T$ in this statement means the restriction fiber; identification with $K$-theory of supported perfect complexes requires a separate localization theorem.

The EM first-Chern class is precisely the natural map of Lemma 5.1/Definition 5.2
$$c_1:R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(-,\mathbb G_m)[-1]\longrightarrow\mathbb Z(1)_{\mathrm{mot}},$$
defined by the weight-one map (4.9) on smooth $F$-schemes. In the projective-line calculation, the canonical filtered lift of $1-[\mathcal O(-1)]$ has graded image $+c_1(\mathcal O(1))$, as in Construction 5.11/Lemma 5.12. The brackets denote the $K_0$ class of the tautological line. Supported comparison maps are the fibers of the specified natural maps with the same triangle convention; no extra sign is inserted. Compatibility with another theory's independently presented Picard map is a separate assertion.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The fields, schemes and open pair in the Statement, AC and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds.

**Goal:** Apply the precise imported tower maps on their actual domains and construct their supported comparisons with the specified normalization.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) discharges the inherited realization premises of [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) and [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration). The same fixed universes and site bounds are used for every restriction diagram. These are local construction premises, not additional geometric hypotheses attributed to EM.

[F1] Reference 0 imports the actual maps $\alpha$ and $\beta$, their augmentations, graded maps and equivalence loci. The classical tower, cdh tower and slice identification are the specific constructions named there; none is replaced by an arbitrary graded-group identification.

[F2] [math:def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical) gives regular local rings on a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme by applying its geometric-regularity criterion to the trivial extension $k/k$. Here regular means regularity of all local rings. [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism) supplies an affine cover by finite-type $k$-algebras. [math:lem-field-is-noetherian](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-field-is-noetherian) and [math:cor-finite-type-algebra-over-noetherian-ring-is-noetherian](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-finite-type-algebra-over-noetherian-ring-is-noetherian) make those algebras Noetherian. [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) defines a Noetherian scheme as a quasi-compact scheme with such a cover. [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism) makes $X$ quasi-compact by evaluating the quasi-compact structure morphism over the one-point affine base. Under AC, [math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum) and [math:lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact) make every open in each Noetherian affine chart quasi-compact. [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme) defines qcqs by quasi-compactness of $X$ and of intersections of affine opens; separatedness is not required.

[F3] [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) defines the coherent fibers and cofibers in a stable infinity-category. [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum) defines augmented decreasing towers and the cofiber convention for graded pieces. [lem-filtered-support-exactness](item-lem-filtered-support-exactness) supplies the natural supported tower and its graded restriction-fiber sequence, without any descent or support-category localization assumption.

[F4] Reference 0 also imports the actual first-Chern map and canonical reduced-line lift in the Statement. In this notation $\mathbb G_m$ is the units sheaf, $R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}$ means derived sections for the Nisnevich topology, and $\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}[2j]=\operatorname{gr}^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K$. [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) supplies the Nisnevich topology: covers are étale families lifting points with their residue fields. [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) supplies derived sections on the chosen bounded sites, using the stated AC and universe premises. The imported map uses the site convention of EM Section 2.1; no identification of different sites is inferred from their names. The induced $H^2$ map is defined on the group of isomorphism classes of line bundles.

### External input
Reference 0 supplies the classical slice tower of Section 3.1, the cdh $KH$ tower of Theorem 3.5, the comparison maps of Constructions 4.34 and 4.36, and their equivalence assertions in Theorem 6.1(2)/Remark 6.3 and Corollary 6.4. Here the $KGL$ slice tower is the tower of effective covers of the motivic spectrum representing $KH$, evaluated as in Remark 3.6. Its existence and identification with the cdh tower are imported, rather than derived from the abstract stable-spectra interface. The first-Chern construction and reduced-line normalization are imported from Lemma 5.1/Definition 5.2 and Lemmas 5.10–5.12.

EM v1 states the cdh/slice identification in Remark 3.6 and attributes its proof to then-in-preparation [7]; that proof is not supplied or reconstructed here. Corollary 6.4 alone is not asserted over arbitrary $k$. The arbitrary-field application uses the regular-Noetherian EM-to-cdh map and the stated slice identification. The following local proof establishes the geometric application and support-fiber consequences of these precise external premises, without claiming a new foundational tower comparison.

### Proof
1.1 By [A1], interpret [F1] in the chosen coherent $K$ and motivic models. On the smooth prime-field domain, [F1] gives $\alpha$, its identity at stage zero, its equivalence and its precise graded maps. For a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme, [F2] gives regularity by taking $k/k$ in the smoothness criterion. Its finite-type affine cover consists of spectra of Noetherian rings, and it is quasi-compact, so it is a Noetherian scheme by [F2]. To check quasi-separatedness, take a finite subcover $V_1,\ldots,V_m$ of these affine charts using quasi-compactness (the empty case has the empty cover). For any open $W\subseteq X$, each $W\cap V_a$ is quasi-compact by the Noetherian-spectrum and open-compactness inputs of [F2]. An open cover of $W$ therefore has a finite subfamily covering each $W\cap V_a$; their finite union covers $W$. Thus every open of $X$ is quasi-compact, in particular each intersection of affine opens, and $X$ is qcqs by [F2]. Viewing it over $F$ changes no local ring or underlying scheme. Therefore [F1] applies to $\beta$ without any finite-generation assumption on $k/F$, giving the augmented filtered equivalence and the stated source-qualified slice identification. The imported normalization [F4] fixes the reduced-line sign before any support construction. [given, A1, F1, F2, F4]

2.1 Apply [F3] to each coherent restriction arrow on the supported pair. It produces exactly the displayed supported tower and graded fiber sequence, including their natural augmentation and layer projections. Naturality of $\alpha$ or $\beta$ gives a map between these restriction diagrams. If its maps at $X$ and $U$ are equivalences at every stage and at the augmentation, the diagrams defining their fibers are equivalent; the universal property of a limit then gives equivalences of their supported fibers. Applying the same cofiber construction identifies the supported layers and the actual stage-to-layer squares. Stage zero follows by taking the fibers of the stage-zero identifications on both terms. This uses equivalence at both terms, including in the smooth finite-type $k$ case of step 1.1. The construction retains the specified natural maps and the cofiber convention in [F3], so it introduces no independent sign choice into [F4]'s normalization and makes no support-category localization assertion. [F1, F3, F4, step 1.1] ∎


## The actual graded determinant map on the current K-space

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-actual-determinant-stack-map) · level 14



### Statement
Assume AC and the fixed compatible universes. For every qcqs scheme $X$ there is a natural pointed map from its specified nonconnective K-space to the stack of graded lines, whose value on a perfect complex $E$ is its Knudsen–Mumford graded determinant and whose value on an object automorphism is its determinant automorphism. Restricting to the locally constant rank-zero fiber gives the actual map to $B\mathbb G_m$. On vector bundles it is the frame-torsor map induced by $\det:GL_n\to\mathbb G_m$; on locally bounded finite-projective representatives it is the alternating graded determinant. Its additivity uses enhanced cofiber sequences and their specified diagrams; the induced class in $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ kills every cofiber relation. No equality of global vector-bundle and perfect K zero, or arbitrary ordinary triangle-diagram functoriality, is assumed.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The stated model hypotheses, AC and fixed universe bounds containing the data and $\omega$, with larger compatible universes for the categories. Qcqs means quasi-compact and quasi-separated, as in [F2]; a perfect complex is locally equivalent to a bounded finite locally free complex. The point of its K-space is the zero complex.

**Goal:** Supply the actual determinant map required by the consumers, with exactly the recorded coherence.

[F1] [thm-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-waldhausen-determinant-one-type](item-thm-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-waldhausen-determinant-one-type) supplies the exact determinant/one-type bridge.

[F2] [lem-author-4f716d60801a-finite-projective-model](item-lem-author-4f716d60801a-finite-projective-model), [thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons](item-thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons) and [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fix the actual affine model, object maps and natural connective comparison.

[F3] The graded-line groupoid $\mathcal G(T)$ is the one in [F1]: pairs $(n,L)$ with $n:T\to\mathbb Z$ locally constant, $L$ a line bundle, and line isomorphisms preserving $n$, with tensor degree addition and symmetry $(-1)^{nm}$. [lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors](item-lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors) identifies line bundles with their frame torsors and defines $\operatorname{Pic}(T)$ and $\mathbb G_m$. Effective and fully faithful descent of quasi-coherent modules, descent of finite local freeness, and the fpqc property of jointly surjective étale families are supplied directly by [lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-fpqc-descent-input](item-lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-fpqc-descent-input). These are groupoid descent inputs, beyond a bijection of Picard classes.

[F4] Use compatible Grothendieck universes as in [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), separately assumed to exist and contain the data. Work on a sufficiently large but small-in-the-next-universe full category $\mathcal C$ of qcqs schemes and all their scheme morphisms, with the topology generated by the Nisnevich families of [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface). Include the affine opens and the fibre products and open refinements used below. The sieve and size conventions are those of [def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification](item-def-scheme-sites-and-sheafification). Write $\mathcal K(T)=\Omega^\infty K(T)$ for the coherent presheaf of [F2]. The precise HTT input in External input supplies space-valued sheafification $a_{\mathrm{Nis}}$, its natural unit, and its mapping universal property. Set-valued sheafification alone is insufficient here. On a fixed qcqs $X$, the same constructions restrict to its small Nisnevich site with qcqs étale objects and affine open refinements; over a fixed base one may also restrict to qcqs smooth objects. All restrictions use this same Nisnevich convention.

[F5] [math:thm-etale-morphisms-open-and-quasi-finite](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-etale-morphisms-open-and-quasi-finite) supplies openness of étale maps under AC. [math:lem-points-of-scheme-fibre-product-residue-tensors](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-points-of-scheme-fibre-product-residue-tensors) supplies points of overlaps via residue-field tensor products. [math:lem-etale-stable-base-change-composition](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-etale-stable-base-change-composition) supplies étale stability under base change and composition under AC. These give the covering-family and locally constant integer grading checks in step 1.2.

[F6] [lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-enhancement-comparison](item-lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-enhancement-comparison) supplies the single localized K-flat family whose perfect part is used by [F2]. [lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-derived-tensor-pullback](item-lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-derived-tensor-pullback), Proof 1.2 and 4.1, supplies the canonical natural chain isomorphism $f^*\widetilde P\cong\widetilde{B\otimes_A P}$ and verifies K-flatness for bounded finite-projective $P$. [lem-author-4f716d60801a-lifted-affine-equivalence](item-lem-author-4f716d60801a-lifted-affine-equivalence) supplies the actual associated-sheaf equivalence on the localized perfect fibers. These are the comparison functors, not independently chosen identifications of abstract equivalent categories.

[F7] [thm-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-enhanced-localization-input](item-thm-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-enhanced-localization-input) supplies the relative-category, pseudofunctor, Grothendieck-construction and cocartesian-family conventions, and functorial localization with its natural unit. In particular a coherent transformation of diagrams of marked categories induces a transformation of their localized diagrams. Its size and saturation conditions apply in step 2.1; no new localization theorem is inferred from a fiberwise equivalence alone.

[F8] Source reference 1 supplies the restriction-square criterion for a groupoid object in spaces and effectiveness of such objects. For a reduced groupoid object, its canonical map from degree one to the based loop space of its realization is an equivalence. Realization here is the colimit of the simplicial diagram in spaces. Steps 1.3, 2.3 and 3.1 construct the particular simplicial groupoids, their determinant maps and their affine coherence before using that theorem. The Picard, symmetric tensor, determinant and Waldhausen S-construction conventions are those explicitly supplied by [F1]; higher-category and coherent-diagram conventions are those of [F7].

[F9] Source reference 2 supplies the S-construction comparison at the level of actual gap diagrams. The natural initial-stage map to the specified nonconnective functor and its object/automorphism normalization are those of [F2]. All affine localizations here are stable and idempotent complete by [F1], so the comparison is an equivalence on the whole connective cover. Step 4.1 checks coherence with the particular pullback diagram of [F6].

[F10] Source reference 3 supplies recognition of equivalences in a functor infinity-category by their vertex components. We use it only after constructing the transformations of diagrams, in order to invert the target delooping comparison in step 3.1 and the single affine K comparison in step 4.1.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is used through the enhanced realizations and small presentations of [F1]–[F2], the torsor supplier in [F3], and étale openness and stability in [F5]. The same AC and universe bounds are retained for the common-family localization in [F6]–[F7] and the small simplicial diagrams in [F8]–[F9]. Universe existence is the separate assumption of [F4], not a consequence of AC.

### External input
Import the covering-sieve definition of sheaves of spaces and space-valued sheafification, its unit and mapping universal property from [HTT Definition 6.2.2.6 and Proposition 6.2.2.7](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HTT.pdf), with the source record's hypotheses and reading limits. Import the effective-groupoid input [F8] at source reference 1, the S-construction comparison [F9] at source reference 2, and the equivalence criterion [F10] at source reference 3, with their recorded hypotheses and reading limits. The affine reconstruction is proved in steps 1.2 and 2.2. The interval-label construction, its determinant map and its affine compatibility in steps 1.3, 2.3 and 3.1 are local arguments. They replace the predecessor’s unsupported coherent crossed-complex comparison; no such comparison is assumed. The full lemma is a locally proved composition of the explicit imports and declared suppliers, not an imported statement.

### Proof
1.1 On $\operatorname{Spec}A$ use $\mathcal W(A)=Ch^b(\operatorname{proj}A)$, with quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and degreewise split monomorphisms as cofibrations. Mapping cylinders give factorization; quasi-isomorphisms between bounded projectives have contractible cones, so saturation holds. The completed enhancement comparison identifies its infinity-localization with the supplied $\operatorname{Perf}(A)$. It is idempotent complete. Hence [F1] and [F2] give the actual determinant map on the connective cover of the current K-spectrum and its space. [F1, F2]

1.2 The Nisnevich families give a covering-family presentation: identities cover; residue-field-preserving lifts compose; and a lift of $x$ base-changes to a lift of any point $v$ over $x$ because its residue tensor is $\kappa(x)\otimes_{\kappa(x)}\kappa(v)\cong\kappa(v)$. Étaleness is preserved in these operations by [F5]. Zariski open covers are included by [F4]. The target $\mathcal G$ is a stack for this presentation. For a Nisnevich family $\{T_i\to T\}$, [F3] descends compatible lines and their isomorphisms effectively and fully faithfully. The descended quasi-coherent module is finite locally free; its rank is one since pullback to the jointly surjective family has rank one: on an affine chart where the descended module is free of rank $r$, its pullback is free of rank $r$, by $B\otimes_A A^r\cong B^r$; a point of the covering above each target point forces $r=1$. For compatible integer gradings $n_i$, a residue-field-preserving lift $u$ of $x\in T$ and any $v$ over $x$ have a common point on their overlap: [F5] identifies its residue tensor with $\kappa(x)\otimes_{\kappa(x)}\kappa(v)\cong\kappa(v)$. Compatibility therefore makes their integer values equal. Thus the $n_i$ define a unique function $n:T\to\mathbb Z$ with the prescribed pullbacks. Each degree locus is the union of images of the corresponding open degree loci in the $T_i$, so it is open by [F5]; hence $n$ is locally constant. The empty scheme has its unique grading and line up to unique isomorphism. Tensor, unit and sign symmetry descend with these data: their local formulas are preserved by pullback, and full faithfulness makes their compatibility equalities global. Taking nerves gives a sheaf of 1-truncated spaces, still denoted $\mathcal G$: for a sieve containing a covering family, a coherent family over that sieve supplies the preceding descent datum. Its descent object identifies with every member of the sieve after pulling back the cover; full faithfulness glues those identifications and their equations uniquely. Thus the covering-sieve condition of [F4] holds, as well as covering-family descent. [F1, F3, F4, F5, A1]

1.3 For a Picard groupoid $\mathcal P$ with unit $\mathbf 1$, define $T_n\mathcal P$ as follows. An object consists of labels $p_{ij}$ for $0\leq i\leq j\leq n$, with $p_{ii}=\mathbf 1$, and isomorphisms $\mu_{ijk}:p_{jk}\otimes p_{ij}\to p_{ik}$. For $i\leq j\leq k\leq l$, the two composites from $p_{kl}\otimes p_{jk}\otimes p_{ij}$ to $p_{il}$ agree after the specified associator. Repeated-index maps are the prescribed unitors. A morphism is a family of label isomorphisms, identity on repeated indices, commuting with every $\mu$. Precomposition by an order-preserving map of finite ordinals defines the simplicial operators; repeated vertices use the unit labels and unitors. The monoidal triangle and pentagon identities make these operators well defined, including on repeated-index tetrahedra, and precomposition makes their identities exact. Thus $T_\bullet\mathcal P$ is a simplicial groupoid with $T_0\mathcal P=*$ and $T_1\mathcal P=\mathcal P$. Restriction to consecutive edges is an equivalence $T_n\mathcal P\to\mathcal P^n$: extend labels by ordered parenthesized tensor products; associators give the $\mu$, and their pentagon gives the tetrahedron equations. Conversely those equations identify any interval label with this product, and uniquely extend each family of consecutive-edge isomorphisms. More generally restriction to two subsets of vertices meeting at a single vertex $s$ is an equivalence to the product of their label groupoids. To see essential surjectivity, regard each given label as an arrow from its first vertex to its second, with composition in the displayed tensor order. Choose inverse labels for the edges incident to $s$, and label each missing edge by the composite through $s$. Tensoring by any label is an equivalence, so the cancellation isomorphisms can be chosen with the triangle identities; associativity and cancellation give all required tetrahedron equations. The original triangle data identify this extension with the given labels on each subset. For full faithfulness, an isomorphism on the two subsets determines the missing-edge isomorphisms uniquely from their triangles through $s$, since tensoring is fully faithful; their remaining compatibilities follow from the tetrahedron equations. This argument includes $s$ outside the interval of a missing edge by solving its triangle through the same tensor equivalence. Nerves therefore satisfy the restriction-square criterion in [F8], since the common-vertex groupoid is terminal. Put $B_\otimes\mathcal P=|NT_\bullet\mathcal P|$. Effectiveness supplies a canonical equivalence $\eta_{\mathcal P}:N\mathcal P\to\Omega B_\otimes\mathcal P$. This uses the target's explicit groupoid object, with no comparison to a crossed-complex model. [F1, F7, F8, construct]

2.1 For every ring map $A\to B$, termwise tensor takes finite projectives to finite projectives and preserves split cofibrations, their pushouts and contractible cones, so it is exact on $\mathcal W(A)$. Use the actual models in compatible larger universes before making any small presentations. For $A\to B\to C$, the comparison $C\otimes_B(B\otimes_A P)\cong C\otimes_A P$ sends $c\otimes(b\otimes p)$ to $cb\otimes p$. Its units and associativity equations hold on elementary tensors, hence on all modules and chain maps. Direct-sum comparisons are the canonical maps on the summands; their unit and associativity equations hold on the inclusions of each summand. These are the coproduct comparisons retained in the S-construction and Picard model. These define a pseudofunctor of relative categories, not just a list of ringwise functors. Quasi-isomorphism markings are saturated because they are inverse images of cohomology isomorphisms; tensor preserves them by the contraction argument. The relative associated-sheaf functors into the K-flat perfect family are connected by the canonical chain isomorphisms in [F6]. Their composition equation also holds before localization: for $A\to B\to C$, both comparisons send the pulled-back elementary tensor to $cb\otimes p$; identity comparisons give $1\otimes p\mapsto p$. Naturality in module maps makes these chain comparisons commute with every differential and with split cofiber diagrams. Thus the Grothendieck constructions give a map of relative families preserving cocartesian arrows. Over invertible base maps the transports are relative equivalences, and the other marked-family conditions in [F7] follow from the preceding marking checks. Equivalently apply its localization functor to this whole transformation of marked diagrams. This gives a homotopy-coherent natural transformation $e:\mathcal W(-)_\infty\to\widehat{\operatorname{Perf}}(\operatorname{Spec}(-))$; its components are exact equivalences by [F6]. The target is exactly the restriction of the specified common family, not an unrelated pullback diagram. [F1, F2, F6, F7, A1, step 1.1]

2.2 For $X\in\mathcal C$, let $\mathcal A_X$ be the category of all affine schemes $U$ in $\mathcal C$ equipped with any morphism $U\to X$, and triangles over $X$ as morphisms. Here the homotopy limit of this groupoid diagram means the nerve of the groupoid of objects $g_U\in\mathcal G(U)$ and isomorphisms $h^*g_U\cong g_V$ for every $h:V\to U$ over $X$, satisfying identity and composition equations with the canonical pullback comparisons; morphisms are compatible families of isomorphisms. There is a natural equivalence $\mathcal G(X)\simeq\operatorname{holim}_{\mathcal A_X^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal G(U)$. Indeed a coherent compatible family on the right gives graded lines on an affine open cover of $X$. On an overlap, cover again by affine opens: the family's comparison isomorphisms identify the two restrictions there. Full faithfulness in step 1.2 glues these isomorphisms, and their cocycle equations hold because they hold on affine refinements. Effectivity glues a graded line on $X$. For any affine $U\to X$, cover $U$ by affine opens mapping into the chosen affine opens of $X$; the same comparisons identify its prescribed line with the pullback of the glued line. These identifications are compatible with all triangles over $X$. Morphisms glue uniquely by full faithfulness. This constructs an inverse equivalence of groupoids, hence of their nerves, naturally in $X$. Only groupoid descent is used: intersections of affine opens of $X$ need not be affine, and no comparison theorem for arbitrary untruncated sheaves on an intersection-unstable basis is asserted. [F3, F4, step 1.2]

2.3 Let $d_A:w\mathcal W(A)\to\mathcal G(\operatorname{Spec}A)$ be the bounded-complex KM determinant, normalized at the zero complex by its canonical unit line. For a filtration with quotient diagram $(P_{ij})$ in $S_n\mathcal W(A)$, assign $p_{ij}=d_A(P_{ij})$ and assign to $\mu_{ijk}$ its determinant additivity for the actual cofiber sequence $P_{ij}\hookrightarrow P_{ik}\to P_{jk}$. The determinant convention in [F1] has exactly the order $d_A(P_{jk})\otimes d_A(P_{ij})\to d_A(P_{ik})$. Staircase associativity is the tetrahedron equation; unit normalization gives the repeated-index equations. An objectwise weak equivalence of quotient diagrams gives the label isomorphisms by $d_A$; determinant naturality says precisely that they commute with $\mu$. Thus this defines a functor $q_{A,n}:wS_n\mathcal W(A)\to T_n\mathcal G(\operatorname{Spec}A)$, not merely an assignment on isomorphism classes. Restricting or repeating indices restricts or repeats exactly those quotient and determinant diagrams, so the $q_{A,n}$ form a simplicial functor. Its degree-zero map is the unique pointed map, its degree-one map is $d_A$, and its degree-two data are exactly the given additivity isomorphisms. All constructions remain in the chosen compatible universes. [F1, F2, F8, A1, step 1.1, step 1.3, construct]

3.1 First normalize each strong symmetric tensor functor $F$ at the unit: replace $F(\mathbf 1)$ by the target unit, and conjugate its arrow and tensor maps by its unit isomorphism at that object. This produces an isomorphic strictly unital tensor functor. Normalize tensor natural isomorphisms by the same conjugations; their component at the unit is then identity. Retain the induced composition comparisons between normalized functors, rather than asserting that normalization commutes strictly with composition. Their coherence equations follow by conjugation from the original equations. The construction $T_\bullet$ now acts on these normalized functors: apply the functor to labels and compose its tensor comparisons with the images of $\mu$. Strict unitality makes this commute with repeating indices, including off-diagonal labels created by a repetition. The tensor coherence equations preserve the tetrahedron and unit equations. A tensor natural isomorphism acts componentwise on labels, and its defining tensor and unit equations make it a morphism of these simplicial groupoids. For $A\to B$, the KM base-change isomorphisms give a natural isomorphism between $q_{B,\bullet}S_\bullet(B\otimes_A-)$ and $T_\bullet(g_{AB})q_{A,\bullet}$, where $g_{AB}$ is pullback of graded lines. They commute with every $\mu$ by KM's base-change compatibility with actual cofiber diagrams. For $A\to B\to C$, the two composites agree on every label and every additivity map by KM's pullback-composition law and the tensor comparisons checked in step 2.1; their identity equations hold as well. These are pseudonatural maps over the product of the affine index category and the simplicial index category. The Grothendieck-construction convention of [F7] therefore gives transformations of the whole coherent diagrams. Taking nerves and geometric realizations is performed on these diagrams. Explicitly, for ordinary category-valued diagrams, a string of natural transformations gives a functor on the product with $[m]$; its nerve is a map on the product with $\Delta^m$. Restricting, composing or inserting identities in the string gives its faces and degeneracies. The tensor-composition equations just checked are the equations needed to assemble these prisms over the affine base. Thus their comparison simplices are present before realization. The natural augmentation defining $\eta$ in step 1.3 commutes with these same maps and isomorphisms. It follows that both the looped realization of $q$ and $\eta$ are transformations of affine space-valued diagrams. Every component of $\eta$ is an equivalence by step 1.3, so [F10] permits its inversion in that functor category. The composite $d^W=\eta^{-1}\Omega|Nq|$ is consequently a coherent pointed transformation from the actual Waldhausen K-space $\Omega|NwS_\bullet\mathcal W(-)|$ to $\mathcal G(\operatorname{Spec}(-))$. The one-skeleton sends an object and its weak automorphisms to their determinant label and isomorphisms; each cofiber two-simplex gives the prescribed additivity. The coproduct commutativity axiom of [F1] retains the Koszul symmetry. No inverse to a pointwise Waldhausen/Picard one-type comparison has been used. [F1, F2, F7, F8, F10, step 2.1, step 1.3, step 2.3, construct]

4.1 Apply the S-construction comparison [F9] to the whole relative diagram of step 2.1. At each simplicial degree a weak-equivalence diagram of split cofibration filtrations maps to that same diagram in the localized category; its quotient squares become the specified cofiber squares. Exact extension of scalars preserves these diagrams. The chain comparison maps checked in step 2.1 give the natural comparison homotopies for composable ring maps; their unit and composition equations are preserved on filtrations and their quotient diagrams. Faces and degeneracies are restriction and repetition of those diagrams, so these comparisons commute with all simplicial operators. Taking cores, realization and loops therefore gives a coherent comparison with connective K-theory of $\mathcal W(-)_\infty$, rather than only pointwise comparisons of homotopy groups. Apply connective K-theory to $e$, then the natural initial-stage nonconnective map of [F2]. This produces a morphism $c:\Omega^\infty K^W(\mathcal W(-))\to\mathcal K(\operatorname{Spec}(-))$ in the functor infinity-category of affine space-valued presheaves. Each component is an equivalence by step 1.1 and [F9], so [F10] makes $c$ an equivalence there. Invert this single equivalence in that functor category, retaining its inverse-equivalence data. The composite $d^Wc^{-1}$ is the required homotopy-coherent affine determinant transformation on the specified $\mathcal K$. Its object, automorphism and additivity data remain those of [F1], since every comparison just used is induced by the same object and diagram maps. No independent ringwise inverses have been chosen. [F1, F2, F6, F7, F8, F9, F10, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 3.1]

5.1 By step 4.1 the affine determinant maps $\mathcal K(U)\to\mathcal G(U)$ form a coherent natural family. Restriction of $\mathcal K(X)$ along every $U\to X$ and then these maps give a cone to the diagram in step 2.2. Its equivalence defines a natural map $\mathcal K\to\mathcal G$ on $\mathcal C$, extending the affine maps. The zero complex has determinant $(0,\mathcal O)$ with its canonical unit comparisons, so this map is pointed. By [F4] and step 1.2 it factors through $a_{\mathrm{Nis}}\mathcal K\to\mathcal G$; composing with the unit recovers that map at every qcqs $X$. This is the required sheafification construction, without assuming descent of the original K-presheaf or that first Postnikov truncation commutes with global sections. The comparison simplices in steps 2.1, 3.1 and 4.1 make this a cone of space-valued diagrams, coherently natural in $X$, as required for step 2.2; it is not inferred from unrelated pointwise maps. KM pullback composition supplies its determinant comparison isomorphisms. On a perfect object $E$ its affine restrictions are sent to the alternating determinant with those same quasi-isomorphism transition maps; step 2.2 glues them to KM's global determinant by [F1]'s extended determinant/base-change data. Its object automorphisms glue with the same identifications. Restricting the construction to the compatible small or smooth big sites of [F4] preserves it. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, F8, F9, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 1.2, step 2.2, step 3.1, step 4.1]

6.1 The rank of the graded line is Euler rank. Its rank-zero substack is $B\mathbb G_m$ with its usual symmetry, giving the stated restricted map. Here $B\mathbb G_m$ means the nerve of the groupoid of $\mathbb G_m$-torsors, rather than the objectwise nerve of the group $\mathbb G_m(T)$. The frame-torsor equivalence retains isomorphisms: local frames identify them with compatible scalar units, and effective, fully faithful descent in [F3] supplies the inverse functor on these data. An enhanced cofiber sequence is represented locally by a genuine cofibration sequence or a mapping-cylinder replacement of such a sequence; its determinant additivity is the one in [F1]. Taking line isomorphism classes yields $[\det Q]=[\det P]+[\det R]$ for every such cofiber relation, independently of a chosen ordinary triangle diagram. Degree-zero vector bundles and their matrix automorphisms give precisely exterior determinant and matrix determinant. For a global cofiber sequence, use its mapping-cone true triangle and the KM additivity isomorphism, with the quasi-isomorphism identifications of [F1]. These are the same pullback-compatible isomorphisms used by the affine determinant data in steps 1.1–5.1, so the global line relation retains its gluing maps. Mere equality of classes on affine opens is not used to infer equality in the global Picard group. [F1, F2, F3, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 5.1] ∎


## Images of support groups in global groups

[Open item](item-def-support-image-groups) · level 15


### Definition
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the size conventions of the support construction. Fix a field $k$, a quasi-compact quasi-separated $k$-scheme $X$, and a closed subset $Y\subseteq X$ with its reduced induced closed-subscheme structure. Put $U=X\setminus Y$. Let $j\geq0$ and $i\in\mathbb Z$.

Use the support spectra and their support-to-global maps from the preceding support definition. In particular, $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_{i,Y}(X)$ is the image of $\pi_iF^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X/U)$ in $\pi_iK(X/U)$, and $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_i(X)$ is the image of $\pi_iF^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)$ in $K_i(X)=\pi_iK(X)$. An image is the subgroup consisting of the values of the indicated homomorphism. Define

$$F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_i(Y;X):=\operatorname{im}\bigl(F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_{i,Y}(X)\longrightarrow F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_i(X)\bigr),$$

where the arrow is induced by the support-to-global map on $K$-theory. For supported motivic cohomology $H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z)$ and global motivic cohomology $H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb Z)$, define

$$H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y;X):=\operatorname{im}\bigl(H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot},Y}(X,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb Z)\bigr)$$

for every integer $a$; in particular this includes the article's degree $a=2j-i$. The arrow is the support-to-global map. Finally, put

$$K_0(Y;X):=\operatorname{im}\bigl(\pi_0K(X/U)\longrightarrow K_0(X)\bigr).$$

These are image subgroups of the respective global groups; the supported groups are their domains before the final image is taken. No injectivity of a support-to-global map is part of the definition.

### Definition references
[def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support)

[def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration)

[def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The field, scheme, closed subset, complement, choice and size conventions, and indices in the definition.

**Goal:** Show that each displayed map has the stated target and that each image is a well-defined subgroup.

[A1] The Axiom of Choice, [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice), discharges the inherited Choice hypotheses of the support and global filtration constructions invoked in [F1] and [F2]. The subgroup-image argument below requires no additional choice principle.

[F1] [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support) supplies the filtered and unfiltered support spectra as fibers, their compatible maps to the corresponding global spectra, and the supported motivic cohomology groups and support-to-global homomorphisms. Its construction also specifies the extension used when $U$ is not quasi-compact.

[F2] [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) defines $K_i(X)=\pi_iK(X)$ and $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_i(X)$ as the image of the global stage-to-$K$ homomorphism. The stage-to-$K$ maps are compatible with restriction.

[F3] [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology) defines the global group $H^{a,j}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X,\mathbb Z)$. The support definition in [F1] identifies its support-to-global target with this group, using the stated cohomological indexing convention.

### Justification
1.1 The compatible support and global stage-to-$K$ maps form a commutative square after applying $\pi_i$. If an element of $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_{i,Y}(X)$ is represented by an element of $\pi_iF^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X/U)$, its image in $K_i(X)$ is represented by the image of that element in $\pi_iF^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K(X)$. It therefore belongs to $F^j_{\mathrm{mot}}K_i(X)$. The map is the restriction of the homomorphism on unfiltered $K$-theory, so it is independent of the chosen representative. [A1, F1, F2, given]

2.1 The image of a group homomorphism is a subgroup: zero is the image of zero, and the difference of two image elements is the image of the difference of their preimages. Apply this to the homomorphism in step 1.1 and to the support-to-global homomorphisms for motivic cohomology and unfiltered $K$-theory. The resulting subgroups are exactly the three displayed definitions. The construction uses no injectivity; it justifies the definitions and their stated targets. [F1, F3, step 1.1] ∎

### Indexing and scope
The supported cohomology supplier uses an explicit $2j$ shift to reconcile the article's displayed $\pi_i$ notation with the global cohomological convention. The image in degree $2j-i$ is taken using that supplier's support-to-global map. The construction does not identify $K(X/U)$ with the $K$-theory of a category of supported perfect complexes.


## Contravariant Hom reverses relative cofiber triangles

[Open item](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-contravariant-relative-hom) · level 15


### Statement
Let $A\xrightarrow f B\to Q\to A[1]$ be a specified distinguished triangle in a triangulated category $\mathcal T$. For any coefficient object $E$ and integer $a$, applying contravariant representable Hom gives the exact sequence containing
$$\cdots\to\operatorname{Hom}(A,E[a-1])\to\operatorname{Hom}(Q,E[a])\to\operatorname{Hom}(B,E[a])\to\operatorname{Hom}(A,E[a])\to\operatorname{Hom}(Q,E[a+1])\to\cdots.$$

For the enhanced conclusion, supply a stable infinity-category $\mathcal C$, an equivalence $h\mathcal C\simeq\mathcal T$ compatible with translation and distinguished triangles, lifts of $A,B,E,f$, and a specified cofiber square $\widetilde Q=\widetilde B\coprod_{\widetilde A}0$ whose induced triangle identifies with the entire given triangle, including its connecting map. Use the hierarchy of universes and smallness conventions of the enhanced interface. Define $R\operatorname{Hom}(-,E)$ in spectra as the left-exact spectral lift of $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C}(-,\widetilde E):\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal S$; tildes on lifted objects will now be suppressed. Then
$$R\operatorname{Hom}(Q,E)\simeq\operatorname{fib}\bigl(R\operatorname{Hom}(B,E)\to R\operatorname{Hom}(A,E)\bigr).$$
For a derived mapping-complex version, additionally supply a commutative ring $R$ and a coherent functor $K_E:\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{HR}$ whose underlying spectrum-valued functor is equivalent to this spectral Hom functor. Interpret $R\operatorname{Hom}(-,E)$ as $K_E$ under $\operatorname{Mod}_{HR}\simeq\mathcal D(R)$. The same formula is then an equivalence in $\mathcal D(R)$, hence an isomorphism in $D(R)$; it does not assert equality of chosen cochain complexes. These enhancement and linear-compatibility data are additional premises, not consequences of the Hom groups alone.

For the support application, let $U\hookrightarrow X$ be an open immersion of schemes. Suppose a covariant motive functor $M$ into the supplied enhancement is given on a category containing this immersion, and define the relative motive $M(X,U):=\operatorname{cofib}(M(U)\to M(X))$. Suppose also that a chosen cohomology theory supplies a restriction arrow $C(X)\to C(U)$ in the same derived target, together with an equivalence of coherent arrow diagrams from $R\operatorname{Hom}(M(X),E)\to R\operatorname{Hom}(M(U),E)$ to that arrow. With $C_{X\setminus U}(X):=\operatorname{fib}(C(X)\to C(U))$, one then has
$$R\operatorname{Hom}(M(X,U),E)\simeq C_{X\setminus U}(X).$$
Thus the relative motive is paired contravariantly with the coefficient object, and restriction goes from the whole scheme to the open. For the article's motivic support spectrum, additionally assume the Axiom of Choice and all domain and weight hypotheses of its support definition, and require the supplied arrow comparison to be with its specified motivic restriction arrow. The abstract triangle and conditional enhanced fiber assertions do not use this additional article-theory hypothesis. It does not construct $M$, assert an enhancement of a classical motive category, or identify support with an independently constructed quotient evaluation without that comparison.

### Statement references
[math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category)

[math:thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle)

[thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input)

[math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes)

[def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The specified triangle, coefficient object and integer; the enhanced and linear conclusions use exactly the additional lifts, cofiber square and compatible mapping functor in the Statement. The support application additionally assumes the supplied motive functor and coherent comparison of restriction arrows. Hom without $R$ means a morphism group in $\mathcal T$.

**Goal:** Establish the direction, degree, enhanced mapping fiber and conditional support identification.

[F1] The translation and distinguished triangles are specified structure of [math:def-triangulated-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-triangulated-category). Contravariant representable Hom applied to a distinguished triangle gives its long exact sequence by [math:thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-hom-sequences-of-a-distinguished-triangle).

[F2] The stable-category, spectral-lifting, linear-target and forgetful-limit interfaces are supplied by [thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input](item-thm-adjudicate-60ab133bec72-enhanced-mapping-input). The corepresentable functor $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}}(E,-)=\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C}(-,E)$ preserves finite limits by that interface; this is also the mapping-space universal property of a limit. Thus its left-exact spectral lift is available.

[F3] The specified cofiber square is part of the enhancement data. The coherent linear functor, when used, carries that square to a cone of $HR$-modules, and its underlying spectral cone is the one determined by the supplied equivalence of functors.

[F4] An open immersion identifies its source with an open subscheme, as in [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes). The support convention is a restriction fiber; for the article's theory this is the motivic fiber in [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support). The comparison of the two restriction arrows, including the homotopy making their square commute, is an additional hypothesis, not a theorem supplied by that support definition.

[F5] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) supplies the additional Choice assumption of the article support interface only; the formal proof makes no choice of enhancements or representing objects, which are supplied data.

### Proof
1.1 Apply contravariant Hom with target $E[a]$ to every shift of the given triangle. The part ending in $\operatorname{Hom}(B,E[a])\to\operatorname{Hom}(A,E[a])$ has preceding term $\operatorname{Hom}(Q,E[a])$ and following term $\operatorname{Hom}(Q[-1],E[a])\cong\operatorname{Hom}(Q,E[a+1])$. Its predecessor is $\operatorname{Hom}(A[1],E[a])\cong\operatorname{Hom}(A,E[a-1])$. These identifications use the translation equivalence; exactness follows from [F1], with connecting maps determined by the specified triangle's shift and rotation convention. [F1, given]

2.1 In the enhanced case the cofiber square in [F3] becomes a pullback square in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}$.
