By [F2] its left-exact spectral Hom image is a pullback square, with $R\operatorname{Hom}(0,E)=0$ since left-exact functors preserve terminal objects. Hence its remaining corner is precisely the fiber displayed in the Statement. In the linear case the coherent image square under $K_E$ has this underlying spectral pullback square; the module-limit criterion in [F2] makes it a pullback square of $HR$-modules as well. Transport across the equivalence with $\mathcal D(R)$ preserves this limit, giving the derived mapping-complex assertion. Finally substitute $A=M(U)$, $B=M(X)$ and $Q=M(X,U)$. The supplied equivalence of restriction-arrow diagrams extends by the zero objects to equivalent cospans defining their fibers. Their limiting objects are equivalent by the universal property of a limit. By [F4] the latter fiber is exactly $C_{X\setminus U}(X)$. For the article's support spectrum, invoke its definition only with its domain and weight hypotheses and the inherited Choice assumption [F5], and only when the comparison is with its specified restriction arrow. This proves all conclusions under their respective stated premises. [F2, F3, F4, F5, given, step 1.1] ∎


## Ideal-power dévissage and compatibility with coherent pushforward

[Open item](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-devissage-naturality) · level 15


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed suitable Grothendieck universes containing $\omega$ and the scheme data, with the enlargements required by the supported perfect and nonconnective constructions. Let $U$ be a regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme and $i:T\hookrightarrow U$ a closed subscheme with coherent defining ideal $\mathcal I=\ker(\mathcal O_U\to i_*\mathcal O_T)$. Write $\mathrm{Coh}(X)$ for coherent $\mathcal O_X$-modules and all their module-sheaf morphisms. Let $\mathcal A_T$ be the full subcategory of $\mathrm{Coh}(U)$ whose objects have zero stalks outside $|T|$, and $\mathcal B_T$ its full subcategory of objects annihilated by $\mathcal I$. For an essentially small abelian category $\mathcal A$, let $G_0(\mathcal A)$ be the group on isomorphism classes with relations $[M]=[M']+[M'']$ for short exact sequences $0\to M'\to M\to M''\to0$, using an equivalent small presentation; set $G_0(X)=G_0(\mathrm{Coh}(X))$.

Closed direct image induces a canonical isomorphism
$$d:G_0(T)\xrightarrow{\sim}G_0(\mathcal A_T).$$
For $M\in\mathcal A_T$, its inverse sends $[M]$ to $\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}[\mathcal I^rM/\mathcal I^{r+1}M]$, where $N\geq1$ is any integer with $\mathcal I^NM=0$ and each quotient is regarded as a coherent sheaf on $T$. Here $\mathcal I^0M=M$ and $\mathcal I^rM$ is the subsheaf locally generated by products of $r$ ideal sections acting on $M$. This sum is independent of $N$ and equals the quotient sum for any finite decreasing filtration of $M$ by coherent subobjects ending at zero with quotients annihilated by $\mathcal I$.

Let $v:G_0(\mathcal A_T)\to G_0(U)$ forget support. Then $v\circ d=i_*:G_0(T)\to G_0(U)$, where coherent pushforward is induced by the exact closed direct-image functor. For the specified supported and global nonconnective K-theory groups, let $q_T:G_0(\mathcal A_T)\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_0K_{|T|}(U)$ and $q_U:G_0(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_0K(U)$ be the inverses of the coherent-Euler comparisons, sending $[M]$ to $[M[0]]$. If $c:\pi_0K_{|T|}(U)\to\pi_0K(U)$ is induced by the actual support inclusion, then $c\circ q_T\circ d=q_U\circ i_*$. No regularity of $T$ is assumed.

Its ideal-power and coherent-sheaf constructions are those in the Statement. It gives the inverse quotient-sum formula, filtration independence and additivity, and the short-exact group presentation used in the Statement. These supply the essential-smallness premise in step 1.1; that premise is not supplied by the ordinary ideal-filtration argument in [F1].

These are precisely the geometric objects required by [F1] and [F3]; regularity is absolute local-ring regularity, without a base-field smoothness assumption.

[F5] Abelian categories are those of [math:def-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-abelian-category), and the short exact sequences defining the relations of $G_0$ are those of [math:def-exact-sequence-and-short-exact-sequence-in-an-abelian-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-and-short-exact-sequence-in-an-abelian-category). Equivalence and quasi-inverse have the meaning of [math:def-equivalence-and-adjoint-equivalence-of-categories](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-equivalence-and-adjoint-equivalence-of-categories). The geometric exact equivalence and exact inclusions used in step 1.1 are supplied by [F1], and the induced homomorphisms on the short-exact group presentations are those in [F2].

[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is used in step 1.1 to select equivalent small presentations for [F2] and is retained for the selected perfect-category realization and nonconnective comparison [F3] in steps 1.1–2.1. The fixed universes satisfy [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and permit the enlargements required by [F3]; their existence is a standing size assumption separate from Choice. Computing the finite quotient sums requires no further choice.

### Proof
1.1 The hypotheses in Given and [F4] allow [F1] and [F3] to be applied. Use the categorical conventions of [F5]. Both abelian categories in the dévissage inclusion are essentially small: by [F3], $\mathcal A_T$ is the heart of the essentially small supported perfect category, and its full subcategory $\mathcal B_T$ is therefore essentially small; [F1] identifies $\mathrm{Coh}(T)$ with $\mathcal B_T$. Choose equivalent small presentations under [A1]. The exactness, fullness, ambient subobject/quotient closure and finite-filtration hypotheses of [F2] are supplied by [F1]. Thus exact inclusion $\mathcal B_T\hookrightarrow\mathcal A_T$ induces an isomorphism. The exact equivalence $i_*$ in [F1] induces an isomorphism on the short-exact group presentations: an exact quasi-inverse gives its inverse on sheaf classes and relations. Precomposing with it gives precisely $d$. The inverse formula is the ideal-power quotient sum by [F2], with each quotient transported through the equivalence in [F1]. The independence statement of [F2] applies to every permitted coherent filtration, and in particular to every annihilating exponent $N\geq1$. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, construct]

2.1 On a generator $[F]\in G_0(T)$, $d$ sends it to $[i_*F]$ in $G_0(\mathcal A_T)$. The exact support-forgetting inclusion of [F1] sends this to the same coherent sheaf class in $G_0(U)$, which is the definition of closed coherent pushforward. The short-exact group presentation of [F2] therefore gives $v\circ d=i_*$ on the entire group. By [F3], $q_T$ and $q_U$ send these sheaf classes to the classes of the actual perfect objects $i_*F[0]$, and the actual support inclusion sends one to the other. Thus $c\circ q_T\circ d=q_U\circ i_*$ on generators and hence on the entire group, proving the spectral compatibility as well. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## Augmented tower comparisons preserve supported graded projections

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport) · level 15


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the universe and smallness conventions of the stable spectra interface. Let $X$ be a scheme, let $T$ be a closed subset, and let $V=X\setminus T$ be its open subscheme. Let $F^\bullet E\to E$ and $G^\bullet E'\to E'$ be coherent augmented decreasing filtered presheaves of spectra on a category containing this open immersion. Here a presheaf is a contravariant coherent functor; its filtration has transitions from larger integer indices to smaller ones and compatible augmentations to the underlying presheaf. Let $\alpha$ be a specified natural comparison commuting coherently with these transitions and augmentations. Suppose $\alpha$ is an equivalence on every stage and on the underlying spectra at both $X$ and $V$. No descent hypothesis is required.

Define the supported objects and their graded pieces by
$$F^j_TE=\operatorname{fib}(F^jE(X)\to F^jE(V)),\qquad E_T=\operatorname{fib}(E(X)\to E(V)),$$
$$\operatorname{gr}^j_TF=\operatorname{cofib}(F^{j+1}_TE\to F^j_TE),$$
and use the analogous definitions $G^j_TE'$, $E'_T$, and $\operatorname{gr}^j_TG$. The graded support object is naturally the fiber of the restriction between the corresponding unsupported graded objects. Write $p_j:F^j_TE\to\operatorname{gr}^j_TF$ for the defining cofiber projection and $\epsilon_j:F^j_TE\to E_T$ for the induced augmentation, and use primes for the second tower. The comparison induces equivalences
$$a_j:F^j_TE\to G^j_TE',\qquad a:E_T\to E'_T,\qquad b_j:\operatorname{gr}^j_TF\to\operatorname{gr}^j_TG,$$
which satisfy the commuting projection and augmentation identities
$$b_jp_j=p'_ja_j,\qquad a\epsilon_j=\epsilon'_ja_j.$$
These identities are in the homotopy category, whose morphisms are homotopy classes of maps of spectra, and come from the coherent comparison diagrams.

If $\epsilon_q$ is an equivalence for an integer $q$, then $\epsilon'_q$ is also an equivalence. Define the **inverse-augmentation leading maps** in that homotopy category by
$$N_q=p_q\epsilon_q^{-1}:E_T\to\operatorname{gr}^q_TF,\qquad N'_q=p'_q(\epsilon'_q)^{-1}:E'_T\to\operatorname{gr}^q_TG.$$
They satisfy
$$b_qN_q=N'_qa.$$
Thus the supported normalization being transported means this specified composite and its naturality, including the induced identities on homotopy groups. Any formula for its value on specified classes transports through these maps; a numerical or generator-value formula must be supplied separately and does not follow merely from equivalence of the target groups.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The scheme and open pair, the two coherent augmented diagrams, their actual comparison, and the stated stage and underlying equivalences on $X,V$, under the Choice and size assumptions above.

**Goal:** Preserve the actual supported projection and the explicitly defined inverse-augmentation leading map.

[A1] Assume the Axiom of Choice, [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice), and the compatible universe and smallness conventions required by [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface). This is the inherited foundational hypothesis for the stable constructions used below; no independent choice principle is used to select inverses.

[F1] Spectra have a zero object and coherent finite limits and colimits; fibers and cofibers are their defining pullbacks and pushouts. Universal properties make these constructions functorial. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

[F2] An augmented decreasing filtration and its graded cofiber are as in [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum). [lem-filtered-support-exactness](item-lem-filtered-support-exactness) constructs the supported augmented tower and identifies its graded cofibers naturally with the fibers of graded restriction maps.

[F3] The support notation uses the fiber-of-restriction convention of [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support). Only that convention is used here; no motivic comparison, quotient evaluation, or identification with a support category is assumed.

[F4] The scheme and open-immersion terminology is supplied by [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) and [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes); $V$ carries the restricted scheme structure. The argument uses only the resulting restriction arrow.

### Proof

1.1 On the restriction arrow for each stage, take the fiber of the given comparison square. A diagram of equivalences has equivalent limits: its componentwise inverse diagram supplies inverse limit maps by the universal property. This gives $a_j$, and the identical construction on the underlying restriction arrow gives $a$. Functoriality preserves the transition and augmentation diagrams, giving $a\epsilon_j=\epsilon'_ja_j$. By [F2], the supported graded objects are the cofibers of these supported transitions and the fibers of the graded restrictions. Taking cofibers of the transition comparison gives $b_j$; it is an equivalence because both input maps are equivalences, by the dual universal property for colimits. The defining cofiber projections are part of these same diagrams, so $b_jp_j=p'_ja_j$. All constructions use the supplied coherent diagrams, before passing to their homotopy classes. This proves all stage, underlying, graded, and projection assertions. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, given]

2.1 Suppose $\epsilon_q$ is an equivalence. The augmentation identity in step 1.1 gives $\epsilon'_q=a\epsilon_qa_q^{-1}$ in the homotopy category, hence $\epsilon'_q$ is an equivalence. Inverses of an isomorphism in a category are unique, so $a_q\epsilon_q^{-1}=(\epsilon'_q)^{-1}a$ without any independently selected normalization. Combining this with the projection identity gives $b_qp_q\epsilon_q^{-1}=p'_qa_q\epsilon_q^{-1}=p'_q(\epsilon'_q)^{-1}a$, the asserted identity for $N_q,N'_q$. The homotopy groups of the supplied stable interface are functorial, so this equality induces the same commuting homomorphisms in every degree. Applying that equality to any specified class transports any supplied value formula through the actual maps. No value on a generator has been inferred from an abstract group identification. [A1, F1, step 1.1, given, algebra] ∎


## External determinant and pointed one-type inputs

[Open item](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input) · level 15


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible Grothendieck universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$; their existence is a separate assumption. All scheme data lie in $U$, site categories are $V$-small, and space-valued constructions use $W$. Work on the universe-bounded big Nisnevich site of qcqs schemes and all scheme morphisms, or its qcqs small Nisnevich or smooth big restrictions over a fixed qcqs base. Here a scheme is quasi-compact if every open cover has a finite subcover, and quasi-separated if intersections of two affine opens are quasi-compact; Nisnevich covers are étale families with a residue-field-preserving lift of every target point. These are the supplied site presentations, including affine open refinements.

For a site object $T$, let $\mathcal K(T)=\Omega^\infty\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}(T))$ be the specified nonconnective perfect K-space, pointed by the zero complex. A perfect complex is locally equivalent to a bounded complex of finite locally free module sheaves. Let $a_{\mathrm{Nis}}$ denote derived sheafification of spaces, and put $\mathscr K=a_{\mathrm{Nis}}\mathcal K$. Write $\underline{\mathbb Z}(T)$ for locally constant integer functions on $T$ and $\mathbb G_m(T)=\Gamma(T,\mathcal O_T)^\times$. The actual graded determinant induces the Euler-rank map $r:\mathscr K\to\underline{\mathbb Z}$. Define $S=\operatorname{fib}_0(r)$ as its pointed homotopy fiber in sheaves of spaces. Define $B\mathbb G_m$ to be the stack of $\mathbb G_m$-torsors, equivalently line bundles and their isomorphisms, with basepoint the trivial line. The graded-line stack has objects $(n,L)$, with $n$ locally constant and $L$ a line bundle, tensor product adding degrees and tensoring lines, and symmetry $(-1)^{nm}$ on degrees $n,m$.

The extended determinant functor on perfect complexes is canonically compatible with true/enhanced cofiber triangles and derived pullback; on bounded locally free representatives its graded line is the alternating tensor product of determinant lines, with alternating rank. It gives the actual natural map $d:S\to B\mathbb G_m$, retaining line-bundle descent morphisms. On affine K-spaces its fundamental-group map is the ordinary matrix determinant, and its object-class map sends a vector bundle to its determinant line. For a nonzero commutative local ring, in particular a Nisnevich local ring, $\pi_0K=\mathbb Z$ by rank and $\pi_1K=A^\times$ by determinant; the affine comparisons identify these same maps in the specified enhanced nonconnective model.

The sheaf $S$ is connected and $\pi_1(S)\cong\mathbb G_m$ through determinant. Pointed connected 1-types in an infinity-topos are classified fully faithfully by their sheaves of fundamental groups. The factor of the already constructed $d$ therefore gives $\tau_{\leq1}S\simeq B\mathbb G_m$. Every map $S\to B\mathbb G_m$ factors through this 1-type by the truncation universal property. On global sections, the map induced by $d$ sends a rank-zero perfect K-class to $c_1=$ the class of its determinant line; connectedness as a sheaf does not assert connectedness of its global sections. On a vector bundle the underlying determinant operation is frame-torsor pushforward along $GL_r\to\mathbb G_m$ by matrix determinant; on a perfect complex it is the extended determinant functor with its stated triangle/pullback coherence. These assertions also hold after consistently taking the further hypercomplete replacement of the space-valued sheaves.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] Choice and universe closure have the meanings of [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention). They are inherited through the enhanced perfect-category small presentations, unit-section and torsor suppliers. Universe existence is separately assumed. Stack comparison uses the stated big site; line-frame identification and global sections may be restricted to the supplied small sites.

**Given:** The sites, $\mathcal K$, $\mathscr K$, $r$, $S$ and $B\mathbb G_m$ in the Statement, with [A1].

**Goal:** Combine the imported determinant and homotopy foundations to establish the sheaf-level first-Postnikov identification of this actual map.

[F1] Knudsen–Mumford Definition 4 and Theorem 2 at source reference 0 supply the alternating graded determinant, true-triangle additivity and derived-pullback composition isomorphisms. True triangles are short exact complex diagrams in the derived diagram category; their morphisms retain diagram information. Compatibility here does not assert functoriality for arbitrary ordinary derived-triangle diagrams.

[F2] [lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-actual-determinant-stack-map](item-lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-actual-determinant-stack-map) supplies the actual pointed determinant map into the graded-line stack, its Euler grading, enhanced cofiber coherence, and its object and automorphism maps, before Postnikov identification. [lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors](item-lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors) supplies units, Picard classes and frame torsors, retaining line isomorphisms in the determinant-map supplier. Weibel IV.4.4.2 at source reference 1 gives the elementary affine normalization. A line bundle means a locally free module sheaf of rank one, and its Picard class is its isomorphism class under tensor product.

[F3] [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) specifies $K(T)=\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}(T))$ for qcqs $T$, with coherent derived pullbacks, and [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) specifies the full stable idempotent-complete perfect category and its cofiber sequences. [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) fixes the residue-field-preserving covering convention. [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme) fixes the qcqs conditions. Use the big qcqs site specified in [F2], its small site of qcqs étale objects, or its smooth big site of qcqs smooth objects; morphisms are all morphisms over the base and covers have sources in that category. Affine open refinements remain in these categories. Source reference 7 supplies left exact reflective space-valued sheafification and its universal unit, separately from the further left exact hypercompletion. No equivalence of the entire Q-space sheaf with a perfect K-space sheaf is needed for this low-degree argument.

[F4] [lem-affine-perfect-k-zero-bridge](item-lem-affine-perfect-k-zero-bridge) identifies affine $\pi_0K$ with the vector-bundle Grothendieck group, with the alternating Euler formula. [thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-classical-comparisons](item-thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-classical-comparisons) identifies affine Q-space $\pi_1$ naturally with $GL(A)_{ab}$, matching actual matrix-automorphism loops. [thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons](item-thm-author-da44cda020c0-units-k1-enhanced-comparisons) transfers these loops through its canonical Waldhausen-to-connective and connective-to-nonconnective comparisons, giving the same affine fundamental group in the specified model. Here $GL(A)$ is the union of invertible finite matrices under $M\mapsto\operatorname{diag}(M,1)$, and the subscript $ab$ means quotient by the subgroup of finite products of commutators $ghg^{-1}h^{-1}$. [lem-unit-section-of-k-one-determinant](item-lem-unit-section-of-k-one-determinant) identifies the same determinant and the unit section $u\mapsto[\mathcal O\xrightarrow{u}\mathcal O]$, whose composite with determinant is identity. Source reference 2 records local $K_0=\mathbb Z$; source reference 3 gives the local $K_1=A^\times$ isomorphism through determinant.

[F5] Source reference 6 supplies truncation, preservation of truncation by left exact colimit-preserving functors, and homotopy-sheaf functoriality and fiber exactness. Applied to source reference 7's space-valued sheafification, this says that $\pi_0\mathscr K$ is the associated sheaf of $T\mapsto\pi_0\mathcal K(T)$, and that $\pi_1(\mathscr K,0)$ is the associated sheaf of $T\mapsto\pi_1(\mathcal K(T),0)$: loops are finite limits, and $\pi_1$ is their zero-truncation. Truncation preserves homotopy sheaves through its degree, and connected means that zero-truncation is terminal. Source reference 5 supplies the pointed connected one-type classification. Truncation is internal to the sheaf infinity-topos; no assertion that it commutes with global sections is used.

[F6] Schemes have local structure rings and affine charts by [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme). A germ is a section modulo equality on a smaller neighbourhood by [math:def-stalk-of-presheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-stalk-of-presheaf); finitely many germs and equalities can therefore be represented simultaneously after shrinking. [math:lem-distinguished-open-refinement-at-a-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-distinguished-open-refinement-at-a-point) and [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion) give an affine neighbourhood inside any such open subset of an affine chart, as a principal localization. These inputs allow the finite matrix argument below on objects of the chosen site, without an unproved continuity theorem for K-theory.

### External input
Import the precise determinant data of source reference 0, the affine normalization of reference 1, the local group calculations of references 2–3, and the truncation/homotopy-sheaf, one-type and localization foundations of references 6, 5 and 7, with their recorded hypotheses and reading limits. The degree-zero and degree-one affine comparisons and the determinant enhancement are used through [F2]–[F4]. The proof below is a local composition and sheaf argument, not a reconstruction of these foundational inputs. Source reference 4 is contextual only: Weibel V.10.10.1 states descent for $K^B$ in its recorded finite-dimensional noetherian context. The present map uses sheafification into the graded-line stack, without importing unrestricted qcqs K-descent from that example.

### Proof
1.1 On an affine chart, [F4] represents each $\pi_0K$ class by a finite sum and difference of bundle classes. Near any point these finitely many bundles trivialize, so the class becomes its rank times $[\mathcal O]$. Thus rank-zero classes vanish locally; conversely locally constant integers are locally represented by signed free-bundle classes. These statements also treat equality of classes, by taking their difference, and prove that the associated sheaf of $\pi_0\mathcal K$ is $\underline{\mathbb Z}$ by the actual rank map. For $\pi_1$, [F4] identifies each class on an affine with the class of a finite invertible matrix $M$. The unit section makes determinant locally surjective. For a class in its kernel, the image of $M$ over the local structure ring $\mathcal O_{T,x}$ is zero in $GL(\mathcal O_{T,x})_{ab}$ by source reference 3. By the definition of abelianization in [F4], after finite stabilization it is a finite product of commutators of invertible matrices over that local ring. Represent the finitely many entries of these matrices and their inverses by sections near $x$; the inverse equations and the product equality hold after a common shrinking by [F6]. Refine that neighbourhood to an affine open by [F6]. There $M$ is a product of commutators, so its K-class is zero by [F4]. This works near every point of every affine chart. A section of an associated sheaf is locally represented by a presheaf section, so these local kernel and lifting checks establish the isomorphism of associated sheaves with $\mathbb G_m$. Zariski refinements are Nisnevich covers by [F3], and the empty object has its unique zero-rank class and unit, so it causes no exception. By [F5], these are exactly $\pi_0\mathscr K\cong\underline{\mathbb Z}$ and $\pi_1(\mathscr K,0)\cong\mathbb G_m$, with maps induced by rank and the already constructed determinant. [A1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, given]

2.1 The target of $r$ is discrete. The fiber exactness in [F5] and the rank isomorphism of step 1.1 give $\pi_0S=*$ and $\pi_1S\cong\pi_1(\mathscr K,0)\cong\mathbb G_m$. By [F2], the restriction of the actual graded determinant to this fiber is the pointed map $d:S\to B\mathbb G_m$, and its fundamental-group map is exactly that determinant isomorphism. The target is connected as a sheaf because every torsor is locally trivial, and is 1-truncated because it is a stack of groupoids. By [F5]'s truncation universal property, $d$ factors through $\tau_{\leq1}S$; both source and target of this factor are pointed connected 1-types. The fully faithful classification of [F5] sends this factor to the isomorphism on $\mathbb G_m$, hence makes the factor an equivalence. The same universal property gives factorization of all maps to $B\mathbb G_m$, including pointed maps when basepoints are specified. The object and automorphism maps remain those of [F1]–[F2], so global evaluation retains the determinant line with its gluing isomorphisms; taking components gives its Picard class and therefore the usual first Chern class on rank-zero perfect classes. The unit section fixes the matrix determinant normalization on loops. No equality of global vector-bundle and perfect $K_0$ is required. Further hypercompletion preserves the fiber and these homotopy sheaves by [F3]–[F5], while $B\mathbb G_m$, being truncated, is already hypercomplete; the same argument gives the asserted version there. This proves every stated identification. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎

### Precise source and enhancement scope
KM's true triangles are represented by actual short exact complex diagrams in its derived diagram category. The forgetful functor to ordinary derived triangles loses morphism information (pp.37–38); the p.41 footnote requires genuine diagram commutativity. The p.43 ordinary-triangle corollary has a perfect-cohomology hypothesis, and p.44 warns against an unrestricted extension. These qualifications retain the determinant/Picard operation needed here. The actual-map supplier gives the required map before its first-Postnikov identification, with true/enhanced cofiber additivity. No published KM error is alleged. Current reading of the cached KM scans and live Weibel/HTT text is restricted to the passages recorded in the source metadata; none of their long foundational proofs is claimed as a new local reconstruction.


## Open-natural smooth cycle layers by separated-open descent

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-37c8a46c3b6b-open-natural-cycle-layers) · level 15


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible nested Grothendieck universes containing $\omega$, the field, schemes, opens and all diagrams below; universe existence is a separate size hypothesis. Let $k$ be perfect, let $F$ be its prime subfield, and let $j\geq0$. On smooth finite-type $k$-schemes and open immersions, including nonseparated schemes, the specified EM tower has a natural equivalence
$$\operatorname{gr}^jK(Y)\simeq\mathcal H(\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y))[2j]\simeq\mathcal H(z^j(Y,\bullet)).$$
Here $\mathcal H$ is the supplied coherent ordinary Eilenberg–Mac Lane realization. On the right a homological complex is reindexed by $A^q=A_{-q}$ with the same differential, so $\pi_i\mathcal H(z^j)=H_i z^j$. Cochain shift is $A[m]^q=A^{q+m}$ with differential $(-1)^m d$, and $[m]$ on spectra means suspension. The comparison is an equivalence of coherent open-restriction diagrams before taking homotopy groups, with each cycle arrow actual open restriction. Consequently it applies simultaneously to a scheme and its open complement and then to their restriction fiber.

The route is the actual augmented EM-to-cdh tower map, its induced graded map, and the standard classical cycle comparison, extended from separated opens as constructed below. It agrees with that fixed standard comparison on separated schemes. This supplies the layer shift and naturality required by the supported second-stage argument. It asserts neither multiplicative compatibility with cycles nor cross-weight compatibility. The source-version qualification in External input remains binding.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** AC, the stated size hypotheses, the fixed perfect field $k/F$, nonnegative weight $j$, and the actual tower.

**Goal:** Extend the standard separated classical comparison to the full open diagram without inventing naturality from objectwise equivalences.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention) specify the inherited Choice and size conventions. All limits and functor categories below lie in the chosen larger universe; no simultaneous selection of unrelated pointwise inverses is made.

[F1] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) gives the actual natural augmented equivalence $F^\bullet_{\mathrm{mot}}K\to F^\bullet_{\mathrm{cdh}}KH$ on smooth finite-type $k$-schemes. Reference 0 identifies its graded targets with $\mathcal H(C_{j,F})[2j]$, where $C_{j,F}=\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{cdh},F}$ is exactly the cdh-local smooth-prime-field extension. Its graded source is the actual $\mathcal H(\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}})[2j]$. The source supplies coherent comparison maps, not merely agreement of cohomology groups.

[F2] Let $\mathcal O_k$ have all smooth separated finite-type $k$-schemes as objects and open immersions as arrows. Use the absolute slice-tower identification already supplied by [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) and reference 0, Remark 3.6, on qcqs schemes of equal characteristic. Explicitly, the evaluated absolute tower is $S^q(Y)=\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,f_q\mathrm{KGL}_Y)$, where $f_q$ is the right-adjoint $q$-effective cover, with its counit and transitions; $s^j=\operatorname{cofib}(f_{j+1}\to f_j)$. The imported identification is one of towers, not merely of their homotopy groups. With [F1]'s graded identification, its degree-$j$ cofiber gives $\mathcal H(C_{j,F})[2j]\simeq\mathbb Z(j)^{\mathbb A}[2j]$ as underlying coherent spectral presheaves. Reference 1, Definition 4.15, defines this latter endpoint by $\mathbb Z(j)^{\mathbb A}(Y)=\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,s^j\mathrm{KGL}_Y)[-2j]$. This uses the evaluated absolute slice tower, rather than a comparison of unsheafified lisse constructions. Restrict the entire tower comparison along $\mathrm{Sch}_k\to\mathrm{Sch}_F$ and then to $\mathcal O_k$; [F5] checks its qcqs equal-characteristic domain, without any smoothness or finite-generation requirement on $k/F$. Reference 1, Remark 4.21, identifies its shifted smooth-$k$ restriction with $R_j(Y)=\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(k)}(M_k(Y),s^j\mathrm{KGL}_k)$. Here $\mathrm{SH}(k)$ is the stable infinity-category of motivic spectra; $M_k(Y)$ is the suspension motive of $Y$; $\operatorname{map}$ is its mapping spectrum; $s^j$ is the cofiber of consecutive effective covers. These are the imported constructions of §§3.1–3.3 and Definition 4.15. Formula (4.7) gives $s^j\mathrm{KGL}_k\simeq\mathbb T_k^{\otimes j}\otimes s^0\mathrm{KGL}_k$, where $\mathbb T_k$ is the reduced suspension motive of the projective line. The proof of Theorem 6.3, before (6.4), recalls the equivalences $s^0\mathrm{KGL}_k\leftarrow s^0(1_k)\to\mathcal Z$; $1_k$ denotes the motivic sphere. Use exactly the Bloch motivic spectrum assembled from Levine's §6.5 complexes, with the sequential models and represented evaluation specified in [F9]. Write $G_j=J(G_j^{\mathrm{s}})$. Step 1.1 derives $R_j\simeq G_j$ from that construction and the graded right adjoint, before composing with the pointwise coniveau chain. For the further leg to $B^{\mathrm{s}}=\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(z^j(-,\bullet))$ use the explicit pointwise coniveau comparisons in [F8]. Neither the wording homotopy category of presheaves before (6.4) nor the derived-sheaf statement in Levine §6.5 alone identifies the required pointwise localization. The functor and additive comparison of [F7] identify $J(B^{\mathrm{s}})$ naturally with $B=\mathcal H(z^j(-,\bullet))$. The resulting fixed-weight chain is $\mathcal H(C_{j,F})[2j]\simeq R_j\simeq G_j\simeq B$. Select this standard comparison once, with ordinary unnormalized alternating-face realization, actual open restrictions and the positive field coefficient specified in [F8]. Formula (6.4) alone is not used to provide global open naturality.

[F3] Reference 0 makes $C_{j,F}$ a derived cdh sheaf, hence gives binary Zariski Mayer–Vietoris and zero value on the empty scheme. [lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent](item-lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent) proves these same binary squares for the raw cycle complexes on possibly nonseparated smooth finite-type schemes. Its canonical raw-to-derived-sections unit is open-natural; thus the raw construction, rather than a substituted definition of cycles, is retained. [lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction](item-lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction) supplies the actual cycle arrow: an admissible integral generator restricts to its open subscheme if nonempty and to zero otherwise, and this rule commutes with the alternating face differential. Successive restrictions compose because successive intersections with opens agree.

[F4] [lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization](item-lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization) preserves all small limits, zero objects, fibers and shifts, with $\pi_i\mathcal H(A)=H^{-i}(A)$. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) supplies coherent limits and their mapping-space universal property. Apply these constructions to the enhanced presheaves of [lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes](item-lem-author-b747b28c359f-elmanto-morrow-complexes), not to diagrams chosen in an ordinary homotopy category. [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum), [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) and [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology) fix the actual tower, graded cofiber and coefficient notation.

[F5] The prime subfield is the smallest subfield, as defined in [math:def-prime-subfield](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-prime-subfield); [math:thm-prime-subfield-classification](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-prime-subfield-classification) identifies it with $\mathbb Q$ or $\mathbb F_p$, so the prime-field suppliers apply. Field, perfectness, structure maps, smoothness and finite type use [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field), [math:def-perfect-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-perfect-field), [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base), [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes) and [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism). The geometric clause of [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) checks regular Noetherianity and the qcqs domain, without separatedness. An open immersion is étale by [math:ex-localization-etale-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-localization-etale-open-immersion), hence smooth by [math:def-etale-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-etale-morphism-schemes); therefore every arrow of $\mathcal O_k$ belongs to the smooth-morphism category used in [F8]. Open restriction and affine charts use [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes) and [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme). Every open is again smooth finite type by the explicit geometric argument in Proof 1.1 of [lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent](item-lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent). In particular it is quasi-compact; [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme) supplies its affine open neighbourhoods, so its affine opens cover it and a finite subcover exists.

[F6] [math:def-separated-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-separated-morphism-schemes) defines separatedness by a closed diagonal. [math:cor-affine-schemes-separated](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-schemes-separated) makes every affine chart separated over $k$. An open of a separated $k$-scheme is separated: its diagonal is the base change of the original diagonal to the product of that open with itself; use [math:lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change). Intersections of separated open subschemes are therefore separated. No separatedness of their union is asserted.

[F7] [math:def-localization-of-a-category-at-a-class-of-morphisms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-localization-of-a-category-at-a-class-of-morphisms) supplies the ordinary localization universal property. For a small ordinary category $I$, write $\mathrm{Spt}(I)$ for strict presheaves of the source's spectrum models and $\mathrm{HSpt}(I)$ for their localization at pointwise stable weak equivalences, as in reference 2, §§1.2–1.4. [lem-adjudicate-a9ee7502efb6-simplicial-spectra-realization](item-lem-adjudicate-a9ee7502efb6-simplicial-spectra-realization) supplies the actual functor $J(E)=\mathcal R(|E|)$ for these sequential pointed simplicial spectra, without assuming their levels Kan. It inverts stable weak equivalences and naturally identifies the source's ordinary additive Eilenberg–Mac Lane models with the fixed $\mathcal H$, including shifts, raw unnormalized complexes, restriction maps and the positive integer unit. Apply this single functor to every value and arrow, then take the homotopy category, to obtain the functor to $h\operatorname{Fun}(N(I)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})$. Here $N(I)$ records composable strings of arrows and $h$ takes components of mapping spaces. Each pointwise stable weak equivalence maps to an isomorphism of coherent diagrams by reference 4, Theorem 4.4.4.4: it is a supplied natural transformation whose components are equivalences. Reference 3 supplies the criterion that an isomorphism in this latter homotopy category is represented by an equivalence of entire coherent diagrams. This does not identify it with $\operatorname{Fun}(I^{\mathrm{op}},h\mathrm{Sp})$.

[F8] Reference 2 supplies the following coniveau models over the fixed perfect field. Its $\mathrm{Sm}/k$ has smooth separated finite-type objects and all morphisms; $\mathrm{Sm}//k$ has the same objects and smooth morphisms. Its support construction $E^{(p)}(Y,n)$ is the homotopy colimit of restriction fibers $E^W(Y\times\Delta^n)$ over closed supports meeting every face in codimension at least $p$; $E^{(p/p+1)}$ is the consecutive cofiber, and $|{-}|$ denotes its total simplicial spectrum as in §§1.6–2.1. These constructions and their functorial replacements are imported, not newly proved foundations. Put $Q_j=K^{(j/j+1)}\in\mathrm{Spt}(\mathrm{Sm}/k)$ for the rectified layer. Corollary 4.1.5 gives $Q_j|_{\mathrm{Sm}//k}\cong A_j$, where $A_j(Y)=|K^{(j/j+1)}(Y,\bullet)|$, in $\mathrm{HSpt}(\mathrm{Sm}//k)$. Crucially, the first sentence of the proof of Theorem 6.5.1 states the separate global isomorphism $\mathcal Z^j\cong Q_j$ in $\mathrm{HSpt}(k)$, meaning the pointwise category of §§1.2–1.3. Here the source has just defined $\mathcal Z^j$ as the ordinary Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectrum of its fibrant complex presheaf. Thus it is exactly the model $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}$ selected in [F9], up to the ordinary additive model comparison of [F7]. Import this assertion for those models; it is not an assertion about an unspecified component of an arbitrary representative of $\mathcal Z$. Theorem 5.3.1(3) and the proof of Theorem 6.4.2 then identify $A_j$ with the total spectrum $T_j$ of $T_{j,n}(Y)=(Q_j)^{(j/j+1)}(Y,n)$. The same proof constructs the weak equivalence $T_{j,n}(Y)\to K(z^j(Y,n),0)$ as its canonical zeroth Postnikov map: Corollary 5.3.2 identifies its summands with the ordinary coefficient $K_0(k(x))=\mathbb Z$ at admissible codimension-$j$ points. It verifies compatibility with the simplicial maps by the displayed generic G-theory/cycle diagrams on pp.36–37. The ordinary coefficient is dimension, sending the one-dimensional field class to $+1$. Theorem 6.4.1 checks homotopy invariance, Nisnevich excision and the finite-field axiom A3 for K-theory; Remark 9.0.4 removes an infinite-field restriction in the stable construction. Thus the required coniveau comparisons apply to this $k$ and $j$. Section 1.6, p.9, supplies functorial Postnikov constructions, applicable to a whole diagram; (6.4.1) uses the ordinary zeroth-layer endpoint $K(\pi_0E,0)$. Here $K(A,0)$ denotes the Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectrum of the abelian group $A$ in degree zero, not algebraic $K$-theory. Retain functorial replacement zigzags if the chosen model requires them. Sections 2.1–2.2, pp.10–12, supply the support construction's functoriality for open immersions, its layers and its natural transformations. In Theorem 6.4.1 and the proof of Theorem 6.4.2, $G(Z)$ means the algebraic $K$-theory of coherent sheaves on the support $Z$, which need not be smooth; the ambient scheme is smooth. For each admissible integral $Z$ with generic point $x$, the supported map $G(Z)\to T_{j,n}(Y)$ of that proof sends $[\mathcal O_Z]$ to coefficient $+1$ at $x$ and zero at the other codimension-$j$ generic points under Corollary 5.3.2. For precision, these imports include the resolution comparison $c_S:K(S)\to G(S)$ on the smooth regular ambient $S$ and its smooth open complement $S\setminus Z$, and the localization equivalence $\lambda_{S,Z}:G(Z)\to\operatorname{fib}(G(S)\to G(S\setminus Z))$. They are the comparisons used in the proof of Theorem 6.4.1, p.35, to obtain $K^Z(S)\simeq G(Z)$, and then in the displayed supported map of the proof of Theorem 6.4.2, p.36. Here $S=Y\times\Delta^n$ and $Z$ is an admissible integral closed subscheme. Throughout this auxiliary coniveau construction, $K$ denotes reference 2's chosen presheaf; it is not being identified with the EM perfect-complex model. The latter enters through [F1], while the connection between the two comparison legs is the assembled motivic spectrum in [F9]. Step 1.1 forms the supported inverse from $c$ and $\lambda$ as a natural zigzag on open pairs, checks the generator restriction square, and constructs the natural inverse group isomorphism before applying [F7].

[F9] Fix the following model interface from references 1–2. Let $L_j$ be the strict fibrant complex presheaf called $Z^j$ in Levine §6.5, pp.37–38, with homological degree $n$ read as cohomological degree $-n$. Set $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}=\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}(L_j)$, with the ordinary additive model comparison in [F7] understood. Section 6.5 supplies its natural localization equivalences $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}\to\Omega_{\mathbb P^1}G_{j+1}^{\mathrm{s}}$. Definition 8.1.1, Example 8.2.2(3) and the opening of §10.4 identify $\mathcal Z$ with the resulting $\mathbb P^1$-Omega spectrum $(G_0^{\mathrm{s}},G_1^{\mathrm{s}},\ldots)$ of ordinary sequential $S^1$-spectral presheaves. These entries are spectra, not their zeroth pointed spaces. Section 8.1 defines positive $\mathbb P^1$ suspension by the tail $(E_1,E_2,\ldots)$; §8.2 gives the derived zeroth-entry right adjoint, and Remark 8.2.1(2) states that weak equivalences between fibrant motivic models induce pointwise weak equivalences on their $S^1$-spectral entries. In the coherent notation of reference 1, §3.2, $\omega^\infty(E)=\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(k)}(M_k(-),E)$ and $\omega^{\infty,\mathrm{gr}}(E)^j=\omega^\infty(E\otimes\mathbb T_k^{\otimes j})$. Its proof of Theorem 6.3 characterizes this same assembled $\mathcal Z$ by $\omega^{\infty,\mathrm{gr}}(\mathcal Z)^j=\mathcal Z^j$, where the right side is the complex-valued presheaf just recalled from Levine §6.5, viewed as a spectral presheaf by ordinary Eilenberg–Mac Lane realization. Consequently its coherent spectral value is $\mathcal H(L_j)$, naturally identified with $J(G_j^{\mathrm{s}})$ by [F7]. This imports the model assembly and its derived-evaluation interface. It does not identify $L_j$ with the raw cycles by a sheaf-local inference; that further pointwise comparison is precisely [F8].

### External input
Reference 0 imports the actual graded identifications, cdh descent and regular-Noetherian tower comparison. Its Remark 3.6 attributes the slice foundation to then-in-preparation work; that foundation is not reconstructed here. Reference 0, Remark 3.6, and the existing augmented-tower supplier import the identification of the cdh tower with the evaluated absolute slice tower on qcqs equal-characteristic schemes. Reference 1, Definition 4.15 and Remark 4.21, supplies its absolute graded evaluation and represented smooth-field restriction in [F2]; the classical motivic spectrum is supplied by its proof of Theorem 6.3. Taking the graded cofiber and restricting these coherent functors is the local application in step 1.1. No affine-lisse-to-scheme-lisse identification is imported or inferred. Its §3.2 and proof of Theorem 6.3, together with reference 2, §§6.5, 8.1–8.2 and 10.4, supply the precise assembled-spectrum and derived-evaluation interface in [F9]. The local application in step 1.1 identifies its entry with the sequential presheaf actually used below, with no extra shift. Reference 2 supplies the separate pointwise isomorphisms and actual coniveau/Postnikov models in [F8], with the exact model meaning of [F7]. Its §1.6 supplies the functorial Postnikov construction, §§2.1–2.2 the open-functorial support operations, and Theorem 6.4.1 with the proof of Theorem 6.4.2 the supported G-theory maps and positive coefficient. The open-restriction square, the supported inverse of the resolution comparison on the whole open-pair diagram, and the natural inverse on abelian-group diagrams in step 1.1 are local deductions from these operations. The auxiliary coniveau K-model is used only in that leg; its interface with the represented leg is the common assembled spectrum, not an unstated identification with global vector-bundle K-theory. They supply the whole-diagram comparison before realization. In particular, use the explicit $\mathrm{HSpt}(k)$ assertion at the start of the proof of Theorem 6.5.1, together with Corollary 4.1.5 and the construction in Theorem 6.4.2; a Nisnevich-derived comparison alone is not promoted to a pointwise comparison. The local simplicial-realization supplier in [F7] identifies the ordinary additive endpoints with the fixed coherent realization. Their hypotheses and reading limits are in the six-field source records. Step 1.1 checks the application to the whole open diagram and then constructs the separated-open extension; step 2.1 composes with the actual tower map. These are local deductions from precise imported premises, not a local proof of their deep foundations.

### Proof
1.1 First check the absolute slice evaluation in [F2]. Use exactly the cdh-to-slice tower identification imported in [F1], including its transition maps, on the qcqs equal-characteristic schemes in [F5]. For each such $Y$, the functor $\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,-)$ preserves limits and zero objects. It therefore commutes with loops, hence also with their inverse suspension; since a cofiber is the suspension of a fiber, it preserves finite cofibers. Taking the degree-$j$ cofiber of that tower identification therefore gives $\mathcal H(C_{j,F}(Y))[2j]\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,s^j\mathrm{KGL}_Y)$. These are maps of the supplied whole presheaves: the tower identification and its graded cofiber retain all restriction maps and composition coherences. Reference 1, Definition 4.15, identifies the right endpoint with $\mathbb Z(j)^{\mathbb A}(Y)[2j]$, and Remark 4.21 identifies its restriction to $\mathcal O_k$ with $R_j(Y)$, using its smooth-pullback comparison. Thus the shift cancels before the represented-model leg, and this leg uses the actual absolute slice map fixed in [F1]. No comparison between scheme-level and affine Kan extensions is needed. This application retains the explicit source-version qualification of reference 0, Remark 3.6; its foundational tower identification remains imported. Now establish the represented-model leg of [F2]. Choose the single equivalence $s^0\mathrm{KGL}_k\simeq\mathcal Z$ supplied by its unit comparison through $s^0(1_k)$. By [F9], applying the coherent functor $E\mapsto\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(k)}(M_k(-),\mathbb T_k^{\otimes j}\otimes E)$ gives $R_j\simeq\omega^{\infty,\mathrm{gr}}(\mathcal Z)^j\simeq\mathcal H(L_j)\simeq J(G_j^{\mathrm{s}})$, using the Bott/slice equivalence in [F2] for the first term and [F7] for the last. This computes the derived evaluation on the selected Omega-spectrum model; it does not evaluate an arbitrary nonfibrant representative. The shift is fixed: $\omega^{\infty,\mathrm{gr}}(\mathcal Z)^j=\mathcal Z(j)[2j]$ in reference 1, §3.2, whereas $R_j$ is already the unshifted graded spectrum, so the entry is the unshifted $L_j$. Every arrow in this calculation is a transformation of whole presheaves on smooth separated schemes. In particular precomposition by $M_k(V)\to M_k(Y)$ for an open immersion $V\hookrightarrow Y$ supplies its restriction and all composition coherences. The additive endpoint identification also commutes with these maps by [F7]. Thus the same $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}$ is available both as the represented endpoint and as the source of [F8]'s isomorphism. Now construct the last leg of [F2] from [F8]. Restrict its global $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}\cong Q_j$ to $\mathcal O_k$, then use Corollary 4.1.5 to compare $Q_j$ with $A_j$, and the coniveau self-layer comparison to compare $A_j$ with $T_j$. These are the specified isomorphisms of whole presheaves in the pointwise localization, not an inference from a derived-sheaf isomorphism. For the final $T_j$-to-cycle leg, use the fixed rectified presheaf $Q_j$ and its strict diagram $(Y,[n])\mapsto T_{j,n}(Y)$ on $\mathcal O_k^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}$. Here $\Delta$ is the category of finite nonempty ordered sets and order-preserving maps. The support construction in [F8] supplies both open and simplicial arrows in this same diagram. For each admissible integral $Z\subseteq Y\times\Delta^n$, let $a_{Y,n}([Z])$ be the image of $[\mathcal O_Z]\in G_0(Z)=\pi_0G(Z)$ under [F8]'s supported map, and extend by integral linearity. By Corollary 5.3.2 and Theorem 6.4.2, $a_{Y,n}:z^j(Y,n)\to\pi_0T_{j,n}(Y)$ is an isomorphism: it sends each basis element to coefficient $+1$ at its own generic point and zero at the others. They also show $\pi_rT_{j,n}(Y)=0$ for $r\ne0$. For an open immersion $h:V\hookrightarrow Y$, put $Z_V=Z\times_Y V$. Exact restriction of coherent sheaves sends $\mathcal O_Z$ to $\mathcal O_{Z_V}$, or to zero if the restriction is empty. Write $S=Y\times\Delta^n$ and $S_V=V\times\Delta^n$. Use exactly [F8]'s auxiliary resolution/localization maps: $c_S$, $c_{S\setminus Z}$ and $\lambda_{S,Z}$, and the corresponding maps for $(S_V,Z_V)$. Exact open restriction commutes with the comparison functors defining $c$ and with closed pushforward from $Z$; its square with localization therefore induces the restriction map on the $G$-theory fibers. Thus $e_{S,Z}:=\operatorname{fib}(c_S,c_{S\setminus Z}):K^Z(S)\to\operatorname{fib}(G(S)\to G(S\setminus Z))$ is a natural equivalence on this diagram of open pairs, since both components of $c$ are equivalences by [F8]. The supported comparison is the natural zigzag $G(Z)\xrightarrow{\lambda_{S,Z}}\operatorname{fib}(G(S)\to G(S\setminus Z))\xleftarrow{e_{S,Z}}K^Z(S)$. Invert $e$ in the localization of the whole diagram, rather than selecting unrelated inverses on its values. If $q_{S,Z}:K^Z(S)\to T_{j,n}(Y)$ denotes [F8]'s supported coniveau map, the generator just defined is consequently $a_{Y,n}([Z])=\pi_0(q_{S,Z}e_{S,Z}^{-1}\lambda_{S,Z})([\mathcal O_Z])$. This makes the particular supported map, its direction and its restriction naturality explicit, using only the source's imported resolution/localization equivalences and its auxiliary $K$-model. The support-index maps, layer maps for the fixed $Q_j$, homotopy colimits and cofibers used in the supported map of Theorem 6.4.2 are functorial in this square; retain the natural zigzags representing any inverted comparison. On $\pi_0$ it follows that $\pi_0(h^*)a_{Y,n}=a_{V,n}h^*$, with precisely [F3]'s raw cycle restriction on the right. This checks the equality on every generator, including empty restrictions; an arbitrary open immersion factors through its open image and uses the same pullback operations. Theorem 6.4.2's two G-theory/cycle squares on pp.36–37 establish the face-map equality for these very generator maps. For a degeneracy, the simplex map is flat; its exact pullback carries the supported structure sheaf to its pulled-back structure sheaf, and the generic lengths give the ordinary flat cycle pullback. This agrees with the simplicial cycle structure of §6.2. Consequently $a$ is a natural isomorphism of abelian-group diagrams on the entire product category. Write $b=a^{-1}$; for every diagram arrow $r$, multiplying $\pi_0(r)a=a r$ by the inverse components gives $b\pi_0(r)=r b$, so these inverses are natural together. Apply the functorial Postnikov construction of [F8] to the entire strict spectral diagram and then apply $K(-,0)$ to $b$. This gives a natural comparison, allowing the functorial connective-cover zigzag, $T_{j,\bullet}\xleftarrow{\sim}\tau_{\geq0}T_{j,\bullet}\longrightarrow K(\pi_0T_{j,\bullet},0)\xrightarrow{K(b,0)}K(z^j(-,\bullet),0)$. Every arrow is a degreewise stable weak equivalence because $T_{j,n}(Y)$ has only its zeroth homotopy group and $b$ is a group isomorphism. The arrows and replacement zigzags are functors on the entire product diagram, so they retain all composition compatibilities before localization. Taking the source's total simplicial spectra gives $T_j\simeq B^{\mathrm{s}}$ in $\mathrm{HSpt}(\mathcal O_k)$. By [F7], ordinary additive realization agrees naturally with $\mathrm{EM}_{\mathrm{s}}$ of the unnormalized alternating-face complex, retaining the positive integer generator. Composing yields $G_j^{\mathrm{s}}\cong B^{\mathrm{s}}$ in that pointwise localization. Apply the single model-to-coherent-diagram functor of [F7]. It inverts pointwise weak equivalences, so the ordinary localization property factors it through $\mathrm{HSpt}(\mathcal O_k)$. The composite isomorphism and its inverse become inverse in $h\operatorname{Fun}(N(\mathcal O_k)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})$. Reference 3 represents this isomorphism by an equivalence of entire coherent presheaves. The additive comparison identifies the right endpoint with the fixed $\mathcal H(z^j(-,\bullet))$, including open restrictions and shifts. Compose with the absolute slice-tower and represented-spectrum maps of [F2], already maps of whole functors, and fix this separated comparison with its stated normalization. Now fix a smooth finite-type $X/k$ and put $P(W)=\mathcal H(C_{j,F}(W))[2j]$ and $B(W)=\mathcal H(z^j(W,\bullet))$ for opens $W\subseteq X$. These are coherent spectral presheaves by [F1]–[F4]. For either $E=P$ or $B$, let $\mathcal S(W)$ be the poset of separated open subschemes $A\subseteq W$ and set $D_E(W)=\lim_{A\in\mathcal S(W)^{\mathrm{op}}}E(A)$. The index is small by [A1]; its arrows are restrictions. This defines a coherent presheaf by restriction of limiting cones and a natural map $u_E:E\to D_E$. If $W$ is separated, the unique arrows from the initial object $W$ in the opposite index identify the space of coherent cones with its component at $W$, so $u_E(W)$ is an equivalence. Here is an explicit comparison of the varying indices. Write $C_W=\mathcal S(W)^{\mathrm{op}}$, and for any open $U\subseteq W$ define $t_U:C_W\to C_U$ by $t_U(A)=A\cap U$. It is defined by [F6], including when $U$ is not separated. Let $i_U:C_U\hookrightarrow C_W$ be inclusion. Then $t_Ui_U=\mathrm{id}$, and the restrictions $A\to A\cap U$ give a natural transformation $\eta_U:\mathrm{id}_{C_W}\to i_Ut_U$ satisfying $t_U\eta_U=\mathrm{id}$ and $\eta_Ui_U=\mathrm{id}$. For a coherent diagram $F:N(C_U)\to\mathrm{Sp}$ and a test spectrum $T$, denote its coherent cone space by $\operatorname{Cone}_{C_U}(T,F)=\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Fun}(N(C_U),\mathrm{Sp})}(\operatorname{const}T,F)$. Restriction along $i_U$ and precomposition along $t_U$ give maps between $\operatorname{Cone}_{C_W}(T,Ft_U)$ and $\operatorname{Cone}_{C_U}(T,F)$. Restriction after precomposition is the identity because $t_Ui_U=\mathrm{id}$. In the other order a cone $\alpha$ is sent to $\alpha i_Ut_U$; naturality along $\eta_U$ identifies it with $\alpha$, since both its constant source and $Ft_U$ send $\eta_U$ to identity arrows. This is a homotopy of maps of cone spaces, not just an equality of their components: the nerve of $\eta_U$ is a map $N(C_W)\times\Delta[1]\to N(C_W)$, and whiskering cones with this map supplies the homotopy and all its simplex compatibilities. Thus the two cone-space maps are inverse equivalences, naturally in $T$ and $F$. By the limit universal property in [F4], restriction along $i_U$ consequently gives $\lim_{A\in C_W}E(A\cap U)\simeq D_E(U)$. These identifications respect inclusions of opens: for $U'\subseteq U$, intersection twice equals intersection with $U'$, and the transformations $\eta$ are the corresponding composable restriction arrows. In particular they identify the maps induced by $E(A\cap U)\to E(A\cap U')$ with the canonical restriction $D_E(U)\to D_E(U')$. Now let $W=R\cup S$. For each $A\in C_W$, [F6] makes $A\cap R$, $A\cap S$ and $A\cap R\cap S$ separated, and [F3]–[F4] make the canonical restriction square a pullback. These squares form a coherent diagram in $A$, because they are restrictions of the single presheaf $E$. Thus the canonical transformation $E(A)\to E(A\cap R)\times_{E(A\cap R\cap S)}E(A\cap S)$ is an equivalence of whole $C_W$-diagrams, by reference 4 applied to its equivalence components. Taking its limit gives $D_E(W)\simeq (\lim_{C_W}E(A\cap R))\times_{\lim_{C_W}E(A\cap R\cap S)}(\lim_{C_W}E(A\cap S))$. To justify this interchange without selecting objectwise pullback inverses, for any $T$ the cone space on the pullback diagram is the pullback of the three cone spaces: a coherent map to a pullback is a pair of maps with their specified homotopy, and imposing compatibility with every simplex of $N(C_W)$ imposes that same condition on the cones. Equivalently, both orders of limits classify cones on the product of $N(C_W)$ and the span indexing the pullback. This proves the interchange by [F4]'s mapping-space universal property, naturally in all restriction maps. Apply the preceding intersection comparison for $U=R,S,R\cap S$. It identifies this pullback with $D_E(R)\times_{D_E(R\cap S)}D_E(S)$ and identifies its map from $D_E(W)$ with the canonical restrictions. This proves binary Mayer–Vietoris for $D_E$, including all higher coherences and naturality under inclusions of separated opens. Now choose a finite affine cover of $W$ by [F5]. Induct on its size using binary Mayer–Vietoris for both $E$ and $D_E$. At an induction step the new intersection is an open of the final affine chart, hence separated by [F6], so $u_E$ is already an equivalence there; it is also one on that chart and on the preceding union by induction. Pullbacks preserve these equivalences. The empty case has value zero on both sides, since the empty open is separated. Therefore $u_E(W)$ is an equivalence for every $W$. The global separated comparison [F2] gives an equivalence of the two limiting diagrams, hence $D_P\simeq D_B$. Compose $P\xrightarrow{u_P}D_P\simeq D_B\xleftarrow{u_B}B$ and invert only natural equivalences. This gives a coherent comparison on all opens, independent of the finite cover used to verify equivalence, and equal to the selected comparison on separated opens. The indexing poset consists intrinsically of separated opens of $W$, so the construction commutes with open immersions also when the ambient $X$ changes. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8, F9, given, construct]

2.1 Apply [F1] on every open, which is in the same smooth finite-type qcqs domain by [F5]. Taking the actual graded cofiber of its augmented tower comparison gives $\operatorname{gr}^jK\simeq\mathcal H(\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}})[2j]\simeq P$. Compose with step 1.1. The shifts cancel because $C_{j,F}$ compares with the reindexed raw cycle complex shifted by $-2j$, and [F4] commutes with integer shifts. Thus the last term is $B$, with $\pi_iB=H_i z^j$. All constructions were maps or equivalences of coherent diagrams, so restricting this diagram to any open-complement arrow and taking its fiber preserves the equivalence. No arbitrary objectwise choice, cross-weight compatibility or cycle multiplication has entered. [F1, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎

## Dévissage of K zero with regular ambient support

[Open item](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage) · level 16


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds of the supplied constructions. Let $U$ be a Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme that is regular at every point and $i:T\hookrightarrow U$ any closed subscheme with defining ideal $\mathcal I=\ker(\mathcal O_U\to i_*\mathcal O_T)$. Let $K_0^{|T|}(U)=\pi_0K_{|T|}(U)$ for the specified nonconnective K-theory of the current supported perfect category. There is a canonical isomorphism
$$K_0^{|T|}(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}G_0(T).$$
For $X=T$ or $U$, $G_0(X)$ is the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes $[F]$ of coherent $\mathcal O_X$-modules, subject to $[F]=[F']+[F'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to F'\to F\to F''\to0$. For a supported perfect object $E$, this isomorphism is the coherent-cohomology Euler class followed by ideal-power dévissage, explicitly
$$[E]\longmapsto\sum_n(-1)^n\sum_{r\geq0}[\mathcal I^rH^n(E)/\mathcal I^{r+1}H^n(E)].$$
Each quotient is regarded as a coherent $\mathcal O_T$-module; both sums are finite after deleting zero terms, and the resulting class is independent of the chosen annihilating powers. The inverse sends $[F]$ to the class of the perfect $U$-object $i_*F[0]$.

Under this isomorphism the support-to-global map $K_0^{|T|}(U)\to K_0(U)$ is coherent pushforward $G_0(T)\xrightarrow{i_*}G_0(U)$ followed by the regular ambient perfect comparison $G_0(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}K_0(U)$, sending $[M]$ to $[M[0]]$. No regularity of $T$, global vector-bundle resolution or resolution-property assumption is imposed. No analogous identification for singular ambient $U$ is asserted.

### Facts & Assumptions

[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is used through the essentially small perfect-category presentations and the specified nonconnective K-theory model in [F1] and [F3]. The fixed universes satisfy [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and allow the enlargements required by these constructions. Universe existence is a standing size assumption separate from Choice; the site bounds remain those fixed in the Statement. These assumptions are retained in all three steps, with no additional choice needed for the finite sums.

**Given:** $U,T,i,\mathcal I$ as in the Statement, AC and fixed suitable universes. The supported category is the supplied full sheaf-derived $\mathrm{Perf}_{|T|}(U)$, not complexes internally constructed in a coherent abelian category.

**Goal:** Prove the stated comparison and support-to-global compatibility in the current nonconnective model.

[F1] Let $\mathcal A_T$ denote the category of coherent $\mathcal O_U$-modules whose stalks vanish outside the underlying closed set $|T|$, with all module-sheaf morphisms. Its group $G_0(\mathcal A_T)$ uses the same isomorphism-class generators and short exact relations as $G_0(X)$ above. [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-heart) embeds each $M\in\mathcal A_T$ as the actual supported perfect object $M[0]$, the complex with $M$ in degree zero and zero in all other degrees. [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-euler](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-euler) and [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-postnikov](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-postnikov) give the finite additive coherent Euler map and its Postnikov inverse, using the general spectral $\pi_0$ premise [thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input](item-thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input). These comparisons commute with support inclusion, and the same construction with $T=U$ gives the ambient comparison. The Euler supplier also checks that Noetherian $U$ is qcqs, as required by the selected nonconnective interface.

[F2] [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-ideal-filtration](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-ideal-filtration) and [lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-devissage-naturality](item-lem-author-7ef3227685d9-supported-k0-devissage-naturality) give finite ideal-power dévissage, its inverse formula and coherent pushforward compatibility. Here $\mathcal I^0M=M$ and $\mathcal I^rM$ is the subsheaf locally generated by products of $r$ sections of $\mathcal I$ acting on sections of $M$. Closed direct image is $(i_*F)(V)=F(i^{-1}V)$, with its $\mathcal O_U$-action induced by the structure-sheaf map. These suppliers give an exact equivalence between coherent sheaves on $T$ and coherent sheaves on $U$ annihilated by $\mathcal I$, so the displayed quotient sheaves on $T$ are unambiguously defined. They supply exactness of closed coherent direct image and the resulting map on short exact presentations.

[F3] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) and [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) supply the full stable idempotent-complete supported categories, their essentially small presentations and the actual support inclusion. [lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation](item-lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation) identifies object/cofiber and triangle presentations.

[F4] The geometric formulation uses [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) for the finite affine Noetherian cover and [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point) for regularity at every point, meaning regularity of every local ring without any base-field smoothness assumption. Finite dimension is the chain dimension of [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space), including its empty-space convention. [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) defines the closed immersion by a homeomorphism onto a closed subset and a surjection of structure sheaves. [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme) supplies the coherent modules in the statement and the exact supplied comparisons. These are precisely the geometric hypotheses and object types of [F1]–[F2].

### Proof
1.1 By [F1], the degree-zero group of the current nonconnective functor identifies with $G_0(\mathcal A_T)$ by the coherent-cohomology Euler map. By [F2], closed coherent pushforward gives $G_0(T)\simeq G_0(\mathcal A_T)$. Compose the former map with the inverse of the latter. This is an isomorphism, and its displayed formula is exactly the Euler sum followed by the ideal-power quotient sum. Finiteness and independence follow from [F1]–[F2]. [F1, F2, F3, F4, A1]

2.1 The two inverse constructions take $[F]$ first to $[i_*F]$ and then to the class of $i_*F[0]$ by [F1]–[F2]. By the exact closed-direct-image identification in [F2], $i_*F$ lies in $\mathcal A_T$; the heart embedding in [F1] therefore supplies this very degree-zero sheaf as an actual supported perfect object. Thus the inverse has the asserted actual-object interpretation without any global locally free resolution. [F1, F2, F3, step 1.1]

3.1 The Euler/Postnikov comparisons commute with supported-to-global inclusion by [F1]. Under abelian dévissage the corresponding map is precisely coherent pushforward by [F2]. Their commutative squares compose to the stated support-to-global formula, using the same ambient comparison with $T=U$. This establishes the full proposition in the specified nonconnective model. [F1, F2, F3, step 1.1, step 2.1] ∎


## Relative objects for empty and split pairs

[Open item](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-relative-empty-split-cases) · level 16


### Statement
Let $\mathcal A$ be an abelian category, and let $A,B,C,D$ be cochain complexes in $\mathcal A$. All maps below are cochain maps, direct sums are biproducts with componentwise differential, and $0$ denotes the zero complex. Use $A[s]^n=A^{n+s}$ with differential $(-1)^sd_A$. Use the cone $Q(f)^n=Y^n\oplus X^{n+1}$, $d(y,x)=(d_Yy+f(x),-d_Xx)$ for $f:X\to Y$. Define the support-fiber model by
$$F(f)^n=X^n\oplus Y^{n-1},\qquad d(x,y)=(d_Xx,f(x)-d_Yy).$$
Its projection to $X$ gives the fiber triangle in $D(\mathcal A)$. Derived-category assertions use the standing size hypothesis of a small category of complexes or supplied small cofinal denominator families. Contractible means that a degree $-1$ morphism $h$ satisfies $dh+hd=\operatorname{id}$.

The cone of $\operatorname{id}_B$ is contractible, the cone of $0\to B$ is $B$, and the cone of the split inclusion $A\to A\oplus B$, $a\mapsto(a,0)$, is quasi-isomorphic to $B$. Dually, the fibers of $\operatorname{id}_C:C\to C$, $C\to0$, and the projection $C\oplus D\to C$ are respectively zero, $C$, and $D$ in $D(\mathcal A)$.

The same boundary calculations hold for chosen cofiber and fiber triangles in any supplied triangulated category, with $A,B,C,D$ now interpreted as objects of that category: a cofiber of $f:X\to Y$ is the third object of a distinguished triangle $X\xrightarrow{f}Y\to R\to X[1]$, and a fiber is the first object of a distinguished triangle $S\to X\xrightarrow{f}Y\to S[1]$. The cofibers of $\operatorname{id}_B$, $0\to B$ and $A\to A\oplus B$ are respectively isomorphic to $0,B,B$; the fibers of $\operatorname{id}_C$, $C\to0$ and $C\oplus D\to C$ are respectively isomorphic to $0,C,D$. The isomorphisms can be chosen compatibly with the maps of the triangles; uniqueness or a functorial choice is not asserted.

For the conditional geometric application, let $U\hookrightarrow X$ be an open immersion of schemes and write $Z=X\setminus U$ for its closed complement as a set. Supply a covariant motive-valued functor $M$ into a specified triangulated category and a contravariant coefficient functor $C$ into a specified triangulated category, which may in particular be a derived category of complexes. Their domains must contain the schemes and inclusion maps being used. Choose a relative object $R(X,U)$ by a distinguished triangle for the actual map $M(U)\to M(X)$, and a support object $S_Z(X)$ by a distinguished triangle for the actual restriction $C(X)\to C(U)$. Empty support gives $R(X,X)\cong0$ and $S_{\varnothing}(X)\cong0$. If $M(\varnothing)\cong0$, full support gives $R(X,\varnothing)\cong M(X)$; if $C(\varnothing)\cong0$, it gives $S_X(X)\cong C(X)$.

For a split pair $X=U\coprod Z$, meaning a decomposition into disjoint open-and-closed subschemes, additionally require that the map $M(U)\oplus M(Z)\to M(X)$ induced by the actual two inclusions is an isomorphism, respectively that the map $C(X)\to C(U)\oplus C(Z)$ given by the actual two restrictions is an isomorphism. These respective hypotheses give $R(X,U)\cong M(Z)$ and $S_Z(X)\cong C(Z)$. An isomorphism of the objects without compatibility with those maps does not suffice. These formal boundary cases do not establish purity for a positive-codimension smooth pair.

[F8] [math:def-complex-homotopy-and-contractibility-in-an-additive-category](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-complex-homotopy-and-contractibility-in-an-additive-category) defines cochain homotopy $f-g=dh+hd$, contractibility and degreewise sums of homotopies in any additive category, hence in $\mathcal A$.
