### Proof
1.1 For $Q(\operatorname{id}_B)$ the degree $-1$ map $h(b_0,b_1)=(0,b_0)$ satisfies $dh(b_0,b_1)=(b_0,-d_Bb_0)$ and $hd(b_0,b_1)=(0,d_Bb_0+b_1)$, so $dh+hd=\operatorname{id}$. The cone of $0\to B$ is exactly $B$. The split inclusion $A\to A\oplus B$ is termwise monic and has quotient $B$; [F2] therefore gives its cone quasi-isomorphism to $B$. For a general $f:X\to Y$, the signed permutation $(x,y)\mapsto(y,-x)$ identifies the displayed $F(f)$ with $Q(f)[-1]$: the shifted cone differential sends $(y,-x)$ to $(-d_Yy+f(x),-d_Xx)$, the image of $d(x,y)$. The signed rotation of the cone triangle has first arrow minus the shifted cone projection; it therefore becomes the displayed projection $F(f)\to X$. This proves the claimed fiber interpretation in $\mathcal A$. For $f=\operatorname{id}_C$, $k(c,b)=(b,0)$ gives $dk(c,b)=(d_Cb,b)$ and $kd(c,b)=(c-d_Cb,0)$, so $F(\operatorname{id}_C)$ is contractible. The formula gives $F(C\to0)=C$. Reordering the three coordinates of $F(C\oplus D\to C)$ gives the complex $D\oplus F(\operatorname{id}_C)$. Write $p$ for projection to $D$ and $i$ for its inclusion. Then $pi=\operatorname{id}_D$, and the degree $-1$ morphism $H=0\oplus k$ satisfies $\operatorname{id}-ip=dH+Hd$ on $D\oplus F(\operatorname{id}_C)$. By [F8] this is a cochain homotopy; reindexing $C_n=C^{-n}$ and $s_n=H^{-n}$ makes it a chain homotopy. The homotopy-class category of [F8] therefore makes $p,i$ inverse. They remain inverse in the derived localization of [F2]. A contractible complex similarly has identity equal to zero in that homotopy category and is isomorphic to the zero complex there and in its derived localization. This proves all complex assertions, with [F3] recovering exactly the existing support convention for abelian groups. [F1, F2, F3, F8, algebra, construct]

2.1 In a general triangulated category, [F4] gives the identity triangle $B\xrightarrow{1}B\to0\to B[1]$, its rotations giving $0\to B\xrightarrow{1}B\to0$, and the split triangle $A\to A\oplus B\to B\to A[1]$. These exhibit the asserted cofibers. The identity triangle $C\xrightarrow{1}C\to0\to C[1]$ exhibits the fiber of $C\to0$; the triangle $0\to C\xrightarrow{1}C\to0$ exhibits the fiber of $\operatorname{id}_C$; and the split triangle $D\to D\oplus C\to C\to D[1]$, transported by the biproduct swap, exhibits the fiber of $C\oplus D\to C$. For any chosen completion of one of these arrows, rotate if necessary to make that arrow first. Apply [F5] with the identity isomorphisms on its source and target to obtain a compatible isomorphism on the third object, and then rotate back. The same argument applies to an arrow identified with one of these arrows by supplied source and target isomorphisms. It asserts neither uniqueness nor a functorial cone. Now [F6] makes the map for $U=X$ an identity, and the empty-value assumptions identify the maps for $U=\varnothing$ with $0\to M(X)$ and $C(X)\to0$. For the split pair the two required isomorphisms identify the actual arrows with the summand inclusion and projection, respectively. The chosen relative/support triangles therefore have exactly the objects asserted in the Statement. When the optional comparison data of [F7] are also supplied, retaining [A1] for an article-theory application, that result applied to the chosen cofiber and the actual restriction arrow identifies its enhanced Hom with this same computed support fiber, in the target and under the additional hypotheses specified there. No such comparison, complex model for an arbitrary motive, or positive-codimension purity is needed for the formal triangulated calculations. [F1, F4, F5, F6, F7, A1, given, step 1.1] ∎


## The supported second stage has the actual augmentation inverse

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-second-stage-inverse) · level 16


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible universes and smallness conventions of the supplied spectral constructions. Let $U$ be a smooth finite-type scheme of pure dimension three over a field $k$, and let $C\subset U$ be an integral closed curve of codimension two. Put $V=U\setminus |C|$ with its open-subscheme structure. Let $F^\bullet K$ be the specified motivic filtration, with its actual stage-zero identification and coherent augmentation to $K$.

For a homological complex $D_\bullet$, write $\mathcal H(D_\bullet)$ for its Eilenberg–Mac Lane realization in spectra, with $\pi_i\mathcal H(D_\bullet)=H_i(D_\bullet)$ for every integer $i$. Set $B_j(Y)=\mathcal H(z^j(Y,\bullet))$ and $M_j(Y)=H(\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}(Y))$, where the latter $H$ realizes a cochain complex with $\pi_iH(A)=H^{-i}(A)$. Assume that, for $j=0,1,2$, the actual smooth comparison supplies specified equivalences
$$\operatorname{gr}^jK(Y)\simeq M_j(Y)[2j]\simeq B_j(Y),\qquad Y=U,V,$$
compatible with the restriction arrow from $U$ to $V$. Thus the actual layer shift and the cycle-spectrum convention are part of the comparison premise, rather than consequences of an abstract isomorphism of groups. Here $[m]$ is spectral suspension by the integer $m$.

Define
$$K_C(U)=\operatorname{fib}(K(U)\to K(V)),\qquad F^j_CK(U)=\operatorname{fib}(F^jK(U)\to F^jK(V)),$$
$$\operatorname{gr}^j_CK(U)=\operatorname{cofib}(F^{j+1}_CK(U)\to F^j_CK(U)).$$
Then the supported graded spectra in weights zero and one are zero, and the augmentations
$$F^2_CK(U)\longrightarrow F^1_CK(U)\longrightarrow F^0_CK(U)=K_C(U)$$
are equivalences. Thus its supported leading weight-two homomorphism is unambiguously
$$\pi_0K_C(U)\xrightarrow{(\pi_0\epsilon_2)^{-1}}\pi_0F^2_CK(U)\longrightarrow\pi_0\operatorname{gr}^2_CK(U),$$
where $\epsilon_2$ is the actual stage-to-zero augmentation. Under the specified comparison its target is
$$\pi_0\operatorname{gr}^2_CK(U)=H_C^{4,2}(U)=H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)=\mathbb Z[C].$$
Here $H_C^{a,j}(U)=\pi_{-a}\operatorname{fib}(M_j(U)\to M_j(V))$, and $\mathbb Z[C]$ is the group of integral multiples of the positive formal cycle $[C]$, not its image in an ambient Chow group.

For any specified natural augmented tower comparison, the leading maps commute with the induced maps on support fibers and supported graded spectra provided the target supported stage-two augmentation is also an equivalence. In particular, this condition holds for comparisons which are equivalences on all stages and on the underlying spectra on both $U$ and $V$. Its generator value remains a separate normalization input; it is not implied by the equivalence alone.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** AC and the compatible size conventions, $U,C,k,V$ as stated, the specified coherently augmented filtration, and the specified restriction-compatible equivalences $\operatorname{gr}^jK\simeq M_j[2j]\simeq B_j$ for $j=0,1,2$ on both schemes. The supported Bloch comparison is deduced below from localization, rather than added as an unstated premise. For inverse-augmentation transport the target supported stage-two augmentation is also an equivalence; equivalences on all stages and underlying spectra on both schemes are a sufficient condition.

**Goal:** Justify the domain, target and exact meaning of the leading supported map before its generator is computed.

[A1] The assumed AC discharges the Choice hypotheses of the supplied K-theory, filtration, stable-spectrum, Eilenberg–Mac Lane and supported Bloch interfaces. The same fixed compatible universes and smallness conventions apply throughout; no additional choice of an inverse or a normalization is made. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice), [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration), [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface), [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index), [def-supported-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-supported-higher-cycle-complex)

[F1] Under AC and the stated smooth finite-type pure-three-dimensional and integral codimension-two curve hypotheses, the two lower supported cycle complexes vanish and the bottom weight-two group is $\mathbb Z[C]$. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom)

[F2] The support construction preserves the graded cofiber sequences and naturally identifies supported graded cofibers with fibers of graded restriction. [lem-filtered-support-exactness](item-lem-filtered-support-exactness)

[F3] The specified motivic filtration has stage zero naturally $K$ and a coherent augmentation. Decreasing filtrations have graded cofibers $\operatorname{cofib}(F^{j+1}\to F^j)$; support spectra and supported cohomology use fibers of restriction. [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration), [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum), [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support)

[F4] A specified natural augmented filtered comparison which is an equivalence on all stages and underlying spectra on a scheme and its open complement induces supported stage, underlying and graded equivalences, preserving augmentations and graded projections. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport)

[F5] The supported Bloch complex $z_C^j(U,\bullet)$ is the free subcomplex on admissible cycles contained in $|C|\times\Delta^n$, with zero negative terms. For $A_\bullet=z^j(U,\bullet)$, $B_\bullet=z^j(V,\bullet)$ and restriction $f:A_\bullet\to B_\bullet$, its inclusion into the homotopy-fiber model $A_n\oplus B_{n+1}$, with differential $(a,b)\mapsto(\partial a,f(a)-\partial b)$, is a quasi-isomorphism. Equivalently, $\ker f=z_C^j(U,\bullet)$ and the cokernel of restriction is acyclic. This localization comparison holds under the stated smooth finite-type hypotheses, without a separatedness assumption. [def-supported-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-supported-higher-cycle-complex)

[F6] Spectra form the supplied stable category; fibers and cofibers are coherent pullbacks and pushouts along zero, and the constructions and all integer homotopy groups are functorial. A zero cofiber makes the original map an equivalence, since its defining pushout square is also a pullback. Spectral suspension is an equivalence and commutes with fibers. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface)

[F7] The motivic coefficient complex is $\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}$; its unshifted realization is $M_j$. The realization convention gives $\pi_iH(A)=H^{-i}(A)$ for cochains, and $\pi_i(E[2j])=\pi_{i-2j}E$. The support convention therefore gives $\pi_i(M_{j,C}[2j])=H_C^{2j-i,j}(U)$, where $M_{j,C}=\operatorname{fib}(M_j(U)\to M_j(V))$. These conventions do not themselves supply the actual graded equivalences assumed in the Statement. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology), [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index), [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support)

[F8] A field and a $k$-scheme have their usual field and structure-morphism meanings. Smoothness and finite type refer to $U\to\operatorname{Spec}k$; finite type includes quasi-compactness. Integral means nonempty, reduced and irreducible; closed subschemes are closed immersions. The complement carries its restricted structure sheaf and open immersion. Dimension is the supremum of lengths of strict chains of nonempty irreducible closed subsets. Pure dimension three means that every irreducible component has dimension three, and a curve has dimension one; codimension two here means that the local ring of $U$ at the generic point of $C$ has dimension two. [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field), [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base), [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes), [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism), [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme), [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes), [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes), [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space)

[F9] Open restriction in a smooth finite-type scheme gives the smooth finite-type open complement and its actual Bloch restriction chain map. Reference 1 in External input makes both schemes Noetherian; reference 2 makes them quasi-compact and quasi-separated (qcqs). These terms have the meanings of the declared scheme definitions, so both schemes lie in the domains of the specified motivic and K-theory functors. [lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction](item-lem-author-75596422a482-open-restriction), [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme), [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme)

[F10] Reference 0 in External input supplies the Eilenberg–Mac Lane functor's limit, epimorphism, quasi-isomorphism and homology properties. Use the underlying naive spectra, passed through the supplied stable interface; this is the same realization convention as the indexing supplier. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface), [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index)

[F11] A short exact sequence of complexes of abelian groups gives a long exact sequence of homology groups. Thus an injection of complexes with acyclic cokernel is a quasi-isomorphism. [math:thm-long-exact-sequence-in-homology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-long-exact-sequence-in-homology)

### External input
Reference 0 imports the realization properties in [F10] from [Schwede–Shipley, Appendix B.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0108143v1). Reference 1 imports finite-type Noetherianity from [Stacks, Section 33.20](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/06LF); reference 2 imports the Noetherian qcqs implications from [Stacks, Section 28.5](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01OU). The restriction-fiber argument and the leading-map construction below are local deductions. The geometric comparisons remain the explicit premises in the Statement; this citation supplies no motivic comparison or generator normalization.

### Proof
1.1 By [F8] and [F9], both schemes meet the domains of [F3], [F5] and the assumed comparison, under [A1]. First justify passage from [F5]'s complexes to spectra. For the actual restriction $f:A_\bullet\to B_\bullet$, write $S=\ker f$ and $Q=A_\bullet/S$. The exact sequence $0\to Q\to B_\bullet\to\operatorname{coker}f\to0$, [F5] and [F11] show that $Q\to B_\bullet$ is a quasi-isomorphism. The epimorphism $A_\bullet\to Q$ realizes by [F10] to a fibration between Omega spectra, and its strict fiber is $\mathcal H(S)$ because realization preserves limits and zero. It is thus its homotopy fiber. Realizing $Q\to B_\bullet$ gives an equivalence by [F10]; replacing that endpoint identifies $\mathcal H(S)$ naturally with $\operatorname{fib}(B_j(U)\to B_j(V))$. The enhanced interpretation and its functoriality are those of [F6]. Now [F2] and the restriction-compatible graded comparison identify $\operatorname{gr}^j_CK(U)$ with this fiber, hence with $\mathcal H(z_C^j(U,\bullet))$. By [F1] the layers for $j=0,1$ are zero. Their supported transition maps have zero cofibers and are equivalences by [F6]. Their composite to $F^0_CK=K_C$ is precisely the actual augmentation by the coherence in [F3]. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F5, F6, F8, F9, F10, F11, given]

2.1 Invert this equivalence on $\pi_0$ and then take the weight-two graded projection to obtain the displayed homomorphism. Step 1.1 and the homology property in [F10] give target $H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)=\mathbb Z[C]$ by [F1]. Independently, the specified equivalence $\operatorname{gr}^2K\simeq M_2[4]$ on both schemes, [F2] and preservation of fibers by suspension in [F6] give $\operatorname{gr}^2_CK\simeq M_{2,C}[4]$. Its zeroth homotopy group is $\pi_{-4}M_{2,C}=H_C^{4,2}(U)$ by [F7]. Thus the cycle target and cohomological target use exactly the stated comparison and shift. [F1, F2, F3, F6, F7, F10, step 1.1, given]

3.1 Let a specified natural augmented comparison have target supported stage-two augmentation $\epsilon'_2$ which is an equivalence. Write $\phi_K$, $\phi_2$ and $\phi_{\mathrm{gr}^2}$ for its maps on underlying support fibers, supported second stages and supported second layers, and write $q_2,q'_2$ for the stage-to-layer projections. Functoriality of support fibers and graded cofibers gives $\epsilon'_2\phi_2=\phi_K\epsilon_2$ and $q'_2\phi_2=\phi_{\mathrm{gr}^2}q_2$ in the homotopy category. Since both augmentations are invertible, these identities imply $q'_2(\epsilon'_2)^{-1}\phi_K=\phi_{\mathrm{gr}^2}q_2\epsilon_2^{-1}$. Taking $\pi_0$ proves commutation of the leading homomorphisms, without requiring the comparison maps themselves to be equivalences. [F2, F3, F6, step 1.1, step 2.1, given, algebra]

4.1 Under the stronger equivalence hypotheses of [F4] on $U$ and $V$, the induced maps $\phi_K$ and $\phi_2$ are equivalences. Their commuting augmentation square and step 1.1 imply that $\epsilon'_2$ is an equivalence. Step 3.1 therefore applies, in particular to any specified classical slice/coniveau comparison satisfying those hypotheses. This preserves the actual leading map; its generator value still requires the separate normalization input. [F4, F6, step 1.1, step 3.1] ∎


## Shifted Nisnevich units and the line-bundle groupoid

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-ef9b31e59a9f-nisnevich-picard-space) · level 16


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible universe bounds. Work on a fixed universe-bounded big Nisnevich site of qcqs schemes over a prime field, or its smooth finite-type subsite over an extension field. The chosen category contains the terminal base scheme and the fibre products and Zariski refinements used in descent. For an object $X$, derived evaluation and torsors use this same site over $X$; no comparison with a different small site is assumed.

Let $G=\mathbb G_m$ be the abelian sheaf $U\mapsto\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_U)^\times$. Define $\mathcal{Pic}(X)$ to be the groupoid whose objects are invertible $\mathcal O_X$-modules and whose morphisms are module isomorphisms, pointed by $\mathcal O_X$. Pullback gives its restriction functors. Its tensor product induces the ordinary group $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ on isomorphism classes. Define $BG(X)$ to be the groupoid of Nisnevich $G$-torsors over $X$ and equivariant isomorphisms, pointed by the trivial torsor. A torsor is a sheaf with a $G$-action which is locally nonempty and simply transitive. Regard a groupoid as a space by its nerve, whose $n$-simplices are strings of $n$ composable morphisms.

Write $HC$ for the Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectrum of a cochain complex $C$, with $\pi_n(HC)=H^{-n}(C)$, and $\Omega^\infty$ for its zeroth space. There are natural pointed equivalences

$$\Omega^\infty H\bigl(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)[1]\bigr)\simeq BG(X)\simeq\mathcal{Pic}(X).$$

The second equivalence sends a line bundle to its frame torsor in the reverse direction. The first identifies the loop sheaf with $G$ by the identity. On sections the components are $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$, the automorphism group of every line bundle is $\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times$, and homotopy groups in degrees at least two vanish. Connectedness here is local in the Nisnevich topology, not connectedness of the section space for each $X$.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** Choice, the fixed compatible bounds, the stated Nisnevich site and its slices, and a scheme $X$ in it.

**Goal:** Identify the actual line-bundle groupoid, rather than only its component set, with shifted derived units.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is retained in the derived and one-type inputs and when choosing local torsor sections; the stated bounds make each site and its slices small in the chosen larger universe.

[F1] [def-nisnevich-covering-interface](item-def-nisnevich-covering-interface) specifies the chosen Nisnevich covering families, their topology and the inclusion of Zariski covers. [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) supplies coherent Omega towers and their limits; zeroth-space evaluation is level-zero evaluation on such a tower. [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) supplies derived evaluation of sheaf complexes, nonnegative resolutions for ordinary sheaves and negative-degree vanishing. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) supplies the natural formula $\pi_n(HC)=H^{-n}(C)$, applied to $C=R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)[1]$.

[F2] [lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-fpqc-descent-input](item-lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-fpqc-descent-input) supplies effective and fully faithful descent of quasi-coherent modules, descent of finite local freeness, the fpqc property of Nisnevich covering families, and the structure-sheaf assertion under its four site hypotheses. [lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-units-sheaf](item-lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-units-sheaf) then makes $G$ an abelian sheaf. Use [lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-h1-torsor-input](item-lem-author-8e1c0251ef0d-h1-torsor-input) for its sheaf-action, equivariant-isomorphism and local-nonemptiness conventions for torsors on the slice over $X$. [math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme) supplies the tensor-product group of line-bundle isomorphism classes.

[F3] Use only the general pointed connected one-type clause of [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input): in an infinity-topos, the loop-group functor is fully faithful and identifies pointed connected one-types with sheaves of groups. Its determinant assertions are not premises of this argument.

[F4] [lem-adjudicate-28f1411ede08-derived-one-space-descent](item-lem-adjudicate-28f1411ede08-derived-one-space-descent) supplies a coherent simultaneous space-valued model of shifted derived sections from one nonnegative injective resolution on the chosen site. It proves descent and identifies the loop sheaf by the identity on the original abelian sheaf. Its small-site and covering-family hypotheses hold for the bounded Nisnevich site and the descent fibre products stipulated here; keep the same compatible universe bounds.

[F5] Reference 0 supplies sectionwise kernels and local lifting of cycles in an exact complex of abelian sheaves. Apply it to $I^0\to I^1\to I^2$ on the original site.

### External input
Reference 0 imports the general-site kernel and local-exactness criteria, with the recorded hypotheses and reading limits. The resolution model and one-type classification use [F4] and [F3]. The frame argument and the application of local lifting below are locally authored.

### Proof
1.1 The chosen site is full in schemes over its base and has a terminal object and Zariski refinements. Its covers are jointly surjective and etale by [F1], and are therefore fpqc by [F2]; the four structure-sheaf hypotheses of [F2] therefore hold, including on slices. A line bundle $L$ gives the $G$-torsor of frames $\operatorname{Isom}(\mathcal O,L)$. Conversely use the torsor convention in [F2] and choose local sections using [A1]; their transition units $g_{ij}$ glue the free rank-one modules with frames $e_j=g_{ij}e_i$ by effective descent in [F2]. Finite local freeness descends, and its rank is one because it is one on the covering members. A torsor morphism, in these frames, is multiplication by units compatible on overlaps; full faithfulness of descent gives exactly one line-bundle isomorphism, and the reverse construction takes a line isomorphism to its equivariant frame map. Thus frames give an equivalence of groupoids, compatible with pullback and tensor products. The same effective and fully faithful descent makes these groupoids a Nisnevich stack. It is a pointed connected one-type locally, since lines trivialize on Zariski covers and all its arrows are isomorphisms. Multiplication by a global unit is every automorphism of $\mathcal O_X$; for another line bundle the same formula holds in frames and glues uniquely, so its automorphism sheaf is also $G$. Its component set is the Picard group of [F2]. [A1, F1, F2, construct]

2.1 Choose a single nonnegative injective resolution $G\to I^\bullet$ in abelian sheaves on the chosen bounded site. Apply [F4] to this site and this sheaf. It constructs the pointed sheaf of spaces $D_I(X)=\Omega^\infty H(I^\bullet(X)[1])$, with coherent restrictions from that one sheaf complex, representing $D(X)=\Omega^\infty H(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)[1])$ on the same site. Thus $D$ is a Nisnevich sheaf of spaces, and its loop sheaf is canonically $G$ through the identity normalization of [F4]. By [F1], $\pi_nD(X)=H^{1-n}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)$ for $n\geq0$. For $n\geq2$ this is zero. A nonnegative resolution gives $H^0_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)=G(X)$ because evaluation preserves kernels; hence the loop sheaf is canonically $G$. To prove local connectedness, represent a class in $\pi_0D(X)=H^1_{\mathrm{Nis}}(X,G)$ by $z\in I^1(X)$ with $d^1z=0$. The injective resolution is exact as a complex of abelian sheaves on this same site. By [F5], some Nisnevich cover $\{U_i\to X\}$ admits $t_i\in I^0(U_i)$ with $d^0t_i=z|_{U_i}$. Thus every component becomes the base component locally, so the sheaf of components of $D$ is terminal. This proves connectedness without a comparison of slice cohomology with derived evaluation. Apply [F3] to $D$ and the stack in step 1.1: the identity of their common loop sheaf $G$ determines a pointed equivalence, naturally on the site. Evaluating this equivalence gives the asserted equivalences on every $X$, not merely isomorphisms of component sets. The tensor/addition structures agree as well: their multiplication maps have common loop homomorphism $G\times G\to G$, and [F3] identifies these maps fully faithfully. Together with step 1.1 this proves all component and automorphism assertions. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## Smooth adjunction identifies the absolute and relative augmented slice towers

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-3b3fcff266fc-smooth-relative-slice-transport) · level 16


### Statement
Assume AC and fixed compatible nested Grothendieck universes containing $\omega$, the fields, schemes and diagrams below, with the site-size bounds of the supplied EM and perfect-$K$ constructions. Universe existence is a separate hypothesis. Let $k$ be a perfect field, let $W\hookrightarrow Y$ be an open immersion, and suppose that $Y$ and $W$ are smooth finite-type $k$-schemes. Put $T=|Y|\setminus|W|$. For any spectrum-valued presheaf $A$, write $A_T(Y)=\operatorname{fib}(A(Y)\to A(W))$.

Use the imported stable infinity-category $\mathrm{SH}(k)$ of motivic spectra, its unit $1_k$, and the suspension motive $M_k(Y)$ of $Y$. The functor $f_q$ is the right-adjoint $q$-effective cover, including its counit to the original spectrum; $s_q=\operatorname{cofib}(f_{q+1}\to f_q)$. Define the relative tower, with its actual augmentation, by
$$R_k^q(Y)=\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(k)}(M_k(Y),f_q\mathrm{KGL}_k)\longrightarrow\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(k)}(M_k(Y),\mathrm{KGL}_k)=KH(Y),\qquad q\geq0.$$
Here $\operatorname{map}$ denotes the mapping spectrum and $KH$ is homotopy invariant $K$-theory. The relative tower means this fixed-base construction, whereas the absolute tower evaluates the slice filtration of $\mathrm{KGL}_Y$ in $\mathrm{SH}(Y)$.

The actual EM-to-cdh augmented comparison and the canonical smooth-pullback comparison give an open-natural augmented filtered equivalence
$$F^\bullet_{\mathrm{mot}}K(Y)\simeq R_k^\bullet(Y)$$
over the canonical $K(Y)\to KH(Y)$. Consequently the supported towers on $(Y,W)$ are equivalent, commuting with their augmentations and all stage-to-layer maps. On graded pieces use the fixed ordinary cycle comparison of the supplied open-natural layer interface. The equivalence just displayed induces its represented relative-slice leg, so the supported graded comparison is that same comparison, with its original positive cycle convention. In particular, whenever the supported stage-$q$ augmentations are equivalences, their inverse-augmentation leading maps commute under this comparison. No smoothness or finite-type hypothesis on $k$ over its prime subfield is imposed.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** AC, the stated universe and site bounds, the perfect field $k$, and the smooth finite-type open pair $(Y,W)$.

**Goal:** Supply the augmented comparison needed before a classical relative-slice projection can normalize the actual EM supported projection.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention) specify the inherited foundational premises. All functors, their mapping spectra and the restriction diagram use compatible universe enlargements; no pointwise choice of inverses is made.

[F1] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) supplies the actual augmented EM-to-cdh equivalence on these regular Noetherian schemes and identifies the latter with the absolute evaluated KGL slice tower. Its geometric argument checks the qcqs domain for smooth finite-type schemes over any field. Its EM v1 Remark 3.6 source-version qualification remains binding. In particular, its prime-field classical-to-EM assertion alone is not used as a relative-$k$ comparison.

[F2] Reference 0 imports $\mathrm{SH}$, effective covers, their canonical filtered base-change map, smooth adjunction and the absolute KGL spectrum. For $g:Y\to\operatorname{Spec}k$, write $g_\sharp\dashv g^*$ with $g_\sharp1_Y=M_k(Y)$. These are coherent adjunctions; mapping spectra and counits are used before taking homotopy groups. The pullback comparison $g^*\mathrm{KGL}_k\simeq\mathrm{KGL}_Y$ is the canonical one representing $KH$.

[F3] [lem-adjudicate-37c8a46c3b6b-open-natural-cycle-layers](item-lem-adjudicate-37c8a46c3b6b-open-natural-cycle-layers) fixes the standard cycle comparison on the whole open-restriction diagram. In its separated comparison, the represented-spectrum identification is BEM Remark 4.21, which explicitly uses Remark 3.41's smooth base-change maps. We use this very identification on the graded cofibers below and the same ordinary cycle leg; neither an independent cycle equivalence nor a sign automorphism is substituted. The nonseparated extension agrees with that comparison on separated opens.

[F4] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport) transports natural augmented filtered equivalences to support fibers and their graded projections, and to inverse-augmentation maps whenever the corresponding augmentation is invertible. [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum) and [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) specify the coherent tower, cofiber, fiber and homotopy-group conventions. [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) fixes the actual EM tower; [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes the current perfect-$K$ model in [F1].

[F5] [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field), [math:def-perfect-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-perfect-field) and [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base) specify the field and structure morphisms. Smoothness and finite type refer to those morphisms as in [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes) and [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism). The arrow $W\hookrightarrow Y$ is the open-subscheme inclusion of [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes). The closed support here is the set-theoretic complement of this specified open; no additional scheme structure is used.

### External input
Reference 0 supplies the motivic categories, smooth adjunction, effective covers and canonical smooth base-change equivalence used in [F2]. These substantial constructions are imported. The current EM-to-absolute-tower comparison is used through [F1], with its existing source qualification. The fixed classical cycle realization and its normalization are used through [F3]. The following proof composes these specified maps and checks their application to the open pair; the whole result is not marked external_input.

### Proof
1.1 For $g:Y\to\operatorname{Spec}k$, [F2] gives the canonical equivalence $a_g^q:g^*f_q\mathrm{KGL}_k\to f_q\mathrm{KGL}_Y$. It is the map obtained by factoring the pulled-back counit through the $q$-effective cover, as in (3.42); it is an equivalence because $g$ is smooth. Apply smooth adjunction and then this map to form $\theta_Y^q:R_k^q(Y)\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,g^*f_q\mathrm{KGL}_k)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{map}(1_Y,a_g^q)}\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{SH}(Y)}(1_Y,f_q\mathrm{KGL}_Y)$. This is an equivalence of augmented towers, not only of layers: $a_g^q$ was obtained over $g^*\mathrm{KGL}_k\simeq\mathrm{KGL}_Y$, and the compatible right adjoints give all transitions. For an open immersion $h:W\hookrightarrow Y$, the composites $h^*a_g^q$ followed by $a_h^q$ and $a_{gh}^q$ are the same canonical comparison: both factor the same pulled-back counit through its effective cover. Its universal mapping-space property supplies their compatibility and higher coherences. Adjunction respects this composition, so $\theta$ is natural for the actual restriction arrow. The same argument applies to $W$, which is in the stipulated domain. Its cofiber transformation is precisely (3.43), since cofibers are functorial in this coherent filtered map. [A1, F2, F4, F5, given, construct]

2.1 Apply [F1] simultaneously to $Y$ and $W$, whose regular Noetherian qcqs hypotheses it verifies from [F5]. Compose its EM-to-absolute-tower equivalence with the inverse of $\theta$ from step 1.1. These are equivalences of entire coherent diagrams, so this gives the asserted augmented relative-$k$ comparison over the original $K\to KH$ map. Its graded relative-slice identification is the smooth-adjunction and (3.43) identification just constructed, namely Remark 4.21's identification used in [F3]. Composing with [F3]'s already fixed ordinary cycle leg therefore retains that very layer comparison and its positive convention on separated opens. Taking the restriction fiber retains it on the pair. Now apply [F4] to obtain the supported stage, augmentation and projection squares. If the augmentations are equivalences, invert their induced isomorphisms in those same squares to obtain the inverse-augmentation identity. This proves the comparison needed for the relative classical projection without applying smooth base change to $\operatorname{Spec}k\to\operatorname{Spec}F$, where $F$ is the prime subfield; the only base-change maps used are the stipulated smooth maps over $k$ and their open restrictions. [A1, F1, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎

Its canonical smooth-base-change and adjunction interfaces, rather than a reconstruction of motivic homotopy theory, are the imported premises. The EM tower, open-natural cycle-layer and support-transport suppliers were inspected in their current mathematical bodies. EM v1's then-in-preparation qualification and the served BEM version/date discrepancy remain explicit. This local composition claims no independent review, publication or library audit.


## Gysin purity for a smooth closed pair

[Open item](item-prop-smooth-pair-gysin-input) · level 17


### Statement
Let $k$ be a perfect field and let $X$ be in $\mathrm{Sm}/k$, meaning a scheme smooth, separated and of finite type over $k$. Let $i:Z\hookrightarrow X$ be a closed immersion with $Z$ smooth over $k$ and of pure codimension an integer $c\geq0$, and put $U=X\setminus Z$ with its restricted structure sheaf. Here pure codimension $c$ means that, for every irreducible component $C$ of the underlying closed subset $|Z|$, the supremum of lengths $n$ of strict chains $C=C_0\subsetneq C_1\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq C_n\subseteq |X|$ of nonempty irreducible closed subsets of $|X|$ is $c$. An irreducible component here means a maximal nonempty irreducible closed subset of $|Z|$; for empty $Z$ this condition is vacuous. Thus codimension is measured inside the actual ambient scheme, without a difference of global dimensions or an algebraic-closure hypothesis.

In the classical category $DM^{\mathrm{eff},-}_{\mathrm{Nis}}(k,\mathbb Z)$ there is the distinguished Gysin triangle
$$M(U)\longrightarrow M(X)\xrightarrow{\gamma}M(Z)(c)[2c]\longrightarrow M(U)[1].$$
Here $M(W)$ is the source motive of $W$, represented by $\mathbb Z_{\mathrm{tr}}(W)$ and isomorphic in this category to $C_*\mathbb Z_{\mathrm{tr}}(W)$; $(c)$ is the Tate twist by tensoring with $\mathbb Z(c)$, and $[2c]$ is cohomological translation. The first arrow is induced by the open immersion, and $\gamma$ is the source's Gysin map.

Equivalently one may take the relative motive of the pair, a cofiber of $M(U)\to M(X)$, to be $M(Z)(c)[2c]$. Any separately chosen cone triangle for that first arrow admits a compatible isomorphism of its third object with this one; a unique or functorial choice of that cone is not asserted from triangulated axioms alone.

This is a classical smooth-pair input, not a purity statement for a singular curve. For the formal coefficient applications, $a,j\in\mathbb Z$. Article-theory or derived-sections applications retain AC and the relevant site and size bounds. The enhanced Hom route additionally requires the supplied enhancement, lifted cofiber and coherent restriction-arrow comparison data; a complex-valued conclusion through that route also requires the coherent linear mapping lift. A separately supplied compatible comparison of theories on both $X$ and $U$ suffices, by the transport construction, to identify the article's support complex; a direct compatible comparison of support objects is another possible input. Computing its cohomological twist shift further requires the relevant coefficient representability and twist comparison in the weights used. Under precisely those premises the formal shift is $(a,j)\mapsto(a-2c,j-c)$, not $(a+2c,j-c)$. No negative-weight convention or comparison outside its given scope is silently introduced.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The perfect field, the two smooth schemes, closed immersion and pure codimension in the Statement. Additional application premises, including the conditional Choice and size assumptions in [A1], are as explicitly stated.

**Goal:** Record the exact imported triangle and give its rigorous formal interpretation without claiming a local proof of Gysin purity.

[F1] MVW Theorem 15.15 supplies the displayed triangle for precisely this smooth pair. The source's category and $M(W)\cong C_*\mathbb Z_{\mathrm{tr}}(W)$ notation are fixed in Definition 14.1 and Lemma 14.4. These are explicit external inputs in the source records.

[F2] For a termwise monomorphic cochain map $f:A\to B$, the canonical projection $\operatorname{Cone}(f)\to\operatorname{coker}(f)$ is a quasi-isomorphism of complexes. For a relative motive, this supplies a quotient-complex model when the supplier's functor, shift identification and complete cofiber-compatible triangle comparison are additionally supplied. These are sufficient conditions; termwise monicity is not asserted necessary. In general the relative object is specified by its cofiber triangle. [F3] Contravariant representable Hom reverses this triangle to the cohomological restriction long exact sequence. Its enhanced restriction-fiber conclusion requires a supplied stable enhancement, a specified cofiber lifting the entire triangle, and an equivalence of coherent restriction-arrow diagrams. A complex-valued conclusion additionally requires the supplier's coherent linear mapping lift; none of these data follows from the Hom groups alone. [lem-author-9415fc97ee80-contravariant-relative-hom](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-contravariant-relative-hom)

[F4] A supplied twist comparison gives the cohomological shift $a-2c$ and weight $j-c$ with its exact weight bounds. [lem-author-9415fc97ee80-purity-shift-index](item-lem-author-9415fc97ee80-purity-shift-index)

The notation $|X|$ refers to the underlying topological space of a scheme. The chain convention for pure codimension is defined inline in the Statement, using irreducibility in the subspace topology; it is not restricted to classical varieties over an algebraically closed field. When using the enhanced Hom route of [F3], also supply its enhancement, lifted cofiber and coherent restriction-arrow comparison data, including its coherent linear mapping lift for a complex-valued conclusion. A direct supplied comparison of support objects needs no construction by that route. Choice is used only when invoking the supplied article-theory or derived-sections constructions; no choice of enhancements or comparison maps is inferred, and the formal cone and Hom calculations add no Choice assumption. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### External input
Import the whole smooth-pair Gysin theorem in [F1]. The five local prerequisites establish setup, covariance, indexing, transport and boundary cases. They do not reconstruct the normal-bundle proof, the affine-space Gysin normalization, or Exercise 15.14. The source's displayed proof invokes those established ingredients; this authoring assistance does not claim to have filled their foundational proofs.

### Justification

1.1 Apply the imported [F1] to the stated perfect-field smooth pair. Its third term, with its map from $M(X)$ and connecting map, is a specified cofiber in the triangulated sense. If another relative cone was chosen for the same first arrow, [F5] applied with identities on $M(U)$ and $M(X)$ gives a compatible isomorphism to this third term. This establishes the relative-motive reformulation without asserting uniqueness of that isomorphism. [F1, F5, F7, F8, given]

2.1 In a complex model, [F2] supplies a sufficient condition for a quotient model; interpreting it as a model of the relative motive also uses its stipulated cofiber-compatible comparison. Apply [F3] to the distinguished triangle of step 1.1 to obtain its contravariant Hom long exact sequence. For the enhanced Hom route, under the exact lifted-cofiber, coherent restriction-arrow and, in the complex-valued case, coherent linear-lift premises of [F3], the relative Hom is the restriction fiber. Invoke [A1] when using the article-theory or derived-sections interfaces. If a second theory has a compatible derived comparison on both $X$ and $U$, [F5] transports that fiber to the second theory's support fiber. These are applications of explicit premises, not automatic identifications with the article's motivic complex. If coefficient representability and the twist identification of [F4] are also supplied, apply it with $A=M(Z)$. The relative Hom group in degree $a$ becomes the coefficient group on $Z$ in degree $a-2c$ and weight $j-c$. Only the supplied weight range is used. For empty or split support cases satisfying the independent functor premises of [F6], its direct calculations give the indicated formal boundary cases without invoking an extension of purity. [F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, A1, step 1.1] ∎

### Scope and application limit
Both smoothness hypotheses matter. In particular a possibly singular integral curve inside a smooth threefold is not covered by this input merely because its embedding has pure codimension. Nor does the source's Sm/k statement automatically extend to every qcqs scheme, a nonseparated smooth scheme, or an arbitrary smooth locus without checking the actual finite-type pair to which it is applied. The article's smooth motivic comparison and any twist/cancellation comparison retain their own authoring and scope obligations; this target supplies neither by renaming the classical cohomology theory.


## Generic rank and point-supported classes on a curve

[Open item](item-lem-curve-generic-rank-localization) · level 17

### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $C$ be an integral Noetherian scheme of dimension one, with generic point $\eta$ and function field $k(\eta)=\mathcal O_{C,\eta}$. The generic-rank homomorphism
$$\operatorname{rk}_\eta:G_0(C)\longrightarrow K_0(k(\eta))\cong\mathbb Z$$
sends the class of a coherent sheaf $F$ to $\dim_{k(\eta)}F_\eta$. Its kernel is generated by classes of coherent sheaves supported at closed points. In fact it is generated by the residue-field sheaves $\kappa(x)$ at closed points $x$.

For every nonempty open subscheme $V\subseteq C$, restriction defines a directed system as opens shrink, and the natural homomorphism
$$\varinjlim_{\eta\in V\subseteq C}G_0(V)\longrightarrow K_0(k(\eta))\cong\mathbb Z$$
is an isomorphism. Thus the displayed kernel is the kernel of localization at the generic point, computed as this filtered limit.

For the following additional supported comparison, fix the compatible site and universe bounds of the supplied perfect-$K$-theory construction. If $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ is a closed immersion into a regular Noetherian finite-dimensional scheme, the supported comparison $K_0^{|C|}(U)\cong G_0(C)$ identifies generic rank with the alternating generic length of a supported perfect complex $E$:
$$[E]\longmapsto\sum_n(-1)^n\operatorname{length}_{\mathcal O_{U,\eta}}H^n(E)_\eta.$$
The sum and each length are finite. No regularity or smoothness of $C$ is assumed.

For a field $L$, $K_0(L)$ is the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes of finite-dimensional $L$-vector spaces, with the short-exact-sequence relations. Here $G_0(X)$ is the abelian group generated by isomorphism classes of coherent $\mathcal O_X$-modules, with relation $[F]=[F']+[F'']$ for every short exact sequence $0\to F'\to F\to F''\to0$. An integral scheme is nonempty, reduced and irreducible; its generic point has dense closure and its local ring there is a field. A specialization of $y$ is a point of $\overline{\{y\}}$; dimension is the supremum of lengths of chains of strict specializations. Write $\kappa(x)=\mathcal O_{X,x}/\mathfrak m_x$ for the residue field at a point of a scheme $X$; at a closed point the sheaf also denoted $\kappa(x)$ is its pushforward along the closed immersion of the point. A sheaf is supported at $x$ if its stalks vanish away from $x$. Length is the number of simple factors in a finite composition series, independent of the series. A perfect complex is locally quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of finite locally free sheaves, and support in $|C|$ means that its cohomology sheaves vanish away from $C$. We use the supplied supported perfect category and its specified $K_0$ comparison; regularity of $U$ means regularity of every local ring. The colimit is in abelian groups, indexed by the set of nonempty opens, with one arrow $V\to W$ when $W\subseteq V$.

[F1] The coherent one-generic-generator dévissage theorem says that on a Noetherian scheme a property of coherent sheaves holds for all coherent sheaves if it holds for zero, is two-out-of-three in coherent short exact sequences, and has a coherent witness of generic length one supported on each integral closed subscheme. It assumes AC. [math:lem-coherent-devissage-one-generic-generator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-coherent-devissage-one-generic-generator)

[F2] Coherent sheaves on a Noetherian scheme form an abelian category; kernels, cokernels and finite direct sums are coherent, and coherence is local. The residue-field supplier establishes coherence on a Noetherian affine neighbourhood of a closed point. Step 2.1 extends this to the global point sheaf by the local criterion.
