[F3] For a regular Noetherian finite-dimensional ambient scheme and any closed subscheme, supported perfect-complex $K_0$ is identified with the coherent $G_0$ of the closed subscheme by the coherent-cohomology Euler class followed by the finite filtration by powers of the defining ideal. [prop-supported-k-zero-devissage](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage)

[F4] A module sheaf is a sheaf of abelian groups with compatible module structures on sections. [math:def-module-on-ringed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-module-on-ringed-space) Exactness means equality of image and kernel sheaves, also for module sheaves. [math:def-exact-sequence-sheaves](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-exact-sequence-sheaves) Exactness can be tested on every stalk, by applying the abelian-sheaf theorem to the underlying sheaves of abelian groups. [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise)

[F5] A small category is filtered if it is nonempty, any two objects map to a common object and parallel arrows can be equalized. A colimit has compatible canonical maps and the universal property that every compatible family of maps to a fixed target factors uniquely through it. [math:def-filtered-category-and-filtered-colimit](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-filtered-category-and-filtered-colimit) [math:def-limit-and-colimit-of-a-diagram](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-limit-and-colimit-of-a-diagram) Abelian groups are exactly $\mathbb Z$-modules, including their homomorphisms, subgroups and quotients. [math:prop-abelian-groups-are-z-modules](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-abelian-groups-are-z-modules) The direct sum consists of finitely supported families with coordinatewise operations and canonical summand inclusions. [math:def-direct-sum-of-a-family-of-modules](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-direct-sum-of-a-family-of-modules) A module map killing a submodule factors uniquely through its quotient. [math:thm-quotient-module-universal-property](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-module-universal-property) Step 4.1 constructs the needed colimit from these inputs rather than assuming its existence.

[F6] A composition series is a finite strictly increasing submodule chain from zero to the module, with simple factors; length is its number of factors and is independent of the series. [math:def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module) In a short exact sequence of modules, the middle term has finite length if and only if both outer terms do, and then the lengths add. [math:cor-length-is-additive-in-short-exact-sequences](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-length-is-additive-in-short-exact-sequences)

[F7] A ring is Noetherian when every ideal is finitely generated; ascending ideal chains then stabilize, since their union is an ideal whose finite generating set is contained in one term. [math:def-noetherian-ring-and-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-ring-and-module) Noetherian for a scheme means locally covered by spectra of Noetherian rings and quasi-compact; an integral scheme has domain coordinate rings on all nonempty affine opens. [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme) In a spectrum, $V(J)$ is the set of primes containing the ideal $J$. [math:def-prime-spectrum-and-vanishing-sets](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-prime-spectrum-and-vanishing-sets) Topological Noetherianity is the descending chain condition on closed subsets, and under AC every subspace of a Noetherian space is Noetherian and quasi-compact. [math:def-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-topological-space) [math:lem-noetherian-subspaces-and-compact-opens](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-subspaces-and-compact-opens) Coherence is the local finite-type quasi-coherent condition with finite-type relation kernels; in particular restrictions are coherent. [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme) Finite type means locally associated to a finitely generated module; equivalently under AC there are local surjections from finite free module sheaves. [math:def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf) Closed immersion means a homeomorphism onto a closed subset and a surjection on structure sheaves; its stalk quotient describes the closed subscheme's local ring. [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) The residue field at a prime is the fraction field of its domain quotient. [math:def-residue-field-scheme-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-residue-field-scheme-point) Regularity is regularity of each local ring. [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point) Distinguished opens form a basis of an affine spectrum, each is the spectrum of a principal localization, and localizations of Noetherian rings are Noetherian. [math:def-affine-scheme-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme-spectrum) [math:lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectrum-localization-open-immersion) [math:thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-quotients-and-localisations) The perfect objects, their cohomology and support are those of the fixed supplied scheme-derived category. [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes)

[F8] The open subscheme on an open subset has the restricted structure sheaf. [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme) Restriction of a sheaf to an open is evaluation on its open subsets. [math:def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace) Its stalk consists of germs of sections on neighbourhoods, with two representatives identified when they agree on a smaller neighbourhood. [math:def-stalk-of-presheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-stalk-of-presheaf) Thus restriction leaves each stalk at a point of the open unchanged.

[F9] Finite-dimensional vector spaces over a fixed field are classified up to linear isomorphism by dimension. Thus a space of dimension $d$ is a direct sum of $d$ copies of that field. [math:cor-finite-dimensional-vector-spaces-are-isomorphic-iff-equal-dimension](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-finite-dimensional-vector-spaces-are-isomorphic-iff-equal-dimension)

[F10] Under AC the coherent category of a locally Noetherian scheme is essentially small and abelian, so its isomorphism-class generators and short-exact-sequence relations define an abelian group. We denote this coherent group by $G_0(X)$; the supplier denotes it by $K_0(X)$. This notation does not identify it with the perfect-complex group on a singular scheme. [math:def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-group-of-coherent-sheaves-and-vector-bundles-on-a-scheme)

### Proof
**Proof technique:** Apply coherent dévissage using only points distinct from the generic point, then localize on shrinking opens and compute supported ideal-filtration lengths.

1.1 First the underlying space of $C$ is Noetherian: on a Noetherian affine chart, write a descending chain of closed subsets as $Z_j=V(I_j)$ and put $J_j=I_0+\cdots+I_j$. Then $V(J_j)=Z_0\cap\cdots\cap Z_j=Z_j$, while the ascending ideal chain $J_j$ stabilizes by [F7]. A finite affine cover gives a common stabilization index for a chain on $C$. By [F7], an open is therefore quasi-compact. Around any of its points, choose inside a Noetherian affine chart a distinguished open contained in that open; its ring is a Noetherian localization by [F7]. Thus the open is also locally Noetherian and is a Noetherian scheme. Every nonempty open $V\subseteq C$ is integral and Noetherian, has dimension at most one, and contains $\eta$. Every point $x\neq\eta$ of $V$ is closed in $V$: a further proper specialization would give a chain of length at least two starting at $\eta$. Consequently, for any integral closed subscheme $Z$ of $V$ with generic point $\xi$, either $\xi=\eta$ and $|Z|=|V|$, or $\xi\neq\eta$ and $|Z|=\{\xi\}$ is a closed point. Only these support descriptions are needed for the witnesses below. The possibility that $V=\{\eta\}$ is open is allowed; then an affine neighbourhood of its only point is all of $V$, and its ring of sections is the unique stalk $k(\eta)$, so $V=\operatorname{Spec}k(\eta)$ and there are no points $x\neq\eta$ in $V$. On any nonempty affine chart $\operatorname{Spec}A$ of $C$, the generic point is the zero prime and [F7] identifies its local ring with $\operatorname{Frac}(A)$. [F7, given]

2.1 By [F10], $G_0(V)$ is a well-defined abelian group with the stated presentation. Let $H_V\subseteq G_0(V)$ be generated by $[\mathcal O_V]$ and $[\kappa(x)]$ for $x\in V\setminus\{\eta\}$. These point sheaves are coherent: on an affine neighbourhood $W=\operatorname{Spec}A$ of $x$, the global closed-point direct image restricts to $\widetilde{A/\mathfrak m_x}$, which is coherent by [F2]; on $V\setminus\{x\}$ it is zero. These two opens cover $V$, so locality of coherence gives the global assertion. Apply [F1] under [A1] to the property $P(F):[F]\in H_V$. It holds for zero and is two-out-of-three by the exact relations in $G_0(V)$. For any integral closed subscheme with generic point $\eta$, use $\mathcal O_V$: it is coherent by [F2], its support is contained in that subscheme by step 1.1, and its generic stalk $k(\eta)$ is annihilated by the zero maximal ideal of $\mathcal O_{V,\eta}$. For an integral closed subscheme with generic point $x\neq\eta$, use $\kappa(x)$: its support is the singleton underlying that subscheme, its stalk at $x$ is one-dimensional over the residue field and it is annihilated by the maximal ideal. All witness hypotheses hold, so $H_V=G_0(V)$, including when $V=\{\eta\}$. [A1, F1, F2, F7, F8, F10, step 1.1, construct]

3.1 Taking stalks at $\eta$ is exact by [F4]. A coherent sheaf locally admits a surjection from a finite free module sheaf by [F7]. Taking its stalk using [F4] gives a finite generating family over the field $k(\eta)$ identified in step 1.1, so the generic stalk is finite-dimensional. For a short exact sequence of finite-dimensional vector spaces, a basis of the subspace together with lifts of a basis of the quotient is a basis of the middle space: projecting a linear relation proves independence, and subtracting lifts proves spanning. Dimension is therefore additive and defines the generic-rank homomorphism. It sends $[\mathcal O_V]$ to $1$ and each $[\kappa(x)]$ with $x\neq\eta$ to zero. By step 2.1, an element of its kernel is an integral linear combination of precisely these point classes. Conversely all such point classes have generic rank zero. For $V=C$, the generic point is not closed because $\dim C=1$, so these are exactly the closed-point classes in the Statement. Coherent sheaves supported at closed points have generic rank zero, and each residue sheaf is such a sheaf. For the inline definition of $K_0(L)$, the same basis argument makes dimension a homomorphism. By [F9] and the split exact sequences for finite direct sums, $[W]=(\dim_LW)[L]$, including $[0]=0$. Hence $m\mapsto m[L]$ is inverse to dimension on all virtual classes; apply this with $L=k(\eta)$ for the stated normalization. [A1, F2, F4, F7, F9, step 1.1, step 2.1, algebra]

4.1 The nonempty opens form a small filtered poset under reverse inclusion: they are a set of subsets of $C$, every finite intersection contains $\eta$, and parallel arrows in a poset coincide. Restriction preserves coherence by [F7], is exact by [F4] and [F8], and preserves the generic stalk and structure-sheaf class, so it gives the asserted diagram of groups. Construct its colimit as follows, using [F5]: put $S=\bigoplus_VG_0(V)$, with summand maps $j_V$, and let $N$ be the subgroup generated by all $j_V(a)-j_W(a|_W)$ for $W\subseteq V$. Explicitly $N$ consists of finite integral sums of these differences. Set $Q=S/N$ and let $q_V:G_0(V)\to Q$ be the induced maps. For any compatible family $f_V:G_0(V)\to B$, the formula $s\mapsto\sum_V f_V(s_V)$ on finitely supported families is additive, kills $N$ and factors uniquely through $Q$. Conversely a map out of $Q$ gives such a family. This proves the colimit universal property and its existence. Its canonical images generate $Q$, because elements of $S$ have finite support. The generic-rank maps form a compatible family, hence induce a well-defined map $\rho:Q\to\mathbb Z$. [F4, F5, F7, F8, step 3.1, construct]

5.1 For $x\in V\setminus\{\eta\}$, the open $V\setminus\{x\}$ still contains $\eta$ and kills the residue-sheaf class. Each class in step 2.1 involves only finitely many such points, so one nonempty open removes them all. Thus the canonical image of every element of $G_0(V)$ is an integer multiple of $t=q_V([\mathcal O_V])$. This $t$ is independent of $V$, by restriction from $C$. The canonical images generate $Q$ by step 4.1, so $Q$ is generated by $t$, and $\rho(t)=1$ by step 3.1. The homomorphism $\mathbb Z\to Q$, $m\mapsto mt$, is consequently inverse to $\rho$, proving the colimit isomorphism. If $V=\{\eta\}$, its only generator is $[\mathcal O_V]$; the generic point is never treated as a removable point class. Composing $\rho$ with the inverse dimension isomorphism $\mathbb Z\to K_0(k(\eta))$ identifies $Q$ with the displayed field group. The localization map is $q_C$ followed by this isomorphism; its composition with field dimension is the rank map from step 3.1, so it has exactly the stated kernel. [F5, step 2.1, step 3.1, step 4.1, algebra]

6.1 Under the additional bounds in [A1], apply [F3] and let $\mathcal I$ define $C$ in $U$. The image of $[E]$ in $G_0(C)$ is the finite sum of the classes of $\mathcal I^rH^n(E)/\mathcal I^{r+1}H^n(E)$ with signs $(-1)^n$. Put $R=\mathcal O_{U,\eta}$ and $\mathfrak m=\mathcal I_\eta$. The closed immersion and [F4] identify $R/\mathfrak m$ with $\mathcal O_{C,\eta}=k(\eta)$, so $\mathfrak m$ is the maximal ideal of $R$. Each stalk quotient in this filtration is finite-dimensional over this residue field, by coherence on $C$ and the finite-type argument in step 3.1. By [F4], taking stalks preserves each short exact sequence in the finite ideal-power filtration. A finite-dimensional vector space over $k(\eta)$ has length equal to its dimension as an $\mathcal O_{U,\eta}$-module: a basis flag has one-dimensional factors, each simple since the action factors through this residue field. Starting with the zero terminal filtration term, apply [F6] to each short exact filtration sequence. This proves that $H^n(E)_\eta$ has finite length and that its length is the sum of these quotient dimensions. Only finitely many $n$ contribute. Applying generic rank gives exactly the alternating generic-length formula, without requiring regularity or smoothness of $C$. [A1, F3, F4, F6, F7, step 3.1, step 5.1, algebra] ∎


## External EM low-weight maps with fixed positive normalization

[Open item](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations) · level 17


### Statement
For the filtration and $K$-space assertions in the selected local realization, assume the Axiom of Choice and its fixed compatible site and universe bounds. Use a universe-bounded big Nisnevich site of qcqs prime-field schemes, and restrict it to smooth finite-type $k$-schemes when specified, for any extension field $k$.

Write $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ for the tensor-product group of isomorphism classes of invertible $\mathcal O_X$-modules. Write $\mathcal{Pic}(X)$ for the groupoid of these modules and their isomorphisms, pointed by $\mathcal O_X$, and $B\mathbb G_m(X)$ for the groupoid of Nisnevich $\mathbb G_m$-torsors and equivariant isomorphisms. Here $\mathbb G_m(U)=\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_U)^\times$. Frame torsors identify the two groupoids; their nerves are the spaces used below. Thus $\mathcal{Pic}$, rather than its component group $\operatorname{Pic}$, is the domain of the line-class map. Derived sections and this torsor interpretation use the same bounded site.

For the actual EM filtration, let $q_j:\operatorname{Fil}^jK\to\operatorname{gr}^jK=\mathbb Z(j)_{\mathrm{mot}}[2j]$ be the cofiber projection. The canonical weight-zero comparison, induced by the unital filtered map of EM Construction 4.36, is $\operatorname{gr}^0K\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cdh}}(-,\mathbb Z)$. On a smooth finite-type scheme over a field its $H^0$ consists of locally constant integer functions, compatibly with evaluation at field points; this is also the weight-zero case of EM Theorem 3.5(8).

There is a natural pointed map $a:\mathcal{Pic}\to\Omega^\infty\operatorname{Fil}^1K$ lifting $L\mapsto1-[L]$. Under the canonical comparison $c_1:R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(-,\mathbb G_m)[-1]\to\mathbb Z(1)_{\mathrm{mot}}$, one has $q_1a(\mathcal O(-1))=+c_1(\mathcal O(1))$ on $\mathbb P^1_k$. The comparison is an equivalence through cohomological degree three; after shifting by two and taking zeroth spaces it identifies $\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^1K$ with the line-bundle stack $B\mathbb G_m$. On sections its $\pi_0$ is $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ and its $\pi_1$ at any line bundle is $\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times$.

Use the projective line glued from $\operatorname{Spec}k[t]$ and $\operatorname{Spec}k[u]$ by $u=t^{-1}$, with twist frames $e_\infty=t^ne_0$ for $\mathcal O(n)$. This is the usual convention in the imported formula: $\mathcal O(-1)$ is the tautological sub-line and $\mathcal O(1)$ its dual. The weight-one projective-bundle formula uses $+c_1(\mathcal O(1))$; under $\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbb P^1_k)\simeq\mathbb Z$, the class of $\mathcal O(1)$ maps to $+1$, so its first Chern class is the positive generator under the same canonical comparison. On smooth prime-field schemes, the proof of EM Lemma 5.10 explicitly identifies $q_0$ with rank.

### Statement references
[def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration)

[def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology)

[math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme)

[math:def-projective-line-two-affine-cover-and-twisting-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-projective-line-two-affine-cover-and-twisting-sheaf)

[math:cor-picard-projective-line-integers](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-picard-projective-line-integers)

[lem-adjudicate-ef9b31e59a9f-nisnevich-picard-space](item-lem-adjudicate-ef9b31e59a9f-nisnevich-picard-space)

### Source contract
[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fixed bounds discharge [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) for the selected local perfect-$K$ tower and its maps, and the derived/groupoid realization in [F2]. The underlying motivic-complex comparison and its source hypotheses are unchanged; these are local realization assumptions rather than new geometric hypotheses of the published result.

**Given:** Qcqs schemes over a prime field, Choice and compatible bounds, the actual EM filtered spectrum and the cited canonical comparisons. Restriction to smooth finite-type $k$-schemes is allowed for any extension field $k$.

**Goal:** Import the actual maps and their sign with their groupoid and twisting conventions.

[F1] EM Examples 4.11/4.26, Construction 4.36, Construction 5.5, Lemmas 5.10/5.12, Lemma 5.1/Definition 5.2, Theorem 5.3 and Corollary 7.11 supply the comparisons and normalized maps in the Statement, with the source hypotheses in reference 0. Construction 5.5 uses the line-class map $L\mapsto[L]$ in $K$-theory, whereas Definition 5.2 uses the additive motivic first Chern map. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology) fixes the latter's complex and cohomological indexing.

[F2] [lem-adjudicate-ef9b31e59a9f-nisnevich-picard-space](item-lem-adjudicate-ef9b31e59a9f-nisnevich-picard-space) supplies the natural equivalence $\Omega^\infty H(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Nis}}(-,\mathbb G_m)[1])\simeq B\mathbb G_m\simeq\mathcal{Pic}$, including morphisms and the component group of [math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme). This distinguishes the stack from its group of isomorphism classes. [math:def-projective-line-two-affine-cover-and-twisting-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-projective-line-two-affine-cover-and-twisting-sheaf) supplies the stated charts and frame convention; with $t=x_1/x_0$, frames $x_0^n,x_1^n$ have exactly the relation $e_\infty=t^ne_0$. This identifies these twists with the standard ones used by [F1] and [math:cor-picard-projective-line-integers](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-picard-projective-line-integers), whose field-level calculation gives the positive generator.

### External input
Import [F1] for the actual EM maps and their comparison homotopies, with the statement, hypotheses and reading limits of reference 0. The groupoid realization is the local result [F2]. In the shifted comparison, the homotopy groups in nonnegative degree $n$ are the cohomology groups of the unshifted complexes in degree $2-n$. These are within the cited comparison range, so the actual $c_1$ map gives an objectwise equivalence on zeroth spaces. Composing with [F2] fixes the claimed identification with the whole line-bundle stack, including its automorphisms.

Define $\kappa=-a$ using additive inversion in the target infinite loop space. It lifts $[L]-1$, and the imported normalization gives $q_1\kappa(\mathcal O(-1))=-c_1(\mathcal O(1))=c_1(\mathcal O(-1))$, since dualizing a line bundle negates its Picard class. This fixes the ensuing positive determinant normalization via the actual $c_1$ comparison, rather than an independently chosen equivalence of Picard groups.


## Classical supported K zero and the current Euler map agree

[Open item](item-lem-adjudicate-ddc29c563c8f-supported-classical-current-comparison) · level 17


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible universes for schemes, sheaves, their enhanced categories and K-theory presentations. Let $V$ be smooth quasi-projective of finite type over a field, and let $i:W\hookrightarrow V$ be a closed subscheme. Write $K^{\mathrm{cl}}(Y)$ for classical vector-bundle K-theory, identified by the canonical inclusion/approximation maps with Waldhausen K-theory of sheaf perfect complexes. Write $K^{\mathrm{cur}}(Y)=\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}(Y))$ for the specified current common-family perfect-complex model. Put
$$K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)=\operatorname{fib}(K^{\mathrm{cl}}(V)\to K^{\mathrm{cl}}(V\setminus W)),\qquad K^{\mathrm{cur}}_W(V)=\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}_{|W|}(V)).$$
There is a natural comparison on these support pairs, induced by localization of the same sheaf complexes and the canonical connective-to-nonconnective map. On degree zero it is an isomorphism
$$c_W:\pi_0K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_0K^{\mathrm{cur}}_W(V).$$
Let $G_0(W)$ be the group of coherent sheaves with short-exact-sequence relations. If $\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$ is the classical support-pair K-to-G map followed by coherent dévissage, and $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}$ is the current coherent-cohomology Euler and ideal-power dévissage map, then
$$\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}c_W=\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}:\pi_0K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)\longrightarrow G_0(W).$$
Both maps send the class of the supported perfect complex $i_*F[0]$ to $[F]$ for every coherent $F$ on $W$. The comparison respects open restriction and support-to-global maps. For integral $W$, it also commutes after coherent generic restriction to $K_0(k(W))$, where $k(W)$ is the residue field of its generic point. No equivalence in negative degrees is needed or asserted here.

### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** The schemes, support pair, AC and compatible universes in the Statement; use small presentations in a sufficiently large fixed universe.

**Goal:** Compare the actual supported degree-zero maps, including their coherent generators.

[A1] [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is retained for the supplied enhancements, K-theory presentations and dévissage; it is not a geometric condition on $W$.

[F1] [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) and [lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-enhancement-comparison](item-lem-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-enhancement-comparison) specify the actual sheaf-derived perfect category and its common K-flat realization, with inclusion-induced equivalences to the full sheaf-complex localization. [prop-perfect-k-localization-input](item-prop-perfect-k-localization-input) identifies its supported category with the restriction fiber. [lem-fiber-sequence-homotopy-exactness](item-lem-fiber-sequence-homotopy-exactness) supplies the natural exact sequences on homotopy groups.

[F2] [prop-supported-k-zero-devissage](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage) supplies $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}$ and its inverse $[F]\mapsto[i_*F[0]]$, including perfection, finite Euler/ideal-power filtrations and support-to-global compatibility.

[F3] The first three references import the canonical BGT comparison, the classical complex approximation/resolution comparisons, and Kahn–Levine's actual support-pair K-to-G transformation. These are morphisms induced by categorical constructions, not unnamed group isomorphisms.

[F4] The final three references make $V$ regular Noetherian and finite-dimensional. They apply equally to its open complement after step 1.1 checks smoothness and finite type using [F5]. These are the ambient hypotheses for [F1]–[F3].

[F5] [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme) gives a finite affine cover by spectra of Noetherian rings for a Noetherian scheme. [math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum) makes each such affine chart a Noetherian topological space under [A1], and [math:lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact) makes every open subset of each chart quasi-compact. [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme) defines qcqs by quasi-compactness of the scheme and intersections of affine opens. [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes) gives the restricted open-subscheme structure. [math:lem-finite-type-local-on-source-and-target](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-type-local-on-source-and-target) makes local finite type invariant under affine refinement, while [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism) defines the needed quasi-compactness by inverse images of quasi-compact opens. [math:def-quasi-projective-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-projective-morphism) and [math:def-locally-closed-immersion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-closed-immersion) define a quasi-projective presentation as a quasi-compact immersion into finite projective space followed by its projection. The immersion factors as a closed immersion into an open subscheme and that open inclusion; quasi-compactness is required of the composite immersion.

[F6] For the generic-point terminology use [math:def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset). An integral scheme is nonempty and irreducible, and its nonempty affine opens are spectra of domains, by [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme). Under the already assumed AC, [math:thm-spectrum-sober](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectrum-sober) gives existence and uniqueness of the generic point on each such affine open. [math:def-residue-field-scheme-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-residue-field-scheme-point) identifies the local ring at the zero prime of a domain with its fraction field, which is also its residue field. The global existence and uniqueness needed here are deduced in step 2.1.

[F7] [math:def-stalk-of-presheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-stalk-of-presheaf) defines a stalk as the filtered colimit of sections over open neighborhoods, with transition maps given by restriction. [math:thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-exactness-of-sheaves-stalkwise) makes taking a stalk exact on short exact sequences of module sheaves by applying it to their underlying abelian sheaves. A coherent module is of finite type by [math:def-coherent-module-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coherent-module-scheme), and [math:def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-finite-type-finite-presentation-module-sheaf) supplies, under AC, its local finite free surjection. These facts justify the generic restriction $\mathrm{res}_\eta:G_0(W)\to K_0(k(W))$, $[F]\mapsto[F_\eta]$, in step 2.1. For a field, the target is the Grothendieck group of finite-dimensional vector spaces with short exact relations. The third reference, Lemma 6.2.1, proof's first restriction display, uses this coherent generic restriction; Lemma 6.4.2, proof, makes it compatible with the actual support-pair K-to-G transformation.

[F8] Weibel V, Exercise 4.3 and Example 6.11, import the classical equivalence $d_W:G(W)\xrightarrow{\sim}G_W(V)$ for the given closed immersion, where $G_W(V)=\operatorname{fib}(G(V)\to G(V\setminus W))$. Together with V.2.2 and V.3.10.2, its object map is $[F]\mapsto[i_*F[0]]$. Here $G(X)$ is classical K-theory of coherent sheaves. The ambient scheme must be Noetherian; $W$ may be nonreduced or singular. The support category uses $|W|$, while $d_W$ uses the actual $\mathcal O_W$-module structure. This is the second source reference, not a consequence of the current K-theory comparison [F2].

[F9] [thm-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-sheaf-input](item-thm-author-c5bc2838d897-kcoherent-kflat-sheaf-input) defines a sheaf complex $P$ to be K-flat when $\operatorname{Tot}^{\oplus}(N\otimes_{\mathcal O_Y}P)$ is acyclic for every acyclic sheaf cochain complex $N$, with its cochain Koszul differential. It supplies K-flat resolutions of arbitrary sheaf complexes, closure under shifts and mapping cones, and preservation of K-flat complexes and their quasi-isomorphisms by degreewise pullback, in particular open restriction. Use its size convention within the compatible universes already assumed: fix Grothendieck universes $\mathcal U\in\mathcal V$ with $\omega\in\mathcal U$, take schemes, underlying ringed spaces and module-sheaf section sets in $\mathcal U$, and form their complex categories and localizations in $\mathcal V$, enlarging further for K-theory presentations as needed. Their existence is a standing size assumption separate from AC. The term Grothendieck universe uses the closure convention of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention); this definition makes no assertion of existence. The input applies to arbitrary ringed spaces; $Y=V,O$ therefore satisfies its geometric scope without any extra flatness assumption.

### External input
Import only the statements and hypotheses in the six references above. In particular BGT's degree-zero comparison requires idempotent completeness, and the classical vector-bundle/perfect comparison is used only on the stated quasi-projective schemes. The comparison theorems, the classical closed-support equivalence [F8], and the regular-scheme and dimension inputs are not proved here. The construction on the selected local model and the equality of Euler maps below are local deductions from those inputs.

### Proof
1.1 Put $O=V\setminus W$ with its open-subscheme structure. By [F4], $V$ is Noetherian. Take a finite affine cover by spectra of Noetherian rings using [F5]. For any open subset $B\subseteq V$, its intersections with these charts are quasi-compact by [F5]. Restricting an open cover of $B$ to each intersection and taking the union of the finitely many finite subcovers proves that $B$ is quasi-compact. This includes the empty case and every intersection of affine opens in $O$. Consequently [F5] makes both $V$ and $O$ qcqs and $O\hookrightarrow V$ quasi-compact. Local smooth charts restrict to $O$, and affine refinement preserves local finite type by [F5]; since $O$ is quasi-compact over the field, it is smooth of finite type. Thus [F4] applies to both schemes. To check quasi-projectivity of $O$, write the supplied immersion of $V$ into projective space as a closed immersion into an open subscheme $H$. The open subset $O$ of $V$ is the inverse image of some open $H\prime\subseteq H$. Restricting the closed immersion identifies $O$ with a closed subscheme of $H\prime$. The composite immersion into projective space is quasi-compact: inverse images of its affine opens are open subsets of $V$ and hence quasi-compact by [F5]. This is precisely the quasi-projective presentation required in [F5]. For $Y=V$ and $Y=V\setminus W$, let $\mathcal C(Y)$ be the Waldhausen category of K-flat sheaf complexes with perfect derived image, quasi-isomorphisms as weak equivalences and degreewise split monomorphisms with such cokernel as cofibrations. By [F9] cones and shifts remain K-flat, and by [F1] their derived images remain perfect; the ordinary mapping-cylinder factorization, formed from finite direct sums, shifts and a cone, stays in this category. A map is a weak equivalence exactly when it is invertible in the localized category, since the inclusion-induced localization in [F1] is an equivalence to the full derived perfect category. This checks factorization and DHKS saturation. The localization is stable and idempotent complete by [F1]. Use [A1] and the fixed larger presentation universe to make this a small presentation without discarding its cylinder constructions. The same discussion holds for the full supported subcategory: The resolutions supplied by [F9] are quasi-isomorphisms and hence preserve supported cohomology, and all intermediate objects quasi-isomorphic to a supported perfect object are again supported and perfect. Thus the inclusion of these models in all sheaf perfect complexes induces the actual derived equivalences of [F1]. The cone/shift and saturation hypotheses of the imported resolution comparison [F3] are now met. Together with the quasi-projective classical approximation, BGT gives natural maps $a_Y:K^{\mathrm{cl}}(Y)\to K^{\mathrm{cur}}(Y)$ inducing isomorphisms on $\pi_0$ and $\pi_1$ and carrying complex classes to the same derived-object classes. Open restriction is exact on sheaves and preserves K-flat complexes and their quasi-isomorphisms by [F9]; by [F1] it preserves perfect derived images and commutes with the inclusions, so the maps induce a map on the actual restriction fibers. By [F1], identify its current fiber with the category-defined current support spectrum. To see that its map $c_W$ on $\pi_0$ is an isomorphism, use the two exact rows $\pi_1K(V)\to\pi_1K(V\setminus W)\to\pi_0K_W(V)\to\pi_0K(V)\to\pi_0K(V\setminus W)$. The four outer vertical maps are isomorphisms. For injectivity, a middle element mapping to zero comes from the preceding group; its image there can be lifted back from the first group and subtracted, proving it was zero. For surjectivity, lift its next-group image through the fourth vertical isomorphism, use the fifth to obtain a middle lift, and correct its difference using the second vertical isomorphism. This proves the assertion about $c_W$ and preserves the actual fiber and support maps. [A1, F1, F3, F4, F5, F9, construct]

2.1 On this regular ambient, the classical K-to-G map is an equivalence both on $V$ and on its open complement by [F3]–[F4], and hence on their fibers. Let $t_W:K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)\to G_W(V)$ be this actual support-pair Cartan equivalence. By [F8], the given closed immersion has the classical equivalence $d_W:G(W)\to G_W(V)$; no replacement of $W$ by its reduction is made. The map in the Statement is $\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}=(\pi_0d_W)^{-1}\pi_0t_W$. Since both maps carry the represented supported complex to that same object in the coherent model, its inverse sends $[F]$ to $[i_*F[0]]$. Such complexes are perfect by [F2], even if $W$ is singular. The comparison in step 1.1 is induced by localization of those same complexes, and thus $c_W$ sends this classical supported object class to the class of the identical object in the current supported category, with its acyclic-on-the-complement trivialization. Applying [F2] gives $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}c_W([i_*F[0]])=[F]=\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}([i_*F[0]])$. These classes generate the classical supported group by the just stated classical dévissage isomorphism, so the equality holds on every class. All comparison functors commute with restriction to opens and forgetting support. For integral $W$, choose a nonempty affine open $B=\operatorname{Spec}A\subseteq W$. By [F6], $A$ is a domain and $B$ has a unique generic point $\eta$, the zero prime. Every nonempty open of the irreducible space $W$ is dense: otherwise it and the nonempty open complement of its closure would be disjoint, contradicting irreducibility. Hence $B$ is dense in $W$. The closure of $\eta$ in $W$ contains $B$ and therefore equals $W$. Every generic point of $W$ lies in $B$, since otherwise the closed complement of $B$ would contain its closure; it is then generic in $B$ and equals $\eta$ by affine uniqueness in [F6]. This proves global existence and uniqueness without an affineness assumption on $W$. The affine identification in [F6] gives $\mathcal O_{W,\eta}=\kappa(\eta)=k(W)$. By [F7], for every coherent $F$ a finite free surjection near $\eta$ gives a surjection $k(W)^n\to F_\eta$, so $F_\eta$ is finite-dimensional. Taking stalks preserves short exact sequences by [F7], so $[F]\mapsto[F_\eta]$ respects every defining relation of $G_0(W)$ and defines $\mathrm{res}_\eta$. Explicitly $F_\eta=\varinjlim_{\eta\in C}F(C)$ over open neighborhoods ordered by shrinking. Every nonempty open of $W$ contains $\eta$, and for such an open $D$, the neighborhoods contained in $D$ are cofinal by $C\mapsto C\cap D$; thus restriction identifies $(F|_D)_\eta$ canonically with $F_\eta$, compatibly with further restriction. This is precisely the coherent generic restriction in [F7] and the third reference. Postcomposing the proved equality $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}c_W=\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$ with this same homomorphism proves the claimed generic compatibility. Since the comparison retains each represented coherent class, a one-dimensional generic stalk still represents $[k(W)]$, the positive residue-field generator. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, F8, step 1.1] ∎


## The actual supported Euler class has the generic length coordinate

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-euler-generic-coordinate) · level 18


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds of the supported perfect-$K$-theory constructions. Let $k$ be a field, let $U$ be a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme of pure dimension three, and let $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ be an integral closed curve of codimension two. Let $\eta$ be the generic point of $C$ and let $E\in\operatorname{Perf}_{|C|}(U)$ be an object of the specified current supported perfect category. Neither smoothness nor normality of $C$, nor global quasi-projectivity of $U$, is required. Identify its spectral degree-zero class with $G_0(C)$ by the completed coherent-cohomology Euler and ideal-power devissage comparison. Its generic-rank coordinate is
$$\chi_\eta(E)=\sum_n(-1)^n\ell_{\mathcal O_{U,\eta}}H^n(E)_\eta.$$
This is a finite sum of finite integers. It is additive under exact triangles, invariant under restricting the ambient scheme to an open neighbourhood of $\eta$, sends $[i_*\mathcal O_C[0]]$ to $+1$, and sends every class supported at finitely many closed points of $C$ to zero. The current support spectrum and the open-complement fiber are identified by the actual localization map, not by a model-free notation change.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) is used through the smoothness characterization, finite-type algebra and Noetherian-topology results below, and through the essentially small supported perfect-category and K-theory comparisons. The fixed universes satisfy [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and admit the enlargements required by the supplied constructions; universe existence is a separate standing size assumption. The compatible site bounds are those of [F1]–[F3] and [F7]. These premises apply in both proof steps; the finite stalk calculation requires no additional choice.

**Given:** The Axiom of Choice and the stated site and universe bounds; a field $k$; a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme $U$ of pure dimension three; an integral codimension-two closed curve $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ with generic point $\eta$; and a supported perfect complex $E\in\operatorname{Perf}_{|C|}(U)$.

**Goal:** Identify the precise coordinate in the current K-theory model and its positive test object.

[F1] The completed supported comparison maps the current $K_0^{|C|}(U)$ to $G_0(C)$ by the coherent Euler class followed by ideal-power quotients, and sends $[F]$ back to $[i_*F[0]]$. Here $G_0(C)$ has coherent-sheaf generators and short-exact-sequence relations; closed direct image is $(i_*F)(V)=F(i^{-1}V)$ with the induced structure-sheaf action. [prop-supported-k-zero-devissage](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage)

[F2] Generic rank on $G_0(C)$ sends $[F]$ to $\dim_{k(\eta)}F_\eta$, where $k(\eta)=\mathcal O_{C,\eta}$, and corresponds under [F1] to the alternating generic length displayed above. Its kernel is generated by closed-point residue-field classes. The supplier includes finiteness of the displayed sum and lengths and the identity $\mathcal O_{U,\eta}/\mathcal I_\eta=k(\eta)$ for the defining ideal $\mathcal I$ of $C$. [lem-curve-generic-rank-localization](item-lem-curve-generic-rank-localization)

[F3] Current support-category K-theory maps naturally equivalently to the fiber of restriction for a qcqs scheme and quasi-compact open complement. This is the actual category-defined support-to-fiber map and is natural for open restriction. [prop-perfect-k-localization-input](item-prop-perfect-k-localization-input)

[F4] For a smooth finite-type scheme over any field, the smoothness characterization in [math:def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical) says that all local rings after every field extension are regular. In particular the identity extension $k/k$ gives regularity of every local ring of $U$. Regularity at a point is the local-ring condition of [math:def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-regular-local-ring-geometric-point). No perfect-field assumption enters this implication.

[F5] Finite type means locally of finite type and quasi-compact, by [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism); a quasi-compact morphism has quasi-compact inverse images of quasi-compact opens, by [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism). Thus a finite-type field scheme has a finite affine cover with finitely generated $k$-coordinate algebras. [lem-author-14463ef2f4e5-finite-type-dimension](item-lem-author-14463ef2f4e5-finite-type-dimension) makes each such algebra Noetherian. A finite affine cover of this kind makes the scheme Noetherian by [math:def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme). By [math:lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-closed-immersion-affine-quotient-and-base-change), the inverse image under $i$ of a chart $\operatorname{Spec}A$ is $\operatorname{Spec}(A/I)$; the images of a finite list of $k$-algebra generators still generate $A/I$.

[F6] By [math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum), the affine spectra just described are Noetherian topological spaces under AC. The ascending-open-chain convention is [math:def-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-topological-space). By [math:lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact), all opens of a Noetherian space are quasi-compact under AC. The qcqs condition means quasi-compactness and quasi-compact intersections of affine opens, as in [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme).

[F7] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) supplies the full stable idempotent-complete category $\operatorname{Perf}_{|C|}(U)$: its objects are locally equivalent to bounded cochain complexes of finite locally free modules and have cohomology sheaves vanishing off $C$. Its triangles are the cofiber triangles in the homotopy category. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) supplies its essentially small current realization, presentation independence and $K_0^{|C|}(U)=\pi_0\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}_{|C|}(U))$. For a small stable idempotent-complete presentation, [thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input](item-thm-author-3a003d7b5208-nonconnective-k-zero-input) gives the object-class cofiber presentation of this degree-zero group, and [lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation](item-lem-author-3a003d7b5208-triangle-presentation) identifies it with the distinguished-triangle presentation.

[F8] Fields and schemes over a base use [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field) and [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base). An integral scheme is nonempty, reduced and irreducible, as in [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme); a closed immersion identifies a closed subspace and has surjective structure-sheaf map, as in [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes). Dimension is the length supremum of strict chains of nonempty irreducible closed subsets, as in [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space). Here a curve has dimension one; pure dimension three stipulates that $U$ has dimension three and each irreducible component has that dimension. Codimension at the generic point of $C$ is the Krull dimension of $\mathcal O_{U,\eta}$; its stipulated value two is not used to infer any regularity. The generic point has dense closure, and [F2] supplies its field and generic stalk. Length $\ell_R(M)$ counts simple factors in a finite composition series, with length zero for the zero module, as in [math:def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module). Restriction to an open subspace evaluates a sheaf on the same smaller opens, as in [math:def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-restriction-sheaf-open-subspace); hence it preserves stalks at points of that open. The notation $H^n(E)$ denotes cohomology sheaves in [F7], and $F[0]$ is the sheaf $F$ placed in degree zero.

### Proof

1.1 By [F5], choose a finite affine cover of $U$ with finitely generated $k$-coordinate algebras; the one-point base $\operatorname{Spec}k$ is quasi-compact. These algebras are Noetherian, so $U$ is Noetherian. The inverse images of the cover in $C$ are affine quotient spectra with finitely generated $k$-algebras, so the same argument makes $C$ Noetherian. Its integrality and dimension one are hypotheses, and $\dim U=3$ gives finite dimension. Apply [F4] with the identity extension $k/k$ to make every local ring of $U$ regular. For completeness, $U$ is also Noetherian as a topological space: any increasing sequence of opens stabilizes on each of the finitely many Noetherian affine charts by [F6], and the maximum of their stabilization indices makes it stabilize on $U$. Every open of $U$ is therefore quasi-compact by [F6]; this includes intersections of affine opens and $U\setminus C$. Consequently $U$ and every open neighbourhood $W$ of $\eta$ are qcqs, and both open complements of the closed supports in use are quasi-compact. These checks discharge the ambient hypotheses of [F1]–[F3] without separatedness, quasi-projectivity or a perfect ground field. [A1, F4, F5, F6, F8, given, choose]

2.1 Apply [F1] to $(U,C)$ and [F2] to its $G_0(C)$ class. Their composite has precisely the displayed finite alternating generic-length formula. To check triangle additivity, use the stable idempotent-complete supported category in [F7] and one of its equivalent small presentations. The degree-zero and triangle-presentation comparisons in [F7] give $[B]=[A]+[D]$ for every exact triangle $A\to B\to D\to A[1]$ in this very category. The composite homomorphism therefore gives $\chi_\eta(B)=\chi_\eta(A)+\chi_\eta(D)$; being a homomorphism alone was not the missing triangle relation. If $\eta\in W\subseteq U$ is open, the stalk restriction convention in [F8] identifies its local ring and all $H^n(E)_\eta$ with their restrictions. Thus the formula is invariant under ambient open restriction. For $i_*\mathcal O_C[0]$, [F1] supplies the actual perfect object and [F2] identifies its only nonzero generic cohomology with $k(\eta)$ in degree zero. This residue module is simple: a nonzero element generates it under the local ring's surjection onto the field. Its one-factor series has length one by [F8], fixing the value $+1$. An object supported at finitely many closed points of the integral curve has zero generic stalk, since $\eta$ is not closed on a dimension-one integral scheme, so it has coordinate zero; linearity gives the same assertion for such classes. Finally apply [F3] using step 1.1. Its natural support-category-to-fiber equivalence transports these same classes and is compatible with open restriction. No new sign or abstract group identification is inserted in the open-complement fiber. This proves every assertion. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F7, F8, step 1.1, algebra] ∎


## Weight zero is the locally constant rank function

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank) · level 18


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds of the supplied constructions. Let $k$ be any field, and $X$ a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme. Under the canonical weight-zero comparison, the actual EM map $\pi_0q_0:K_0(X)\to H^0(X,\mathbb Z(0)_{\mathrm{mot}})$ is the locally constant rank function, component by component. Consequently $\pi_0\operatorname{Fil}^1K(X)=\ker(\operatorname{rank}:K_0(X)\to H^0(X,\mathbb Z))$, and $\pi_1\operatorname{Fil}^1K(X)\to K_1(X)$ is an isomorphism. These are statements about stage groups, not an assumed equality of a stage with its image.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] Assume [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and fix compatible Grothendieck universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$, all scheme and field data in $U$, and compatible bounds for the actual EM tower, perfect-$K$ model and derived evaluation. Existence of these universes is a separate size assumption; use the closure convention of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention). For cdh sections fix the same universe-bounded general qcqs prime-field scheme site used by the canonical comparison in [F1], small in the larger universe; evaluate at its representative of the scheme in question. Apply the general derived-sections interface to that site itself, without replacing it by a finite-type or small Nisnevich site. These bounds discharge the supplied constructions below. Choice is retained for the perfect-$K$ and affine comparisons, the Noetherian-spectrum and open-compactness inputs of [F0], the derived-sections input of [F1], and the spectral indexing of [F3]. The unital integer calculation and exact-sequence argument introduce no further choice principle. Choice also discharges the identity-smoothness input of [F2], used for the field comparison in step 1.1.

**Given:** The stated Choice and size assumptions, smooth finite-type $X/k$, and the actual unital multiplicative EM tower, restricted from the prime field to $k$-schemes.

**Goal:** Normalize $q_0$ and identify its first-stage homotopy groups.

[F0] Smoothness means smoothness of $X\to\operatorname{Spec}k$ in [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes). By [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism), finite type gives local finite type and a quasi-compact structure morphism. By [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-morphism) and the one-point description of [math:ex-spectrum-field-one-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-spectrum-field-one-point), $X$ is quasi-compact and has a finite affine open cover by spectra of finitely generated $k$-algebras. By [math:lem-field-is-noetherian](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-field-is-noetherian), [math:cor-finite-type-algebra-over-noetherian-ring-is-noetherian](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-finite-type-algebra-over-noetherian-ring-is-noetherian) and, under [A1], [math:thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-noetherian-ring-has-noetherian-spectrum), those affine opens are Noetherian topological spaces. Noetherian means that every increasing sequence of opens stabilizes, as in [math:def-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-noetherian-topological-space). Such a sequence on $X$ stabilizes on each member of the finite cover, hence on $X$ by taking the maximum of the finitely many stabilization indices. Thus $X$ is Noetherian, also if empty. Under [A1], [math:lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-noetherian-open-subsets-are-quasi-compact) makes every open subset of $X$ quasi-compact, including intersections of affine opens. Therefore $X$ is qcqs in [math:def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quasi-compact-and-quasi-separated-scheme), without a separatedness hypothesis. The specified [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) defines $K_i(X)=\pi_iK(X)$, its unit $[\mathcal O_X]$ and derived pullbacks; [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) supplies the functorial prime-field-restricted tower and $\operatorname{Fil}^0K(X)\simeq K(X)$.

[F1] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations) supplies the actual natural unital comparison $\operatorname{gr}^0K\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cdh}}(-,\mathbb Z)$, and identifies its $H^0$ on smooth finite-type schemes over any field with locally constant integer functions, compatibly with field-point evaluation. Here $\mathbb Z$ is the ordinary constant integer sheaf placed in degree zero. On the very same bounded cdh site of [A1], [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) supplies derived evaluation of an ordinary abelian sheaf and $H^n_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)=0$ for $n<0$, retaining its Choice and size hypotheses. Write $H^0(X,\mathbb Z)$ for this degree-zero group, identified with those locally constant functions. No concentration of the entire derived-sections complex in degree zero is assumed.

[F2] [lem-affine-perfect-k-zero-bridge](item-lem-affine-perfect-k-zero-bridge) identifies $K_0(\operatorname{Spec}L)$ with the vector-bundle Grothendieck group for every field $L$, taking a bundle in degree zero to its bundle class. By [math:ex-spectrum-field-one-point](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-spectrum-field-one-point), this is a one-point scheme with structure ring $L$, so its finite locally free sheaves and short exact sequences are precisely finite-dimensional $L$-vector spaces and their short exact sequences. [math:ex-the-grothendieck-ring-of-finite-dimensional-vector-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-the-grothendieck-ring-of-finite-dimensional-vector-spaces) identifies this group with $\mathbb Z$ by dimension, taking $[L]$ to $1$. Under [A1], the $n=0$ case of [math:ex-polynomial-ring-flat-smooth](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-polynomial-ring-flat-smooth), applied to the commutative ring $A=L$, makes the identity $\operatorname{Spec}L\to\operatorname{Spec}L$ smooth. This identity is finite type: its single affine chart has coordinate algebra $L$ generated over itself by the empty list, and its one-point source makes the structure morphism quasi-compact, using the definitions in [F0]. Thus the comparison of [F1] applies to $\operatorname{Spec}L$ over $L$ itself. No finite-type assumption on $L$ over the prime field is needed.

[F3] [lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence](item-lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence) supplies the fiber sequence and long exact homotopy sequence of a decreasing filtration. At $j=0$, [F0] identifies this with $\operatorname{Fil}^1K(X)\to K(X)\xrightarrow{q_0}\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)$. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) supplies the graded homotopy/cohomology dictionary; at weight zero, the comparison in [F1] gives $\pi_i\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)$ for every integer $i$.

[F4] [math:lem-field-valued-points-of-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-field-valued-points-of-schemes) supplies the residue field $\kappa(x)$ and canonical morphism $p_x:\operatorname{Spec}\kappa(x)\to X$ for each $x\in X$, compatibly with structure morphisms. Thus $\kappa(x)$ is an extension of $k$; its one-point spectrum is qcqs by the definitions in [F0]. For $\alpha\in K_0(X)$, define its rank at $x$ to be the integer corresponding under [F2] to the derived pullback $p_x^*\alpha\in K_0(\kappa(x))$ of [F0]. On a bounded local vector-bundle representative this is the alternating sum of its fibre dimensions, by the affine comparison formula in [F2]. This rank is independent of representatives because pullback and the field comparison are maps on $K_0$.

### Proof
1.1 The domain check in [F0] places $X$ in the qcqs source of the supplied constructions. For any field extension $L$ of the prime field within the fixed bounds, [F2] gives $K_0(L)=\mathbb Z$ generated by $[L]$, while [F1] gives the target $\mathbb Z$. The comparison is unital, so the homomorphism $\pi_0q_0:\mathbb Z\to\mathbb Z$ sends $1$ to $1$ and is identity. For $x\in X$, use $p_x$ of [F4]. Both schemes are in the common prime-field qcqs category, so naturality and field-point evaluation in [F0]–[F1] identify the value of $q_0(\alpha)$ at $x$ with the integer corresponding to $p_x^*\alpha$, its rank at $x$. Sections of the target in degree zero are locally constant integer functions and are equal if their values at every point agree. Thus $q_0$ equals the full rank function, including on disconnected $X$, and this rank is locally constant. For empty $X$ the conclusion holds because the function group on the empty space is zero. [A1, F0, F1, F2, F4]

2.1 By [F1] and the indexing in [F3], $\pi_i\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)=0$ for $i=1,2$. Apply [F3] at $j=0$ and $i=1$: these two vanishings make $\pi_1\operatorname{Fil}^1K(X)\to K_1(X)$ both injective and surjective. At $i=0$, vanishing of $\pi_1\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)$ gives an injection $\pi_0\operatorname{Fil}^1K(X)\hookrightarrow K_0(X)$ whose image is $\ker(\pi_0q_0)$. Step 1.1 identifies that map with rank and its degree-zero target with $H^0(X,\mathbb Z)$, giving exactly the asserted stage-group identifications. [A1, F0, F1, F3, step 1.1] ∎


## External supported slice projection with positive generic coefficient

[Open item](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-slice-positive-cycle-input) · level 18


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed compatible site and universe bounds for the supplied K-theory realizations. Let $k$ be perfect, let $V$ be a smooth quasi-projective finite-type $k$-scheme, and let $i:W\hookrightarrow V$ be an integral closed subscheme with generic point $\eta$. Here codimension means $q=\dim\mathcal O_{V,\eta}$, using Krull dimension, and $k(W)=\kappa(\eta)$ is its residue field; thus $q\geq0$.

Use $K^{\mathrm{cl}}$ for classical K-theory and $K^{\mathrm{cur}}$ for the specified nonconnective perfect-complex K-theory. For any spectrum-valued theory $E$, put $E_W(V)=\operatorname{fib}(E(V)\to E(V\setminus W))$. The current category-defined supported spectrum is identified with this fiber by its actual localization map. Let $f_q$ be the classical motivic $T$-effective cover and $s_q=\operatorname{cofib}(f_{q+1}\to f_q)$, with $T=(\mathbb P^1,\infty)$ and the source's Nisnevich/A1-local conventions. In classical slice K-theory the actual stage augmentation
$$\epsilon_q:(f_qK^{\mathrm{cl}})_W(V)\longrightarrow K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)$$
is an equivalence. Set
$$\psi^{\mathrm{cl}}_W=\pi_0\bigl((f_qK^{\mathrm{cl}})_W(V)\to(s_qK^{\mathrm{cl}})_W(V)\bigr)\circ(\pi_0\epsilon_q)^{-1}.$$
Let $c_W:\pi_0K^{\mathrm{cl}}_W(V)\xrightarrow{\sim}K_0^W(V)$ be the canonical comparison induced by localization of the same sheaf complexes, where $K_0^W(V)=\pi_0K^{\mathrm{cur}}_W(V)$. For the transported projection $\psi_W=\psi^{\mathrm{cl}}_Wc_W^{-1}$, the canonical slice-to-cycle comparison identifies its generic coefficient with
$$K_0^W(V)\xrightarrow{\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}}G_0(W)\longrightarrow K_0(k(W))\xrightarrow{\dim}\mathbb Z.$$
Here $G_0(W)$ is the coherent-sheaf Grothendieck group with short exact relations, $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}$ is the coherent-cohomology Euler/ideal-power dévissage map, the next arrow is generic restriction, and dimension sends $[k(W)]$ to $+1$. This statement includes support: the $q$-th supported slice restricts equivalently to its codimension-$q$ generic-point coefficient. It does not identify a supported class with its possibly zero image in the ambient Chow group. Neither smoothness nor normality of $W$ is required.
