### Facts & Assumptions
**Given:** AC, the fixed compatible site and universe bounds, and exactly the perfect-field, smooth quasi-projective ambient and integral codimension-$q$ support hypotheses above. Choice and size bounds are inherited from the selected local model suppliers, not additional geometric hypotheses attributed to Kahn–Levine.

**Goal:** Import the actual supported projection and its positive cycle normalization, and specify its canonical current-model interpretation.

[F1] Kahn–Levine §§1.1–1.2 define the slice objects used here. Proposition 2.2.2 gives the compatible coniveau/slice towers; Lemmas 2.3.1–2.3.2 give the supported augmentation equivalence and generic detection. Definition 3.1.5 defines precisely $\psi^{\mathrm{cl}}_W$.

[F2] Their Definitions 3.1.9–3.1.15, Theorem 3.2.4 and Lemma 6.4.2 identify the slice-cycle map with the classical support-pair K-to-G map and generic restriction constructed explicitly in Lemma 6.2.1.

[F3] Section 5.9 identifies $K_0(F)$ with $\mathbb Z$ by dimension, compatible with the Serre face convention. In the ordinary case no reduced-norm scaling or negative generator is inserted.

[F4] [lem-adjudicate-ddc29c563c8f-supported-classical-current-comparison](item-lem-adjudicate-ddc29c563c8f-supported-classical-current-comparison) constructs $c_W$ from the canonical categorical maps and proves $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}c_W=\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$, including open and generic restriction compatibility. [prop-supported-k-zero-devissage](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage) gives the current Euler/ideal-power map; [prop-perfect-k-localization-input](item-prop-perfect-k-localization-input) identifies the current support category with the actual restriction fiber. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) specifies that model; [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) fixes its spectral notation. The [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and the fixed size bounds discharge these local constructions.

### External input
Import the restricted classical package [F1]–[F3] with the exact hypotheses and reading limits in the first three references. It supplies the actual inverse-augmentation map and its generic coefficient, rather than an inference from equality of groups. The current interpretation uses the explicit local comparison [F4]: composing the classical coefficient with $c_W^{-1}$ gives $\dim\circ\mathrm{res}_\eta\circ\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}$. Thus the imported classical theorem and the locally proved model comparison have separate provenance; no new literature proof of the whole current-model application is claimed.

The $S^1$ coniveau/slice construction is connected to the $\mathbb P^1$-stable KGL tower by Levine §8.3 and Theorem 9.0.3, as specified in the fourth reference. Here KGL denotes the Bott-periodic motivic K-theory spectrum. That augmentation-adjoint stabilization is a separate imported map-level input, with the perfect-field and fibrant-spectrum hypotheses recorded there. It does not replace the classical-to-current sheaf-complex comparison [F4].

The full coniveau, purity and moving-lemma constructions remain substantial imported inputs. Any application to a smooth ambient which is not quasi-projective must first restrict to an affine neighborhood of the support's generic point and separately prove detection on the intended global target. The present theorem does not assert that extra global extension. In particular no global quasi-projectivity assumption is imposed on such a consumer merely by this local application.

### Proof
1.1 Apply [F1] to the stated perfect-field, smooth quasi-projective pair and its integral codimension-$q$ support. It gives the actual augmentation equivalence and the inverse-augmentation projection $\psi^{\mathrm{cl}}_W$ in the displayed order, together with equivalence on restricting its supported slice to the codimension-$q$ generic point. By [F2], the coefficient of this very projection is the classical support-pair K-to-G map $\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$, followed by coherent generic restriction. By [F3], the latter field group has its dimension normalization, sending the residue-field class to $+1$. Thus the classical coefficient is $\dim\circ\mathrm{res}_\eta\circ\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$, without assuming smoothness or normality of $W$. [F1, F2, F3, given]

2.1 The ambient hypotheses also meet [F4], with the Choice and size assumptions already in Given. Its actual comparison $c_W$ is an isomorphism on the supported degree-zero domain and satisfies $\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}c_W=\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}$. Compose the map identified in step 1.1 with $c_W^{-1}$, as required by the definition of $\psi_W$. The resulting coefficient is $\dim\circ\mathrm{res}_\eta\circ\beta_{\mathrm{cl}}c_W^{-1}=\dim\circ\mathrm{res}_\eta\circ\beta_{\mathrm{cur}}$, which is precisely the displayed current Euler/generic-dimension composite. The support target and its positive normalization are those in step 1.1; no passage to ambient Chow cohomology occurs. This proves the stated current-model interpretation by a local composition of the imported classical result and the proved comparison helper. [F4, step 1.1] ∎


## Positive generic cycles determine the curve-supported homomorphism

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-positive-generic-cycle-detection) · level 19


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible site and universe bounds of the supplied geometric and supported comparisons. Let $k$ be a field, let $U$ be a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme of pure dimension three, and let $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ be an integral closed curve of codimension two with generic point $\eta$ and function field $k(\eta)=\mathcal O_{C,\eta}$. A curve has dimension one, pure dimension three means that every irreducible component has dimension three, and codimension two here means $\dim\mathcal O_{U,\eta}=2$. Neither smoothness nor normality of $C$, nor global quasi-projectivity of $U$, is assumed.

Write $G_0(C)$ for the abelian group on coherent-sheaf classes with the short-exact-sequence relations, and $\mathbb Z[C]$ for the integral multiples of the single formal positive cycle $[C]$. Let $h:G_0(C)\to\mathbb Z[C]$ be a group homomorphism. If $h$ kills every closed-point residue-field class and $h([\mathcal O_C])=[C]$, then
$$h([F])=\dim_{k(\eta)}F_\eta\,[C]$$
for every coherent sheaf $F$, and $h(x)=r(x)[C]$ for every virtual class $x\in G_0(C)$, where $r$ is generic rank.

Equivalently, use the supplied actual supported perfect-$K$-theory group $K_0^{|C|}(U)$ and its coherent Euler and ideal-power devissage isomorphism $q:K_0^{|C|}(U)\to G_0(C)$. To prove that a homomorphism $g:K_0^{|C|}(U)\to H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)$ is the positive generic-length map, it suffices to identify its generic restriction with $r\circ q$, using the positive cycle generator and $K_0(k(\eta))\cong\mathbb Z$ by dimension. Explicitly, if for an open $V\subset U$ containing $\eta$ the restriction of $g(y)$ is $r(q(y))[C\cap V]$ for every $y$, then $g(y)=r(q(y))[C]$. On a supported perfect-complex class $[E]$, this is $g([E])=(\sum_n(-1)^n\ell_{\mathcal O_{U,\eta}}H^n(E)_\eta)[C]$. A sign is fixed only once the residue-field or structure-sheaf generator has been sent to $+1$; an unspecified isomorphism of groups does not supply that condition.

### Facts & Assumptions

[A1] The assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) discharges the coherent generic-rank supplier [F1] in step 1.1. Together with the fixed site and universe bounds it discharges the geometric and actual supported-coordinate premises [F2]–[F3] in step 2.1. The universes use [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), contain $\omega$ and the scheme data, and admit the enlargements required by those constructions; their existence is a separate standing size assumption. Once these supplied identifications are fixed, the homomorphism argument is finite algebra.

**Given:** The Axiom of Choice and the stated site and universe bounds; the field $k$, smooth finite-type pure threefold $U$, integral codimension-two closed curve $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ and its generic point $\eta$ as in the Statement; and the homomorphism $h$ with its two stated conditions. For the supported formulation, use the stated current group, comparison $q$ and homomorphism $g$.

**Goal:** Isolate the exact generator/sign test needed from the map comparison, without presupposing that an abstract group isomorphism has a positive normalization.

[F1] Generic rank $r:G_0(C)\to\mathbb Z$ sends $[F]$ to $\dim_{k(\eta)}F_\eta$, sends $[\mathcal O_C]$ to $1$, and has kernel generated by closed-point residue-field classes. The supplier defines $G_0(C)$ by coherent-sheaf generators and short-exact-sequence relations, and $\kappa(x)$ as the closed-point direct image of its residue field. Its generic-point $K_0$ is identified with $\mathbb Z$ by dimension. Here $\ker r=\{x\in G_0(C):r(x)=0\}$. [lem-curve-generic-rank-localization](item-lem-curve-generic-rank-localization)

[F2] The notation $z_C^2(U,\bullet)$ means the supported Bloch subcomplex on admissible cycles supported over $|C|$, and $H_0$ is its degree-zero cycles modulo boundaries. [def-supported-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-supported-higher-cycle-complex) The supported target is $\mathbb Z[C]$, not the image of $[C]$ in an ambient Chow group. Restriction to any open $V\subset U$ containing $\eta$ is an isomorphism sending $[C]$ to $[C\cap V]$. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom)

[F3] In precisely the stated geometric setting, the actual supported Euler-coordinate supplier verifies that $C$ and $U$ are Noetherian, that $U$ is regular and qcqs with quasi-compact open complements, and supplies the current devissage comparison $q$. Under $q$, the generic-rank coordinate is the finite sum $\sum_n(-1)^n\ell_{\mathcal O_{U,\eta}}H^n(E)_\eta$, with value $+1$ on $i_*\mathcal O_C[0]$. Thus the hypotheses of [F1] are satisfied here. The coordinate and its sign are preserved by open restriction. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-euler-generic-coordinate](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-euler-generic-coordinate)

[F4] The ground field and structure morphism $U\to\operatorname{Spec}k$ use [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field) and [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base). Smoothness is the supplied smoothness-over-a-field convention in [math:def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-to-field-classical); finite type means locally of finite type and quasi-compact in [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism). Integral means nonempty, reduced and irreducible in [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme), and $i$ is a closed immersion in [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes). Dimension is the supremum of lengths of chains of nonempty irreducible closed subsets in [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space). The generic-point convention is $\overline{\{\eta\}}=|C|$ in [math:def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset); the function field and generic stalk are supplied in [F1].

[F5] A group homomorphism preserves the group operation; in the additive notation here $h(x+x')=h(x)+h(x')$, and likewise for $g$. [math:def-group-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-homomorphism) In particular $h(0)=0$ by cancellation in $h(0)=h(0)+h(0)$, and $h(-x)=-h(x)$ by applying additivity to $x+(-x)=0$. Induction on nonnegative integers and this inverse identity give $h(nx)=n h(x)$ for every integer $n$.

[F6] The supported perfect category consists of perfect objects whose cohomology stalks vanish off $|C|$; perfect means locally equivalent to a bounded complex of finite locally free modules, and $F[0]$ places a sheaf in degree zero. [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) Its current degree-zero group is $K_0^{|C|}(U)=\pi_0\mathbb K(\operatorname{Perf}_{|C|}(U))$ in the chosen compatible realization. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) The separate comparison with $G_0(C)$ is [F3], rather than a consequence of this definition. Module length $\ell_R(M)$ counts the simple factors in a finite composition series, with length zero for the zero module. [math:def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module) Finiteness and the generic-length identity are supplied by [F3].

### Proof

1.1 In the stated setting [F3] verifies the Noetherian hypothesis for [F1]. For any $x\in G_0(C)$, $x-r(x)[\mathcal O_C]$ belongs to $\ker r$. By [F1] it is a finite integral sum of closed-point residue-field classes. The first hypothesis on $h$, together with the integral additivity in [F5], kills that difference, while its second hypothesis gives $h(r(x)[\mathcal O_C])=r(x)[C]$. Thus $h(x)=r(x)[C]$, proving the displayed formula and uniqueness on all virtual classes. [A1, F1, F3, F5, given, algebra]

2.1 By [F2], restriction to the stated $V$ is injective and sends $[C]$ to $[C\cap V]$. Hence the assumed generic restriction of $g(y)$ equals the restriction of $r(q(y))[C]$, so these elements already agree on $U$. Equivalently, transport $g$ along $q$ and the positive target identification to a homomorphism $h:G_0(C)\to\mathbb Z[C]$. The dimension-normalized coefficient kills the closed-point classes and sends $[\mathcal O_C]$ to $[C]$ by [F1], so step 1.1 gives the same equality. For a supported perfect-complex class in [F6], [F3] identifies $r(q([E]))$ with exactly the finite alternating generic-length sum in the Statement. Its value on $i_*\mathcal O_C[0]$ is $+1$. This retains the positive sign but does not infer it from an unnamed group isomorphism. [A1, F1, F2, F3, F5, F6, step 1.1, given] ∎


## The EM weight-one Picard normalization is positive identity

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-weight-one-positive-picard) · level 19


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and the fixed compatible universe and site-size bounds of the supplied constructions.

On Sm_k for any field k, write S=Ω∞Fil¹K and identify Ω∞gr¹K with $BG_m$ by the canonical EM c₁ comparison. Let κ:$BG_m$→S lift L↦[L]−1. Then the based map q₁κ:$BG_m$→$BG_m$ is identity as a map of Nisnevich stacks, hence on both the Picard classes and their automorphism units. This identifies the sign of the actual projection and not merely its target group.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] Assume the Axiom of Choice as formulated in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and the stated universe and site-size bounds. They are inherited when applying the determinant/one-type input in [F2], the motivic tower, derived evaluation and spectral indexing in [F3], the lift-space helper in [F4], and the standard-smooth, affine quasi-compactness and torsor inputs in [F5] and [F6] at steps 1.1–1.2. Use the nested universes $U\in V\in W$, $\omega\in U$, and scheme/site bounds of [F2], with closure as in [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention); their existence is separately assumed. The stack comparison is on a universe-bounded smooth big Nisnevich site. For the weight-zero derived evaluation in [F3], use the same bounded general cdh site of qcqs prime-field schemes as the EM comparison in [F1], small in the larger universe, and evaluate at each represented smooth finite-type $k$-scheme. Apply the derived-sections interface to this site itself; do not replace it by a finite-type cdh site or a small Nisnevich site. Frame torsors use the compatible small Nisnevich sites of [F6]. The Laurent-unit calculation and the integer sign test add no further choice principle.

**Given:** The normalized maps and the field hypothesis in the Statement. Maps and homotopies involving raw $K$ or its filtration stages are taken in pointed coherent presheaves of spaces on the bounded category $\mathrm{Sm}_k$. The source $B\mathbb G_m$ and the discrete target identified in [F3] are Nisnevich sheaves; the final endomorphism of $B\mathbb G_m$ is a map of Nisnevich stacks. Assume Choice and the universe and site-size bounds in [A1].

**Goal:** Identify the chosen lift with the normalized lift, then prove the identity on the whole line-bundle stack.

[F1] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations) supplies the particular pointed lift $a$ of $L\mapsto 1-[L]$, the canonical identification $\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^1K\simeq B\mathbb G_m$, and $q_1(-a)(\mathcal O(-1))=c_1(\mathcal O(-1))$. It also fixes $\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbb P^1_k)=\mathbb Z$ with generator $c_1(\mathcal O(1))$. This fact does not assert that an arbitrary lift is $-a$.

[F2] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input) supplies the fully faithful classification of pointed connected one-types by sheaves of groups. In particular pointed maps $B\mathbb G_m\to B\mathbb G_m$ correspond to sheaf-group endomorphisms of $\mathbb G_m$. Nisnevich sheaves form the full subcategory of presheaves satisfying descent: between two sheaves the mapping space, including pointed homotopies, is the presheaf mapping space. Apply this classification only to the resulting endomorphism of $B\mathbb G_m$.

[F3] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank) identifies the actual $q_0$ with rank on smooth finite-type schemes over any field. [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) supplies the actual coherent decreasing EM tower restricted from the prime field, with $\operatorname{Fil}^0K=K$; here the field is the arbitrary field $k$ in the Statement. Its Facts [F2] and Proof 1.1 at stage zero, in [lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence](item-lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence), supply the natural spectral fiber sequence $\operatorname{Fil}^1K\to K\xrightarrow{q_0}\operatorname{gr}^0K$, retaining its coherent zero composite. For every smooth finite-type $X/k$, [F1] supplies the canonical equivalence of its last term with the Eilenberg–Mac Lane realization of $C_X=R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)$. On precisely the bounded cdh site in [A1], [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) defines this derived evaluation and gives $H^n_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)=0$ for every $n<0$, because $\mathbb Z$ is an ordinary abelian sheaf placed in degree zero. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) then gives $\pi_i\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)$ for every integer $i$. Thus all positive homotopy groups of its zeroth space vanish, while its component group is the locally constant integer functions by [F1]. These natural identifications make $\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^0K$ the discrete locally constant integer sheaf. No concentration of the entire derived-sections complex in degree zero, or comparison of cdh and Nisnevich sites, is asserted.

[F4] [lem-adjudicate-fd6358d333ef-spectrum-lifts-to-discrete-target](item-lem-adjudicate-fd6358d333ef-spectrum-lifts-to-discrete-target) proves that $\Omega^\infty$ preserves the defining spectral fiber and that pointed mapping spaces carry it to a homotopy pullback. For a fixed pointed $f$ with nullhomotopic composite into a zero-truncated target, its full space of lifts, including homotopies over $f$, is contractible. Here use $\mathcal X=\operatorname{Fun}(N(\mathrm{Sm}_k)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal S)$, where $N(\mathrm{Sm}_k)$ is the nerve of the bounded smooth-scheme category and $\mathcal S$ is the category of spaces in the compatible universes. Thus objects are coherent space-valued presheaves, rather than presheaves assumed to satisfy descent. The finite-limit and spectral-object hypotheses for this application are verified in step 1.1.

[F5] A field has the axioms of [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field). Write $k[t,t^{-1}]$ for finite sums $\sum_i a_it^i$ with $i\in\mathbb Z$, multiplied by $t^it^j=t^{i+j}$; equivalently it is $k[t,s]/(ts-1)$ with $s=t^{-1}$. [math:thm-morphisms-into-affine-scheme-global-sections](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-morphisms-into-affine-scheme-global-sections) supplies the natural correspondence between morphisms to an affine scheme and ring maps into global sections. [math:def-ag-standard-smooth-algebra](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-ag-standard-smooth-algebra) and [math:thm-ag-standard-smooth-geometric-regularity](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-ag-standard-smooth-geometric-regularity) supply the Jacobian presentation criterion for smoothness in [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes). Here $\mathrm{Sm}_k$ denotes smooth finite-type $k$-schemes, with all $k$-morphisms; finite type has the meaning of [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism). Under [A1], [math:cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-affine-scheme-quasi-compact) supplies quasi-compactness of an affine spectrum. [math:cor-contravariant-yoneda-lemma](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-contravariant-yoneda-lemma) supplies evaluation at the identity, including its inverse formula, for natural transformations from a represented presheaf. Apply it in the fixed universe-bounded category of smooth $k$-schemes.

[F6] [lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors](item-lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors) supplies $\mathbb G_m(U)=\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_U)^\times$, the frame-torsor identification of line bundles, effective descent from their transition units, and the tensor-product Picard group law on the compatible small Nisnevich sites. Thus the stack notation in [F2] retains frame isomorphisms. Define $L^{\otimes 0}=\mathcal O$ and negative tensor powers using the dual line.

### Proof
1.1 Put $T=\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^0K$ and $f:B\mathbb G_m\to\Omega^\infty K$, $L\mapsto[L]-1$. For every smooth finite-type $X/k$ and every $i>0$, the comparison, indexing and negative-degree vanishing in [F3] give $\pi_iT(X)=\pi_i\operatorname{gr}^0K(X)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(X,\mathbb Z)=0$. The component identification in [F1] and [F3] is natural in $X$, so $T$ is the discrete locally constant integer sheaf. Work in the presheaf infinity-category $\mathcal X$ of [F4]. Its terminal object is the constant point. For presheaf maps $A\to C\leftarrow B$, form at each test scheme the space of triples consisting of an element of $A$, an element of $B$, and a path between their images in $C$, with restriction maps induced by those of $A,B,C$. A coherent map from any presheaf into this construction is precisely a pair of maps into $A,B$ with a coherent homotopy between their composites into $C$; this is the pullback mapping-space universal property. Hence these pointwise pullbacks and the terminal object give finite limits in $\mathcal X$. Its mapping spaces are small in the chosen larger universe by [A1]. Currying a spectral presheaf gives a reduced excisive functor from finite pointed spaces to $\mathcal X$: reduction and the required pullback squares can both be checked at each test scheme by the pointwise construction. Thus the supplied coherent spectral presheaves are spectrum objects in the sense of [F4]. The actual tower and stage-zero fiber sequence in [F3] meet the spectral fiber hypothesis of [F4]. Finally, a map into a presheaf of discrete spaces is a compatible assignment of component maps; its higher homotopies, if they exist, are unique. Consequently its mapping spaces are discrete, so $T$ is zero-truncated in $\mathcal X$. The rank identification in [F3] makes $q_0f$ the zero map, since every line bundle has rank one. Apply [F4] in $\mathcal X$, with source $B\mathbb G_m$. It identifies $S$ with the fiber over zero of $\Omega^\infty K\to T$, and identifies the space of coherent natural lifts of this fixed $f$, including homotopies over $f$, with the paths from $q_0f$ to zero in $\operatorname{Map}_*(B\mathbb G_m,T)$. Since the two endpoints agree, the lift space is contractible. The later comparison of maps from $B\mathbb G_m$ to itself is also a comparison of sheaf maps by the full-subcategory observation in [F2]. [F1, F2, F3, F4, given, A1]

1.2 By [F2], $q_1\kappa$ corresponds to a sheaf-group endomorphism $h:\mathbb G_m\to\mathbb G_m$. Put $R=k[t,s]/(ts-1)=k[t,t^{-1}]$. The derivative of $ts-1$ with respect to $s$ is the unit $t$ in $R$, so the finite presentation and standard-smooth criterion in [F5] make $G=\operatorname{Spec}R$ smooth over $k$. This affine scheme is quasi-compact by [F5]; the displayed finite generation of $R$ over $k$ gives local finite type, hence finite type by the definition in [F5]. Thus $G$ is an object of $\mathrm{Sm}_k$. The affine-target correspondence in [F5] identifies $k$-morphisms $U\to G$ with units of $\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_U)$: a ring map sends $t$ to a unit $u$ and $s$ necessarily to $u^{-1}$, and each unit defines exactly that map. The base-$k$ condition says that the ring map restricts to the given structure map on $k$. Thus $G$ represents the underlying sheaf in [F6] on $\mathrm{Sm}_k$. By the contravariant Yoneda bijection in [F5], $h$ is determined by $h_G(t)\in R^\times$. Every unit of $R$ is $bt^n$ for $b\in k^\times$ and $n\in\mathbb Z$: for two nonzero finite Laurent sums the largest and smallest exponents of their product are the sums of the respective extreme exponents, because products of nonzero field coefficients are nonzero. Their widths are nonnegative and additive, so a product equal to one forces both widths to be zero. Evaluation at the identity $t=1$ and preservation of the group identity give $b=1$. Yoneda's inverse formula now gives $h_U(u)=u^n$ for every $U$ and every unit $u$, without assuming $k$ infinite. For local frames $e_j=g_{ij}e_i$ of a line bundle, the pushforward torsor has transitions $h(g_{ij})=g_{ij}^n$. These are precisely the transitions of $L^{\otimes n}$, with dual powers when $n<0$; frame descent and the tensor law in [F6] identify the resulting line bundle with that tensor power. This proves the power-map assertion on Picard classes while [F2] retains the entire pointed stack map. [F2, F5, F6, given, A1]

2.1 Inversion in the infinite loop space sends the particular lift $a$ in [F1] to a pointed lift $-a$ of $f$. Both the specified $\kappa$ and $-a$ are thus lifts of the same fixed map. By step 1.1 they agree up to pointed homotopy over $\Omega^\infty K$. Consequently the normalized evaluation from [F1] applies to $q_1\kappa$, giving $q_1\kappa(\mathcal O(-1))=c_1(\mathcal O(-1))$. [F1, step 1.1]

3.1 Apply step 1.2 to $\mathcal O(-1)$ on $\mathbb P^1_k$. Step 2.1 says its image is $\mathcal O(-1)$, while step 1.2 gives $\mathcal O(-n)$. The specified infinite cyclic Picard group in [F1] therefore forces $n=1$. Full faithfulness in [F2] identifies the whole pointed stack map $q_1\kappa$ with the identity, including its action $u\mapsto u$ on automorphism units. The sign is inherited from the actual normalized projection $1-[\mathcal O(-1)]\mapsto+c_1(\mathcal O(1))$, rather than chosen through a new target identification. [F1, F2, step 2.1, step 1.2] ∎


## Actual first-Chern and determinant maps of the first EM layer

[Open item](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-first-chern-and-k-one-determinant) · level 20


### Statement
Assume the Axiom of Choice and fixed universe bounds for the supplied perfect K-model and derived sections. For each scheme use the small Nisnevich site specified by the torsor comparison: a sufficiently small full subcategory of étale schemes containing the terminal scheme and closed under the fibre products and Zariski refinements used in descent. Work on a compatible universe-bounded smooth big Nisnevich site when comparing stack maps, and restrict to these small sites for derived sections.

Let X be smooth of finite type over any field k. Under the canonical weight-one comparison, the actual first-stage projection induces

$$\widetilde K_0(X)=\pi_0Fil^1K(X)\longrightarrow H^{2,1}_{mot}(X)=\operatorname{Pic}(X),\qquad x\longmapsto c_1(x)=\det(x),$$

and

$$K_1(X)=\pi_1Fil^1K(X)\longrightarrow H^{1,1}_{mot}(X)=\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times,\qquad z\longmapsto\det(z).$$

The first determinant/Pic map is the actual natural perfect-complex determinant map, with the alternating graded-line convention, and on [E]−rank(E) it is the determinant line of E. It is not inferred by comparing Picard groups only on affine trivializations. No smooth projection is identified here with a singular motivic projection without a separate functorial comparison.

### Facts & Assumptions
[A1] Assume the Axiom of Choice as formulated in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and the stated universe and site-size bounds. Use compatible nested Grothendieck universes $U\in V\in W$ with $\omega\in U$, with the closure convention of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention); their existence is separately assumed. Retain the supplied perfect-model, torsor and derived-section bounds. For the weight-zero comparison use the very same universe-bounded general qcqs prime-field cdh site used by [F1], small in the larger universe, with its specified scheme objects; do not replace it by a small Nisnevich or finite-type site. Stack comparison uses the compatible smooth big Nisnevich site, and line-bundle evaluation uses the stated small sites. Choice is inherited by the enhanced-model and spectral inputs in [F2] and [F5], by derived sections in [F5], and by the torsor-section selections in [F3]. These uses occur at steps 1.1–2.1.

**Given:** Choice, the stated universe and site-size bounds, the smooth scheme, the actual EM projection and its canonical gr¹ comparison.

**Goal:** Identify both homotopy-group maps with the fixed first-Chern/determinant operations.

[F0] The field and scheme meanings are those of [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field) and [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme). A $k$-scheme is a scheme with structure morphism $X\to\operatorname{Spec}k$, as in [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base); smoothness and finite type refer to that morphism, using [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes) and [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism). The rank supplier in [F1] proves its qcqs domain check for these schemes in its Facts [F0] and step 1.1. Perfect complexes, the actual perfect K-space, its zero pointing, graded lines, the Euler-rank map and the line-bundle stack are specified in [F2]. The line-bundle, Picard group and unit conventions are those in [F3]. Write $\widetilde K_0$ for the kernel of the locally constant rank map. The complexes and cohomological notation $H^{p,q}_{\mathrm{mot}}(X)=H^p(\mathbb Z(q)_{\mathrm{mot}}(X))$ use [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology), with the arbitrary field specified here.

[F1] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank) identifies $\pi_0q_0$ with the locally constant rank function and identifies the first-stage groups. [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-em-map-normalizations) supplies the actual natural weight-zero equivalence $\operatorname{gr}^0K\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cdh}}(-,\mathbb Z)$, with $\mathbb Z$ in degree zero and $H^0$ equal to locally constant integers on smooth finite-type field schemes. It also identifies $\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^1K$ with the actual line-bundle stack $B\mathbb G_m$ via $c_1$, and supplies the natural pointed lift $\kappa=-a$ of $L\mapsto[L]-1$, retaining line isomorphisms.

[F2] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-determinant-one-type-input) supplies the canonical perfect-complex determinant/Pic map and its first-Postnikov description, with local $\pi_0K=\mathbb Z$ and π₁K=$G_m$ through the actual rank/determinant maps.

[F3] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-weight-one-positive-picard](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-weight-one-positive-picard) proves q₁κ=id as a stack map under [A1]. Under the same assumptions, [lem-unit-section-of-k-one-determinant](item-lem-unit-section-of-k-one-determinant) supplies the actual K₁ determinant and its section through the specified enhanced realizations. [lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors](item-lem-nisnevich-line-bundle-torsors) fixes the frame-torsor conventions for Pic and units on the specified small Nisnevich sites; its inverse uses Choice to select a set-indexed family of local torsor sections, followed by effective descent. These inherited uses enter steps 1.1–2.1.

[F4] In [F2], the connected rank fiber is formed after ordinary space-valued Nisnevich sheafification. Its left exact sheafification preserves the rank-fiber square and its unit has the mapping universal property into sheaves. Thus a comparison proved on that sheafified fiber must be precomposed with this unit before it is evaluated on the original perfect K-space. No commutation of truncation with global sections is asserted.

[F5] [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration) supplies the actual tower and its stage-zero equivalence with $K$. [lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence](item-lem-filtered-stage-exact-sequence) supplies its defining spectral fiber sequence $\operatorname{Fil}^1K\to K\xrightarrow{q_0}\operatorname{gr}^0K$. On the same cdh site of [A1], [def-derived-sections-on-sites](item-def-derived-sections-on-sites) gives $H^n_{\mathrm{cdh}}(T,\mathbb Z)=0$ for $n<0$. [lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index](item-lem-eilenberg-maclane-shift-index) gives $\pi_i\operatorname{gr}^0K(T)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(T,\mathbb Z)$. Use the homotopy-group and sphere-suspension conventions of [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) and the pointed adjunction $\Sigma^\infty\dashv\Omega^\infty$ of [thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input](item-thm-author-e0b0bf18ed2b-stable-enhancement-input). In particular, applying that adjunction to pointed spheres identifies $\pi_i\Omega^\infty E$ with $\pi_iE$ for $i\geq0$ (components at $i=0$). [lem-adjudicate-fd6358d333ef-spectrum-lifts-to-discrete-target](item-lem-adjudicate-fd6358d333ef-spectrum-lifts-to-discrete-target) proves preservation of the defining spectral fiber by $\Omega^\infty$, for any infinity-category with finite limits. Apply it to spaces object by object; naturality gives the corresponding fiber identity of presheaves.

[F6] Reference 0 supplies recognition of discrete spaces from vanishing positive homotopy groups and the universal factorization of a map into a discrete space through the component set. Apply this objectwise to spaces; no hypercompleteness or homotopy-sheaf detection principle is used.

### External input
Import only [F6] from [Lurie's Higher Topos Theory](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HTT.pdf), at reference 0's locators and hypotheses. The rank-target identification, fiber comparison and determinant equality below are local deductions from the declared suppliers and this foundational input.

### Proof
**Given:** The actual first-stage projection and line-class lift, the canonical weight-one comparison, and the Choice and compatible site and universe hypotheses above.

**Goal:** Identify both original, globally evaluated maps by first comparing their factorizations into the line-bundle sheaf.

1.1 Work on the bounded smooth finite-type $k$-site of [F0], before sheafification. Put $D=\Omega^\infty\operatorname{gr}^0K$ and let $T$ be any object. By [F1] and [F5], $\pi_iD(T)=H^{-i}_{\mathrm{cdh}}(T,\mathbb Z)=0$ for every $i>0$. Translation in the infinite loop space identifies the homotopy groups in all components with those at zero, so [F6] makes $D(T)$ discrete. Its canonical component map and the natural $H^0$ identification in [F1] therefore give an objectwise natural equivalence $D\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}$, where $\underline{\mathbb Z}(T)$ is the set of locally constant integer functions. The truncation universal property in [F6] applies to compatible presheaf maps as well: their factors are determined by their component functions. Consequently [F1]'s equality of $\pi_0q_0$ with rank identifies the actual space map $\Omega^\infty q_0$ with rank into this discrete presheaf, not just its induced group map. The target is the locally constant integer sheaf of [F2]. Apply [F5]'s fiber-preservation result objectwise to the supplied spectral fiber sequence; its naturality retains all presheaf restrictions. It gives $S_0:=\Omega^\infty\operatorname{Fil}^1K\simeq\operatorname{fib}_0(\Omega^\infty K\to\underline{\mathbb Z})$, naturally with its map to the original K-space. Now let $a$ be the ordinary space-valued Nisnevich sheafification of [F2] and [F4]. Left exactness and the rank-map comparison therefore identify $\widehat S=aS_0$ with the connected sheaf rank fiber in [F2]. Let $\eta:S_0\to\widehat S$ be the unit. Let $d:S_0\to B\mathbb G_m$ be the restriction of the actual determinant map of [F2]. Both $d$ and the actual $q_1:S_0\to B\mathbb G_m$ factor through $\eta$, since their target is already a sheaf. Write their factors as $\widehat q_1,\widehat d$ and put $\widehat\kappa=\eta\kappa$. The supplied determinant map makes $\widehat d_*:\pi_1\widehat S\to\mathbb G_m$ an isomorphism. The lift $\kappa$ lies over the actual line-class map $L\mapsto[L]-1$ of [F1], retaining line isomorphisms: an automorphism $u$ of the trivial line maps to its K-theory automorphism loop. By the unit-section and determinant normalization of [F2] and [F3], $\widehat d_*\widehat\kappa_*=1$. Also $\widehat q_1\widehat\kappa=q_1\kappa\simeq1$ by [F3]. Hence $\widehat\kappa_*=\widehat d_*^{-1}$ and $\widehat q_{1,*}=\widehat d_*$. Both factors have the pointed connected one-type $B\mathbb G_m$ as target, so truncation and the fully faithful one-type classification in [F2] identify them as pointed maps from $\widehat S$. Precomposing their homotopy with $\eta$ gives $q_1\simeq d$ on the original presheaf. This step uses neither sheafhood of the original K-space nor a claim that its global sections are connected. [A1, F0, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6]

2.1 Evaluate this single homotopy of original maps at $X$, then take components and fundamental groups. By [F1], the source groups are the actual reduced $K_0$ and $K_1$, with the canonical stage identifications stated there. The canonical weight-one comparison and [F3] identify the target groups with $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ and $\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times$. By [F2], the determinant's component map is the class of the alternating determinant line of a perfect complex; at rank zero this is its ordinary determinant/Picard class, and on $[E]-\operatorname{rank}(E)$ it is $\det(E)$. Its loop map is the actual K-one determinant of [F3], normalized by matrix automorphisms and the unit section. These give exactly the two displayed formulas with the positive normalization fixed by $q_1\kappa\simeq1$. Pullback of the homotopy along the sheafification unit occurred before evaluation, so the conclusion retains global line-bundle gluing data. No assertion of global-section invariance under arbitrary sheafification, or comparison with a singular projection, is used. [A1, F0, F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## Smooth rank, first Chern, determinant and supported positive leading cycle

[Open item](item-lem-smooth-leading-cycle-normalization) · level 21


### Statement
Assume AC and the compatible universe and Nisnevich-site size bounds of the supplied constructions, and use the specified perfect-complex nonconnective K-spectrum and its actual EM motivic filtration. For a smooth finite-type scheme $X$ over a field $k$, the weight-zero projection is the locally constant rank function. The first-stage projection gives
$$\widetilde K_0(X)=\pi_0F^1K(X)\longrightarrow H^{2,1}(X)=\operatorname{Pic}(X),\quad x\longmapsto c_1(x)=\det(x),$$
and
$$K_1(X)=\pi_1F^1K(X)\longrightarrow H^{1,1}(X)=\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times,\quad z\longmapsto\det(z).$$
Here reduced $K_0$ means the kernel of the locally constant rank map, component by component; determinant on perfect complexes uses its alternating graded-line convention.

For the supported assertion let $k$ be perfect (in particular the article's algebraically closed field qualifies), let $U$ be smooth of finite type and pure dimension three over $k$, and let $i:C\hookrightarrow U$ be an integral codimension-two closed curve. Neither smoothness nor normality of $C$, nor global quasi-projectivity of $U$, is required. Write
$$K_C(U)=\operatorname{fib}(K(U)\to K(U\setminus C)),\quad F^j_CK(U)=\operatorname{fib}(F^jK(U)\to F^jK(U\setminus C)).$$
The actual augmentation $\epsilon_2:F^2_CK(U)\to K_C(U)$ is an equivalence, and the supported layer is canonically
$$\pi_0\operatorname{gr}^2_CK(U)=H_C^{4,2}(U)=H_0z_C^2(U,\bullet)=\mathbb Z[C].$$
This is the supported cycle group, not the image of $[C]$ in ambient Chow cohomology. For $\xi\in\pi_0F^2_CK(U)$ let $E$ denote its augmented class in the actual supported $K_0$, using support-category localization. Then its specified stage-to-layer projection is
$$p_2(\xi)=\left(\sum_n(-1)^n\ell_{\mathcal O_{U,\eta}} H^n(E)_\eta\right)[C],\qquad \eta=\operatorname{generic}(C).$$
The notation for a virtual class $E$ means the additive extension of this formula from supported perfect complexes. Equivalently $p_2\circ(\pi_0\epsilon_2)^{-1}$ on supported $K_0$ is alternating generic length times the positive cycle. In particular $i_*\mathcal O_C[0]$ maps to $+[C]$, and every closed-point-supported class maps to zero.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** AC, the stated universe and site-size bounds, the smooth scheme/field hypotheses in each part, and the current augmented tower, support and K-model contracts. The supported part uses a perfect field only; it does not extend the imported classical supported comparison to an imperfect field.

**Goal:** Normalize the actual maps, including the domain and positive generator of the supported weight-two projection.

[A1] The stated Choice and size assumptions are inherited from the determinant and weight-one suppliers in [F1] at step 1.1. They cover the enhanced K-model, the compatible big Nisnevich stack comparison, and the specified small-site frame-torsor and derived-sections constructions. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F1] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-zero-projection-rank) and [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-first-chern-and-k-one-determinant](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-first-chern-and-k-one-determinant) identify the actual weight-zero and weight-one maps, using the canonical EM comparisons and the actual determinant map supplied before the Postnikov identification. The determinant input now specifies true/enhanced cofiber coherence and its Waldhausen-to-current-model bridge; arbitrary ordinary-triangle-diagram functoriality is not used.

[F2] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-lowweight-augmented-slice-comparison) supplies the actual augmented EM-to-cdh equivalence on regular Noetherian schemes and its absolute KGL-slice interpretation. Its direct classical-to-EM assertion is on smooth schemes over the prime field; it does not alone identify the relative-$k$ tower for arbitrary $k$. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-augmented-filtered-support-transport) transports specified augmented comparisons to support fibers and their maps. [def-supported-higher-cycle-complex](item-def-supported-higher-cycle-complex) supplies the compatible supported Bloch localization fiber.

[F3] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-curve-supported-bloch-bottom) computes the supported layers in weights zero and one and the positive degree-zero cycle in weight two. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-second-stage-inverse](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-second-stage-inverse) therefore supplies the actual equivalence $\epsilon_2$ and target, without using any later second-stage article consumer.

[F4] [thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-slice-positive-cycle-input](item-thm-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-slice-positive-cycle-input) supplies the actual supported inverse-augmentation slice projection and its generic coefficient on a smooth quasi-projective scheme over a perfect field. Its coefficient is restriction to $G_0(k(\eta))=\mathbb Z$ by dimension, with positive residue-field generator.

[F5] [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-euler-generic-coordinate](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-supported-euler-generic-coordinate), [prop-supported-k-zero-devissage](item-prop-supported-k-zero-devissage) and [prop-perfect-k-localization-input](item-prop-perfect-k-localization-input) identify that coefficient in the current support-spectrum model with alternating generic length. [lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-positive-generic-cycle-detection](item-lem-author-16f30f6b95cb-positive-generic-cycle-detection) makes this coefficient a detection of the full supported degree-zero map. [def-cohomology-with-support](item-def-cohomology-with-support) fixes the layer shift $\pi_0\operatorname{gr}^2_C=H_C^{4,2}$.

[F6] [lem-adjudicate-37c8a46c3b6b-open-natural-cycle-layers](item-lem-adjudicate-37c8a46c3b6b-open-natural-cycle-layers) supplies the missing actual open-natural graded comparison for every nonnegative weight over the perfect field, including the shift and the original raw Bloch model. It applies simultaneously to $U$ and $U\setminus C$, before taking their restriction fiber. The raw unit is the one in [lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent](item-lem-adjudicate-f6007757a8ac-raw-simplicial-descent); that unit alone would not construct a motivic comparison. The exact source-version qualification of [F2], including EM v1 Remark3.6’s then-in-preparation input, remains binding.


[F7] The field, structure morphism, smoothness, finite-type, perfectness, integral closed support and open complement use [math:def-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-field), [math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base), [math:def-smooth-morphism-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-morphism-schemes), [math:def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-finite-type-and-finite-type-morphism), [math:def-perfect-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-perfect-field), [math:def-integral-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-integral-scheme), [math:def-closed-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-closed-immersion-schemes) and [math:def-open-immersion-schemes](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-open-immersion-schemes). A curve has dimension one; pure dimension three means every irreducible component has dimension three, using [math:def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dimension-noetherian-topological-space). Codimension two means $\dim\mathcal O_{U,\eta}=2$, with ring dimension as in [math:def-krull-dimension-of-a-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-krull-dimension-of-a-ring). The point $\eta$ has closure $|C|$, as in [math:def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-generic-point-irreducible-closed-subset). The geometric clauses in [F5] verify regularity, Noetherianity, qcqs and quasi-compact open complements, so the current support-localization hypotheses hold without separatedness. For the parenthetical algebraically closed case, [math:def-algebraically-closed-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-algebraically-closed-field) gives a root of each irreducible nonconstant polynomial, and [math:cor-factor-theorem-over-a-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-factor-theorem-over-a-commutative-ring) makes that polynomial linear. A linear polynomial has no repeated root by [math:def-repeated-root-and-separable-polynomial](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-repeated-root-and-separable-polynomial), proving perfectness in the stated definition.

[F8] [def-scheme-perfect-complexes](item-def-scheme-perfect-complexes) defines perfect objects, their cohomology sheaves and support. [def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface](item-def-nonconnective-k-spectrum-interface) fixes the current category-defined K-spectrum and its homotopy groups; the fiber identification is the actual localization in [F5]. [def-motivic-filtration](item-def-motivic-filtration), [def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum](item-def-decreasing-filtered-spectrum) and [def-stable-spectra-interface](item-def-stable-spectra-interface) fix the actual augmented tower, graded cofibers, spectral fibers, suspensions and homotopy groups. [def-motivic-cohomology](item-def-motivic-cohomology) and [lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization](item-lem-adjudicate-d5306176fce5-coherent-realization) fix the motivic complexes and coherent realization, with the layer/cohomology indexing of [F5]. [math:def-picard-group-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-picard-group-scheme) defines Picard groups by invertible sheaves under tensor product; the actual determinant operation and its graded-line signs are supplied by [F1]. Units $\Gamma(X,\mathcal O_X)^\times$ are globally invertible sections. Length $\ell_R(M)$ counts factors in a finite composition series, with zero length for the zero module, as in [math:def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-composition-series-and-length-of-a-module); its finiteness here is supplied by [F5]. Use the universe closure convention of [math:def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-grothendieck-universe-closure-convention), with existence and compatible enlargements assumed as in the Statement.

[F9] By [math:def-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme), every point of $U$, in particular $\eta$, has an affine open neighbourhood. An affine open neighbourhood carries its restricted structure sheaf, as in [math:def-affine-open-subscheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-open-subscheme). The perfect field and source hypotheses of [F4] are retained.
